0

Upon thy brow the autumn shadows weep,  
My heart, a withered rose, doth silent pine.  
The golden fields in mournful slumber sleep,  
Whilst fading sun resigns its last design.  
The whispering wind recounts the summer's song,  
Its echoes tangled in thy sable hair.  
The days grow short, the nights are deep and long—  
All nature sighs beneath the cooling air.  
Yet in thy gaze, a flicker still remains,  
A spark that braves the dying of the light.  
Though time encroaches, breaking gentle chains,  
Thy spirit glimmers bravely through the night.  
  So let the seasons pass, as seasons do,  
  For love endures, eternal, ever true.

1

Within my breast doth storm a silent sea,  
Where love, like midnight fire, consumeth me.  
The moon’s pale gaze upon my longing bleeds,  
While hope, a trembling bird, in shadow feeds.  
Each sigh I breathe is salt upon the tide,  
That yearns to break but never dares to part,  
And secret waves of passion, long denied,  
Ebb gently on the shores within my heart.  
Yet in the hush, thy memory’s silver light  
Illuminates the darkness of my soul,  
Transforming sorrow’s blackest, starless night  
To golden dawns where broken spirits whole.  
  Though tempests rage and silent seas may swell,  
  My love, like fire, within, shall ever dwell.

2

Upon the dusk where sorrow weaves its thread,  
I find thee, star, yet grasp thee not in hand,  
Thy silver gaze above my aching head  
Casts gentle light upon the shadowed land.  
Thou art the dream that trembles in the night,  
A hope aloft beyond my earthbound reach,  
A silent guide whose whispers, soft and slight,  
Reveal what joy nor pain can ever teach.  
Though distance parts us—sky and longing heart—  
Thy shimmer stirs the courage in my breast,  
And though we dwell in worlds so far apart,  
Thy constancy commands my soul to rest.  
  So let me love thee, though we stand afar:  
  My sorrow eased by longing for thy star.

3

My heart, a lantern dimmed by thy disdain,  
Burns on within the fog of thy neglect,  
Its trembling flame persists despite the pain,  
Yet finds no warmth thy coldness to deflect.  
Each night I wander through the silent mist,  
Where memories like ghosts about me fall,  
Recalling every touch, each word I missed,  
And longing for thy voice’s distant call.  
Yet hope, a stubborn weed, breaks through the stone,  
Defying frost that settles on my soul,  
It whispers soft that I am not alone,  
Though shadows gather, seeking to control.  
  If thou wouldst turn thine eyes on me once more,  
  My lantern’s light would blaze as ne’er before.

4

Thou art the moon that lights my lonely sea,  
Yet ne’er dost turn thy gaze in search of me.  
Thy silver beams upon the waters play,  
But in thy realm, I find no place to be.  
Aloft thou sail’st through every clouded night,  
Untroubled by the storms that toss my soul.  
My longing heart, adrift and void of light,  
Is captive to thy pull I can’t control.  
The tides obey thee, silent in their pain,  
As do I, drawn by hope I scarce confess.  
For though I reach, the space between remains,  
A gulf of dreams the daylight can’t redress.  
  Yet still I watch thy journey through the blue,  
  And love thee more for never knowing who.

5

Upon the shore where moonlight weeps its gold,  
I wait, a shadow cast by love’s decree,  
The restless tide recalls the tales once told  
Of hearts that wandered far across the sea.  
The night wind whispers secrets through the sand,  
Entreating dreams to drift upon the foam;  
I stretch my longing fingers to the land  
That lies beyond the reach of wave and loam.  
The stars above, in silent counsel, gleam,  
Reflecting hopes too fragile to confess;  
Yet in their gaze, I find a gentle dream,  
A promise veiled in sorrow and caress.  
  Though dawn may steal the silver from the bay,  
  My heart remains where moonlit waters play.

6

Upon the midnight glass my soul is cast,  
A lantern lost within its shadowed keep,  
The silent hours like drifting phantoms passed,  
And dreams unbidden rouse me from my sleep.  
The silver moon in trembling silence gleams,  
Her borrowed light a shroud upon the floor,  
She weaves the night with threads of haunted dreams  
And bids my spirit wander evermore.  
The ticking time, a distant, muffled bell,  
Reminds me life is fleeting, swift, and strange;  
Yet in this hush where shadowed secrets dwell,  
The heart recalls its longing for a change.  
  So when the dawn dispels the sable gloom,  
  May hope arise and banish night’s perfume.

7

My soul, a vessel tossed on love’s dark sea,  
Doth wage with tempests born of thine and me.  
The moon, pale witness to our midnight cries,  
Draws tidal grief from salt within mine eyes.  
Upon the prow, I clutch thy whispered name,  
While hope and dread like sirens sing their part.  
The stars, indifferent, flicker all the same,  
Yet chart no course for this bewildered heart.  
Each wave that breaks recalls the joy we knew,  
Its foam dissolves to memories grown cold.  
Yet still I sail, though faith be thin and few,  
On promises that once were bright and bold.  
  If gentle winds should bear thee back to me,  
  My harbor’s light shall burn eternally.

8

My soul, a ship adrift on moonless seas,  
Doth yearn for harbors love may ne'er reveal;  
The winds of fate, capricious as they please,  
Do churn the waves with secrets they conceal.  
Upon these depths, reflected stars appear,  
Yet distant, cold, they mock my fevered quest.  
Each beacon dims, dissolves to naught but fear,  
And shadows coil like serpents in my chest.  
Yet hope, that phantom lantern in the mist,  
Still glimmers faint beyond the tempest's roar.  
It bids me dream of ports I've never kissed,  
Of gentle hands upon a distant shore.  
  Though lost, I sail—undaunted, I believe—  
  That love awaits where moonlight weaves reprieve.

9

My soul, a lantern dimmed by thine neglect,  
Still seeks thy sun, though night is all I find.  
In silent halls where echoes recollect  
The golden warmth thy absence left behind.  
The shadows crowd the corners of my mind,  
Yet hope, a stubborn flame, refuses death;  
It burns, though battered by the ruthless wind,  
And whispers of thy name with every breath.  
I pace the hours, counting each regret,  
A pilgrim lost upon a starless sea,  
Ensnared in webs my longing can't forget—  
The ghost of joy that once belonged to me.  
  Yet if thy favor graced my heart once more,  
  I'd blaze anew, brighter than e'er before.

10

My soul, a silent lyre in shadow laid,  
Doth pine for thee as night for absent dawn,  
Within the hush where tender hopes do fade,  
I seek the promise daylight smiles upon.  
Each hour apart, a petal lost to air,  
Each breath, a whisper calling out thy name;  
Desire’s ghost doth haunt the hollow stair,  
And all my dreams with longing set aflame.  
Yet still I wait, as patient stars await  
The golden chariot’s slow return to sky,  
Enchained by love, resigned to fickle fate,  
Unwilling yet to let my spirit die.  
  O grant me light, or gentle mercy send—  
  Let union heal what absence cannot mend.

11

Within the twilight chamber of my soul,  
A silent flame for thee doth ever burn,  
Thy memory, like tides, doth ebb and roll  
Upon the shores where weary spirits yearn.  
The hush of night, in velvet silence dressed,  
Unfolds thy name upon the dreaming air,  
And every star that trembles in the west  
Doth paint thy image, radiant and rare.  
Though shadows creep and ancient fears arise,  
My steadfast heart refuses to depart;  
It seeks thy light amid encircling skies  
And finds a haven deep within my heart.  
  So let the world in fleeting darkness fade—  
  My soul’s true dawn is by thy presence made.

12

Within my breast a tempest fierce doth rage,  
Love’s phantom sails upon a sunless tide,  
My heart, a battered ship, endures the cage  
Of longing’s chains, by fickle winds supplied.  
The moon, pale guardian of the sleepless night,  
Reflects the ache that stirs these restless veins;  
Her silver gaze condemns my soul’s delight  
To wander darkened shores beset by pains.  
Yet hope, like dawn, intrudes with gilded fire,  
Dispelling shadows clinging to my mind;  
She weaves with gentle hand the frailest lyre  
And bids my spirit leave despair behind.  
  O tempest wild, be calmed by morning’s breath,  
  And grant my love the strength to conquer death.

13

Within my breast a storm of longing brews,  
Love’s ghostly shadow darkens all my day,  
Each gentle wind recalls thy vanished hues,  
And hope’s bright lantern flickers far away.  
The hours crawl slow beneath this mournful weight,  
While memory’s garden withers, petal-thin;  
I wander lost along the paths of fate,  
Desiring touch I dare not seek again.  
Night’s silver cloak conceals thy whispered name,  
Yet in my dreams thy voice returns to me,  
Rekindling the soft, forbidden flame  
That burns for what the waking cannot see.  
  Though time conspires to keep our hearts apart,  
  Thy absence is the presence in my heart.

14

Within the shadowed chambers of my breast,  
Love wars against the silence of my gloom,  
Its restless sighs deny my heart its rest,  
And passion’s flame illuminates my tomb.  
By night, pale phantoms haunt my troubled mind,  
Their whispers bidding hope to rise or fall;  
Yet in the dark, faint promises I find  
Of morning’s light that waits beyond it all.  
Though sorrow weaves its thorns around my soul,  
And memory’s ghosts in bitter silence dwell,  
Still love persists to make the broken whole,  
And dares to ring the midnight’s hollow bell.  
  O gentle force that will not be suppressed,  
  Thou art both curse and gift within my chest.

15

Upon the silent shore my heart doth break,  
A ship unmoored, adrift on seas of woe,  
The stars bear witness to each risk I take,  
While moonlit tides in mournful rhythm flow.  
The salt-kissed wind recalls thy vanished name,  
Within the hush, thy memory unfolds,  
Each wave repeats the echo of my shame,  
And in the dusk, a tale of loss it holds.  
Yet hope, like dawn, with gentle breath returns,  
Igniting embers in the ashen night;  
My soul, though battered, quietly still yearns  
For sun to pierce the shadow with its light.  
  Though tempests rage within this heart of mine,  
  Love’s compass ever north, to thee, doth shine.

16

Upon the midnight lake my heart doth row,  
A vessel lost where no bright beacon shows.  
The wind in whispers bids the water flow,  
Yet silence deep beneath the blackness grows.  
Above, the moon in spectral silver gleams,  
Its trembling path a thread upon the wave.  
I drift on tides of half-remembered dreams  
And seek the dawn my weary soul doth crave.  
The shadows gather close about my oar,  
Each ripple tells of time that will not stay.  
Though distance veils the long-forgotten shore,  
Hope rises faint, a star to light the way.  
  So through the darkness, steadfast let me steer,  
  Until the morning’s promise shall appear.

17

My heart, a lantern dimmed by hopeless flame,  
Doth wander night’s vast halls in silent woe,  
Each shadow whispers softly of thy name,  
While weary stars attend my steps below.  
The moon, pale guardian of my ceaseless ache,  
Doth pour her silver balm upon my grief,  
Yet dreams, like fleeting mist, at dawn forsake  
The fragile soul that seeks in sleep relief.  
What hope have I, who treads this lonely ground,  
When time conspires to keep our fates apart?  
No sweeter solace in the dark is found  
Than memory’s cruel echo in my heart.  
  Yet still I bear this torch through sorrow’s night,  
  In faith that love may once again burn bright.

18

When Sorrow woos my heart in midnight’s gloom,  
Thy phantom whispers soft within my breast,  
A fleeting fragrance lingers in my room,  
And soothes my weary soul to gentle rest.  
The starlight weaves thy image in the dark,  
A silver shadow dancing on the floor;  
Each silent hour bears thy tender mark,  
And draws me closer, longing evermore.  
Yet dawn betrays the secrets of the night,  
Unveiling empty air where dreams have been;  
Still, hope revives upon the morning light,  
For love’s sweet ghost remains, though none may seen.  
  O, Sorrow, though thy touch is cold and deep,  
  Thy memory is mine, and mine to keep.

19

Oft have I watched fair Love in shadows stray,  
While midnight sighs do haunt my weary breast,  
The silver moon doth steal the warmth of day,  
And leaves my heart with longing unconfessed.  
Her footsteps fade upon the silent floor,  
A whisper caught between the dark and dawn;  
Each memory drifts, a ghost upon the door,  
That hope once bright is now but nearly gone.  
Yet still I court the promise of her eyes,  
Where gentle fires flicker through the gloom,  
And in their depths a thousand mornings rise,  
Dispelling sorrow’s ever-looming doom.  
  Though Love in shadow oft may hide her face,  
  I’ll seek her light, nor falter in the chase.

20

When midnight’s breath doth whisper of thy name,  
My soul, in longing, weeps with silent dread;  
The silver shadows tremble with the same  
Soft ache that haunts the corners of my bed.  
The moonlight spills her secrets on the floor,  
Each beam a memory I dare not speak,  
And through the hush I hear thee evermore  
In every sigh the restless night doth seek.  
The roses wilt beneath the weight of tears,  
Their petals bruised by dreams I cannot quell;  
The ticking hours recount my lonely fears  
And toll the grief my tongue would never tell.  
  Yet hope, a fragile star, remains above—  
  That dawn might wake the world, and wake thy love.

21

O Love, thou wander’st cold through midnight air,  
And weep’st in silence for my heart’s despair.  
Thy silver footsteps brush the frosted ground,  
Yet none but I can hear their mournful sound.  
The moon, a witness, veils her gentle light,  
And shadows gather close to shield my pain.  
My soul, enshrouded deep within the night,  
Calls out for thee, but calls to thee in vain.  
Thy breath is mist upon the trembling pane,  
Thy touch a memory that will not stay.  
Each hour without thee is a lengthened chain  
That binds my hopes and steals the dawn away.  
  Yet still I wander, searching through the gloom,  
  For love’s own dawn to break my spirit’s tomb.

22

When silent Night in sable shroud doth weep,  
My heart, poor wretch, in longing vigil keeps,  
The stars, like watchful eyes, their distance steep,  
Attend my soul while all the city sleeps.  
A silver hush enfolds the dreaming street,  
As moonbeams paint their sorrow on the stone;  
Within the hush, my pulse and shadows meet,  
And I, with yearning, pace the halls alone.  
Yet hope, a trembling flame, resists the gloom,  
Her gentle hand upon my spirit’s brow,  
She scatters seeds of dawn about the room  
And whispers: patience, love shall find thee now.  
  So let the night in weeping darkness stay—  
  For soon, with sun, my sorrows melt away.

23

O mournful Night, why dost thou veil her face,  
Whilst longing Stars in silent sorrow pine?  
The silver moon withdraws her gentle grace,  
And shadows weave their secrets into mine.  
A hush descends upon the restless earth,  
As dreams like wraiths glide softly through the air;  
The weary world forgets the light of mirth,  
Embraced by gloom, enshrouded by despair.  
Yet in the darkness, hope begins to grow,  
A tender bud concealed from mortal sight;  
For dawn prepares her golden eyes to show,  
And banish hence the tyranny of Night.  
  So let me trust the promise of the skies:  
  That darkest hours shall birth the brightest rise.

24

My soul, a shadowed minstrel, weeps in night,  
Whilst Love, in sable robes, denies me light.  
The moon, pale witness to my silent tune,  
Casts silver sorrow on this lonely room.  
Each echo lingers, trembling with my pain,  
As hope dissolves beneath the weight of gloom.  
The roses wilt, their blush betrayed by rain,  
Their petals scattered far from summer’s bloom.  
Yet in the hush, a whisper stirs the air—  
A promise woven through the threads of grief—  
That dawn’s embrace may banish cold despair  
And bring the slumbering heart gentle relief.  
  O, though Love hides behind the shroud of night,  
  Still must I seek her in the coming light.

25

My soul, a vagrant shadow in the night,  
Doth wander lost where love’s pale torches fade,  
It seeks the sun, but shuns the harshest light,  
In haunted realms of memory’s masquerade.  
Upon the moor of longing’s silent plea,  
Each footstep echoes soft with hope’s lament;  
The stars above, indifferent, watch o’er me,  
As time dissolves in dreams that came and went.  
Yet in the hush before the dawn’s embrace,  
A whisper stirs within my weary breast—  
A promise kindled by thy phantom face,  
That bids my restless spirit find its rest.  
  Though shadows twine about this heart of mine,  
  Thy love’s faint glow shall ever, ever shine.

26

My soul, a weeping minstrel in the night,  
Doth wander lost 'twixt longing and despair,  
Its song, a ghostly flame of silver light,  
Ascends in silence, trembling on the air.  
The moon, pale witness to my secret fears,  
Casts down her gaze upon these shadowed lands,  
And bathes my cheeks in sympathetic tears—  
A gentle balm delivered by her hands.  
Yet hope, elusive as the morning mist,  
Slips through my grasp ere I can call it mine;  
Each dream I cherished, fate hath softly kissed,  
And left me yearning for a clearer sign.  
  Still, in the dark, my heart doth softly sing:  
  From deepest sorrow, sweetest joys may spring.

27

My soul, a silent lute in night’s embrace,  
Doth pine for thine, yet finds thee not in dream,  
The moonlight weaves thy shadow on my face,  
And longing drifts upon a silver beam.  
Each hour, the stars rehearse thy gentle name,  
And whisper secrets to the trembling air;  
My heart, a moth, is ever drawn to flame,  
Though hope is frail and solace ever rare.  
The world in slumber holds its breath for thee,  
While time in hushéd footsteps passes by,  
I wander lost within this memory,  
And search for comfort in a distant sky.  
  Yet if thy love should wake at dawn’s first light,  
  My soul would sing anew, dispelling night.

28

O gentle Soul, why dost thou pine in shade,  
Whilst Love, unheeding, turns his golden face?  
Thy silken hopes in silent sorrow laid,  
Soft petals fallen in a dark embrace.  
The day unfolds with laughter not thine own,  
And distant music mocks thy aching breast;  
Yet in the hush, where lonely seeds are sown,  
Desire’s gentle pulse will not be suppressed.  
For though the world doth pass in heedless flight,  
And shadowed hours draw heavy o’er thy heart,  
The dawn, in mercy, brings returning light,  
And bids the dreams that tremble not depart.  
  So lift thy gaze—let hope anew begin,  
  For Love oft enters where the night hath been.

29

My soul, a silent minstrel in the night,  
Doth pine for love that ne’er returns its gaze,  
Upon the velvet hush of fading light,  
It weaves a longing song through shadowed haze.  
Each star, a witness to my quiet woe,  
Adorns the sable dome with silver fire,  
Yet in their gaze, no solace do I know,  
Nor balm to quell this ever-burning pyre.  
The wind, conspiring with my secret pain,  
Whispers her name in sighs across the moor,  
While memories, like softly falling rain,  
Revisit joys that now can be no more.  
  Yet hope, though frail, within my breast remains,  
  To see love rise anew, and break these chains.

30

O Love, thou ghost that haunt’st my silent breast,  
Thy shadowed fingers clutch my heart in woe,  
Like moonlit mist, thou wilt not grant me rest,  
But bid’st my thoughts on wildest tempests go.  
Thy whispers pierce the hush of midnight gloom,  
A dulcet pain that will not be denied,  
Thy presence lingers heavy in my room,  
A secret ache I struggle not to hide.  
Oft have I sought to banish thee in vain,  
Yet sweeter grows thy torment with each day,  
For in thy chains I find a dearer pain  
Than any liberty could e’er convey.  
  So haunt me, Love, until my breath is done—  
  For life is dim without thee, shining sun.

31

O Love, thou art a shadow at my side,  
That walks with me when all the world hath fled,  
A silent wraith whose presence will not hide,  
Yet softly whispers dreams within my head.  
When daylight wanes and hope’s pale embers glow,  
Thy hand I feel, though unseen by the eye;  
Through tempests wild where bitter sorrows blow,  
Thou art the hush beneath the storm’s harsh cry.  
In gardens lost, where memory’s petals fall,  
Thy gentle breath revives the faded bloom;  
Though time may build between us sorrow’s wall,  
Thou linger’st still within the silent room.  
  No sun nor star nor midnight’s sable dome  
  Can banish thee, for thou art always home.

32

When Love doth haunt the chambers of my mind,  
She weaves her sable threads through hope's decay,  
A whisper soft, yet stubbornly unkind,  
That chides the light and beckons night to stay.  
She treads the silent halls with ghostly grace,  
Her fingers cold upon the tender past,  
Yet in her gaze, a thousand dawns I trace,  
Though shadows fall, their beauty cannot last.  
With every breath, she stirs both joy and woe,  
Her presence sweet, yet laden with regret,  
She teaches hearts the heights and depths to know,  
And bids us cherish pain we can't forget.  
  Yet, though she wounds, her power I confess:  
  For Love, in haunting, grants the soul its rest.

33

When heavy Night her sable arms doth spread,  
My longing heart in silent sorrow wakes,  
A thousand dreams like ghosts around my bed  
Do flit and weave their shadows as it takes  
Its gentle toll upon my restless mind.  
The moon’s pale fingers trace upon the wall  
A silver script of secrets left behind,  
While time’s slow river hushes down the hall.  
Sweet memories, like petals lost to air,  
Descend in silence, soft upon my breast,  
Each whispering of joys that once were fair,  
Now faded with the sun’s departing west.  
  Yet hope, a fragile star, through darkness gleams,  
  And soothes my soul with softly shining dreams.

34

When Sorrow weaves her fingers through my breast,  
And paints my dreams with shades of longing's hue,  
The silent moon attends my soul's unrest,  
And drips her silver balm on midnight's blue.  
My heart, a harp unstrung by winter's hand,  
Resounds with memories lost to distant years;  
The rivers of remembrance flood the land,  
And salt the soil beneath a rain of tears.  
Yet in the hush that sorrow's touch bestows,  
A seed of hope lies dormant in the gloom,  
Awaiting gentle sun and wind that blows,  
To coax a tender blossom forth to bloom.  
  Thus, from the ache that time cannot erase,  
  New strength is born, and sorrow finds its grace.

35

O Love, thou art a shadow on my brow,  
That weeps in silence where my hopes did spring,  
A fleeting mist that time will not allow  
To settle kindly, nor with sweetness cling.  
Thy touch, once gentle, now is cold and bare,  
A phantom breeze that chills the summer air.  
Though once I dreamed thy golden light to wear,  
I wake to find but ashes of despair.  
Yet in the hush where memory takes flight,  
Thy echo lingers soft upon my heart,  
And bids me wander restless through the night,  
Still seeking where our destinies depart.  
  O Love, though sorrow crowns thee with regret,  
  Thy shadow falls, and I cannot forget.

36

When gentle love doth wage its cruel war,  
My heart’s delight becomes my soul’s unrest,  
A thousand wounds concealed behind a scar,  
Yet hope persists, a guest within my breast.  
The moon, a silent witness in the night,  
Beholds my longing with a silver gaze,  
While distant stars, with cool and patient light,  
Bear witness to the flame that in me stays.  
Each breath I take is measured by thy name,  
And every hour is marked by thy decree,  
Desire and doubt in equal portion claim  
The tender space that once belonged to me.  
  Yet if thy smile should banish all my fears,  
  Love’s victory is worth a world of tears.

37

When love’s bright torch doth flicker in the gloom,  
My heart, divided, seeks both light and shade,  
Fair hope and doubt alternate in their room,  
As sun and cloud in fleeting masquerade.  
The silent night enfolds my restless mind,  
With whispers soft as petals in the rain,  
Yet shadows deep a firmer hold do find,  
And joy’s sweet flower wrestles with its pain.  
O would that certainty might calm my soul,  
And banish tempests wrought by longing’s fire;  
Yet love, in mystery, maintains control,  
And fans to life both trembling and desire.  
  Thus in this dance of dark and gleaming light,  
  My heart endures the passion of the night.

38

How sweet the shadow cast by thy fair light,  
Yet bitter grows my heart in love’s despite.  
Thy gentle beams do chase away the night,  
But leave my soul in ever-tender plight.  
A golden glow upon my visage falls,  
Yet deeper grows the longing in my breast.  
Thy voice, more soft than any songbird calls,  
Stirs echoes of a hope denied its rest.  
O, would that I might bask within thy gaze,  
And in thy presence let my sorrows cease;  
But distance weaves its melancholy maze,  
And grants my restless heart no sweet release.  
  So bound am I to shadows thou hast made,  
  By love’s bright torch and memory’s gentle shade.

39

When night unveils the sun’s concealed despair,  
My heart doth bloom where thine affection dies;  
The silver hush of stars adorns the air,  
Yet shadows linger deep within thine eyes.  
The perfumed dusk betrays the whispered pain  
That lingers faint upon thy parted lips,  
While memories, like softly falling rain,  
Slip through my grasp, elusive as eclipse.  
Yet hope, a fragile bud, begins to rise  
Beneath the weight of sorrow’s silent art,  
For though the moonlight sorrowfully sighs,  
I find new courage kindled in my heart.  
  So let the darkness falter, fade, and flee—  
  For dawn returns, and brings thee back to me.

40

When brightest stars do pale before thy face,  
My shadowed heart doth dwell in silent night,  
The silver moon retreats without a trace,  
As dawn is banished by thy sovereign light.  
The gentle rose, in envy, bows its head,  
While zephyrs hush their song in soft despair,  
For every hue and fragrance softly fled  
When thou dost bless the waking world with care.  
Yet in thy gaze, a tempest stirs my soul,  
A sea of longing restless as the tide;  
No art nor wisdom can my will control,  
Nor reason bid this aching hope subside.  
  So let me dwell in darkness, loved in vain,  
  If but thy memory with me remain.

41

When hope in shadows weaves its gentle snare,  
Thy eyes, indifferent, mock my soul’s despair.  
A silent hush upon the evening falls,  
While longing’s specter treads the candle’s flare.  
Each whispered promise lost within these walls  
Returns as echoes heavy with my care.  
The moon, in pity, veils her silver face,  
Lest night reveal the wounds I cannot hide.  
Thy laughter, distant, haunts the empty space  
Where once our dreams together did abide.  
Yet still I linger, bound by love’s cruel art,  
And wear the chains that memory has spun.  
  Though silence reigns between us, heart to heart,  
  My hope endures, though thou art long since gone.

42

Within my breast two warring spirits dwell,  
One bound to hope, one chained in sorrow’s night,  
They struggle fierce, yet neither breaks the spell,  
Nor yields the field, nor claims unchallenged right.  
The first lifts up my gaze to skies of dawn,  
And whispers of tomorrows yet unspun,  
While th’other weeps for golden days long gone,  
And counts the losses darkness has begun.  
Yet in their strife, my soul is forged anew,  
As iron, tempered by opposing flame;  
From joy and grief, a deeper strength I drew,  
And learned that both are facets of the same.  
  Thus, let them war within this fragile chest—  
  For in their clash, my spirit finds its rest.

43

Within my breast doth love and sorrow war,  
A gentle flame beset by shadowed doubt,  
As moonlight fights the dark behind a bar  
Of clouds that seek to keep its longing out.  
My heart, a garden wild with hope and fear,  
Receives the rain of memory’s refrain,  
Each drop a wish for thee to linger near,  
Yet feeds the root of ever-present pain.  
Oh, would my soul be rid of such debate!  
To yield to love or bid its fire cease;  
But reason’s warden guards the heavy gate  
And will not grant my restless mind release.  
  Yet in this storm, thy name my beacon be—  
  A trembling light that ever comforts me.

44

Within my soul doth love and sorrow strive,  
A silent war where joy and grief contrive  
To paint the chambers of my restless heart  
With hues that rend the light and shade apart.  
Sweet hope, like morning’s kiss, bids darkness flee,  
Yet memory’s shadows linger in their place;  
Desire and doubt entwine in subtle plea,  
And longing weaves a mask upon my face.  
Each breath becomes a prayer for gentle peace,  
A wish that time might heal what wounds remain;  
Yet passions rise and grant me no release,  
For every joy is haunted by its pain.  
  Still in this storm, my spirit seeks the day,  
  When love alone shall guide me on my way.

45

My soul doth wander where thine eyes forbid,  
In shadows bright, thy absent presence dwells,  
The garden’s light from gentle gaze is hid,  
Yet in the silent dusk thy longing swells.  
Upon the breeze thy memory is cast,  
A whisper’d vow that circles in the night,  
Reminding me of dreams that could not last,  
Yet fanning hope with every silver light.  
I seek thee in the hush of waking morn,  
Where dew upon the rose recalls thy tears,  
And in the dusk, when all the world is worn,  
Thy laughter echoes softly in my ears.  
  Though fate decrees our stars must stand apart,  
  In secret, thou art sovereign of my heart.

46

My soul doth wander where thine eyes forbid,  
Yet ever seeks the warmth thy heart withholds,  
In silent shadows, love and longing hid,  
The tale of hope within my breast unfolds.  
Thy gaze, a gate that guards the dawn from me,  
Denies the sun to rise upon my days,  
Yet in the gloom, thy phantom smile I see,  
A gentle ghost that haunts my lonely ways.  
Though tempests rage and fates conspire to part  
The trembling hands that reach across the night,  
Still do I nurse the embers in my heart,  
And dream of dawn restored by thy delight.  
  If thou would grant thy grace, my soul would soar,  
  And dwell in joy where shadows fall no more.

47

Oft doth my soul in silent shadow pine,  
Whilst thine, unmov’d, doth bask in golden light,  
My heart, a captive bound by love’s design,  
Beholds thee distant, ever out of sight.  
Yet dreams do bid thee softly to my side,  
Their whispered promises a gentle balm,  
Within their realm, my longing cannot hide,  
Nor can my hope, nor can my aching calm.  
The day departs, and with it, warmth and cheer,  
Night’s curtain falls and deepens my despair,  
Still, in the hush, thy memory draws near,  
And paints thy image on the trembling air.  
  If fate be kind, thy heart may turn to mine—  
  And end the shadow where my thoughts entwine.

48

Within my breast both fire and frost contend,  
Love’s gentle birth doth oft to sorrow bend.  
The morning breaks with hope upon the wing,  
Yet dusk returns with shadows gathering.  
A war of light and dark within me grows,  
Sweet longing’s flame and icy doubt oppose.  
My heart, a captive tossed on passion’s sea,  
Finds not the shore where peace might dwell with me.  
The laughter once as bright as springtime air  
Is now a sigh that drifts through branches bare.  
Yet in this tumult, something pure remains—  
A spark unquenched by all these fleeting pains.  
  So let the storm within me rage and roll,  
  For love alone can reconcile the soul.

49

Within my breast doth love and sorrow war,  
A gentle flame that burns yet breeds despair,  
Its golden light illumes my wounds afar,  
And kindles hope where shadows foul the air.  
The nightingale laments beneath the moon,  
Her song a mirror to my silent ache,  
Each note a promise faded all too soon,  
Each sigh a memory I cannot wake.  
Yet in this tempest, sweet affection grows,  
Unyielding root within a storm-tossed heart,  
For though my suffering forever flows,  
Love weaves its solace in the pain’s own art.  
  Thus bound by bliss and grief, I stand confessed—  
  A soul undone, yet by its longing blessed.

50

When love within my breast doth wage its war,  
Yet reason’s chill denies what passions plead,  
My thoughts are soldiers lost, unsure, afar,  
And wisdom’s voice but whispers in my need.  
The moon, a watchful guardian of the night,  
Beholds my heart beset by hopes and fears,  
Its silver beams bestow a gentle light  
Upon the garden watered by my tears.  
Desire, like roses, climbs my trembling walls,  
While caution lays its thorns beneath my feet;  
In silent rooms, my longing softly calls,  
Yet prudence counsels me in slow retreat.  
  O, wretched strife!—for neither side shall win,  
  Till love or reason yields its crown within.

51

When silent night with longing doth conspire,  
My heart in frost, yet ever set on fire,  
The moon, pale witness to my soul’s desire,  
Draws silver threads across my tangled lyre.  
In shadowed hours, memories awake,  
Their whispered voices drifting through the gloom.  
Each echo stirs the ache I cannot slake,  
A rose that withers, yet defies its doom.  
O gentle sleep, deny thy fleeting grace,  
For dreams are but the embers of the day,  
And in their glow I seek thy vanished face,  
A vision time and reason steal away.  
  Yet hope abides—though night be cold and deep,  
  Love’s flame endures where even shadows sleep.

52

When longing’s flame within mine heart doth burn,  
Yet icy silence answers every plea,  
My soul in shadows wanders, swift to yearn  
For whispers lost upon a frozen sea.  
The moon observes my vigil from above,  
Her silver gaze unmoved by mortal woe;  
A chill pervades the memory of love  
Where once did hope in golden radiance glow.  
Yet still I pace the corridors of night,  
In search of solace drawn from distant stars,  
And pen in trembling hand this woeful plight,  
Imprisoned by invisible old bars.  
  But dawn may break this silence, fierce and bright,  
  Restoring warmth and dreams to banished light.

53

Within my breast love’s gentle flame doth burn,  
Yet cold neglect returns for all I yearn.  
The silent night doth echo with my sighs,  
As hope, once bright, now dims behind my eyes.  
Fair dreams like fragile glass in darkness fall,  
And every dawn reveals the shadows’ stain.  
I plead with fortune, deaf unto my call,  
While longing’s whisper weaves its tender chain.  
The roses blush, yet wilt beneath disdain,  
Their petals pale with sorrow’s silent dew.  
My heart, a captive bound by silken pain,  
Still clings to visions time cannot undo.  
  Though chilled by frost, this love shall not depart—  
  It lives, a steadfast ember in my heart.

54

My heart’s a ship that drifts on restless seas,  
Estranged from shores where once your laughter lay,  
The stars conceal their light with jealous ease,  
And night devours the gentle hues of day.  
The salt-wind sings of promises unkept,  
While memories like sirens haunt the prow;  
Upon the deck, where hope and longing wept,  
Your phantom smile still graces every bow.  
Yet time, that tempest, pulls me further still,  
Its currents strong as sorrow’s heavy hand,  
And though I strain against its ruthless will,  
No anchor drops to let me near your land.  
  But should your beacon blaze across the foam,  
  These wayward sails would swiftly carry home.

55

My heart’s a lantern lost in endless mist,  
It seeks your warmth but trembles in the cold,  
The moon’s pale watch upon my longing wrist  
Counts silent hours as emptiness takes hold.  
Each dawn I search for footprints in the dew,  
A trace of you, soft echoes on the breeze;  
Yet hope’s frail thread is spun of silver blue,  
Entwined with yearning none but I appease.  
The distant lark recalls your gentle song  
That once did charm the shadows from my mind,  
But absent now, the nights are far too long,  
And solace in the dark I cannot find.  
  Oh, grant my lantern light from love’s domain,  
  And guide me safely through this ghostly plain.

56

My heart’s a lantern flickering in the rain,  
Yet longs to burn for you despite the night.  
Each droplet weaves a shroud to mask my pain,  
But hope ignites where shadows meet the light.  
The world conspires to hush my trembling flame,  
As tempests gather round with jealous glee,  
Yet still your whispered name defies the same  
And stirs the spark that dreams you’ll turn to me.  
Oft do I fear the dawn may never break,  
And cold may quench the glow I hold inside,  
But for your warmth, each risk I gladly take—  
No storm can snuff the longing I confide.  
  So let the heavens pour, the darkness fall:  
  My heart endures, for you, through it all.

57

You are the moon that haunts my endless night,  
Unseen, I chase your silver through the gloom,  
Your gentle glow transforms my dark to light,  
A distant hope that shimmers in my room.  
My dreams are tides that answer to your will,  
They rise and fall within your silent gaze;  
Your phantom touch my restless heart can still,  
Yet leaves me wandering through empty haze.  
Each star a witness to my longing’s pain,  
For though I reach, I never hold you near.  
Your beauty, cold and constant, does remain  
A beacon bright, yet ever out of sphere.  
  O moon, reflect my love and set me free,  
  Or keep me bound in yearning’s mystery.

58

My heart, a silent lighthouse in the dusk,  
Still waits for ships that never seek its light,  
Its lantern veiled in grief’s unyielding musk,  
A beacon hung with longing through the night.  
The tides depart with secrets left unsaid,  
Salt shadows painting sorrow on the stone,  
The wind repeats the names of loves long dead,  
Its voice a mournful echo of my own.  
Yet hope endures within the lantern’s core,  
A flame that flickers but refuses rest,  
Each wave that breaks, I count them evermore,  
In dreams that some lost vessel might be blessed.  
  So stands my heart, steadfast amidst the foam,  
  Guiding in vain, but never calling home.

59

You are the lighthouse shining through my storm,  
Yet I, a drifting ship, remain forlorn.  
Your steadfast beacon, bright, and ever warm,  
Calls out to me though battered, lost, and worn.  
The wildest winds may howl upon the sea,  
And shadows coil and gather round my bow,  
But in your light, a hope returns to me,  
A promise whispered through the tempest now.  
Your radiance breaks the bleakest, darkest night,  
Dispelling doubts that thunder through my soul.  
I bend my course toward your gentle light,  
And in your glow, I find myself made whole.  
  Though waves may rise and skies with fury cry,  
  With you, my heart shall safely harbor nigh.

60

My soul, a lantern flickering in the rain,  
Seeks warmth in shadows cast by distant fire,  
Each droplet weaves a tapestry of pain,  
Yet hope persists, refusing to retire.  
The wind, a silent mourner at my side,  
Whispers of solace lost within the mist,  
While trembling flames with gathering darkness bide,  
And dreams, like dew upon the dusk, persist.  
Still, in the hush, a gentle promise gleams,  
A golden thread spun through the sodden night,  
Unfolding softly in the world of dreams,  
It draws my spirit closer to the light.  
  Though tempests rage and weary lanterns fall,  
  The dawn shall rise, and joy return to all.

61

My soul, a lantern dimmed by longing’s shade,  
Still seeks the dawn within a night unmade.  
Each silent hour distills a deeper ache,  
As hope’s frail embers flicker in the wake.  
The stars, indifferent, whisper from afar  
Of dreams dissolved beneath their distant fire,  
Yet through the hush, I chase a morning star  
That kindles in the heart a lost desire.  
The world in shadows holds its breath for light,  
While memory paints gold on ashen air,  
And I, adrift between the dark and bright,  
Recall sweet warmth that once was kind and rare.  
  Though sorrow weaves its shroud with gentle art,  
  Love’s spark endures, unyielding in my heart.

62

My soul, a lantern dimmed by longing’s flame,  
Seeks solace in the shadows of your name.  
Each night I wander through the halls of thought,  
Where echoes of your laughter still remain.  
The moonlight weaves a tapestry I sought,  
Of memories by gentle fingers wrought.  
Upon the breeze your whispered promise clings,  
A silent vow the lonely hours have brought.  
In gardens pale where silent petals fall,  
I trace your absence in the morning dew.  
My heart, a captive at your distant call,  
Beats out the hope of someday finding you.  
  Yet in this ache, a tender truth I see:  
  My love endures, though lost to mystery.

63

My soul, a lantern flickering in the rain,  
Seeks out your warmth but wanders lost and cold,  
Each hope it kindles quickly met with pain,  
As night’s embrace grows silent, dark, and bold.  
The world around me murmurs distant fears,  
Yet in your gaze a gentler dawn appears.  
Though tempests howl and sorrow draws me near,  
Your memory the storm’s wild fury clears.  
No gilded sun can dry these tears I weep,  
Nor time erase the longing in my chest;  
Yet in the shadows where my heart doth keep,  
Your gentle voice bids troubled thoughts to rest.  
  So let my lantern brave the driving rain,  
  If love’s true light within me shall remain.

64

My soul, a silent lantern in the mist,  
Still burns for you, though never to exist.  
The moonlight weaves remembrance through the night,  
As shadows dance and vanish from my sight.  
A wistful breeze that stirs the sleeping grass,  
Recalls your voice in echoes faint and low.  
The hours like gentle strangers slowly pass,  
Unveiling dreams the daylight cannot know.  
Upon the glass, your image softly stays,  
A fleeting ghost the morning cannot keep.  
Yet every dawn, in hope’s enduring haze,  
My heart ascends where longing dares to leap.  
  Though fate denies what longing would pursue,  
  My lantern glows, forever lit for you.

65

My soul, a lantern dimmed by longing’s shade,  
Still burns for you, though hope begins to fade.  
The night’s embrace recalls your gentle light  
That soared within me, vanquishing my fear.  
Yet absence weaves its silence through the night,  
And memories like phantoms linger near.  
I search the dawn for whispers of your name,  
But morning’s gold gives neither sign nor trace.  
The world moves on, unchanged by love or shame,  
While time erases footprints from your place.  
Yet in my breast, a stubborn ember glows,  
Defying winds that seek to snuff its fire.  
  Though distance grows and colder still it grows,  
  Still in this heart lives unextinguished desire.

66

Within my chest, a storm of longing brews,  
Yet calm deceives the world that I present.  
The silent ache that neither rest subdues  
Nor laughter’s mask can ever circumvent.  
I bear my secret tempest with a smile,  
While thunder shakes the corridors of thought,  
Disguising all my yearning all the while  
With painted peace that desperation wrought.  
O gentle night, unveil my hidden ache,  
And let the moonlight witness what I feel.  
For in your silver gaze my sorrows wake,  
And shadows teach my spirit how to heal.  
  Yet when the dawn dispels what darkness hides,  
  Once more, a tranquil mask my heart provides.

67

My heart’s a ship that sails a midnight sea,  
Adrift between the stars and memory.  
The waves of longing break upon my side,  
While distant hopes like northern lanterns gleam.  
Each whispered wind recalls what once could be,  
A gentle promise woven in a dream.  
Yet shadows gather where the bright stars hide,  
And tempests echo truths I dare not deem.  
The moon, my compass, pulls with silver thread,  
Guiding my soul through silence and through storm.  
Yet in the dark, old fears are gently fed,  
And solitude becomes a second form.  
  Still, through the night my ship must onward roam,  
  Until love's harbor calls my heart back home.

68

Within my chest, a hollow star does ache,  
Its light consumed by shadows of my doubt,  
Each breath I take, a trembling dawn I make,  
Yet night persists, and hope is driven out.  
The silent hush of longing fills the air,  
As dreams, like petals, scatter on the floor;  
My heart, a vessel brimming with despair,  
Still seeks the warmth it once was longing for.  
Yet in this gloom, a gentle whisper grows,  
A promise faint, but steadfast in its rise,  
It teaches me that even sorrow knows  
The secret art of birthing new sunrise.  
  For though the star within me fades to grey,  
  Its light reborn with every breaking day.

69

My heart’s a lighthouse beaming through the mist,  
Yet ships of hope avoid its mournful glow,  
Each beam a longing neither seen nor kissed,  
A silent pulse where wayward wishes go.  
The fog enfolds the rocky shoals of fear,  
Its silver shroud conceals the jagged truth;  
The waves lament in voices faint but clear,  
Their tide receding from the shores of youth.  
Yet still I burn, my signal undeterred,  
Though no soul steers to claim my steady light,  
My warnings lost like many a whispered word  
That vanish in the hollow arms of night.  
  But hope may find, when tempests rage and roar,  
  A harbor safe within my shining shore.

70

My heart’s a lighthouse casting light in vain,  
Its beam ignored by ships that never turn.  
Upon the rocks I stand through wind and rain,  
While distant vessels glide and never yearn.  
The fog rolls in and muffles every plea,  
Yet still I burn with hope against the night.  
No sailor’s song drifts faintly back to me,  
No answer flickers in my patient light.  
Each dawn I watch horizons cold and bare,  
Expectant for a sail that won’t appear.  
The salt and silence settle in the air,  
Companions to my solitary fear.  
  Yet steadfast, through the darkness I remain—  
  A beacon bright, though hope may burn in vain.

71

My heart’s a lantern dimmed by your neglect,  
It flickers shadows where our dreams once lay,  
The flame once bright now struggles to connect  
With hope that dawn might break this endless gray.  
Your absence weaves a silence through the room,  
Each echo sighs the memory of your voice.  
What once was spring now bears the breath of gloom,  
Where love’s green garden falters in its choice.  
Yet still within the glass a spark remains,  
A stubborn ember fighting through the chill,  
Resisting every storm of doubt and pains,  
Desiring warmth your presence used to fill.  
  If love be light, return and let it shine—  
  Restore the lamp and make its glow divine.

72

O Love, you haunt the corners of my mind,  
A shadowed guest I never can unbind.  
You whisper secrets through the trembling night,  
And draw me trembling toward forbidden light.  
With every breath, your presence lingers near,  
A ghostly touch that chills and yet delights;  
You paint the dusk with longing, hope, and fear,  
Enfolding dreams in velvet, starless nights.  
I chase your form through halls of memory,  
Each echo grows more distant as I run.  
What solace lies in sweet uncertainty,  
When dawn reveals the phantoms you have spun?  
  Yet still, O Love, I beg you not depart—  
  For even pain is precious to my heart.

73

When midnight sighs and sorrow strokes my heart,  
Your name, like haunted wind, refuses rest.  
The shadows from my longing will not part,  
But linger, close as breath within my chest.  
I count the silver hours by moon’s gaze,  
Each one a silent echo of your voice.  
Your absence weaves a labyrinthine maze  
Where hope and memory in turn rejoice.  
Yet morning breaks with pale and trembling light,  
Unraveling the dreams your spirit spun,  
And leaves me stranded, vanquished by the night,  
Uncertain if my yearning’s lost or won.  
  Still, in the hush where all the world is still,  
  I love you, and perhaps I always will.

74

When midnight’s longing whispers through my chest,  
My heart debates the shadows of your name,  
The moon hangs low, a silent, silver guest,  
And stirs my blood with memories aflame.  
Each breath concedes what reason would deny,  
For in the hush, your absence speaks so loud,  
A gentle ache beneath the velvet sky  
That weaves your ghost in every passing cloud.  
The stars confess the secrets lovers keep,  
Their pale confessions trembling in the air,  
While dreams, like tides, pull softly from the deep  
And leave me stranded, lost within your stare.  
  Yet hope persists, though night be long and cold:  
  Love’s ember glows where midnight tales are told.

75

The night wraps longing round my restless heart,  
While Hope, a jealous ghost, keeps us apart.  
The silver moon observes my quiet ache,  
Its borrowed light a balm I dare not take.  
A whisper stirs the silence of my room,  
As dreams parade in shadows on the wall.  
Your absent touch is woven through the gloom,  
A silent promise I can scarce recall.  
Yet dawn betrays the secrets night has spun,  
And paints my yearning bright upon the day.  
No gentle word, no glance from you, not one—  
Only the echo of your steps away.  
  Yet still my soul, in darkness and in light,  
  Persists in love’s sweet folly through the night.

76

The night inhales the longing from my chest,  
While moonlight weeps for love that won’t return.  
The silent stars bear witness to my quest,  
And in their gaze, my aching embers burn.  
Cool shadows tiptoe softly on the floor,  
Their velvet hush conceals my whispered plea.  
Each midnight hour reopens every sore  
That time and reason promised would not be.  
Yet hope, rebellious, shivers in the gloom,  
A fragile flame that will not be denied—  
For though the dawn may banish dreams too soon,  
Desire endures where memories reside.  
  So let the night its mournful vigil keep,  
  While I in dreams of you surrender sleep.

77

Love knocks upon the door of your closed heart,  
While Hope, a beggar, waits outside in rain.  
Within the silent halls, shadows depart,  
Yet echoes of old sorrows still remain.  
Through frosted panes, the dawn attempts to peer,  
And paints with gold the corners of your room.  
The trembling light dispels a little fear,  
And scatters gently all the gathered gloom.  
O, let the hinges yield to gentle plea,  
Though wary walls have weathered many storms;  
For sweet renewal grows from misery,  
When sun-warmed faith with patient longing forms.  
  Open, and let Love’s quiet feet come in—  
  A new world waits where tenderness begins.

78

My soul, a silent lover in the dusk,  
Whispers of longing clothed in shadowed trust,  
The evening’s breath perfumes the air with musk,  
And stirs the heart to hope because it must.  
Soft starlight glimmers on my secret thought  
Which hides behind the trembling of my gaze,  
Unspoken dreams, with tender yearning wrought,  
Entwine with night and set my mind ablaze.  
A hush falls gently on the world outside,  
Yet in my breast, a thousand tempests start;  
Desire and fear together there abide,  
Companions in the chambers of my heart.  
  Yet, should your spirit answer mine in time,  
  Love’s dawn would break and end this midnight rhyme.

79

My soul walks lonely corridors of doubt,  
While love, a silent ghost, drifts in and out.  
The moonlight paints its sorrow on the floor,  
And shadows gather secrets near the door.  
A chill invades the corners of my mind,  
Where memories, like autumn leaves, unwind.  
The hours stretch with longing unconfessed,  
And hope, a fragile bird, forsakes its nest.  
Yet in the hush, a gentle promise grows,  
A whisper soft as petals on the rose.  
Despair may weave its thorns around my heart,  
But dreams, undying, will not soon depart.  
  For though I wander lost in gloom’s embrace,  
  Love’s dawn awaits to gild my soul with grace.

80

My soul, a restless guest in halls of doubt,  
Whispers of love yet tremble, locked within.  
The moonlight weaves its silver threads throughout  
The silent chambers where my hopes have been.  
A shadow dances softly on the wall,  
Entwined with dreams too fragile yet to speak;  
It listens for a voice, a distant call,  
Or gentle hands to soothe the world they seek.  
Yet time moves on, and twilight chills the air,  
Each heartbeat echoing with longing’s ache;  
Desires unspoken linger in despair,  
Like petals falling for remembrance’ sake.  
  But dawn may rouse my spirit from its night,  
  And teach my soul to trust the coming light.

81

My soul, a lantern flickering in the rain,  
Keeps vigil where your shadow used to lie,  
Each droplet etches memories of pain  
Upon the trembling glass I hold so high.  
The midnight wind recalls your whispered name,  
Its breath upon my cheek both sweet and cold.  
The world unchanged, yet I am not the same—  
An ember lost, a heart grown faint and old.  
Yet hope, unbidden, kindles in the gloom,  
A stubborn spark that will not be denied.  
Though sorrow weaves its tapestry of doom,  
Still, love endures where silent ghosts reside.  
  O, let my lantern burn against despair,  
  And guide thee home upon the midnight air.

82

My soul, a silent guest at love’s parade,  
Stands weeping in the shadows of your light,  
Where hope, in fragile silks of longing, laid  
Its trembling heart upon the edge of night.  
Your laughter dances distant through the air,  
A melody that haunts the mournful breeze;  
I reach for warmth, but find the empty stare  
Of dreams undone by time’s relentless seas.  
Yet still I linger, bound by gentle chains,  
A captive to the promise of your gaze—  
Each memory a ghost that softly reigns  
Within the silent chambers of my days.  
  If love be sorrow, let me bear the cost,  
  For in your absence, all the world is lost.

83

My soul, a ghost, pursues your distant light,  
While hope, a traitor, flees into the shade,  
The hours stumble, blind, at edge of night,  
And dreams, like broken glass, in silence fade.  
Your memory haunts the corners of my mind,  
A silver wraith that will not let me rest;  
In starless dark, your absence I do find,  
A shadowed ache that dwells within my breast.  
Yet dawn, relentless, paints the world anew,  
Its gold indifferent to my longing pain.  
I search the morning sky in search of you,  
But find the echo of your voice in vain.  
  O, would that time might mercy grant to me,  
  To bind my soul where yours forever be.

84

Love haunts the hollow chambers of my chest,  
She whispers doubts that wither hope’s embrace,  
Her shadowed fingers rob me of my rest,  
And draw a pallor o’er my dreaming face.  
Each night she winds her sorrow through my bones,  
A silent chill that freezes beating hearts;  
She sings of loss in melancholy tones,  
And thus the seed of longing she imparts.  
Yet in her wake, a tender ache remains,  
A fragile proof that I have loved and lost;  
For joy and pain are tangled in her chains,  
And every gift of love exacts its cost.  
  Though haunted, still I cherish her domain—  
  For where she haunts, my soul is not in vain.

85

My heart, a silent actor on the stage,  
Performs its grief in shadows cast by doubt,  
It hides its wounds behind a painted rage,  
While longing for a truth it lives without.  
The whispered lines of hope die on my tongue,  
As curtains fall on dreams I dared to write,  
My soul laments the songs it left unsung,  
And shivers in the chill of endless night.  
Yet still, the spotlight draws me to confess  
The fragile yearnings trembling in my breast,  
For in the dark, despair and hope coalesce,  
And love’s lost memory will not let me rest.  
  Thus, in this play, my secret must remain:  
  A heart that breaks, and mends, and breaks again.

86

When Love, a silent guest, invades my mind,  
She stirs the dust of doubts I’ve left behind.  
Her fingers trace the corners of my thought,  
And wake old hopes my wary heart had fought.  
She walks in shadows where my fears reside,  
Yet brings a gentle dawn to every shade,  
Her presence bids my secret wounds confide,  
And grants a peace no solitude had made.  
Though reason’s gate protests her subtle art,  
She slips through cracks that logic cannot seal,  
And plants a trembling garden in my heart,  
Where blooms the trust I dare again to feel.  
  So let her haunt these chambers, soft and true,  
  For through her light, my soul is born anew.

87

My heart, a silent clock, forgets to chime,  
While longing paints your absence on the air,  
Each passing hour dissolved in wistful time,  
And shadows gather softly in despair.  
The moon laments across the midnight blue,  
Its borrowed silver wan upon my face;  
The hush of night recalls the loss of you,  
And memory drifts in your vanished place.  
Yet hope, a fragile bud, begins to rise,  
Unfolding petals in the light of dawn;  
The sun awakens dreams behind my eyes,  
And stirs the world to sing when night is gone.  
  So till we meet, my soul shall keep its tune,  
  And measure love by sun, by stars, by moon.

88

My heart, a silent actor on this stage,  
Performs for love that never reads its part,  
In shadowed wings it paces through its cage,  
Entrapped by longing, cloaked in practiced art.  
It speaks in gestures, delicate and slight,  
A trembling hand, a glance that slips away,  
Yet you, unknowing audience, take flight  
And leave my soliloquy to fade to gray.  
The script is written in a tongue unheard,  
Each line rehearsed in hope you’ll learn your cue;  
But silence drowns the meaning of each word,  
And veils the truths my trembling heart once knew.  
  Yet still I play this role, though curtains fall—  
  For love, unseen, is sweetest pain of all.

89

Love lingers here, a shadow at my door,  
She sings my name yet never calls me home,  
Her whispers drift like petals on the floor,  
And haunt the silent corners where I roam.  
The moonlight weaves her memory in my mind,  
A tapestry of longing, soft and bright,  
Yet every touch I reach for stays confined  
Within the gentle prison of the night.  
She bids me wait beneath the trembling stars,  
Her promise sweet, though distant as a dream,  
I count the hours by invisible scars,  
And lose myself in hope’s uncertain gleam.  
  Still, in the hush where waking meets repose,  
  Love’s shadow stays, the dearest guest I know.

90

I love thee most when silence fills the air,  
Yet ache with words my trembling lips forbear.  
Thy gentle glance compels my soul to dare,  
To paint my longing hopes upon the bare  
And moonlit canvas of the midnight sky.  
A hush descends, and in its depths I find  
The echo of thy breath, the softest sigh,  
A promise whispered to my heart and mind.  
If only courage served my tongue as well  
As passion stirs the secret pulse within,  
Then every glance would let my spirit tell  
The sacred truths that silence can’t begin.  
  Yet in this quiet, love divine is shown—  
  For in thy hush, my heart is fully known.

91

My heart is torn between desire and doubt,  
Your gentle smile ignites my silent ache,  
Yet shadows linger when the stars burn out,  
And reason trembles with each choice I make.  
The moon observes my restless, longing eyes,  
While whispers drift upon the midnight air;  
In dreams, your voice both comforts and belies,  
A hope uncertain, fragile as a prayer.  
I walk the line where passion meets regret,  
Afraid to lose what never has begun—  
Each promise clothed in longing and in threat,  
Each moment lost or gained, a race half-run.  
  Yet for your touch, my soul remains a flame,  
  Conflicted still, yet loving all the same.

92

In loving you, I lose the self I knew,  
Yet find a stranger’s heart within my chest;  
My soul, unmoored, drifts on a sea of blue,  
Adrift where longing’s tempests grant no rest.  
Each glance you cast unravels threads of me,  
Rewoven by your gaze in patterns new,  
And in your laughter, lighter winds set free  
The burdens I believed I must accrue.  
A thousand versions of myself have died  
Upon the altar of your gentle touch,  
Yet from the ashes, hope is born with pride—  
A self remade in loving you so much.  
  So let me lose myself and yet be whole,  
  For in your love, I find my truest soul.

93

Your laughter rings where silence fills my days,  
Yet in your warmth, I wander lost and cold.  
Bright memory, a sun through shadow’s haze,  
Yet night descends before the dawn unfolds.  
I chase your echo down the empty hall,  
Where hope is draped in tapestries of blue.  
Each whispered joy, a ghost within the wall,  
Reminds me how I lived when loved by you.  
The roses wilt, but still their fragrance stays,  
A promise lingering beyond its bloom.  
Though distance stretches time in weary ways,  
Your presence lights the corners of my room.  
  If absence is the price my heart must pay,  
  I’ll treasure pain, for love will find its way.

94

Your laughter bright as dawn, my soul’s eclipse,  
I taste the warmth of hope with every chill.  
A gentle sun that breaks through sorrow’s grips,  
Reviving dreams that slumbered, cold and still.  
The silver echo rings where shadows lie,  
Unveiling light within the darkest heart;  
With every breath, my spirit learns to fly,  
Unraveling the threads that kept apart.  
O, fortune’s favor, found within your smile,  
A beacon cast across the storm-swept sea—  
The night’s despair is vanquished for a while,  
Enchanted by the joy you grant to me.  
  Thus, let your laughter ever be my guide,  
  And in its grace, I’ll evermore abide.

95

When hope and sorrow wrestle in my breast,  
Your smile ignites the ache I can't confess.  
The dawn arrives, yet leaves my soul unblessed,  
For longing grows with every soft caress.  
The silent hours stretch with dreams of you,  
A gentle ghost that haunts the waking day.  
In shadows cast by all I thought I knew,  
Desire and doubt entwine and lose their way.  
Yet in the hush between each word we speak,  
A fragile hope persists beneath despair;  
The trembling faith that what I longingly seek  
May blossom if your heart should find me there.  
  So let this sonnet hold what lips conceal:  
  A love both bound by fear and made to heal.

96

Within my chest, two warring spirits dwell,  
One sings of love, the other weeps farewell.  
Their voices rise and clash within my breast,  
A tempest caught between both east and west.  
One wraps my heart in silks of gentle fire,  
The other chills with frost of long regret.  
Sweet hope’s entreaties lift my soul up higher,  
While sorrow’s chains would bid me to forget.  
They wrestle on through every waking hour,  
Their shadows flicker softly on my face.  
Desire and grief entwined in secret bower,  
Each striving for dominion in this place.  
  Yet from their war, a richer song is spun—  
  A harmony of loss and love begun.

97

Within my soul, where shadows vie with light,  
Love’s gentle warmth contends with chill of doubt,  
Uncertain dawns precede the restless night,  
Where whispered fear and longing wrestle out.  
I pace the borders of my trembling heart,  
Each beat a hush that trembles with your name,  
Desire and timorous reason kept apart,  
Yet both ignite within a single flame.  
O, let the sun break through this clouded thought,  
And chase the phantoms clinging to my breast;  
For in your gaze, all solace may be wrought,  
And wearied hopes be gathered into rest.  
  If love be true, then shadows lose their might,  
  And all my soul shall blossom in your light.

98

My soul divides where love and sorrow meet,  
A silent war that trembles in my chest,  
The memories like ghosts that haunt defeat,  
Yet urge my heart to strive and not to rest.  
The morning’s light reveals what night concealed,  
Yet shadows linger, clinging to the day;  
A wound half-healed, a sorrow never healed,  
The joy of hope, the ache that will not stay.  
Yet in the garden where my heart does roam,  
I see the rose that braves the winter’s chill,  
Its fragile stem as steadfast as a home,  
Its crimson bloom defies the coldest will.  
  So love and sorrow both reside in me,  
  As night and dawn embrace eternally.

99

My soul pursues where thine will never stay,  
Yet finds its peace within the ache of loss,  
The dawn arrives, but night must hold its sway,  
And hope is weighed by sorrow’s heavy cost.  
I wander forests thick with whispered pain,  
While shadows twist the footprints that I made,  
In every leaf thy memory remains,  
A gentle ghost that will not, cannot fade.  
The world moves on in ever-turning dance,  
Yet I am stilled by longing’s quiet thread,  
Each passing hour denies me sweet advance,  
But fills my heart with echoes of what’s fled.  
  Though distance keeps our fates forever twined,  
  In loss’s arms, thy spirit I shall find.

100

My soul, a lantern flickering in the rain,  
Still seeks your warmth, yet shivers in the cold,  
Each memory a softly burning pain  
That haunts the empty hours, pale and old.  
The silver moon peers through a clouded sky,  
Its borrowed light a balm upon my grief,  
Yet shadows gather when your name drifts by,  
And every breath is longing's stolen thief.  
I wander through the garden of our days,  
Where blossoms droop beneath the weight of loss,  
And trace your footsteps through the misty haze,  
Recalling summer’s light, now touched by frost.  
  Yet hope endures within this trembling flame—  
  For hearts once joined are never quite the same.

101

Within the quiet chambers of my soul,  
Love’s fire burns cold where hope once made me whole.  
The shadows lengthen where the sun withdrew,  
And dreams lie scattered, faded, out of view.  
A silent longing echoes in the gloom,  
While memory drapes the air in fragrant bloom.  
Yet every hour, I search the darkened hall,  
For whispers of the joy I can’t recall.  
Though time doth steal the warmth from every kiss,  
And winter’s frost encircles gentle spring,  
I nurse the embered promise in the mist,  
Awaiting dawn and what new light may bring.  
  For even embers, smoldering and slight,  
  May kindle hope, and call the day from night.

102

I love myself yet mourn the self I lose,  
A heart divided, yearning to refuse.  
In shadowed glass, I seek the eyes I knew,  
While dawn betrays the midnight’s fragile muse.  
I walk the line where old and new contend,  
My tangled roots and branches yet unpruned,  
The self that was, unwilling yet to mend,  
The self becoming, not quite yet attuned.  
Each breath a trial, memory’s gentle war,  
Yet in the ache, a promise softly grows:  
For from the ashes, I may find yet more  
Than what the mirror’s haunted silence shows.  
  So let me grieve, and in that grieving, find  
  A deeper love for self I leave behind.

103

I love myself, yet war within me grows,  
Desire and doubt entwined in silent fight,  
A tempest stirs beneath my calm repose,  
My reason shrouded, yearning for the light.  
The mirror’s gaze reveals a fractured view,  
A heart at odds with dreams it cannot claim,  
Yet hope persists, as morning brings the new,  
And softly soothes the embers of my shame.  
Oft have I wandered through the tangled mind,  
Seeking the peace that in my chest eludes,  
With every fault and virtue intertwined,  
I walk my path through light and shadowed moods.  
  Yet in this strife, a deeper love I find—  
  For self and soul, in unity combined.

104

Within my chest two warring tempests rage,  
Love’s gentle hand and sorrow’s iron fist  
Contend for right upon a fragile stage,  
A battleground of moments lost and missed.  
The morning’s light would warm me with its grace,  
Yet shadowed clouds obscure the hopeful sky;  
I wear the mask of laughter on my face,  
But silent tears like midnight rivers lie.  
Sweet memory, thou art a double blade,  
Reviving joy, yet deepening my ache;  
Each whispered promise tenderly is made,  
Yet leaves behind the ghost of hearts that break.  
  O grant me peace, or teach my soul to find  
  A harbor from the storms within my mind.

105

Within my chest, a fire coldly glows,  
Desire blooms where hope’s pale shadow lies,  
A trembling seed beneath December snows,  
Unseen by waking heart and weary eyes.  
The midnight hush attends my silent plea,  
As dreams like gilded phantoms drift and part,  
Each heartbeat tolls an old uncertainty,  
Yet still I cup this ember to my heart.  
The world in gray procession marches on,  
Yet in my core, defiance softly burns,  
For though the light of yesterday is gone,  
A secret dawn in hidden chambers turns.  
  So from this frost, I’ll forge my summer’s song,  
  And in the dark, I’ll find where I belong.

106

Within my chest, a hope that will not die,  
Yet shadows bloom where light should warm my days,  
The silent ache beneath a restless sky  
Mocks every sunbeam with its ashen haze.  
Though morning sings in gold upon the field,  
I walk in dreams where sorrow’s roots run deep,  
And fragile joys, like petals, softly yield  
To winds of doubt that trouble every sleep.  
Yet in the hush between the rise and fall  
Of breath, I glimpse a flame that will not fade—  
A whisper brave enough to still recall  
The sweeter hours that memory has made.  
  So let the night descend with veiling art,  
  For hope, undying, lingers in my heart.

107

Within my chest, a fire and frost entwine,  
Love’s gentle hand rejects this heart of mine.  
Sweet hope and fear together dare to grow,  
Their silent wars in secret chambers show.  
A glance, a word—my spirit’s feast and fast,  
Each joy that comes is shadowed by its past.  
Desire, like summer’s sun, consumes my peace,  
Yet winter’s chill commands that warmth to cease.  
I court the night, where dreams in darkness bloom,  
And see your face shine softly through the gloom.  
My thoughts, like restless stars, refuse to sleep,  
They wander skies where distant memories weep.  
  Yet still I hold, though reason bids me flee,  
  For love, denied, is love that will not be.

108

Thou art the gall within my honeyed wine,  
A thorn that weaves through every rose I twine.  
Thy shadow stains the light of summer’s day,  
And in thy smile, I glimpse the storm’s dismay.  
Each gentle word is barbed with secret spite,  
A velvet glove that masks the iron hand.  
Thy laughter rings with echoes of the night,  
And joy dissolves like footprints in the sand.  
Yet still, my longing will not let thee go,  
For pain and pleasure share a tangled root.  
Though reason bids me flee, my heart says no,  
And seeks in bitter fruit its sweetest fruit.  
  O cruel delight, my curse and my desire,  
  I burn in ice and freeze within thy fire.

109

Thou art the thorn that rends my heart in twain,  
Yet still I crave the rose that bears the pain.  
Thy beauty, sharp as steel, dost pierce me through,  
And in thy gaze, my soul is lost again.  
I court thy shadow, though it dims my view,  
For love hath made my reason frail and vain.  
The cruelest sweetness lingers on thy breath,  
And every promise teeters close to death.  
Upon the night’s pale brow thy whispers fall,  
Their velvet mournfulness a haunted tune.  
I am thy slave, though freedom I recall,  
Bound to thy will beneath a waxing moon.  
  Yet if thy heart should yield, my wounds would close,  
  For even thorns are gentle to the rose.

110

Thou art the frost that chills my budding spring,  
A serpent’s kiss disguised in silken thread,  
Thy whispered words a hollow echo bring,  
Like winter’s shadow o’er the roses spread.  
Thy eyes, two lanterns in the midnight’s gloom,  
Illuminate the thorns beneath thy grace.  
A garden’s promise wilted ere its bloom,  
Deceitful warmth within thy cold embrace.  
Yet still my heart, ensnared by thy delight,  
Returns as moth unto the fatal flame,  
Desiring day, yet lost to endless night,  
Entranced by love that dares not speak its name.  
  O cruel tempter, ever shall I yearn,  
  For spring’s sweet hope that never shall return.

111

Thou art the flame that scorcheth, not to warm,  
A siren’s lure that leads my heart to harm.  
Thy honeyed words, though sweet, foretell a storm,  
And yet I rush to shelter in thine arm.  
Thy gaze, a tempest masked in summer skies,  
Does steal my reason with a fleeting glance.  
Unwary hope within thy shadow lies,  
Enthralled by every subtle circumstance.  
Oft have I sworn to flee thy burning light,  
Yet find my steps returning to thy fire.  
Though wisdom pleads that I resist thy might,  
Desire outstrips the caution I require.  
  So let me perish, scorched by thy delight—  
  For pain from thee is pleasure in disguise.

112

Thou art the frost that chills my fervent spring,  
A flame I feed, yet perish in its glow.  
Thy shadow dims the joy that love would bring,  
Yet closer still to thee my wishes grow.  
Upon the trembling verge of night I wait,  
To hear thy voice, a whisper on the breeze.  
My hope, like fragile glass, doth hesitate,  
For every glance thy wintry eyes do freeze.  
Yet in thy silence dwells a secret heat,  
A promise hid beneath the frozen earth.  
Though bitter winds my budding heart defeat,  
I dream of dawn and of a gentler birth.  
  If thou art cold, let me be warmth anew,  
  And thaw the world for both of us—and you.

113

Thou art the winter sun that will not warm,  
A gilded blade that cuts with sweetest harm,  
Thy smile, a beacon in the gathering storm,  
Yet brings to gentle hearts a cold alarm.  
Thy voice is music sung from distant halls,  
That echoes soft, yet keeps its secrets close,  
A painted rose that blooms within stone walls,  
Its beauty rare, but rooted in repose.  
Upon thy brow, a crown of frosted light,  
Adorns the midnight of thy noble gaze;  
Thy touch, though brief, transforms the day to night,  
And leaves the soul to wander in a maze.  
  Yet still I seek thee, though the frost be deep,  
  For in thy shadow, love doth wake from sleep.

114

My soul, a ship becalmed on love's false tide,  
Doth wrestle 'gainst the winds thy vows provide.  
The moonlight weeps upon these restless waves,  
While memory’s anchor binds what hope yet craves.  
A siren’s promise tempers every gale,  
And whispers sweet of harbors never near.  
Yet tempests rise where gentle breezes fail,  
My compass lost between desire and fear.  
The stars confound the course my heart would steer,  
Their silver eyes betray the path I chose.  
In silent nights thy absence draws me near  
To shores unseen, where longing never goes.  
  O grant me wind, or else release my sail—  
  For anchored thus, my soul is doomed to pale.

115

My soul, a ship cast out upon the brine,  
Doth founder 'neath the weight of love's disdain,  
The stars, indifferent, in cold silence shine,  
While memory's tide returns me to my pain.  
The winds that once did fill my hopeful sail  
Now whisper only sorrow to the night,  
And all the vows we swore begin to pale  
Beneath the moon's indifferent, watchful light.  
Yet still I cling to shreds of vanished bliss,  
A phantom echo on the salt-streaked air,  
Each wave a bitter shadow of your kiss,  
Each gust a ghostly hand that is not there.  
  Yet hope, though drowned, may rise with morning's gleam,  
  For souls that wander oft return to dream.

116

Within my soul, love festers like a thorn,  
A serpent coiled in chambers dark and deep,  
It wakes with every blush of dewy morn,  
Yet through the dusk, in silent gloom, does creep.  
Its poison sweet, it courses through my veins,  
And every heartbeat whispers of its fire,  
It weaves a crown of melancholy chains  
That both imprison and inflame desire.  
I long to tear its shadow from my breast,  
To cast away the ache that will not die,  
Yet in its hold my spirit finds no rest,  
Nor can my tongue explain the reason why.  
  So bound am I to love’s relentless art,  
  It reigns, unchallenged, sovereign of my heart.

117

My soul, a ship cast far from gentle shore,  
Doth brave love's storm whilst thou dost turn away,  
The stars, once guides, their golden course ignore,  
Whilst hope’s pale lantern flickers with dismay.  
Upon the waves, thy absence is the gale  
That tears the canvas of my tranquil peace;  
Each memory unfurls a tattered sail,  
And time’s relentless winds will not release.  
Yet, o’er the roaring deep, my heart remains  
Aloft with faith that morning’s sun may rise,  
Dispersing night and all its bitter chains,  
Restoring light to thy once-tender eyes.  
  If fortune’s winds deliver thee to me,  
  Love’s harbor waits with arms as wide as sea.

118

My soul, a barren field in winter's keep,  
Unfed by thine, lies fallow in its pain,  
The silent furrows buried, dark and deep,  
Await the gentle touch of summer rain.  
No golden seed doth quicken in the earth,  
No lark ascends to greet the blushing morn,  
Yet in the frost I dream of distant mirth,  
A promise whispered by the buds unborn.  
O, let thy light break through the leaden skies,  
And thaw the ice that binds my longing heart,  
For in thy gaze new hope and harvest lies,  
And every wounded root would swift restart.  
  Restore me, love, with thee at season’s end—  
  From barren ground, let living bloom ascend.

119

My soul, a barren field where hope hath died,  
Seeks sun in thine, but finds the shadows spread.  
The gentle spring that once in me relied  
Now withers ‘neath the chill where dreams have fled.  
Yet, in thine eyes, a dawn is faintly cast,  
A golden thread that weaves through endless gloom.  
Though tempests rage and sorrow holds me fast,  
Thy voice recalls the rose’s subtle bloom.  
If fate decrees our hearts must walk alone,  
Still, by thy memory’s light am I made whole.  
Though seeds of joy upon my path are sown,  
They struggle through the furrows of my soul.  
  For in thy warmth, my barren hopes would grow—  
  Yet I, unwatered, reap what I must sow.

120

Within my breast, a winter’s thorn hath grown,  
Where once the summer’s gentle rose did reign,  
The warmth and fragrance of the past now flown,  
And in its stead, a chill and silent pain.  
The silver frost upon my hopes doth lie,  
Enshrouding dreams with icy breath of night;  
No lark ascends beneath this barren sky,  
Nor does the dawn restore the faded light.  
Yet in this cold, a subtle strength I find,  
For hardship teaches roots to deeper go;  
What blooms in ease may fade with fickle wind,  
But hearts that weather storms more steadfast grow.  
  So let the winter’s thorn within me bide—  
  It shapes the soul that summer cannot guide.

121

My heart, a barren field where roses die,  
Once tilled by hope, now sown with salt and rue,  
The sun departs, and silent shadows lie  
Upon the dreams that never quite came true.  
The gentle rain recalls a lover’s hand  
That once caressed the furrows of my soul,  
Yet seasons pass, and none may understand  
How absence weaves its tapestry of toll.  
Yet in this wasteland, stubborn roots remain,  
Defiant buds that press against the gloom,  
And whisper softly through the ache and pain  
Of petals lost, yet longing still to bloom.  
  Though grief may reign, and thorns may pierce me through,  
  Some distant spring shall paint my fields anew.

122

Within my breast a serpent coils with spite,  
And love, once fair, now gnaws my peace by night.  
Its venom whispers doubts that cloud my mind,  
And leaves the sweetest memories confined.  
Once gentle words now echo cruel and cold,  
Transforming warmth to frost within my chest.  
My hopeful heart grows weary, wan, and old,  
By shadows of the joy I once possessed.  
I see thy face in every waning dream,  
Yet wake to find the dawn bereft of grace.  
Desire and dread are tangled in a seam  
That time and reason struggle to unlace.  
  Yet in this pain, a sharper truth I find:  
  That love and loss are woven, intertwined.

123

Upon the rocks of scorn my hope is cast,
