Love’s golden ship hath wrecked upon thy shore;  
The gentle winds of promise could not last,  
Nor save my soul from tempests I abhor.  
Thy eyes, once stars to guide me through the night,  
Now coldly glimmer, distant and unkind;  
The morning’s glow is swallowed by their blight,  
And all my dreams to shadows are resigned.  
Yet still my heart, a foolish, faithful thing,  
Returns to thee despite the biting gale;  
It beats within the cage of suffering,  
And sings thy name upon the midnight pale.  
  If love be wreckage scattered on the sand,  
  Let memory’s tide erase my trembling hand.

124

Within my breast, a winter’s thorn doth dwell,  
Love’s fire denied, yet still it burns me sore;  
My silent heart becomes a frosted shell,  
Yet dreams of springtime knock upon its door.  
The moon observes my lonesome, aching plight,  
Her silver gaze illumes my midnight fears,  
While hope, a shadow trembling in the night,  
Competes with memories that draw forth tears.  
O, gentle winds that wander through the glade,  
Convey my longing to the sleeping rose,  
Perhaps its blush may find what I have prayed—  
A warmth to thaw the bitter chill I chose.  
  Yet if my soul must bear this wintry gloom,  
  Let love’s lost ember through the darkness bloom.

125

Upon the barren field of hope I tread,  
Where love’s sweet harvest withers into stone,  
The golden dreams that once the springtime fed  
Lie hushed beneath the earth, unseen, unknown.  
The zephyrs whisper tales of yester-year,  
When laughter danced like sunlight on the grain,  
But now the air is thick with silent fear,  
And longing’s seeds lie dormant in their pain.  
Yet still I turn the furrow with my hand,  
And cast my prayers like grain upon the ground,  
Believing rain may bless this thirsty land,  
That from this dust some future joy be found.  
  For hope endures when all the rest is fled—  
  A single bloom where once the heart has bled.

126

O thou, false Love, dost wield a sugared tongue,  
Yet piercest deep where hope and heart were young.  
Thy honeyed words, like dew on blushing rose,  
Conceal the thorn that underneath thee grows.  
What gentle promise glimmers in thine eyes,  
Yet shadows gather where thy favor lies.  
A fleeting warmth thy fickle flame bestows,  
But leaves behind the chill the wise man knows.  
Within thy smile, a tempest softly wakes,  
And dreams, once bright, now vanish in thy mist.  
The sweetest fruit thy tempting hand oft makes  
Turns bitter on the lips that once were kissed.  
  Yet, false or true, my soul remains thy thrall—  
  For love, though false, is fairest of them all.

127

O thou, whose smile did once my soul enthrall,  
Now art thou winter’s breath on summer’s rose;  
The gilded dawn that heard my spirit’s call  
Is shrouded now where silent sorrow grows.  
In days when laughter lit thy gentle eyes,  
The world seemed bathed in ever-golden rays,  
Yet now the sun within my bosom dies,  
And night invades the memory of those days.  
What cruel wind hath swept thy warmth away,  
And left me wandering through frost and shade?  
The garden’s blush is paler by the day,  
Its petals droop where once thy light was laid.  
  Yet hope remains, though tempests round me close:  
  That spring may wake anew my slumbering rose.

128

O Love, thou art a tyrant dressed in light,  
Thy kisses burn as winter's frost at night;  
Thy whispered vows are daggers in disguise,  
Yet fools and sages both thy touch invite.  
With honeyed words thou draw’st the dreaming eyes  
To castles built of hope on shifting sand;  
And though thy chains be silken, none denies  
The tender bruise that lingers on the hand.  
In blooming fields thy laughter haunts the air,  
A siren’s song that beckons hearts to stray.  
Thy rose conceals the thorn none can forbear,  
And petals fall as swiftly as the day.  
  Yet in thy shadow, mortals seek delight,  
  For none escape the sovereign of the night.

129

O cruel Love, thou tyrant of my breast,  
Thy icy fingers clutch what once was fire,  
Thou turn’st my days to nights of scanty rest,  
And feed’st my hope with thorns of sharp desire.  
Where once thy whisper soothed my waking soul,  
Now bitter winds of longing scour my mind;  
Within thy chains I play a hapless role,  
A captive heart to fate and will resigned.  
Yet still thy glance is honey on my tongue,  
Thy promise, gold that glitters in my dreams;  
For though thy song is sweetest when unsung,  
Its echo breaks my reason at the seams.  
  So let me live, though riven and undone—  
  For none escape thee, Love, nor would I run.

130

O thou, whom cruel Love hath crowned my bane,  
Thy silence strikes my heart with frost and scorn,  
Each word unsaid doth echo in my brain,  
A ghostly whisper, lost, unloved, forlorn.  
The midnight’s hush, thy absence doth proclaim,  
And shadows paint thy likeness on my wall,  
In dreams I chase the embers of thy name,  
Yet waking finds me captive to thy thrall.  
If but thy voice would thaw this wintry grief,  
And grant my soul a balm to soothe its ache,  
Perchance my hope might blossom with belief,  
And from despair, a gentler morning make.  
  Yet still I pine, beneath this silent doom,  
  For love unspoken is a lover’s tomb.

131

O cruel Love, thou tyrant of my breast,  
Dost mock my heart with hope, yet grant no rest.  
Thy golden promises, so finely dressed,  
Are but the chains in which my soul is pressed.  
Upon the night’s soft wing thy whispers fall,  
And stir the tender embers of desire,  
Yet vanish ere the morning’s earnest call,  
And leave me prisoner to longing’s fire.  
I court thy favor with a trembling hand,  
And beg thee, gentle tyrant, be thou kind;  
But still I wander in a barren land,  
With haunted dreams and reason left behind.  
  Yet though thy arrows wound me to the core,  
  I yield, sweet Love, and crave thy pain once more.

132

O wretched soul, thou art Love’s captive thrall,  
Whose fettered sighs in shadowed chambers dwell,  
Each heartbeat chained within a silent hall,  
Where hope and sorrow side by side repel.  
Upon the midnight’s mournful, moaning breath  
Thy longing whispers rise, yet soon descend;  
Despair entwines thee in its woven wreath,  
And dreams of joy to bitter phantoms bend.  
Yet in the prison of thy aching breast,  
Some spark of gentle light begins to burn,  
A promise, faint, by patient faith confessed,  
That Fortune’s wheel, in time, may turn and turn.  
  Endure, poor soul, though Love thy peace hath riven—  
  From darkest night, the dawn is often given.

133

O wretched Soul, why dost thou wound thine own,  
In love's deceitful grasp art captive thrown?  
Why feed the flame that leaves thee burnt and bare,  
Yet shun the gentle balm that soothes despair?  
Each midnight sigh that weaves through empty air  
Doth echo hopes once bright, now frail and lone;  
Thy heart, a prisoner by longing sown,  
Finds naught but thorns where roses once were fair.  
Canst thou not see the shackles round thy mind,  
Forged by thine hand, to keep thy spirit bound?  
Release the chains that thou thyself dost find,  
And let thy soul in sweeter peace be crowned.  
  For self-inflicted grief doth deepest sting—  
  Yet self-forgiveness may thy freedom bring.

134

O soul, thou traitor dressed in love’s array,  
Why dost thou mock my heart with honeyed wiles?  
Thy gentle words, like dawn, the night betray,  
Yet hide behind the veil of secret guiles.  
Thou steal’st my peace with every whispered vow,  
And bind’st my hopes in silken threads of lies;  
My trembling faith is bruised and broken now  
By glances masked in sweetly feigned disguise.  
Did ever sun beguile the trusting rose  
To bloom for light that soon would turn away?  
So hast thou drawn my heart, and then dispose  
Of all my dreams as if they were but clay.  
  Yet still, despite the wounds thy hand imparts,  
  I wear thy chain and bless thee in my heart.

135

My soul, a vassal chained by Love’s disdain,  
Doth nightly plead to stars that mock its woe,  
Their silver eyes indifferent to my pain,  
Yet on their frozen gaze my hopes bestow.  
The moon, pale witness to my weary plight,  
Unfolds her arms yet offers no embrace,  
She bathes my longing in her borrowed light,  
And carves remembrance on my longing face.  
Each sigh, a petal blown on midnight air,  
Falls dead upon the garden of my heart,  
Where roses, once in bloom, now grow despair,  
And thorns of absence tear my dreams apart.  
  Yet faithful still, my soul endures the fire,  
  And feeds on ashes, kindled by desire.

136

My soul, a wailing ghost in twilight's keep,  
Doth haunt the silent halls where love lies dead,  
The shadows whisper secrets as I weep,  
And sorrow paints the dawn with mournful red.  
Upon the marble floor my footsteps fall,  
Each echo bearing witness to my pain,  
While memories, like tapestries, recall  
The golden days now washed away by rain.  
The moon, pale guardian of broken hearts,  
Doth cast her silver gaze upon my woe,  
And with her light, a fleeting hope imparts  
That from these ruins, joy might someday grow.  
  Yet still I wander, lost, from night to morn,  
  A soul undone, by love and longing torn.

137

O treacherous Love, thou thief that plunders soul,  
Thou dost with bitter mirth my peace betray,  
Thy honeyed words in shadowed whispers toll,  
And lead my trusting heart a-wild astray.  
With gentle sighs thou paint’st the sky anew,  
Yet underneath thy cloak, sharp daggers gleam,  
The fairest smiles conceal what mischiefs brew,  
And waking hours dissolve into a dream.  
Thy promises, like roses, sweet and red,  
Conceal the thorn that waits in sweet disguise;  
Upon thy altar, countless tears are shed,  
And hope, once bright, in silent sorrow dies.  
  Yet still I yield, though reason bids me flee—  
  For captive to thy art, I long to be.

138

O Love, thou art a serpent in my breast,  
Whose honeyed tongue dost turn my peace to gall,  
Enwinding all my thoughts in sly unrest,  
A gilded chain that binds yet doth enthrall.  
Thy whispered vows are roses tipped with thorn,  
That bleed me sweetly with each perfumed lie;  
Thy glance, a dawn where hope and ache are born,  
A fleeting sun that sets within mine eye.  
I drink thy poison gladly, cup by cup,  
And chase the fevered dreams thy wiles impart,  
For though my reason bids me give thee up,  
I clasp thee closer, prisoner of my heart.  
  O Love, thou art both tempest and embrace—  
  My sweetest ruin, and my soul’s solace.

139

O bitter Love, thou tyrant of my breast,  
In chains of longing dost thou keep me bound,  
Thou mak’st my quiet heart a stormy nest,  
Where once was peace, now tumults do confound.  
Thy honeyed words are daggers in disguise,  
That pierce the softest chambers of my soul;  
Thy gentle smile conceals a world of lies,  
And in thy gaze does my undoing roll.  
Yet though I curse thee, still I seek thy flame,  
Compelled by hope that thou wilt turn and see  
The smoldering ruin, bearing thy sweet name,  
And set me from this cruel bondage free.  
  O Love, thou art both prison and release—  
  My greatest torment, and my only peace.

140

O Love, thou art a tyrant dressed in light,  
That wounds mine heart e'en as thou bidst me live,  
With silken chains thou bind'st my soul so tight,  
Yet sweetest pains thy gentle torments give.  
Thy eyes, twin stars, do pierce the midnight gloom,  
And draw me forth from reason's quiet shore,  
Within thy gaze my fated hopes assume  
A fire that burns, and leaves me longing more.  
Thou speak'st in whispers soft as April rain,  
But every word a tempest in my breast;  
I chase thy shadow, courting noble pain,  
And find in restlessness my only rest.  
  Yet though I bleed, thy mercy I implore—  
  For in thy bondage, freedom I adore.

141

O cruel Love, thou tyrant of my breast,  
Thou mock’st my hopes and robb’st me of my rest.  
With sugared words thou bid’st my soul be blest,  
Yet turn’st thy face and leave’st me sore oppressed.  
Thy gentle hand, once soft as summer rain,  
Now draws the blade that wounds me every eve.  
I drink thy poison, sweetened with my pain,  
And in thy snares I linger, loath to leave.  
Thou art the sun that wakes the budding rose,  
Yet scorch’st the petals with thy burning gaze.  
I chase thy shadow everywhere it goes,  
Entrapped within the labyrinthine maze.  
  Yet though thou art both torment and delight,  
  My heart is thine, for thou art all my night.

142

O Love, thou art a tyrant dressed in grace,  
With honeyed tongue, yet venom in thy kiss,  
Thy gentle hand conceals a ruthless mace,  
Thy promised heaven veils a deep abyss.  
Thou lead’st the heart through fields of budding bloom,  
Then cast’st it down where thorns and brambles grow;  
In laughter’s light, thou hid’st impending doom,  
And mask’st despair with every rosy glow.  
Yet still we chase thy ever-shifting shade,  
And barter sense for folly’s fleeting fire,  
Enchanted by the games that thou hast played,  
Ensnared within the cords of wild desire.  
  Though cruel, thou art the breath by which I live,  
  For pain from thee is joy none else can give.

143

O cruel Love, thou tyrant of my breast,  
Thy icy hand forbids my soul to rest.  
Thou draw’st the daylight from my weary eyes,  
And in its stead, a thousand sleepless sighs.  
Thy silent arrows pierce, yet leave no scar,  
Invisible, but keen as winter’s air.  
I beg thy mercy, but thou turn’st away,  
And mock’st my prayers at dawning of the day.  
Thy power bends the proud and lays them low,  
And kings and beggars equally must know  
The fevered longing burning in thy chain,  
The bitter sweetness wedded to thy pain.  
  Yet though thou art a tyrant, still I crave  
  To live and die thy captive and thy slave.

144

When love, once sweet, now tastes of sourest brine,  
My heart doth war 'twixt worship and disdain,  
Each memory a ghost of sugared wine  
That stains the lips with aftertaste of pain.  
The golden words once whispered in the night  
Are echoes now, their music turned to stone;  
The gentle touch that set my soul alight  
Hath fled, and left my longing twice alone.  
Yet in this tempest, reason bends and breaks,  
For still I seek the hand that dealt the blow;  
A fool’s devotion, heart that never wakes,  
Chained to the past and loath to let it go.  
  Yet, hope remains where bitter sorrows dwell—  
  That love may bloom anew from such farewell.

145

Thou art my joy, yet sorrow's root thou art,  
Thy gentle smile doth wound as much as heal,  
A glance from thee doth quicken yet depart,  
And leaves behind a longing none conceal.  
Thy voice, a melody of sweet despair,  
Doth lift my soul and cast it to the deep,  
I taste the honeyed promise in the air,  
Yet nightly in thy absence, wake and weep.  
The roses of thy cheeks outblush the dawn,  
Yet thorns within their beauty pierce me through,  
Each fond embrace, ere morning, is withdrawn,  
And dreams dissolve like early drops of dew.  
  Yet still I love, though love be fraught with pain:  
  For joy and sorrow, both, in thee remain.

146

Thy honeyed speech, a venom in disguise,  
Did teach my heart to cherish and despise.  
Thine eyes, twin stars, did promise gentle light,  
Yet drew my soul to wander through the night.  
With every vow, a subtle shadow grew,  
And hope, once bright, did flicker, pale and thin.  
What once was sweet became a bitter rue,  
As trust decayed to dust and dwelled within.  
Yet still I linger where thy whispers call,  
Entranced by echoes laced with hidden pain;  
A captive to the rise and cruel fall,  
The pleasure tangled ever with the bane.  
  O teach me then to love and loathe thee well,  
  For in thy charm, both bliss and torment dwell.

147

When thou dost smile, the sun itself turns pale,  
Yet in thy gaze, my hopeful heart doth freeze;  
Thy laughter rings as music through the vale,  
And all the world grows gentle by degrees.  
The roses blush to see thy blush anew,  
While zephyrs pause to listen to thy voice;  
The morning dew reflects thy spirit true,  
And in thy presence, even stars rejoice.  
But when thy eyelids close against the day,  
A shadow steals across the golden light;  
The lark grows mute, the sky seems cold and gray,  
The fields lie hushed, awaiting thy delight.  
  Yet, grant me but thy smile, so pure and bright—  
  And all my darkness blossoms into light.

148

Thou art the sun that warms yet leaves me cold,  
A flame that burns my soul, yet turns away.  
Thy golden gaze is distant, bright and bold,  
Yet shadows linger where thy light should stay.  
I seek thee in the blush of breaking dawn,  
Yet find thee hidden in the noontide glare.  
Thy presence brief as dew upon the lawn,  
And absence heavier than midnight air.  
Thy laughter calls me forth and bids me trust,  
Yet silence follows, echoing my doubt.  
My yearning heart, a compass set to rust,  
Still turns to thee though hope is cast about.  
  O sun, return and let thy warmth be mine,  
  Else leave me not with longing as my sign.

149

Thou art the sun that warms yet leaves me cold,  
A gilded dream that turns my hope to stone,  
Thy golden light is distant, bright yet old,  
And in its shine, my longing stands alone.  
Thy voice, a melody that haunts the air,  
Doth stir my soul and bid my heart to leap,  
Yet every word reminds me of despair,  
For promises thou makest, never keep.  
Thy beauty, like the dawn, doth pierce mine eyes,  
But fleeting as the morning's gentle hue,  
A phantom hope that ever onward flies,  
And leaves me yearning vainly after you.  
  Yet though I freeze beneath thy radiant gaze,  
  I chase the warmth of thee through endless days.

150

When love’s sweet balm doth poison mine own soul,  
Yet healing’s kiss doth leave a deeper scar,  
In passion’s fire I play the wayward role,  
And chase the phantom light from star to star.  
The gentlest hand may deal the cruelest blow,  
While honeyed words do mask a bitter heart;  
I drink the draught that longing doth bestow,  
Yet thirst anew ere solace can impart.  
Why must the rose bear thorns beneath its red,  
And summer skies conceal impending rain?  
The sweetest song is oft the most unsaid,  
And laughter hides the echo of my pain.  
  Yet still I love, though wisdom bids me flee—  
  For hope is blind, and wills what cannot be.

151

My soul, in shackles bound by sweetest hate,  
Doth yearn for freedom from love’s cruel art;  
Yet every breath doth seal a harsher fate,  
And passion’s chains close tighter round my heart.  
The moon delights to mock my nightly woe,  
With silver beams that dance upon my tears,  
While shadows whisper truths I dare not know,  
And memory’s echo magnifies my fears.  
In dreams I chase the light that once was mine,  
But wake to find thee vanished with the dawn;  
The sun ascends, yet all its rays decline  
When hope that warmed my spirit now is gone.  
  Yet still I wear these fetters, proud and true,  
  For even bound, my soul belongs to you.

152

My soul, a tempest tossed by love's deceit,  
Yet craves the calm where bitter passions meet.  
Each whispered promise, sweet as summer's air,  
Turns winter-cold, dissolving into pain.  
The moon observes my longing and despair,  
And bathes my restless heart in silver rain.  
I chase illusions dancing on the tide,  
A fleeting hope that slips between my hands.  
No gentle harbor left where I may hide,  
No anchor holds where shifting sorrow stands.  
Yet in this storm, a stubborn hope remains,  
A star that guides me through the dark unknown,  
For though love’s tempest binds me fast in chains,  
Its fury carves a strength I call my own.  
And when the dawn ignites the weary sea,  
Perhaps its light will set my spirit free.

153

When soul to soul in silent longing pleads,  
Yet thine, untouched, my pining spirit spurns,  
The night descends and with it sorrow breeds  
A garden wild wherein my passion burns.  
The moon doth pale before thy distant gaze,  
While hope’s frail bud is trampled by despair;  
Each whispered wish is scattered in the haze,  
And dreams fall silent, heavy in the air.  
Yet still I wait, a shadow by the door,  
Entranced by visions thou wilt ne’er fulfill—  
For love, unkind, demands that I implore  
And cast my heart against thy colder will.  
  But if one glance thy gentle soul betrays,  
  My weary heart would kindle in thy blaze.

154

My soul, though chained to thine in longing's night,  
Doth battle hope with sorrow's cruel delight,  
The moon doth pale beneath our silent vow,  
While stars bear witness to our secret plight.  
Upon the breath of dreams I seek thee now,  
Yet waking hours deny thy visage fair;  
Each moment lost becomes a solemn brow,  
A crown of absence none but I can wear.  
Yet still, thy memory doth gentle grow,  
A balm upon the wounds that time imparts,  
And though we drift where shadowed currents flow,  
Thy name resounds in chambers of my heart.  
  Thus, bound by fate, our spirits intertwined,  
  In love’s embrace both lost and yet enshrined.

155

How oft my soul, in bitter longing caught,  
Doth mock the sweetness thou wilt ne’er bestow,  
Each night I pace the chambers of my thought,  
Entrapped by hope, yet fettered by my woe.  
The moon, pale witness to my ceaseless pain,  
Casts silver pity on my sleepless eyes,  
And whispers comfort, gentle and in vain,  
While shadows echo with my silent cries.  
O would that fortune, fickle in her game,  
Might turn her wheel and grant thee to my arms,  
But winds are deaf and stars ignore my claim,  
And time dissolves the memory of thy charms.  
  Yet still I wait, where dreams and sorrows blend,  
  For love that mocks my longing without end.

156

Within my breast doth love and loathing strive,  
A serpent’s kiss, yet nectar to contrive.  
Desire’s flame doth smolder in my veins,  
Whilst reason’s voice attempts to cool its fire.  
The sweetest joy is often laced with pains,  
And hope’s bright wings grow heavy with desire.  
I feast on dreams that flicker in the night,  
Yet morning’s truth dispels the golden spell;  
Each glance, a spark that sets my soul alight—  
Each word, a tolling of the warning bell.  
My heart, a stage where passions cruelly play,  
Is torn between devotion and disdain,  
  Yet in thy eyes the storms are swept away—  
  For love, though fraught, shall evermore remain.

157

How oft my heart would rise, yet sinks for thee,  
In love’s bright flame, I freeze in shadowed doubt,  
The hope that dawns, like morning on the sea,  
Is quenched by tempests swirling round about.  
Thy smile, a sun, doth melt my icy fear,  
Yet clouds of silence steal thy rays away;  
Each word unsaid becomes a falling tear,  
And night usurps the promise of the day.  
Would I could speak the longing in my breast,  
Or find in thine a gentle, kind reply;  
Yet silence binds my tongue, denies me rest,  
As yearning grows beneath the watchful sky.  
  Oh, grant me courage, or thy heart’s release,  
  That I may soar in love, or find my peace.

158

O, bitter-sweetness dwelleth in my breast,  
Love’s honeyed curse with venom is oppress’d.  
Fair hope, thou art a thief in silken guise,  
Who weav’st thy golden snares with gentle jest.  
Upon my nights, thy shadow softly lies,  
Yet in thy dawn, a thousand sorrows nest.  
The rose thou giv’st, in fragrance doth disguise  
The hidden thorn that pierces at thy behest.  
Yet still I drink thy draught, though brimming woe,  
And chase the fleeting sunbeams of thy smile.  
Each joy a ghost, each promise but a show,  
Yet willingly I walk this thorn-clad mile.  
  For in thy pain, my soul hath found its part:  
  A crown of fire, and ashes in my heart.

159

Oft have I sung of love’s enchanting fire,  
Yet chilled I lie, denied its warm embrace,  
A captive soul ensnared by dark desire,  
That shadows hope and veils my longing face.  
The springtime blooms and all the birds rejoice,  
While I am left to wander winter’s night,  
With silent lips and heart that lacks a voice,  
Entombed in frost, estranged from pure delight.  
Yet still I dream, as moonbeams touch the sea,  
And paint with silver all the world below,  
That love one day might turn its gaze to me,  
And melt the ice with gentle, golden glow.  
  For though I linger lonely, cold, and true,  
  Hope’s tender flame endures because of you.

160

How sweet the poison thou hast poured in me,  
A feast of longing starved by thy disdain,  
Thy eyes, twin lanterns, burn relentlessly  
Upon my heart, which loves despite the pain.  
In midnight’s hush, thy absence rings so loud,  
A silent tempest raging in my breast.  
I wear my sorrow like a mourning shroud,  
Yet hope persists that I may find some rest.  
Each word thou speak’st, a droplet on my tongue,  
Both balm and bane, they linger and they sear.  
Thy image haunts my dreams, forever young,  
The ghost I chase, forever held so near.  
  Yet though thy love be venom, fierce and sweet,  
  I drink, and beg the fates we never meet.

161

When sweetest hope doth turn to ashes cold,  
My heart in longing burns, yet shivers still,  
Fair promises decay, their luster old,  
And reason bends beneath this wintry chill.  
The bloom of youth, once bright upon my cheek,  
Is withered by the frost of heavy hours;  
No gentle comfort does my spirit seek,  
But wanders, lost, through desolated bowers.  
Yet in the dusk, a trembling star appears,  
Its silver light upon my sorrow falls,  
Whispering courage, gently dries my tears,  
And bids me rise when deepest darkness calls.  
  For though hope’s flame may falter, faint, or part,  
  Its embers glow eternal in the heart.

162

Your love’s a winter flame that chills my bones,  
A rose whose thorns have bled me pale and dry;  
It haunts the hollow wind with aching moans,  
And paints a frost upon the morning sky.  
In silent glades where once my laughter grew,  
Now shadows linger, cold beneath your gaze;  
Yet still I seek the sun that broke in you,  
And wander lost within your icy maze.  
Your voice, a distant bell in drifting snow,  
Calls forth my heart to dance upon the air;  
Though midnight’s hush would bid me not to go,  
I chase the echo, tangled in despair.  
  For love, though sharp, endures beyond the pain—  
  Its embers glow in memory’s silent reign.

163

Your love, a winter flame that chills my core,  
Yet draws me close, though frost consumes my will.  
A silent hearth that beckons evermore,  
Its embers bright, though air around is still.  
The icéd breath between us drifts and sways,  
Yet in its dance, a secret fire is born.  
Through crystal nights and pale, unyielding haze,  
Desire persists, though hope be bruised and worn.  
Each touch a spark, yet shadows linger near,  
As longing battles with the biting cold.  
Within your gaze, the thawing dawn is clear,  
Promising warmth in stories yet untold.  
  So let this winter’s passion ever be  
  A flame that burns, yet sets my spirit free.

164

Your love, a blade concealed beneath a rose,  
Has cut my trust where sweetest promise lay,  
A gentle bloom whose thorns the night bestows  
Upon the heart that dares to trust the day.  
Your words, like petals drifting on the breeze,  
Did scent the air with hope’s enchanting song,  
Yet hidden sharpness brought me to my knees  
And proved the fairest things may turn to wrong.  
I bleed in silence, learning not to yearn,  
For beauty’s mask oft veils a secret pain,  
The lesson carved in flesh is slow to burn,  
Yet from the wound, I rise and love again.  
  Though roses wound, their fragrance haunts me still—  
  I seek their touch, despite the sharpened will.

165

You are the winter sun that warms no skin,  
A promise made of frost that will not thaw,  
A flame entombed in glass, denied within,  
A hope that shivers underneath the law.  
Your light, though bright, is distant as a star,  
It dances on the fields but leaves them bare,  
And though you gild the world from realms afar,  
No blossom dares to bloom beneath your care.  
Yet still I turn my face and seek your rays,  
And barter dreams for shadows on the snow,  
Content with fleeting hints of golden days,  
And memories of warmth I cannot know.  
  For in your cold embrace, my heart remains,  
  A captive sun, in winter’s gentle chains.

166

You are the winter sun that warms me not,  
A distant fire that flickers out of reach.  
Upon the frostbit field my hope is caught,  
Its tender green now buried, cold as bleach.  
Your smile, a pale and melancholy ray,  
Falls faint upon the shivering, frozen ground;  
Though day arrives, the shadows seem to stay,  
And silence answers every hopeful sound.  
Yet still I turn my face to you each morn,  
Desiring warmth that never truly comes.  
In barren hours, my longing is reborn,  
A song unsung, a drum that never drums.  
  Yet in this chill, your memory abides—  
  A ghostly sun, whose light my heart derides.

167

You are the winter sun I never feel,  
A distant warmth denied my frozen skin,  
Your golden promise lost to clouds of steel,  
Yet hope persists where light should once have been.  
Your rays, though bright, find no embrace in me,  
They falter at the threshold of my soul;  
I chase their glimmer through a barren lea,  
But shadows lengthen, swallowing me whole.  
Still, in my heart, your memory remains—  
A phantom fire that flickers in the frost;  
A gentle echo threading through my veins  
Of sunlit laughter I believed was lost.  
  Though cold endures and silent snow be spun,  
  My longing turns forever to your sun.

168

My soul’s a locked room, echoing your name,  
Yet love corrodes the key with rusted spite.  
The walls remember passion’s fleeting flame  
And shadows stretch the boundaries of night.  
A window’s pane admits the silver gloom,  
Where every sigh condenses on the glass.  
Unanswered prayers drift across the room,  
While time, with silent steps, prepares to pass.  
Your absence carves its letters on the floor,  
Each syllable a hollow, aching sound.  
I pace the corners, searching for the door  
That leads to you, but none is ever found.  
  Still hope, a stubborn light, will not depart—  
  It flickers, locked within my captive heart.

169

My soul’s a locked room echoing with spite,  
Where love sits caged, and starves for want of sun.  
The window’s grimed with memories of night,  
Its panes admit no hope, nor let joy run.  
Each shadow stretches long across the floor,  
A silent keeper of my secret ache,  
While time beats slow against the bolted door,  
And dreams, denied their wings, refuse to wake.  
Yet in the gloom, a trembling candle’s gleam  
Defies the dark and whispers what could be—  
A distant promise, frail as morning’s dream,  
That warmth might pierce these walls enclosing me.  
  O, grant me strength to turn the rusted key,  
  And set love free, that I at last be free.

170

My soul’s a broken mirror in your hands,  
Reflecting shards of love that cut me deep,  
Each fractured beam obeys your mute commands,  
Yet in your palm, my scattered sorrows sleep.  
The silvered scars recall your gentle gaze,  
A thousand splinters glinting in the night,  
Yet in their pain, the memory betrays  
A softer truth concealed from searching sight.  
I bleed in secret, silent as the dawn,  
My longing hidden in the glass’s gleam;  
What once was whole by cruel fate was drawn  
To fragment hope and sever every dream.  
  Yet let these ruins in your keeping stay—  
  For even shattered, love will find its way.

171

My soul’s a moth that circles round your flame,  
It burns for you, yet you forget its name.  
The night is thick with longing’s silver thread,  
Each hope ignites and falters into shame.  
I chase the light your distant eyes have shed,  
A trembling shadow dancing on the wall,  
Desire’s candle flickers blue and red,  
Yet silence answers when I dare to call.  
The wings of yearning beat against the glass,  
Unseen, unheard, they flutter and they plead,  
While time in patient bitterness must pass,  
And dreams, like ashes, scatter from the seed.  
  Yet still I turn, though pain be all I gain—  
  For love is drawn to love, though lost in vain.

172

My soul’s a locked room, echoing your name,  
Yet you pass by, unheeding of the key.  
The dust of longing settles in my frame,  
While shadows stretch where light would long to be.  
Each silent night, the moonlight strokes the floor  
With silver hopes that tremble at your step,  
And every dawn I find behind my door  
A wilted dream, a promise never kept.  
You haunt the corridors I cannot leave,  
A gentle ghost that stirs the breathless air,  
And though I plead for solace, still I grieve,  
For love is absent, though your shade is there.  
  Yet hope endures, beyond my reason’s doom—  
  Some day you'll turn, and sunlight flood this room.

173

My soul’s a ship that sails your distant shore,  
Yet storms of silence dash me from your light.  
The lonely wind laments what came before,  
Each wave a memory lost to the night.  
I chart the stars, yet find no guiding sign,  
The moon’s pale lantern flickers in my wake.  
Between us lies an ever-widening line,  
A boundless sea I cannot hope to break.  
Yet hope, like salt, still stings upon my brow,  
And yearning’s tide returns with every breath.  
Your absence is the anchor weighing now,  
That holds me fast and threatens me with death.  
  Still, steadfast in the dark I brave the foam,  
  In dreams, your distant harbor calls me home.

174

My heart, a rusted lock, resists the key,  
Love’s thief departs but leaves the scars in place,  
No gentle hand can set this prisoner free,  
Nor sunlight’s warmth unmask this shadowed face.  
Each whispered hope dissolves in bitter rain,  
The silent night conspires to keep me bound,  
Old memories, like thorns, revive the pain,  
And lost desires grow wild without a sound.  
Yet in the gloom a distant promise gleams,  
A silver thread that tides of doubt defy,  
It weaves through broken trust and shattered dreams,  
And dares my weary soul again to try.  
  Though rust and sorrow weigh upon my chest,  
  I dream that love might grant my heart its rest.

175

My heart’s a wilted rose clenched in my fist,  
Each thorn a memory I can’t resist.  
The petals, faded, trace what once was kissed,  
And fragrance lingers, haunted by the mist.  
The crimson blush of youth has long since fled,  
Yet in the withered bloom, old passions lie.  
A thousand gentle words remain unsaid,  
While hope’s pale shadow flickers in my eye.  
In silent dusk, I count the scars I bear,  
Each prick a testament to love’s embrace.  
Still, fingers tighten, bound by old despair,  
Reluctant to release what time can’t erase.  
  Yet from this sorrow, something sweet may grow:  
  A wiser heart, and strength I did not know.

176

My heart’s a locked room, echoing with spite,  
Where love’s old embers smolder in the dark,  
Each memory flickers, warring with the night,  
And shadows carve their secrets, cold and stark.  
I pace the prison walls of my regret,  
The ghost of laughter haunting every stone,  
The promises I made, I can’t forget,  
Now linger like a sigh, forlorn, alone.  
Yet through the cracks, a slender ray appears,  
A golden thread that trembles in the gloom,  
It weaves through sorrow, into hope from tears,  
And dares to dream of life beyond this room.  
  O, may some gentle hand the key impart,  
  And loose the iron sorrow from my heart.

177

My heart’s a locked room, echoing your name,  
While you stroll free, untouched by any chain.  
Within these walls, desire kindles flame,  
Yet outwardly, I dare not show the pain.  
Each silent night, your memory takes flight,  
A ghostly shadow dancing on the wall.  
I chase in dreams your ever-distant light,  
And waking, find I’ve never known you at all.  
The window’s barred, and time drips down the pane,  
Each drop a hope, each hope a fading spark.  
Your laughter lingers, sweet and bright, then wanes,  
Leaving my soul to wander through the dark.  
  Yet should you turn and seek the key one day,  
  You’d find my love, unguarded, there to stay.

178

My heart’s a locked room you have never tried,  
While you chase sunlight, I remain inside.  
The window’s dust is thick with silent years,  
And hope, a moth, beats softly at the pane.  
Your laughter drifts like music past my fears,  
Yet leaves me tangled in a velvet chain.  
I watch your shadow paint the garden wall,  
Too shy to beckon you to cross my gate.  
The echo of your footsteps in the hall  
Reminds me how desire must learn to wait.  
If once you turned the key with gentle hand,  
You’d find the warmth that slumbers out of sight,  
A room of petals, dreams at your command,  
Unveiled by trust and opened to the light.  
Yet still I linger, silent and apart,  
A fortress built to guard a tender heart.

179

My heart’s a locked room echoing your name,  
Yet you stroll past, the key within your hand.  
A silent plea concealed, a quiet flame  
That flickers shadows on the empty land.  
The window’s frost preserves your fading trace,  
Each pane a mirror longing for your eyes.  
I count the footsteps lost within this space,  
While hope in corners quietly defies.  
The garden’s breathless hush awaits your tread,  
As petals close for night’s uncertain guest.  
Upon my chest, your absence blooms instead,  
A thorny rose that will not grant me rest.  
  Yet if you’d turn, the dawn might gently start—  
  Unbar the door, and walk into my heart.

180

Love scrawls its name across my sleepless mind,  
A thief that whispers promises, then lies—  
It weaves through night on winds both cold and kind,  
And paints devotion’s fire behind my eyes.  
It treads the haunted halls where doubts reside,  
A shadow shifting softly on the floor,  
Yet in its arms my trembling fears confide,  
And hunger aches for what it can’t restore.  
The dawn betrays the secrets dreams have kept,  
Each breath a memory, each sigh a plea,  
Within my chest, the seeds of longing slept,  
Awakened now by what may never be.  
  Yet though love toys with hope and cruel delight,  
  I beg the thief return again tonight.

181

Love sharpens knives and whispers in my chest,  
It dresses hope in black and calls it friend.  
The moonlight paints a longing on my vest,  
While midnight’s silence dares my heart to mend.  
With every breath, a thousand doubts arise,  
Like thorns entwined around a trembling rose.  
Desire’s shadow flickers in my eyes,  
And every promise breaks before it grows.  
Yet passion’s ember glows beneath the ache,  
A secret warmth that will not yield to frost.  
Though reason pleads for caution I forsake,  
For love persists despite the battles lost.  
  So let it wound, and let it heal in time—  
  This bittersweet, relentless, holy crime.

182

Love wears a mask and mocks me with her smile,  
Her laughter sharp, it echoes through my chest;  
She dances just beyond my reach, the while  
She plants false hope and urges on my quest.  
Her eyes, twin lanterns, promise sweet delight,  
Yet vanish when I dare to draw too near;  
She weaves her riddles through the hush of night,  
And leaves me tangled in my longing's fear.  
Her silken words are veiled in fleeting mist,  
Her touch a breeze that cools and burns the same;  
She tempts my heart with dreams that can't exist,  
Yet I am helpless, captive to her game.  
  Though love may jest and shroud herself in jest,  
  I chase her shadow, never granted rest.

183

Love gnaws my heart with teeth of sharpened spite,  
While Hope, a liar, whispers you’ll be mine—  
Each day I perish, hungering for your light,  
Yet shadows mock the longing in my spine.  
Your absence carves its hollows in my chest,  
And silence drowns the music of my soul;  
Desire devours the fragments of my rest,  
While reason falters, far from self-control.  
I chase illusions painted on the air,  
And grasp at dreams that crumble into dust;  
Your memory burns with beauty, fierce and rare,  
A fevered ache I nourish out of trust.  
  Still, let me love, though hope and sense decay;  
  For even pain is sweet when you’re the prey.

184

O Love, you mock me with your honeyed lies,  
You wear her face and haunt my sleepless night,  
You dress in longing, cloaked in sweet disguise,  
And draw my soul to shadows out of sight.  
Your whispered vows, like wind, are never true,  
They vanish swift as dawn breaks through the gloom;  
Yet still I chase the vision that I knew,  
A rose in winter, longing just to bloom.  
You steal my peace with every gentle word,  
And plant a hope that withers in the day.  
Yet in your silence, aching thoughts are stirred,  
And reason’s voice is chased so far away.  
  O cruel Love, your jest will be my end—  
  My heart, my foe, yet evermore my friend.

185

Love sharpens knives and whispers through the night,  
It wears your face, but turns away from mine.  
A phantom clothed in hope, then lost from sight,  
It flickers faint where longest shadows twine.  
It haunts the hush between the clock’s dull chime,  
A breath withheld before the breaking dawn,  
It turns the honeyed hours into rime,  
And leaves the garden’s blush to wilt and fawn.  
Yet in the lattice of your absent gaze  
A thousand silent promises remain,  
Unraveling the gold of former days  
To knot afresh the gentle threads of pain.  
  Though love may wound, its whisper still I chase—  
  A ghost that bears the memory of your face.

186

My soul, that traitor, courts the night’s embrace,  
It whispers lies in tongues I thought were mine,  
A mask of calm conceals its furtive chase,  
While shadowed secrets through its chambers twine.  
It tempts my heart with dreams that never wake,  
And paints the moon with longing’s fevered hue,  
Each star it counts, a promise doomed to break,  
Each silent hour, a hope it will undo.  
Yet in its dark, a spark of dawn persists,  
A stubborn flame that will not be denied,  
Though sorrow’s hand my trembling spirit twists,  
And fear and yearning walk the night beside.  
  O faithless soul, who courts the velvet shade,  
  Still, in thy depths, some light will not be swayed.

187

My soul betrays me, whispering of lies,  
It paints your shadow where my solace dies.  
The midnight wind recites thy faithless art,  
And weds a chill to this forsaken heart.  
A tapestry of doubts is gently sewn  
By fingers made of memory and dread,  
And seeds of sorrow, silent, swiftly grown,  
Awake in dreams upon my lonely bed.  
Yet still I seek thy voice within the rain,  
And gather hope from echoes on the breeze;  
Though reason bids me cast away my pain,  
I cling to visions only longing sees.  
  For love that lingers, though in shadow cast,  
  Still haunts the present, fettered to the past.

188

My soul, that fickle traitor, plays with fire,  
It courts the ash of love, then shuns desire.  
In twilight’s hush it weaves a silken lie,  
Yet flees the gentle hand that would inspire.  
A whispered promise drifts upon the air,  
But shadows chase the truth my heart would claim;  
For every hope, a silent, cold despair  
Doth steal the color from affection’s flame.  
I long to trust the dawn, to rise anew,  
But turn from morning’s gaze with trembling will.  
What solace lies in dreams I cannot rue,  
When longing’s cup is never quite fulfilled?  
  Yet still, my soul returns to love’s cruel game,  
  A moth that circles ever round the same.

189

My soul, a beggar knocking at your gate,  
Finds silence dressed in velvet, cold and sly,  
It waits beneath the arch of hope and fate,  
A shadow pressed against the moonlit sky.  
Your eyes, twin lanterns shuttered to my plea,  
Hold secrets locked in chambers of the night;  
I count the silver hours that flow from me  
And barter dreams for fragments of your light.  
Yet longing grows where distance sows its seed,  
And hunger sharpens on your absent breath;  
Desire, unfed, becomes a thorny creed,  
Each heartbeat tolling love’s imagined death.  
  Still, at your door, I kneel with empty hands—  
  A beggar lost where silent beauty stands.

190

My soul, a beggar, knocks at love’s shut door,  
While bitter winds of longing sweep the floor.  
Within these halls, the silence grows so deep  
That shadows stretch like ghosts upon the keep.  
A flicker, hope—a lantern in the night—  
Yet each faint glow is swallowed by the gloom.  
I search for warmth, but find the chill of blight,  
And footsteps echo, hollow in this room.  
O gentle grace, why dost thou stay thy hand?  
What secret locks do guard thy heart from mine?  
Upon the threshold, still I humbly stand,  
My plea for mercy tangled in the vine.  
  Yet grant me but a whisper, soft and true,  
  And from thy love, my soul shall bloom anew.

191

My soul, a beggar, knocks at love’s cold gate,  
While hope, her shadow, mocks my empty plea.  
The silent moon observes my woeful state,  
And stars, indifferent, blink above the sea.  
Each whispered wish dissolves in winter’s breath,  
A frail petition lost to midnight’s ear;  
Desire, entwined with doubt, courts gentle death,  
Yet clings to memories I hold most dear.  
O heart, why dost thou labor in despair,  
And sow thy longing where no harvest grows?  
The frozen garden yields no blossom fair,  
Its barren soil unsoftened by my woes.  
  Yet still I knock, though frostbite seals the air—  
  For love’s embrace could melt eternal snows.

192

My heart, a traitor, whispers lies each night,  
It paints my longing’s ghost in bitter hue,  
It shrouds the honest day in furtive light,  
And forges shadows where hope once was true.  
The silent hours breed phantoms of regret,  
Each sigh a tempest on my weary breast;  
Unbidden dreams my waking mind beset,  
And rob my soul of solace and of rest.  
Yet still, I court the ache I cannot quell,  
Enamored of the wounds that will not heal;  
For in the darkness, sweet illusions dwell,  
And promised joys my reason would conceal.  
  O heart, thy treason I must yet forgive—  
  For by thy lies, I learn again to live.

193

Love knocks with knuckles cold upon my chest,  
Yet mocks my heart, which once she called her own,  
A silent guest who will not let me rest,  
But haunts the hollow halls when I’m alone.  
She weaves her sighs into the midnight air,  
And paints her shadow on the window pane;  
Her laughter lingers where the moon is fair,  
Yet leaves my soul to languish in its pain.  
O bitter-sweet remembrance of her smile,  
That glimmers faintly through the shroud of years,  
My hope is tethered to her gentle guile,  
My joy is mingled with my secret tears.  
  Though love departs and leaves me longing still,  
  Her ghost remains—my heart bends to her will.

194

Love grins at me with teeth of sharpened spite,  
Its shadow dances close, then steals my day.  
It stains the gentle blush of morning light,  
And sours silver dreams that drift away.  
A whisper in the dark, it chills my rest,  
Yet draws me nearer with a velvet snare.  
Its honeyed poison seeps within my chest,  
A wounding sweetness tangled in my care.  
I chase its phantom through the tangled wood,  
Each step betrayed by hope I cannot flee.  
It sculpts my longing into knotted good,  
A riddle sung by night’s uncertainty.  
  Yet though love wounds with malice, sly and rough,  
  Still, I return—its cruelty is enough.

195

Love knocks upon my ribs with knuckled spite,  
And laughs as I unlock the door each night.  
Her shadow slips along the candle’s glow,  
A flicker in the silence, swift and slight.  
She weaves her questions where the heartbeats go,  
Yet answers with a kiss both sweet and slow.  
I bargain with her presence for my peace,  
And find my rest in what I cannot know.  
She paints my pulse in colors none release,  
And binds my tongue with longing’s rare caprice.  
Her promise is a riddle, sharp and deep,  
She stirs me gently when I wish to sleep.  
  Though reason bids me bolt the door and flee,  
  Still, love returns—forever haunting me.

196

Love slams my door and laughs behind the frame,  
She wears my hope like trophies on her wall,  
Her shadow sweeps the hall and speaks my name,  
While silent echoes down the stairway fall.  
She binds the sun and reins the wanton breeze,  
Yet leaves my heart to wander, lost and cold;  
She weaves a spell that brings me to my knees,  
Her distant glance is silver, sharp, and bold.  
Yet still I wait, a sentinel in gloom,  
For gentler hands to guide the night away,  
And search for roses budding out of doom,  
Or morning’s blush to bring a kinder day.  
  But love, that jester, dances out of sight—  
  And leaves me longing for her ghost each night.

197

Love mocks my heart and laughs behind my back,  
She paints with ice the warmth I sorely lack.  
Her distant eyes, like winter’s starless night,  
Unravel dreams and turn my hope to black.  
Her words, once honey, now are edged with spite,  
Yet still I linger, bound by cruel delight.  
She moves with grace through halls where I despair,  
A phantom clothed in moon and silver air.  
No gentle touch, nor glance to ease my pain,  
Just shadows dancing where her smile had been.  
Each day I chase her, running lost and vain,  
Entrapped by longing, haunted by what’s seen.  
  Yet in this torment, sweetened by her name,  
  I burn for love, and gladly feed the flame.

198

You are the fire that chills my longing heart,  
A gentle wound that tears my soul apart.  
Your eyes, twin stars that pierce the velvet night,  
Do set my hopes ablaze, then swift depart.  
Upon your breath, the scent of spring takes flight,  
Yet winter’s frost remains where once was May.  
With every glance, you grant me brief delight,  
Then steal the dawn, and leave me cold as clay.  
Your laughter rings like bells in distant halls,  
A haunting chime that memory recalls.  
Though close in flesh, in spirit we remain  
As distant as the moon from restless seas.  
  Yet still, I cherish sweet, tormenting pain,  
  For in my longing, I am most at ease.

199

Your smile, a dagger wrapped in sugared grace,  
My heart, both feast and famine at your sight,  
Within your eyes, I glimpse a shadowed place  
Where longing lingers, trembling in the light;  
Your laughter dances just beyond my reach,  
A silver thread that binds me to your will;  
Each word you speak becomes a whispered breach  
Between resolve and wish I can’t distill.  
The world dissolves when you are near to me,  
Yet distance sharpens every aching need;  
Desire, a tide that will not set me free,  
Draws hope and dread from the same secret seed.  
  Yet though I bleed beneath your gentle art,  
  With every wound I love you more, sweetheart.

200

I love thee most when hatred fills my chest,  
For every wound you give, my heart protests.  
Yet in the storm, thy absence brings me rest,  
But longing grows when silence manifests.  
Thine eyes, both curse and solace to my soul,  
Do spark a fire that pain could not subdue.  
In bitter nights, thy memory takes its toll,  
Yet dawn reveals my love is ever true.  
Oh, twisted fate, that binds my heart to thine,  
Despite the thorns that pierce me through and through,  
Each tear I shed is sweeter than the wine  
Of joy unspoiled by sorrow's bitter hue.  
  Thus love and hate in equal measure dwell,  
  For loving thee, I know both heaven and hell.

201

You drenched my days in honey, nights in frost,  
Yet beg for warmth while leaving me so cold,  
A fickle sun whose gentle beams are lost  
Behind the clouds of promises you told.  
You scatter petals just to feed the wind,  
And gather shadows where the lilies grew.  
I loved the laughter, though it was thinned  
By silent tears that only I once knew.  
The silver thread of hope is frayed and worn,  
Yet still I tie it to the edge of dawn,  
Believing spring might wake what you have torn,  
And gentle light restore what has been gone.  
  Yet, in your eyes, the seasons do not stay—  
  They dance and vanish, always swept away.

202

Your laughter rings where silence fills my chest,  
Yet longing grows where hope has lain to waste.  
The golden dawn once crowned my dreams with rest,  
Now shadows chase the light I cannot taste.  
Your footsteps haunt the corridors of thought,  
A gentle echo lost in halls of time;  
The warmth you gave—so freely, now is sought  
Through distant memories and aching rhyme.  
The winds recall your voice in trembling sighs,  
Soft petals fall where once our promise grew.  
I beg the stars to answer silent cries  
And paint the midnight sky with shades of you.  
  Though absence weaves a veil of sorrow's art,  
  Your love remains, unbroken, in my heart.

203

Your smile, a sun that burns me as I freeze,  
You warm the air, yet leave my heart in cold.  
In winter's grip, you whisper summer's breeze,  
Yet from your gaze, a silent frost takes hold.  
Your laughter rings, a bell of golden light,  
That stirs the morning shadows from their gloom.  
And though you banish sorrow from my sight,  
Within your glow, my doubts and longings bloom.  
You are the fire that dances in the snow,  
A paradox that draws me ever near.  
For every joy your gentle touch bestows,  
A trembling fear of loss is always near.  
  So let me bask, though burned and left undone,  
  For love is ice and flame, and you are sun.

204

My soul, in love's embrace, finds only cold,  
Desiring warmth, yet shivers when it's told  
That passion's fire, by time, is slow controlled,  
And hope grows faint as gentle dreams unfold.  
The moonlight weeps upon my windowpane,  
Its silver beams recall your absent face;  
Each whispered breeze repeats your name in vain,  
While shadows gather in this lonely space.  
Once laughter bloomed where silence now prevails,  
And roses thrived where icy thorns entwine.  
Yet still, my heart in fervent faith inhales  
The memory of love that once was mine.  
  Though winter reigns and summer’s lost its hue,  
  I wait for spring to come again with you.

205

My soul is torn where love and loathing meet,  
A battlefield within my quiet chest,  
Where hope and fear in secret conflict greet,  
And neither grants my weary heart its rest.  
Desire’s gentle hand extends in vain,  
Yet shadowed doubts like tempests cloud the sky;  
Soft whispers promise comfort after pain,  
But bitter echoes hush their lullaby.  
How fragile stands my reason in this strife,  
Its trembling scales unsure of wrong or right;  
Each thought a soldier in the war of life,  
Each memory both solace and despite.  
  O, grant me peace or teach me to endure—  
  For only time can make these wounds obscure.

206

My soul, once bright, now tangled in disdain,  
Love's gentle touch replaced by silent war,  
Where laughter danced, now echo only pain,  
And hope retreats behind a bolted door.  
The summer's gold has withered into frost,  
Sweet memories decay in shadow's hold;  
What once was found, by bitterness is lost,  
The flame of youth grown weary, faint, and cold.  
Yet in this night, a distant star appears,  
Its trembling light defies the heavy gloom;  
It whispers softly, brushing back my fears,  
A promise blooming where the shadows loom.  
  Though sorrow binds me in its sable thread,  
  I dream of dawn, when love returns, undead.

207

My soul, a feast denied, starves at your gate,  
Yet feeds on hope’s decay, both love and hate.  
The banquet once prepared now gathers dust,  
Its silver tarnished by the tears I shed.  
In longing’s chains I lose both faith and trust,  
The echoes of your voice within my head.  
I pace the silent halls, a restless guest,  
Whose every plea dissolves into the night.  
The empty plates bear witness to my quest,  
For crumbs of warmth to set my hunger right.  
Yet in the famine, dreams refuse to die,  
Surviving on the promise of your light.  
  If ever you should bid me to your side,  
  My famished heart would bloom, its ache denied.

208

My soul, a host of longing yet denied,  
Finds warmth in love, but chills when you reside.  
The moonlight paints your shadow on my wall,  
Its silver touch a memory's disguise.  
Each whispered hope becomes a silent call,  
A plea that seeks the mercy of your eyes.  
The dawn unfolds, yet still you do not come,  
And time in patient sorrow gently weeps.  
I count the hours, every heavy drum  
That marks the distance longing ever keeps.  
Yet love endures, though absence breeds despair,  
A fragile flame that trembles in the night.  
  If fate be kind and guide you to my care,  
  Our hearts shall meet and set the world alight.

209

My soul, a vessel split by hope and spite,  
Is drawn to yours, yet banished from its shore;  
Like moon-chased tides that yearn for distant light,  
I drift in longing, lost for evermore.  
Within your eyes, a storm of promise brews,  
A tempest fierce that beckons me to break,  
Yet shadows bind me fast with ancient truths,  
And courage falters for my future’s sake.  
If love be wind, then let it fill my sails  
And bear me forth where golden harbors gleam;  
But if it be a ghost that howls and wails,  
Let silence wrap me in a gentle dream.  
  Still hope’s dim candle flickers in the night,  
  And lights the way where hearts may reunite.

210

I love the warmth your absence brings to me,  
My heart finds peace within its agony.  
Though shadows stretch where once your light would be,  
A quiet strength grows out of memory.  
The gentle hush that follows after pain  
Becomes a balm that soothes my wounded soul;  
Such loss, though sharp, has never been in vain,  
For in its void I find myself made whole.  
The echo of your laughter haunts the air,  
Yet teaches me the beauty of release.  
I learn to cherish all that isn’t there,  
And in that emptiness, discover peace.  
  Though parted now, your ghost is not unkind—  
  In absence, love’s true presence is defined.

211

Love’s sugar stings my tongue with bitter lies,  
Desire wars with reason in my chest.  
The candle’s flame is mirrored in thine eyes,  
Yet shadows gather where I seek my rest.  
The whispered promise, sweet as April rain,  
Conceals a storm that rumbles in the dark.  
I hold thy laughter close to soothe my pain,  
But gentle touch doth leave a deeper mark.  
Though hope may bloom beneath the weight of doubt,  
And morning’s gold may gild the edge of fear,  
Within my heart thine absence echoes out,  
A hollow song that only I can hear.  
  Yet still I drink of love’s ambrosial well,  
  Though every sip be sweet, and sharp as hell.

212

I love myself as much as I despise  
This heart that aches with truths my mind denies;  
A mirror's glance reveals both wound and prize,  
Reflections veiled beneath my own disguise.  
I walk the line ‘twixt loathing and embrace,  
Where shadows dance with hope upon my skin,  
And every fault I see, I must replace  
With gentle words that blossom deep within.  
Yet oft I wage a war of doubt and pride,  
A tempest born of longing and regret,  
My soul, a restless sea with tides untried,  
Still seeks a shore where sun and calm have met.  
  Though torn between affection and disdain,  
  I learn to love the self that bears the pain.

213

I hate the sweetness dwelling in your eyes,  
Yet crave the venom each glance still supplies.  
Your honeyed words, a cloak that love belies,  
Still draw me closer, helpless to your lies.  
A rose, you bloom with petals laced in spite,  
Each thorn a whisper of your hidden sting.  
I curse the dawn that brings your beauty’s light,  
Yet worship shadows where your tempests cling.  
I scorn the gentle warmth your touch imparts,  
But shiver for the frost when you are gone.  
You haunt the silent chambers of my heart,  
A ghost that mocks me with the love I’ve drawn.  
  Oh, bitter-sweet, within your gaze I drown;  
  I wear your poison kiss as if a crown.

214

I loved you fiercely, though you turned away,  
My heart burned bright while yours grew cold and dim.  
The morning sun could never chase the gray  
That settled in your gaze, both stark and grim.  
I whispered truths that hung as fragile dew  
Upon the trembling petals of your will,  
Yet every vow I made, you misconstrue,  
And all my hope was left to time to kill.  
Still, in the quiet night, your shadow stays,  
A ghost that haunts the corners of my mind,  
Reminding me of love's forsaken blaze  
And dreams we lost that I will never find.  
  Yet from these ashes I will rise anew,  
  For though love died, my soul remembers you.

215

I loved with hope, yet hope became my chain,  
Desire and doubt in equal measure grow,  
A sunlit field soon darkened by the rain,  
The seeds of longing sown in silent woe.  
Each glance a promise trembling on the air,  
Yet shadows linger in the heart’s domain;  
I weave my wishes with a fretful care,  
And find the threads unravelled once again.  
Thy words, like gentle winds, do stir my soul,  
But fickle breezes cannot calm the sea;  
Within my breast conflicting tempests roll—  
A storm of dreams, of what may never be.  
  Yet chained by hope, I cherish every pain,  
  For even hope in bondage shall remain.

216

O gentle flame that flickers in my breast,  
Why dost thou flee when most I need thy fire?  
Within my soul, thy warmth is sorely pressed,  
Yet slips away when hope climbs ever higher.  
Thou art a wisp that dances on the gale,  
A trembling light, elusive as the dawn;  
In darkest hours, thy embers wax so frail,  
And in their waning, all my strength is gone.  
O fickle spark, return and banish night,  
Restore the glow that once within me burned.  
Without thy touch, the world is stripped of light,  
And dreams to ashes, cold and pale, are turned.  
  Yet still I shield thee, fragile though thou art,  
  For thou alone dost kindle in my heart.

217

O fairer light that steals my soul by night,  
Why dost thou flee as shadow from my plea?  
Thy gentle rays do turn my grief to bright,  
Yet vanish swift when I would hold to thee.  
Like silver veils that dance upon the stream,  
Thy presence lingers soft on midnight's air;  
A fleeting vision caught within my dream,  
A hope that slips away ere I am ware.  
Thou art the dawn that wakes my longing heart,  
And in thy absence, darkness claims its due.  
Though close I reach, the space keeps us apart,  
As moon betrays the sky with changing hue.  
  Yet still, sweet light, return unto my eyes,  
  And grant my soul the peace thy love supplies.

218

O gentle heart, thy silence is my night,  
Where hope, a waning moon, denies me light.  
I wander lost within this shadowed vale,  
Each whispered breeze retells my woeful plight.  
The stars above bear witness to my tale,  
Their distant gleam a comfort frail and slight.  
Thy absence weaves a shroud both cold and pale,  
And dreams, like ghosts, elude my longing sight.  
Yet in the hush where all my wishes dwell,  
I hear thy memory in every breath;  
Its dulcet echo rings a silver bell  
That stirs my soul from sorrow’s sleep to death.  
  O, grant me speech or let thy silence cease,  
  For only thus my restless heart finds peace.

219

O turn thee not, thou radiant moon above,  
Let not thy silver gaze my hope betray;  
For in thy light, the shadows whisper love  
And tempt my restless soul from night to day.  
Thy gentle beams do gild the midnight air,  
Awakening the roses in my breast,  
And hush the world to dreams beyond compare,  
Wherein my secret longing is confessed.  
Yet oft thou hid’st behind a clouded veil,  
And all the stars grow cold with jealous spite;  
My heart, in darkness, weeps a lover’s tale,  
Still yearning for thy promise of delight.  
  Return, sweet moon, and in thy beauty shine,  
  That all my dreams may evermore be thine.

220

O turn thine eyes, thou lantern of my night,  
And grant my soul a glimpse of borrowed flame;  
Within thy gaze, the shadows take to flight,  
And all the world is gentled by thy name.  
The silver hush of eve upon thy brow  
Outshines the boast of noon with subtler power.  
What star, in all the heavens, dares avow  
A radiance such as this ephemeral hour?  
Thy light, a balm, dissolves the fretful dark  
And paints with hope the corners of despair;  
No wayward tempest leaves a lasting mark  
If in thy warmth my trembling heart may share.  
  So let thy beacon through the blackness gleam,  
  And guide me, love, within thy golden dream.

221

O gentle star, why turn’st thou from my night,  
That longs to bask within thy silver gaze?  
Thy absence hides the world from mortal sight  
And leaves my soul to wander in a maze.  
Thou once didst guide me with thy patient gleam,  
A lantern bright upon the shadowed plain,  
But now I drift within a broken dream,  
Bereft of comfort, lost in silent pain.  
Yet still I raise my eyes and search the skies,  
In hope thy light will pierce the sable gloom,  
For even distant hope the heart belies  
And bids the withered rose again to bloom.  
  Return, sweet star, and let thy glory shine,  
  For in thy light, my world and I align.

222

O gentle soul, why dost thou shrink from light,  
When love’s bright sun would bid thy shadows flee?  
What secret grief hath veiled thy inward sight,  
Or wrapped thy heart in silent misery?  
The morning sings, yet still thou turn’st away,  
And shunn’st the dawn that would thy spirit raise;  
Yet hope, like spring, entreats thee not to stray  
But calls thee forth to bask in warmer days.  
Wilt thou deny the world thy gentle fire  
And let thy beauty wither in the shade?  
Nay, let love’s gentle hand thy fears inspire  
And lift the gloom that time and tears have made.  
  For in thy light the world anew would start,  
  If thou but let love’s sun possess thy heart.

223

O gentle love, thou art my trembling flame,  
Within my soul, thy light doth war with shade,  
Thy whisper shapes the air and carves my name  
Upon the silent dreams that time hath made.  
How subtly dost thou move in secret ways,  
A phantom touch that stirs my sleeping breast,  
And turns the weary night to golden days  
Where hope and longing ever are my guest.  
Thine eyes, twin stars that banish all despair,  
Illuminate the gloom within my heart,  
Their tender fire outshines the midnight air  
And bids the coldest winter warmth impart.  
  So long as breath and faithful longing prove,  
  My life shall burn with thy immortal love.

224

O gentle soul, why dost thou flee from me,  
As shadows part when dawn begins to rise?  
Art thou a wisp upon the restless sea,  
Or but a dream that fades from waking eyes?  
Thy laughter lingers sweet upon the air,  
Yet slips through fingers eager for its grace;  
In silent halls I seek thee everywhere,  
But find no trace within this lonely place.  
The world grows pale where once thy presence shone,  
And time itself doth slow in thy retreat.  
If love be true, why must it walk alone,  
With aching heart and footsteps incomplete?  
  Yet still I hope that fate shall turn again,  
  And bring thee back to heal my silent pain.

225

O gentle soul, why dost thou flee my light,  
When all my stars do bend to thee their gaze?  
What shadows twist thy hope to shroud the night,  
And draw thee far from love’s unending blaze?  
The silver moon, in silence, weeps for thee,  
And sighs her longing through the trembling air;  
The dawn, in rose and gold, implores to see  
Thy countenance and banish all despair.  
Yet still thou wander’st where the cold winds mourn,  
Unmoved by all the beauty I bestow.  
Shall fate remain unkind, shall hearts be torn,  
While time in quiet sorrow onward flows?  
  Return, sweet soul, and let thy darkness cease—  
  For in thy presence dwells my only peace.

226

O gentle soul, attend my piteous cry,  
For love unheeded wastes as stars in gloom,  
Though passion's fire within me cannot die,  
It flickers faint, imprisoned in this tomb.  
Thy shadow haunts the silent midnight hour,  
While hope, like wilted roses, droops and fades;  
Yet still my heart, compelled by secret power,  
Pursues thy steps through memory’s winding glades.  
I plead with fate to turn her face and smile,  
To grant me leave to bask in thy sweet gaze;  
For absence stretches time to endless mile,  
And longing wraps my world in misty haze.  
  Yet if thy heart remains forever free,  
  Still, gentle soul, my love shall follow thee.

227

O gentle soul, why dost thou flee from mine,  
Whilst I, a shadow, chase thy light divine?  
The dawn’s first blush reveals thy form so fair,  
Yet slips from grasp like mist upon the brine.  
Thy laughter, distant music in the air,  
Doth haunt my dreams and leave my spirit pined.  
I seek thee in the golden rays that flare,  
In whispered winds by tender branches signed.  
Thou art the hope that kindles in my breast,  
A fleeting vision dancing out of view,  
The silent prayer that will not let me rest,  
A memory in every drop of dew.  
  Yet if thy heart and mine were bound as one,  
  No night could fall that dims our rising sun.

228

If I be but the shadow of thy grace,  
Then let me dwell where light and longing meet,  
And haunt the gentle contours of thy face,  
A silent echo at thy passing feet.  
Let not the dawn dispel what night reveals,  
Nor chase away the comfort of thy eyes;  
For in thy gaze, a deeper magic heals  
The wounds that even time itself belies.  
If fortune frowns and tempests rise above,  
Still shall I linger, steadfast as the stars,  
Content to serve in silence and in love,  
Though fate may bind my heart with unseen bars.  
  Yet if thy favor shine and lift this shade,  
  I’ll stand in sun, thy glory unafraid.

229

O gentle Love, let not thy torch withdraw,  
For in thy fire my trembling soul is cast,  
Thy golden beams do keep my heart in awe,  
And bend the iron fetters of my past.  
Thou art the dawn that breaks my weary night,  
A whisper’d promise glimmering and sweet,  
When shadows fall, thou art my guiding light,  
And lift’st me up when earth is at my feet.  
Yet oft thy arrows wound with tender pain,  
And tears, like pearls, do from thine altar spill,  
But even sorrow binds me in thy chain,  
And I submit, obedient to thy will.  
  So, Love, remain—let not thy passion cease,  
  For in thy keeping do I dwell in peace.

230

O gentle love, why dost thou flee my breast,  
Thou art the sun, yet I remain in shade,  
Thy golden beams, denied, withhold my rest,  
And in thy absence, all my joy doth fade.  
Like wayward winds that toss the autumn leaf,  
Thy whispers haunt the corners of my mind,  
Thou leav’st my soul in silent, aching grief,  
Yet hope, relentless, will not lag behind.  
I seek thee in the blush of morning’s hue,  
In petals soft, where dewdrops gently weep,  
But every trace dissolves as shadows do,  
And longing’s vigil drives away my sleep.  
  Yet if thy heart should wander back to me,  
  No sweeter spring than this my heart shall see.

231

O gentle flame, why burn’st thou not for me?  
My heart’s petition falleth on deaf air,  
The nightingale sings hope in yonder tree,  
Yet I remain forsaken in despair.  
Would that thy warmth could thaw this wintry soul,  
Or spark within the ash a golden gleam;  
But still I wander, seeking to be whole,  
Haunted by embers of a distant dream.  
Thou dancest bright for others, not for me,  
Unmindful of the tears that silent fall;  
Thy light adorns the shadows I must flee,  
While shadows mock the longing of my thrall.  
  Yet hope persists, though reason bids it cease—  
  That one day, flame and heart may burn in peace.

232

O gentle flame, why dost thou flee my hand,  
Whilst I, a moth, am drawn to thy bright fire?  
Thy golden tongue doth beckon o'er the land,  
And tempts the heart with ever-deep'ning desire.  
Yet every step I take, thou dost retreat,  
A waltz of longing twixt thy glow and me;  
Thy warmth, a promise never made complete,  
Unfolds a dream I may not hope to see.  
In twilight's hush, thy shimmer haunts my sight,  
A phantom dancing just beyond my grasp;  
I chase thee through the corridors of night,  
With hope as thin as air I strive to clasp.  
  Still, though thy light be always out of reach,  
  Thy beauty burns in lessons thou dost teach.

233

O gentle heart, why dost thou turn away,  
Whilst I, a moth, do burn before thy flame?  
The silver moon withdraws the night from day,  
Yet still my soul doth whisper forth thy name.  
Thine eyes, twin stars, beacons within the gloom,  
Do guide me lost, through tempests of desire.  
Though hope lies buried deep in sorrow’s tomb,  
My longing, ever-patient, shall not tire.  
If thou wouldst grant a single fleeting glance,  
A balm to heal the wounds thy silence gave,  
Then fortune’s wheel might turn, and love advance  
To lift me from the shadow’s silent grave.  
  Yet here I stand, in yearning’s gentle thrall—  
  A moth, undone, who dares to love at all.

234

O gentle Love, why dost thou wound me so,  
And bid my soul at thine own altar weep?  
Thy arrows fly where silent sorrows grow,  
And plant their seeds where once my joys did sleep.  
Thou art a flame that burns yet brings me cold,  
A gentle hand whose touch is edged with pain.  
Thy promises, like silver dreams, grow old,  
And bloom as thorns within my heart’s domain.  
Yet still I chase thee through the tangled night,  
Pursuing shadows shaped by hope’s decree,  
And find in darkness some forgotten light  
That whispers soft of what we two might be.  
  Though wounded deep, I would not flee thy art:  
  For Love, thy wounds have written on my heart.

235

O gentle heart, why dost thou shun my plea,  
When Love himself doth kneel and beg for grace?  
The moonlight weeps in silence over me,  
And shadows linger softly on thy face.  
Thy eyes, twin stars, refuse to light my night,  
Yet hope, a stubborn guest, will not depart.  
I send my sighs as prayers in silent flight  
To find a welcome home within thy heart.  
What charm or art can melt thy frozen will,  
Or teach thy lips to speak a tender word?  
Must I in dreams pursue thy phantom still,  
And have my longing ever go unheard?  
  Yet though thou turn'st away, I love thee more,  
  And guard thy image, cherished at my core.

236

O gentle Love, why dost thou flee my breast,  
When night entreats thee, hush'd by moon's decree?  
Art thou a wraith, by weary hearts addressed,  
Or but a dream that slips away from me?  
Thy silver whispers haunt the silent air,  
Yet shadows steal thee softly from my sight.  
I seek thee deep where longing turns to prayer,  
And find but echoes fading in the night.  
If hope could bind thee fast within my hand,  
I’d weave thy form from starlight and from sighs,  
Yet like the tide that flees the hungry sand,  
Thou vanishest, though still my heart complies.  
  O Love, return ere dawn dispels the gloom,  
  And grant this heart a rose instead of doom.

237

O gentle Love, why dost thou flee my side,  
Whilst Hope, thy handmaid, weeps in silent night?  
Thy whispered vows on fickle winds do ride,  
And leave my soul bereft of sweet delight.  
Thine eyes, once lanterns in my shadowed gloom,  
Now turn away and kindle naught but pain.  
The fragrant rose that in my heart did bloom  
Is withered by neglect and longing’s stain.  
Yet still I seek thy footfalls in the dark,  
And dream of warmth that once did grace my days;  
A single glance, a smile, a fleeting spark  
Would banish all despair with golden rays.  
  Though thou art gone, thy memory remains—  
  A haunting song that echoes through my veins.

238

O gentle heart, why dost thou flee my cry,  
When Love himself doth bend to beg thy grace?  
Upon thy cheek the blush of dawn doth lie,  
Yet shadows linger soft about thy face.  
The lark ascends to sing of hope anew,  
While I remain in night’s unyielding hold.  
If but a word or glance thou wouldst bestow,  
My soul, long chilled, would banish all its cold.  
A thousand vows I’d whisper on the breeze,  
And scatter roses where thy footsteps fall.  
But silence deeper than the ancient seas  
Doth bind my tongue and cast a mournful pall.  
  Yet still I wait, though hope be frail and slight—  
  For love’s true dawn may chase away the night.

239

O gentle Love, why dost thou turn thy face,  
And wear the mask of silence in thine eyes?  
Hast thou forsaken me in this lone place,  
Or hid thy truth beneath a cloak of lies?  
Thy whispers once did warm my wintry heart,  
And in thy gaze the summer sun would burn;  
Now shadows in thy countenance impart  
A chill I fear may never more return.  
Yet still I beg thee, speak in tender tones,  
Restore the music to thy dulcet breath;  
For absence weaves a shroud of heavy stones,  
And longing leads me to the brink of death.  
  Unmask thyself, dear Love, and let me see  
  The light that once did set my spirit free.

240

O gentle Soul, why dost thou flee from me,  
When Love entreats thee with a trembling tongue?  
The moon doth pale in shameful modesty  
To see thee wander, lost and yet so young.  
Dost thou not hear the sighs that haunt the night,  
Or feel the whispers drifting on the breeze?  
My heart doth yearn to draw thee into light,  
Yet finds thee hidden in a maze of trees.  
If I could teach the stars to write thy name,  
Or bid the dawning sun to seek thee out,  
Then all the heavens would declare my claim  
And hush the world’s uncertain, faithless doubt.  
  Return, sweet Soul, and let our fates entwine—  
  For Love’s own grace hath made thee ever mine.

241

O gentle love, incline thy ear to me,  
My soul doth wrestle 'twixt thy will and mine,  
A tempest tossed upon a restless sea,  
Where stars of hope in cloudy darkness shine.  
Thy whisper, soft, commands my stubborn heart,  
Yet reason’s chains would bid my passion still;  
Between thy light and shadow I depart,  
A captive roaming at thy sovereign will.  
In dreams I see thy form, both bright and near,  
Yet waking finds thee distant as the dawn;  
Sweet promise hovers ever out of fear,  
While longing lingers long when thou art gone.  
  Grant peace, O love, and end this ceaseless strife—  
  Let heart and soul in thee be bound for life.

242

O gentle soul, why dost thou flee from me,  
When love, thy master, calls thee home to rest?  
Thy wings outspread on winds of memory,  
Dost bear thee far from where my heart is blest.  
The moonlight weeps upon the silent stone,  
And shadows dance where laughter once did dwell.  
I pace the garden’s paths, now overgrown,  
And seek the petals of thy vanished spell.  
Yet hope, that stubborn star, will not depart,  
But clings unto the verge of night and day.  
Though absence trails its thorns about my heart,  
My faith endures, though dreams are swept away.  
  Return, sweet soul, ere sorrow’s song be done—  
  For in thy presence shines my only sun.

243

O gentle soul, why dost thou shun mine eye,  
When Love himself doth on my spirit pine?  
The golden sun grows pale as thou pass’st by,  
And sighs are drawn from hearts that once were thine.  
Thy silence weaves a shroud about my days,  
A tapestry of longing, rich and deep;  
Yet hope, like dawn, still finds a thousand ways  
To rouse my soul from shadows where I weep.  
If fortune grants that I might touch thy hand,  
Or glimpse the starry thoughts behind thy gaze,  
No sweeter bliss could ever bless this land  
Than knowledge thou art moved by all my praise.  
  So speak, fair heart, and end this cruel refrain—  
  Let love’s bright light shine forth and end my pain.

244

O gentle Soul, why dost thou flee from me,  
When Love, my master, calls thee night and day?  
Art thou a wisp that rides the restless sea,  
Or doth thy heart in shadowed silence stay?  
I seek thee where the pale moon stains the lawn,  
And chase thy echo through the twilight air;  
My hope endures from dusk’s first breath till dawn,  
Yet finds thee ever distant, ever fair.  
If dreams could bind, I’d hold thee close and true,  
And banish sorrow with thy tender smile;  
But waking hours the fragile spell undo,  
And longing grows with every passing mile.
