  Yet still I call, though hope itself may wane:  
  Return, sweet Soul, and end this gentle pain.

245

O gentle Soul, why dost thou flee from me,  
When Love, thy suitor, kneels at Hope’s command?  
The moon doth weep upon the restless sea,  
As longing’s tide sweeps footprints from the sand.  
Thine eyes, twin stars, evade my searching gaze,  
Yet draw me on with promise faint and bright.  
I wander lost within this aching maze,  
Led by the fragrance of thy hidden light.  
Wilt thou not turn, and grant a gentle word,  
To still the tempest raging in my breast?  
Or art thou, like the silent, distant bird,  
Content to watch while longing knows no rest?  
  If thou wouldst yield, my soul would find its home;  
  If not, I live in hope—and ever roam.

246

O gentle Love, why dost thou flee mine arms,  
When night entreats thee linger with thy charms?  
Thy rosy breath, that once did calm my fears,  
Now fades like mist beneath the sun’s alarms.  
Each shadowed hour I call thee, but in vain—  
Thy whispered vows dissolve upon the breeze.  
The stars bear witness to my silent pain,  
As dreams of thee my restless mind appease.  
If morning light could bind thee to my side,  
And banish flight from thy capricious heart,  
No more in sorrow would my soul abide,  
Nor beg the moon to play a lover’s part.  
  Yet hope endures, though night and day depart,  
  That Love returns and heals my longing heart.

247

O sweet and wayward heart, why dost thou stray,  
When Reason’s gentle hand would hold thee fast?  
What lure of fleeting fancy leads away  
Thy steps from wisdom’s path, so sure and vast?  
Doth not the morning hush thy restless fire,  
Or evening’s calm invite thee to be still?  
Yet ever dost thou chase thy fond desire,  
And bend thy will to shadows on the hill.  
Though counsel’s voice would guard thee from thy fall,  
And prudence build a wall against thy fears,  
Thy longing leaps beyond each careful wall,  
And turns deaf ear to all the sage appears.  
  O heart, though wild, in loving let thee find  
  A gentle peace to soothe thy wandering mind.

248

O gentle Love, why dost thou flee my side,  
And leave my soul in sorrow’s grasp to pine?  
Thy golden beams once did my heart betide,  
But now in shadows cold, I make thee mine.  
The springtime blush that danced upon my cheek  
Hath faded, chased by winter’s mournful breath;  
Thy whispered vows, so tender and so meek,  
Are now but echoes in the halls of death.  
Yet still I seek thee, though thou dost evade,  
And cast my hopes upon the fickle wind;  
In dreams, thy visage heavenly displayed,  
Doth mend the wounds which waking hours rescind.  
  Return, sweet Love, and let my torment cease—  
  Within thine arms, restore my soul to peace.

249

O gentle Love, why turn’st thou from mine eyes,  
And let’st my longing heart in shadow pine?  
Thy golden torch, which once did sweetly rise,  
Now flickers faint where hopes no more entwine.  
Did I offend with prayers too oft implored,  
Or did my words grow wearisome and cold?  
The silent night recalls thy vows once poured,  
When every whispered promise shone like gold.  
Each dawn I seek thee in the morning’s hue,  
Yet find but empty air and silent dew.  
My soul, a garden robbed of sun and rain,  
Still waits for thee to chase away the pain.  
  Return, O Love, and let thine eyes impart  
  The gentle balm that heals my wounded heart.

250

O gentle Love, why dost thou flee my arms,  
Whilst Hope, thy handmaid, mocks my longing sighs?  
Thy presence once adorned my nights with charms,  
Now shadows linger where thy brightness lies.  
Thou art a phantom dancing in the mist,  
A whispered promise borne on fickle air;  
My heart, by sweet remembrance oft is kissed,  
Yet wakes to find thee vanished, cold, and bare.  
Thy arrows, Love, have grazed me but to tease,  
And left no wound save hunger in my breast;  
Still, in thy absence, longing finds no ease,  
Nor can my soul in solitude find rest.  
  Return, thou wayward lord, and end this pain,  
  Or teach my heart to love thee not again.

251

O gentle Love, why dost thou flee my heart,  
And mock my soul with hope thou wilt not stay?  
Thou art the thief that steals with subtle art,  
Then hides thy footsteps ere the break of day.  
I chase thy shadow through the waking hours,  
Yet find but whispers trembling in the air;  
Thou scatter’st petals from thy faded flowers,  
And leave’st me longing, lost in deep despair.  
Thy promise lingers sweet upon my tongue,  
A melody that haunts my midnight mind.  
Yet still, thy presence leaves my praises sung  
To empty skies, with only dreams to find.  
  O gentle Love, return and end this strife—  
  For without thee, I have but half a life.

252

O, grant me grace, thou light and shadow's blend,  
For love compels my heart and bids it rend.  
Thy visage, caught between the dusk and dawn,  
Doth haunt my dreams when waking hours are gone.  
Sweet mercy, temper passion’s burning fire,  
That I may worship yet not be undone.  
Thy silken voice, a balm to my desire,  
Draws forth my soul as rivers to the sun.  
If hope doth flicker, trembling on the breeze,  
Let not despair consume my gentle thought.  
For in thy gaze, my every tempest flees,  
And peace is in the presence I have sought.  
  So grant me grace, if grace in thee reside—  
  And let our hearts, in shadowed light, abide.

253

O let thy heart not turn from me away,  
Nor bid my soul in lonely shadows pine;  
For in thine eyes, the promise of the day  
Doth glimmer soft, and makes my darkness shine.  
The gentle whisper of thy breath I crave,  
More precious far than summer’s fragrant air;  
Thy memory within my breast I save,  
A talisman against the weight of care.  
Though tempests rage and fickle fortune frown,  
Though time may steal the blushes from thy cheek,  
No storm nor age can cast my spirit down  
So long as love thy silent lips may speak.  
  O grant me this: thy favor to restore,  
  And I shall ask of fate nor gods no more.

254

O gentle heart, why turn’st thou thus from me,  
Whilst night and day within my breast contend?  
The moon dost pale for lack of company,  
And sighing winds my silent pleas extend.  
Where once thy voice did fill the hours with grace,  
Now shadows dwell where golden laughter lay;  
No rose can bloom, nor sun reveal its face,  
Since love’s sweet light from me hath turned away.  
Yet hope, though frail, within my soul abides,  
A trembling flame that will not soon depart;  
If fortune’s wheel returns and fate provides,  
Restore the gentle warmth that left my heart.  
  For though thou stray, my constancy is shown:  
  My soul is thine, though I remain alone.

255

If icy scorn within thy bosom dwell,  
Why burns my soul with fires it cannot quell?  
Thy frosted glance, a silent, distant knell,  
Turns hope to ash, my heart to icy shell.  
Yet still I seek the light within thine eye,  
Though reason bids me from thy presence flee;  
Desire defies the wisdom of the sky,  
And makes a willing captive out of me.  
Thy words, like wintry winds, do pierce me through,  
Yet from their sting my longing will not part;  
For even frost may melt and bloom anew  
If once the sun should thaw the frozen heart.  
  So let me burn, though cold thy breast remain—  
  For love, unquenched, endures through joy and pain.

256

If love be light, then let me dwell in night,  
Since in thy eyes my hope is cast away,  
What joy remains when shadow veils the sight,  
And dawn is banished by the close of day?  
Yet still thy memory burns against my will,  
A candle flickering in endless gloom;  
Its slender flame the silent dark doth fill,  
And paints thy visage on my empty room.  
For every star that pricks the sable sky  
Reminds my soul of glances once so sweet,  
But now thy favor, like a whispered lie,  
Retreats and leaves me mourning in defeat.  
  Yet if to love thee is to know despair,  
  I’ll wrap my heart in darkness, and not care.

257

O, turn thine eyes from frost to gentle fire,  
For cold disdain doth burn whilst warmth inspire.  
Let rigid winter’s silence fade away,  
And grant thy heart to greet the blush of day.  
The icèd glance that chills the budding rose  
Can ne’er compare to love, whose passion grows.  
In ember’d gaze, the frozen dusk will yield,  
And barren fields are clothed in golden shield.  
What beauty lies in steely, distant snow  
When honeyed sunlight bids the blossoms grow?  
Let not thy spirit wear a shroud of sleet,  
But dance where vernal winds and longing meet.  
  So melt thy walls and let affection reign,  
  For love’s sweet fire doth conquer every pain.

258

O gentle love, why dost thou vex my soul,  
With sweetest peace and most tormenting strife?  
Thy presence weaves a fire beyond control,  
Both balm and blade within the heart of life.  
Thou art the dawn that wakes my spirit bright,  
Yet too, the dusk that chills my hope to stone.  
A thousand joys doth blossom in thy light,  
And yet I wander lost, bereft, alone.  
Each whispered vow, a promise fraught with fear,  
Each tender glance, a storm that shakes the day.  
Yet in thy thrall, all sorrows disappear,  
And reason’s voice is gentle swept away.  
  O love, thy power none can e’er withstand—  
  A tyrant’s rule, yet sweet as heaven’s hand.

259

O sweet, yet cruel, love that rends my soul,  
Thou art my solace and my sharpest woe.  
Within thy grasp, I find myself made whole,  
Yet in thy absence, bitter tempests blow.  
Thy glance ignites a thousand suns in me,  
Yet shadows deepen when thou turn’st away.  
In longing’s prison, never am I free,  
For hope and dread in equal measure stay.  
Thy gentle words can heal the deepest scar,  
Yet careless speech may wound beyond repair.  
No armor shields me from thy distant star,  
Nor keeps my heart from trembling in despair.  
  Yet, though thou art both torment and delight,  
  I yield to thee, my love, both day and night.

260

O gentle soul, why dost thou flee from rest,  
Yet crave the peace that shuns thy troubled breast?  
Like waning moon that hides behind the west,  
Thy weary hopes in shadowed doubt are dressed.  
Within the hush where midnight secrets dwell,  
Thine anxious mind doth wander, lost, alone;  
The world’s soft lullabies, a distant knell,  
Strike hollow chords within thy weary bone.  
Yet dawn may break upon thy clouded fears,  
And light may pierce the gloom that veils thine eyes;  
Each sorrow wept, the morning dew appears,  
And bids the silent heart at last arise.  
  Find solace, soul, in gentle dawn’s embrace,  
  For peace awaits where dreams the darkness chase.

261

O turn thine eye, where mine in darkness dwells,  
Though light thou art, my soul in shadow stays,  
No golden dawn the heavy night dispels,  
Nor hope ascends to gild my weary days.  
Yet in thy glance a gentle solace gleams,  
A quiet beacon through my tempests cast,  
Thy laughter stirs the silence of my dreams,  
And bids the bitter hour be overpassed.  
If I could borrow but a breath of grace  
From that fair sun which in thy spirit lies,  
I’d shed the sable sorrow from my face  
And find new morning breaking in thine eyes.  
  But till thy light upon my darkness fall,  
  I dwell in night, and dream thou art my all.

262

O, turn thine ear unto my trembling soul,  
Whose silent cries with hope and sorrow vie;  
Within the heart where secret tempests roll,  
The stars bear witness to each muted sigh.  
Thy shadow lingers on the waking morn,  
A memory that haunts the rising day;  
In dreams, I find thy gentle presence worn,  
Yet dawn’s bright fingers brush thy shade away.  
Though time conspires to steal thee from my gaze,  
And distance weaves a veil of cold regret,  
My steadfast love endures through all decays,  
A flame unquenched, its longing firmly set.  
  O, let thy mercy light my darkened night,  
  And guide me homeward by thy beacon bright.

263

O gentle soul, why dost thou flee my cry,  
Yet haunt mine every thought with silent grace?  
Thy shadow lingers when the moon is high,  
And paints thy memory upon my face.  
In waking hours, thou art the breath I chase,  
In dreams, thy whisper drifts upon the air;  
Each fleeting moment marks thy absent place,  
And longing grows from hope’s despairing prayer.  
I watch the stars and wonder if they bear  
Thy gaze, as once thy eyes did look on me—  
Their pale devotion cannot quite compare  
To what thy gentle presence used to be.  
  Yet though thy form escapes both hand and eye,  
  In heart and soul thou never shalt pass by.

264

O, hear me, Love, who dost my soul confound,  
Yet grant me peace where fiercest tumults rage,  
Thy gentle hand can heal each mortal wound  
And calm the storms that in my breast engage.  
When midnight shadows veil the weary mind  
And restless hopes like tempests toss the sea,  
Thy presence, sweet, restores my sightless, blind  
Despair and bids my heart once more be free.  
No golden sun nor silver moon above  
Can match the light that in thy eyes does dwell;  
No melody is half so sweet as love  
Whose tender touch dissolves the coldest spell.  
  So, let me live beneath thy sovereign gaze,  
  And in thy mercy find my soul’s true blaze.

265

O Love, that dost both wound and heal my breast,  
Thou art my bane and yet my sweetest cure,  
Within thy fire my heart is sore distressed,  
Yet in thy flame my spirit doth endure.  
Thy arrows pierce where gentle hopes reside,  
And bleed me with a rapture bittersweet;  
Thou art the tempest, yet the peaceful tide  
That lifts my soul and lays it at thy feet.  
When shadows fall and lonely hours creep,  
Thy memory stirs the longing in my veins;  
I drink thy joy, though oft I taste thy weep,  
And treasure e’en the echo of thy pains.  
  For though thy power brings both joy and smart,  
  Still, Love, I yield thee all my willing heart.

266

O let my heart, in chains of longing bound,  
Yet soar for freedom, where no peace is found.  
Upon the wind it rides, in hope’s disguise,  
And seeks the golden dawn behind the night.  
Though tempests toss my dreams with angry cries,  
And shadowed doubts obscure my soul’s delight,  
Yet still I chase the sun that will not stay,  
A fleeting promise painted on the air.  
The world’s cold grasp would draw my will away,  
And burden youth with sorrow’s heavy care.  
But in the quiet hush before the morn,  
My silent spirit dares to rise anew,  
  For from the ache of love am I not torn,  
  But made more whole by yearning ever true.

267

O turn thine eyes, for love’s sake, unto me,  
Yet gaze thou cold as marble’s frozen gleam;  
Fair hope’s bright blush now withers on the lea,  
As morning fades into a shadowed dream.  
The trembling rose that in my breast did grow  
Droops, longing for the sun of thy regard;  
A silent moon, I watch thy mercy go,  
My heart, a castle stormed and battle-scarred.  
Yet still I plead, though reason bids me cease,  
And lay my soul before thy distant throne;  
Let but thy voice, like gentle winds of peace,  
Breathe summer on these fields of winter sown.  
  If e’er thy heart should soften, come to me—  
  For in thy love alone my soul is free.

268

If thou couldst see the tempest in my breast,  
Yet know’st no calm within thy heart for me,  
Each sigh that stirs my soul denies me rest,  
And longing’s chains keep hope in custody.  
Thy glance, a fleeting star in distant gloom,  
Ignites wild fires where shadows softly creep;  
Yet cold indifference seals my spirit’s tomb,  
And still I wake while thou art lost in sleep.  
O would that fortune’s wheel might turn anew,  
And place thy gentle hand within my own!  
For love, unspoken, ever deeper grew,  
Though barren ground is all it’s ever known.  
  Yet let my silent suffering confess:  
  I love thee still, though fate would grant me less.

269

If love be light, why doth it breed my night,  
And in thy glance, my heart doth lose its day?  
Thy beauty, like the moon, obscures my sight,  
Yet draws my soul, a helpless moth to stray.  
I seek the dawn within thy shadowed eyes,  
But find instead a dusk that will not break.  
Each whispered promise clothed in sweet disguise,  
Doth leave me longing, hungered and awake.  
O fickle star that guides me through despair,  
Thou art both tempest wild and gentle breeze.  
Within thy smile, I taste both hope and care,  
Yet tremble, knowing joy is hard to seize.  
  So let me burn beneath thy borrowed light,  
  And live in love, though blinded by the night.

270

If I am but a ship and you my sea,  
Why toss me from your tides and set me free?  
Your currents once enwrapped me tenderly,  
Now tempests bid my fragile hull to flee.  
What stars or winds decree this course we take,  
That once entwined, we sundered wave from keel?  
Do moon’s caprice or sun’s harsh glare awake  
Such distance none but time itself could heal?  
Yet still your salt upon my timbers clings,  
And echoes of your roar haunt every mast.  
I drift on memories your tempest brings,  
A sailor lost in longing for the past.  
  O grant me anchor, or return my breeze—  
  For I am naught but wreck without your seas.

271

If love be fire, then let me gladly burn,  
Consume my doubts until the ash is clear,  
Let every troubled thought to cinders turn,  
And leave but hope unscorched to persevere.  
Thy glance, a spark that kindles in my soul  
A blaze that tempests vainly seek to quell;  
Though shadows gather, darkening the whole,  
Thy light remains, where all the shadows fell.  
I tremble, knowing passion’s eager cost,  
Yet find no solace in a cooler fate;  
For what is gained, if gentle heat be lost,  
Or hearts grow cold behind a guarded gate?  
  So let this fire, unfettered, rise above—  
  And in its glow, I’ll know the truth of love.

272

O let me be the lantern in your night,  
Though fear within me battles hope’s fair spark,  
To banish shadows trembling from your sight,  
And guide thee gently through the restless dark.  
Let every trembling flame within me burn  
Against the chill where sorrow’s specters creep;  
If lost in tangled woods, to me return,  
For faithful watch beside thy path I keep.  
Yet should my light grow faint with weary hours,  
Recall the golden mornings we have known,  
When laughter danced as dew upon the flowers  
And solace in my steadfast heart was sown.  
  So lean upon this glow, however small—  
  For love, though frail, may yet outshine them all.

273

If I am but a shadow at your door,  
Let mercy be the moon that lights your heart,  
Though silence weaves its hush across the floor,  
Let hope’s soft lantern never drift apart.  
If memory, like winter, chills the night,  
Recall the sun that once adorned your face;  
Let not the cold eclipse your gentle light,  
Nor sorrow’s clouds obscure your tender grace.  
For in the hush where longing takes its hold,  
A whisper stirs the darkness with your name,  
And every dream, though distant, bright and bold,  
Returns to kindle love’s enduring flame.  
  So if I fade, a ghost in borrowed air,  
  Know shadows live where once the light was fair.

274

My heart’s a lantern burning in the rain,  
It calls your name through every shadowed street,  
A trembling beacon pierced by longing’s pain,  
Its glow persists though storms and darkness meet.  
Each drop that falls recalls your gentle touch,  
A memory dancing softly on the air,  
While distant thunder whispers, “Miss you much,”  
And hope ignites despite the world’s despair.  
I wander lanes where moonlit puddles gleam,  
Their silver mirrors haunted by your face,  
The night unfolds the fabric of a dream  
That time nor tempest ever can erase.  
  So let my lantern guide you through the night,  
  Toward love’s warm haven, ever burning bright.

275

If I could be the sun to warm your days,  
Yet find my light denied your distant heart,  
I'd paint the dawn with gentle golden rays,  
And hope that warmth and comfort I impart.  
Though shadows stretch between us, long and deep,  
I send my beams across the darkened sea;  
Within my silent orbit, still I keep  
A promise that my glow shall faithful be.  
Though clouds may hide my face from mortal sight,  
And time may turn horizons cold and gray,  
Each eve I linger in the edge of night  
To dream anew the birth of golden day.  
  For though your heart be distant as the dawn,  
  My love, like sunlight, ever will live on.

276

My soul, a ship adrift on stormy seas,  
Seeks harbor in the light behind your eyes,  
The tempests howl, yet gentle is the breeze  
That carries hope where silent longing lies.  
Night’s shadowed waves may batter at the prow,  
Yet steadfast is your beacon through the gale.  
The stars themselves their silver oaths avow  
To guide me homeward when my senses fail.  
Your voice, a compass drawn from distant skies,  
Directs my heart to shores I’ve never known;  
Within your touch, the weary tempest dies,  
And sorrow’s salt is sweet where love has grown.  
  Though fate may toss us far from where we start,  
  Your light remains the North Star of my heart.

277

My soul, a ship that drifts on storm-tossed seas,  
Still calls your light to guide me through the dark,  
Though tempests howl and batter fragile ease,  
Your memory ignites a steadfast spark.  
The stars are veiled by clouds of bitter doubt,  
The winds of longing tear my canvas thin,  
Yet in the gloom, your voice dispels the rout  
And stirs a hope that sails my heart within.  
Upon the waves, your laughter softly rings,  
A beacon bright beyond horizon’s edge;  
It soothes the ache that every sorrow brings,  
And bids my faith upon your love to pledge.  
  Though storms may rage and night may never cease,  
  Your guiding light shall steer me back to peace.

278

My soul, a lantern flickering for your light,  
In shadows begs your warmth to end its night.  
Thy absent hand hath chilled the breath of day,  
And left my heart to wander, lost, astray.  
Each hope, a moth, that circles round thy flame,  
Consuming wings in longing, sweet and dire.  
I carve thy name in silence, still the same,  
A sacred wish that burns but won’t expire.  
The hours drip like wax from candle’s core,  
While dreams of thee ignite the dark anew.  
If but thy gaze would cross my heart once more,  
The dawn would blush and paint the heavens blue.  
  Yet till thy sun dispels this aching gloom,  
  I am but shadow seeking out thy bloom.

279

My soul, a lantern lost within the night,  
Still seeks your flame, though kept beyond its reach,  
A spark that flickers just beyond my sight,  
Yet warms the dreams that silent darkness teach.  
The hush of dusk recalls your whispered name  
Upon the winds that wander through the trees,  
And every star above repeats the same  
Sweet memory that never fades or flees.  
Though shadows stretch between our distant hands  
And time conspires to keep our hearts apart,  
My longing, like the restless ocean, stands  
Unyielding in the chambers of my heart.  
  So till dawn breaks and night’s despair is done,  
  I’ll search for you, my love, my only sun.

280

My soul, a lantern lost without your flame,  
Yet wanders dark where hope and longing dwell.  
Each shadowed hour, I softly speak your name,  
In silent halls where love was wont to swell.  
The moon, a witness to my restless plight,  
Casts silver on the path I walk alone,  
Her gentle beams recall your eyes’ soft light,  
Yet cannot warm the coldness I have known.  
How oft I dream that dawn might bring you near,  
That morning’s breath might stir your voice anew,  
But day dissolves, and you do not appear—  
Still night returns, and sorrow’s seeds accrue.  
  Yet in this dark, my heart, undimmed, shall burn:  
  For love awaits the hour of your return.

281

My soul, a lantern flickering in the gloom,  
Seeks warmth within the shadow of your heart,  
Each hope a petal yearning yet to bloom,  
While doubt and longing pull my light apart.  
Night’s velvet shroud descends upon my mind,  
Yet in your gaze, a dawn begins to rise;  
Within your gentle touch, my fears unwind,  
And promise kindles stars within your eyes.  
The world may turn with tempests wild and cold,  
Its silence pressing heavily on me,  
But in your arms, I find a truth retold:  
A haven for my restless soul to be.  
  Let darkness come—so long as you are near,  
  No shadow falls that I have cause to fear.

282

Within my chest a restless ocean swells,  
Each wave a longing word I cannot say,  
The secret tide in silent darkness dwells,  
And pulls my heart in moonlit disarray.  
The shore of hope recedes with every crest,  
Yet still I chase the music of the brine,  
For in its song my soul would find its rest,  
If only I could claim what might be mine.  
The storm of dreams breaks gently on my mind,  
Its salt and spray ignite my waking hours,  
Yet reason's anchor leaves me moored, confined,  
Beset by tempests far beyond my powers.  
  Though silent, still this ocean shapes my part:  
  A surging, speechless longing in my heart.

283

My heart’s a ship adrift on stormy seas,  
It begs your guiding star to grant it peace.  
The midnight swells are filled with mournful pleas,  
Each wave a memory that will not cease.  
I scan the heavens for your distant light,  
A beacon burning through the clouded gloom,  
To steer me safely through the endless night  
And promise dawn will banish all this doom.  
Yet tempests rage and toss me from my course,  
Despairing winds that echo with your name;  
I fight in vain against their bitter force,  
Still longing for the solace never came.  
  O, let your love be harbor, calm and true—  
  And guide me home through storms I brave for you.

284

My heart’s a lantern, begging for your flame,  
It shivers in the dusk, unsure, alone;  
It trembles softly at the sound your name  
Imprints upon the hush—a hopeful tone.  
The night grows thick with longing’s whispered plea,  
Each star a silent witness to my ache;  
I hold my breath and wish that you might see  
The light within me, kindled for your sake.  
Yet shadows curl where certainty departs,  
And silence tiptoes, cautious, through my chest;  
Still, hope endures in corners of my heart,  
A gentle ember clinging to its quest.  
  So let your spark descend, dispelling night,  
  And set my lantern’s soul in gentle light.

285

My heart’s a lantern begging for your flame,  
Yet shadows feast where once your promise lay,  
The night’s cold fingers carve your absent name  
In trembling sighs that dawn cannot allay.  
I pace the silent chambers of my mind,  
Where echoes of your laughter haunt the air,  
In memories your tenderness I find,  
But waking, taste the bitterness of care.  
The stars conspire to whisper you away,  
Yet hope, defiant, flickers in the gloom;  
Though petals wilt and silver turns to gray,  
The thought of you in darkness still may bloom.  
  So grant my heart a spark, or set it free—  
  To burn in love, or rest in memory.

286

If I am but a shadow at your door,  
Let not your silence turn my hope to dust,  
For though I haunt the threshold evermore,  
It is for gentle love, not lonely lust.  
The moonlight weaves your absence on the floor,  
A silver tapestry I dare not trust;  
Yet in the hush, your memory I implore—  
A whispered promise, faithful as I must.  
Should dawn unveil the truth and bid me part,  
Let kindness beam where once your gaze was cold;  
For shadows fade, but not the earnest heart,  
Nor all the silent dreams we once foretold.  
  Though I remain unseen, my vow is clear:  
  I love you still, though darkness keeps me near.

287

My heart’s a ship that drifts your distant shore,  
Yet windless sails can’t bring me to your light,  
The tide of longing pulls me evermore  
Toward horizons hidden from my sight.  
Upon the glassy sea I cast my plea,  
Each wave a wish that trembles in the gloom,  
The stars above are silent company,  
Their silver eyes unmoved by hope or doom.  
I chart your name upon the midnight deep,  
A secret map that only dreamers find,  
While memory, like salt, will slowly seep  
Into the wounds that time must leave behind.  
  Yet though I wander lost and tempest-tossed,  
  Without your beacon’s flame, I am but lost.

288

O gentle heart, why do you flee my night,  
When even stars confess their longing true?  
The moon in silence weeps with borrowed light,  
As I in shadows linger, seeking you.  
Each whispered breeze declares thy name anew,  
Yet still you drift beyond my trembling reach.  
My hopes, like petals, scatter in the dew,  
While patient dawn prepares her golden speech.  
If fate decrees our souls must dwell apart,  
Let memory be solace for my pain;  
For every throb and tremble of my heart  
Is testament of love not spent in vain.  
  So wander if you must, O spirit fair—  
  My dreams will find thee, ever, everywhere.

289

O gentle heart, why do you flee my plea,  
When night itself implores you not to stray?  
The silver moon hangs weeping in the tree,  
Her luster dimmed by sorrow's deep decay.  
Thy laughter haunts the hollows of the air,  
A fleeting echo lost to time’s swift stream;  
Yet in the dark, I find thy shadow there,  
A ghost that wanders through my every dream.  
O wouldst thou turn, and grant a hopeful sign,  
A single glance to bless this longing soul,  
Then stars would blush and yield their place to thine,  
And broken hours at last would be made whole.  
  Yet still I wait, through silence and through pain—  
  For love once lost may find its life again.

290

O gentle love, why do you flee my arms,  
And hide your face behind the fickle day?  
Thy laughter echoes sweet in distant charms,  
Yet vanishes when I would bid thee stay.  
The morning sun, in envy, steals thy light,  
And shadows chase thee through the trembling air;  
I seek thy presence in the silent night,  
But only dreams reveal thy beauty rare.  
Why must desire be tangled in delay,  
As rivers pine to reach the distant sea?  
My heart, a captive, wanders lost and stray,  
Still yearning for the grace that once touched me.  
  Return, fair love, and end this mournful plight;  
  Restore the dawn, and banish endless night.

291

O gentle heart, why turn thy gaze away,  
When Hope herself kneels weeping at thy door?  
The silver morn would banish leaden gray,  
If thou but let her footsteps cross the floor.  
Within thy breast, where silent sorrows dwell,  
A garden waits the blessing of thy light;  
For grief, though fierce, hath not the power to quell  
The seeds of joy that slumber out of sight.  
Let not the world’s cold shadow dim thine eyes,  
Nor memory’s ghosts persuade thee to despair;  
For in the hush where broken promise lies,  
A sweeter song prepares to fill the air.  
  Turn not aside from Hope’s outstretched embrace—  
  The dawn is thine, if thou wilt grant it place.

292

O dearest heart, let Mercy guide thine ear,  
For Hope, my servant, kneels to beg thee near.  
Within thy gaze the shadows melt away,  
And morning’s gold adorns the trembling day.  
Thy gentle words outshine the brightest sun,  
Dispelling doubt where heavy sorrows run.  
Like blossoms waking to the softest breeze,  
My soul finds rest, its ageless tempests cease.  
Yet fear, that thief, doth whisper in the night,  
Wouldst thou depart, and leave me void of light?  
But Mercy’s hand, so tender and so wise,  
Unbinds my heart and lifts my weary eyes.  
  So let our faith, entwined, defy despair—  
  For in thy arms, my hope is ever fair.

293

O gentle heart, why wilt thou not incline  
To let sweet Mercy dwell within thine eye?  
The rose that blooms with thorns doth still entwine,  
Yet grants its fragrance freely ere it die.  
What gain is found in holding anger fast,  
When time itself will swiftly steal its sting?  
The bitter winds of wrath are seldom cast  
In gardens where forgiving voices sing.  
Let not thy pride be prison to thy soul,  
Nor let old wounds a tender love betray.  
For hearts that cleave to kindness shall be whole,  
And shadows born of vengeance fade away.  
  So yield, sweet heart, and let thy mercy flow—  
  In gentle grace, thy noblest self shall grow.

294

My soul, a prisoner, begs Love to see,  
Unbind these restless chains that dwell in me.  
Within my breast, wild tempests rise and fall,  
Yet silent hope attends thy gentle plea.  
The moon doth pale before thy radiant thrall,  
Her silver face eclipsed by memory’s sea;  
Each sigh a wave that breaks against the wall  
Of longing built by fate’s cruel decree.  
What freedom lies beyond thy guarded gate,  
Where roses bloom beneath the morning’s fire?  
If mercy be thy gift, then grant me late  
Release from bonds that never seem to tire.  
  For in thy gaze, my soul would gladly rest,  
  A bird set free, returning to its nest.

295

O soul, why do you close your gates to me,  
When love, unbridled, begs to be let in?  
The moonlight weeps upon the silent sea,  
And hearts grow wild where once they had been thin.  
Within your halls, a thousand echoes call,  
Yet each is met with silence, cold and deep.  
My longing shadows tremble on your wall,  
And in your watchful dusk, I wake and weep.  
What fortress forged of sorrow guards your heart,  
That even hope must perish at the door?  
What ancient wound compels you to depart  
From gentle hands that ache to heal your sore?  
  Yet still I knock, with faith that will not die—  
  For love endures, though worlds may pass it by.

296

O gentle soul, why do you turn from me,  
When love, your guest, still knocks upon your door?  
The moonlit tide doth rise upon the sea,  
Impatient waves that beg to kiss the shore.  
In silence, shadows linger on thy face,  
The sweet confession trembling on thy tongue.  
My heart, a captive yearning for thy grace,  
Is tossed by hope that ever keeps me young.  
Dost thou not see the lantern in my eyes,  
Or hear the trembling echo of thy name?  
The garden blooms beneath our shared sunrise,  
Yet fears unspoken set thy cheeks aflame.  
  O yield, dear heart, to what the fates decree:  
  Let love’s bright fire unite thy soul with me.

297

O gentle soul, why do you turn away,  
When every breath I take pleads for your light?  
The morning weeps, deprived of golden day,  
As shadows stretch to steal thee from my sight.  
Thy laughter lingers, haunting silent air,  
Like echoes lost upon a distant shore;  
The rose’s blush, once vibrant, pales in care,  
Since absence paints my world with longing more.  
Yet hope, a stubborn star, awaits the night,  
To pierce the gloom that sorrow’s hand has spun;  
It kindles dreams that thou wilt end thy flight,  
And greet my heart anew with rising sun.  
  O let thy mercy guide thee back to me,  
  And grant my soul the peace that thou canst be.

298

O gentle soul, why turn thy face from mine,  
When every hope within me calls thy name?  
The stars grow pale, and silent shadows pine  
For whispers warm that once like sunlight came.  
Thy absence weaves a winter through the air,  
While memory’s lantern flickers, faint and low.  
I wander lost, a pilgrim in despair,  
Where once thy laughter taught my heart to glow.  
Yet if thy gaze should grace me but once more,  
The frozen hours would blossom into spring;  
My spirit, freed, would dare to hope and soar,  
And gladly bear what future days may bring.  
  O turn to me, and let thy light be shown—  
  For in thy eyes, my soul hath found its own.

299

O gentle Soul, why knock upon her door,  
When every echo answers with disdain?  
The nightingale no longer sings of yore,  
Her silence falls like unforgiving rain.  
Thy trembling hand, outstretched in hope’s pursuit,  
Finds naught but shadowed spaces in the gloom;  
Yet still thou lingerest, steadfast, resolute,  
To gather petals from a faded bloom.  
What draws thee forth, when reason bids thee stay,  
And wisdom whispers, “Let her memory go”?  
Still, love’s bright torch will ever light the way,  
Though winds may strive to quench its gentle glow.  
  O heart, persist, though sorrow be thy guide:  
  For beauty oft in suffering doth abide.

300

O Heart, why do you barricade your door,  
When Hope, in trembling arms, begs to be let in?  
The gentle knock you’ve braved so oft before  
Now shivers on the threshold, worn and thin.  
Each shadow cast within these guarded halls  
Is shaped by memories you dare not trust,  
Yet even silence, echoing, still calls  
For dreams you buried deep beneath the dust.  
Must every wound be nurse to endless night,  
And every promise met with anxious doubt?  
For in the gloom, a single ray of light  
Yearns for the chance to coax the darkness out.  
  Unbolt the lock, let gentle voices start—  
  And grant sweet Hope a dwelling in your heart.

301

O gentle Heart, why do you turn away,  
When all my soul entreats for you to stay?  
The moonlight weeps upon the silent ground,  
And shadows stretch where once our laughter lay.  
In gardens pale, where fragrant hopes abound,  
Your absence chills the rose and dims the day,  
Yet still I wander, searching for the sound  
Of whispered vows that memory cannot sway.  
What fickle fate doth drag thee from my side,  
When steadfast love would bind thee ever near?  
Alas, my truth and longing both denied,  
I plead against the silence I most fear.  
  Yet if thy heart be lost, and mine must break,  
  I love thee still, for my own sorrow’s sake.

302

O Heart, why do you tremble at her name,  
And beg the night to cloak your longing eyes?  
What secret fire hath lit thy gentle flame  
That burns in silence, masked in coy disguise?  
The moon conspires to witness every sigh,  
And gentle winds repeat thy whispered plea.  
Yet hope and doubt in restless union vie,  
Each weaving dreams, then tearing tapestry.  
Her voice, a melody that haunts the air,  
Doth bid thy reason falter, faint, and stray;  
Her shadow lingers soft upon thy care,  
And turns the golden noon to misty gray.  
  Yet courage, heart, for love may still reply—  
  And lift thee up to rapture or to die.

303

O gentle Love, why turn thy face from me,  
And whisper hope to every ear but mine?  
Thy rosy fingers beckon hearts with glee,  
Yet leave my longing soul in cold confine.  
The lark that sings at dawn receives thy grace,  
While shadows linger where I dare to tread;  
Thy golden light adorns another’s face,  
And I am wreathed in loneliness instead.  
Hast thou no pity for a heart so true,  
No balm for wounds thy arrows did impart?  
Or must I dwell in night bereft of you,  
A silent mourner of a silent heart?  
  Yet still I plead, though hope be ever slight:  
  Return, sweet Love, and set my world aright.

304

O gentle heart, why do you turn away,  
When every hope within me calls your name?  
The golden dawn bestows another day,  
Yet shadows linger, veiling love in shame.  
Soft winds recall the laughter we have known,  
Their whisper stirs the petals of regret,  
While silent tears fall for the seeds we've sown,  
And memory weaves a web I can't forget.  
If fate would grant me but a single chance  
To mend the rifts that time and pride have made,  
I'd brave the storms, defy the boundless dance,  
And gather light where once our joy did fade.  
  Yet still I wait, though hope may shift and bend—  
  O gentle heart, return and wounds will mend.

305

O gentle Love, why do you turn away,  
And leave my soul to wander in your wake?  
The morning’s gold dissolves to ashen gray  
When absence proves the dawn itself a fake.  
You drew me forth with whispers soft and sweet,  
Your promises as bright as summer’s sun,  
Yet now I pace the cold and empty street,  
Forsaken, lost, and longing for the one  
Who taught my heart to hope, then bade it cease—  
A lesson carved in silence and regret.  
If I could barter sorrow for release,  
Or teach my heart the art of self-forget,  
  I’d trade these tears for laughter, night for day—  
  If only gentle Love would choose to stay.

306

Though reason bids my heart to let thee go,  
Desire rebels and binds me close to thee,  
Against the tide of sense my passions flow,  
A captive to the waves of memory.  
Thy absence haunts the chambers of my mind,  
Yet echoing thy laughter, hope remains;  
In every shadow, traces I still find  
Of love that lingers, sweet despite its pains.  
The hours march onward, stubborn, cold, and slow,  
Yet time cannot erase what once had been;  
For even as I strive to loose my woe,  
Thy image in my soul is ever seen.  
  O cruel war 'twixt heart and reason's art,  
  For still, thou art the sovereign of my heart.

307

Though reason bids me hold my heart in check,  
Desire rebels and storms against its cage,  
It shuns the counsel calmness would direct,  
And kindles wild the fires I strive to gauge.  
No steely will can hush the restless beat  
That pounds within when thou art drawing near;  
My thoughts, unbridled, rush on reckless feet  
To paint thy beauty bright and ever clear.  
Yet wisdom whispers softly in the night,  
Entreating me to guard what love might cost,  
But passion’s flame leaps higher out of spite,  
Refusing peace until all sense is lost.  
  So, torn between the mind's cool, measured art  
  And love’s revolt, I yield unto my heart.

308

O grant me peace, though storms within me rage,  
For love’s sweet light contends with shadowed doubt,  
My heart, a tempest, wars within its cage,  
And silent fears would seek to drive love out.  
Yet hope, a lantern flickering but bright,  
Illumes the corners where my sorrows hide,  
And teaches day to banish brooding night,  
Restoring grace that wounded dreams denied.  
Though reason’s voice would caution me refrain,  
And whisper warnings of a future torn,  
Still, passion’s fire courses through each vein,  
And bids my soul embrace the breaking morn.  
  O gentle peace, descend and calm the sea—  
  Let love’s true harbor hold and shelter me.

309

If love is war, then let me be your peace,  
While you, unmoved, ignite my heart’s release.  
Though tempests rage within your silent eyes,  
I stand, a shore untouched by wild surmise.  
Your words, like arrows, fly and find their mark,  
Yet in their wounding, gentleness is found.  
I bear the scars of longing in the dark,  
And wear your absence as a sacred crown.  
Let not the night divide us with its gloom,  
Nor let the dawn betray what dreams conceal.  
For in your shadow, every rose may bloom,  
And in your truth, my fractured soul may heal.  
  So if love’s war must wage, let me remain  
  The quiet balm that soothes your every pain.

310

Though night denies the sun its warming grace,  
My heart pursues your light though hope is thin,  
Each shadowed hour reveals your shining face  
Where dreams of you in silent dark begin.  
The moon may pale against your golden fire,  
Yet still I trace your memory through gloom,  
And, lost within the maze of my desire,  
I find a garden where your roses bloom.  
The world may wither, starless and austere,  
But thoughts of you ignite the frozen air;  
Your gentle voice, imagined, draws me near  
And bids me rise above my deep despair.  
  For though the dawn may tarry out of sight,  
  Within my soul endures your steadfast light.

311

When light of you denies my heart its day,  
Yet shadows bloom where hope refuses night,  
My longing soul in silent disarray  
Finds comfort only in remembered light.  
Thy absence weaves a tapestry of shade,  
Each thread a wish, each knot a whispered plea,  
While dreams of thee in restless slumber fade,  
Still I await what waking eyes might see.  
The world turns pale when severed from thy grace,  
Its music dulled, its colors washed and worn,  
Yet in the hush, thy phantom I embrace—  
A ghost of love that leaves me more forlorn.  
  Return, sweet sun, and banish sorrow’s art;  
  Restore the dawn within my darkened heart.

312

My soul is torn between the night and you,  
Desire wars with reason in my chest,  
The moonlight pales beside your ardent hue,  
Yet wisdom bids my restless heart to rest.  
Your voice, a siren’s call upon the breeze,  
Invites me to abandon caution’s keep;  
But shadows whisper secrets through the trees,  
And gently sow their doubts before I sleep.  
The dawn betrays the promises of dreams,  
When silver hope dissolves in morning’s fire;  
Still memory returns in golden streams,  
Awakening forbidden, sweet desire.  
  Though night and reason strive to hold their ground,  
  In you, my soul’s true peace at last is found.

313

My soul is torn between the dark and light,  
It yearns for love yet shivers in its doubt,  
A beacon dimmed by shadows of the night,  
Yet hopeful still, though tempests swirl about.  
The gentle dawn would draw me from despair,  
And warm the frost that clings upon my heart,  
Yet phantoms whisper truths I cannot bear,  
And from their grasp, I struggle to depart.  
O fickle fate, why dost thou press me so,  
To wander paths where thorns and lilies grow?  
Is there a peace where sun and shadow blend,  
Or must I choose the one and lose the rest?  
  Still, in this war within, I seek its end,  
  That I may find at last what soul loves best.

314

My soul, a battlefield of hope and dread,  
Entreats your heart to see the war within.  
Where shadowed doubts and gleaming wishes tread,  
And battles lost and won are born akin.  
Each dawn revives the scars that night has made,  
Yet sunlight dares to pierce the iron gloom;  
Despair and longing, by each promise swayed,  
Exchange their armor in this restless room.  
Your glance—a truce, a balm for wounds unseen—  
Kindles the courage trembling in my core;  
With every word, you render sorrows lean,  
Until my fears are captives of your lore.  
  If love can conquer all that lies unsaid,  
  Let peace descend where hope and dread have bled.

315

My soul cries out while yours retreats in shade,  
Yet hope persists where silent sorrow grows,  
The dawn’s pale fingers touch the vows we made,  
But night conceals the garden where love sows.  
Oft have I wandered through the mist of fear,  
And called thy name to chase the dusk away,  
Yet shadows lengthen when thou art not near,  
And dreams, unspoken, wilt in cold decay.  
Still, in the hush between each heavy sigh,  
A spark endures against the pressing gloom,  
For hearts, though parted, learn again to fly  
On wings of promise, banishing all doom.  
  Thus, though our fates in different orbits move,  
  My soul, undaunted, waits the dawn of love.

316

My soul divides where hope and longing meet,  
Yet stands alone where shadows shun the light,  
The dawn’s pale promise falters at my feet,  
While memory and dream begin to fight.  
Within the hush, a whisper bids me stay,  
Though distant bells entice my heart to roam;  
Desire and doubt contend at close of day,  
And every path recalls the ache of home.  
What solace lies in silent, yearning hours,  
When moonlit visions tremble on the verge?  
The world recedes as sorrow’s gentle powers  
Draw forth old songs no morning can diverge.  
  Yet in this dusk, my spirit dares confess—  
  Hope’s quiet flame endures through loneliness.

317

My soul cries out where silence holds its reign,  
Yet hope persists though love returns no sign.  
The moon observes my solitary pain,  
Its silver gaze a witness to decline.  
The shadows linger long upon the floor,  
And memory’s hand stirs embers of regret.  
I pace the hours, yearning evermore  
For comfort from a heart that can’t forget.  
Yet in the hush, a gentle promise grows,  
Like dawn that breaks the bondage of the night;  
Though sorrow’s frost upon my spirit shows,  
I feel within the stirrings of new light.  
  For though bereft, I bear my hope anew—  
  One day, love’s silent lips may whisper true.

318

I beg my heart to silence all its cries,  
Yet louder grows the longing I despise.  
The moon, in pallid watch, with patient eyes  
Observes the tides that in my bosom rise.  
In vain I bid these restless thoughts to sleep—  
They toss and churn beneath a fragile seam.  
Unwelcome memories like shadows creep,  
Disturb the gentle surface of my dream.  
O, stubborn soul, why court forbidden pain,  
And chase the echo of a vanished song?  
Desire, unchecked, runs wild through every vein  
And turns my steadfast reason to the wrong.  
  Yet hope endures where sorrow’s roots are sown:  
  A garden blooms where longing once had grown.

319

Within my breast wage hope and fear their war,  
For love’s bright promise dims with each regret,  
The dawn that once did gild my heart before  
Now hides behind remembrance, cold and wet.  
Yet still, the sun’s pale fingers stroke the sky,  
And urge my weary soul to dream anew;  
Though shadows clutch, and bitter winds pass by,  
A whispered faith persists that love is true.  
Oft have I faltered, doubting fortune’s hand,  
Yet gentle voices bid me not despair;  
For every thorn that Mars this mortal land  
Yields roses, sweet, that blossom unaware.  
  So let hope’s banner flutter, proudly shown,  
  And fear’s dark seeds be in the light o’erthrown.

320

O teach my heart to quiet all its cries,  
For love within me fights with reason’s will,  
In silent wars concealed from watchful eyes,  
Where longing’s tempest rages, never still.  
Though wisdom bids the fevered pulse be calm,  
Desire in shadows weaves its subtle thread,  
And every word of caution turns to balm  
That soothes the ache, yet leaves the hunger fed.  
Why must affection dwell in such dissent,  
As thought and passion strive for fleeting reign?  
The mind would seek a gentle, cold relent,  
But heart insists on tasting joy and pain.  
  Yet in this strife I find my truest part—  
  A soul divided, yet a single heart.

321

I beg thee, love, to see me as I am,  
A flame that burns while wrapped in coldest stone,  
My heart, a captive lion, proud and calm,  
Yet roars in silence, aching and alone.  
Thine eyes, twin lanterns, guide me through the night,  
And gentle words like summer breezes blow,  
They lift my soul to unexpected height,  
And melt the frost that time and sorrow sow.  
O judge me not by shadows of my past,  
Nor hold my faults as chains about my name,  
For in thy gaze, I long to be recast,  
And rise anew, unburdened by old blame.  
  If thou wilt see me true, as I see thee,  
  Then love shall set us both forever free.

322

I beg thee, see the fire within my chest,  
Yet cold thy gaze, as if my heart were stone.  
The dawn implores thy mercy manifest,  
While night repeats my longing all alone.  
Thy silence—crueler than the winter’s breath—  
Extinguishes the hope I dare not speak.  
Each word unspoken draws me close to death,  
And leaves my trembling spirit frail and weak.  
Yet still I burn, a taper in the gloom,  
Awaiting but a spark to banish frost.  
For love, once kindled, can defy its doom,  
And light anew what once was nearly lost.  
  O grant one glance, to melt this frozen pain—  
  And I shall live, rekindled, whole again.

323

If love be light, then why am I in shade,  
My heart’s loud plea returns in silence cold,  
The golden sun of hope begins to fade,  
And passion’s fire, once bright, grows weak and old.  
Thy distant eyes, like stars, refuse to shine,  
Upon this night where longing takes its toll.  
Each breath I draw recalls thou art not mine,  
A captive yearning chained within my soul.  
Yet even shadows whisper of thy grace,  
And memory’s bloom defies the frost of pain.  
I wander lost, yet see thy ghostly face  
In every dream—thy absence, my refrain.  
  But if love’s light through darkest clouds can gleam,  
  Then hope survives within the faintest beam.

324

Upon the stage where trust and shadows meet,  
Thou play’st the serpent 'neath a lover’s skin,  
With honey’d words thy forkèd tongue doth greet  
The gentle heart thou mean’st to snare within.  
Thy glances flicker swift as candle’s flame,  
Yet feign devotion’s light to mask thy guile;  
Behind the mask, thy purpose stays the same—  
To charm, betray, then vanish with a smile.  
I, caught between the music and the lie,  
Hold hope’s frail lantern trembling in my hand,  
And wonder if the truth will e’er draw nigh  
Or if I wander lost in thy command.  
  Yet trust betrayed shall teach the soul to see:  
  False love’s brief play births wisdom’s legacy.

325

When trust, a fragile glass, in shadows lies,  
My heart, a storm-tossed bark, in silence weeps,  
The moon observes, unmoved, our mute goodbyes,  
While night’s cold promise in the darkness creeps.  
Each whispered hope dissolves in misty air,  
As gentle vows turn sharp and drift away,  
The garden of belief grows thin and bare,  
Its petals stolen by the break of day.  
Yet still within the hollow of the chest,  
A spark endures, defying reason’s call,  
Persisting, though betrayed and dispossessed,  
It waits for trust anew to rise from fall.  
  For hearts, though shattered, gather shards of light,  
  And dream of dawn to heal the wounds of night.

326

Upon the stage where trust in shadow lies,  
Thy honeyed tongue concealed the serpent’s art,  
A mask of warmth obscured thy colder guise,  
Each gentle word, a dagger to the heart.  
Thy promises, like dew, do quickly fade,  
And glances sweet disguise a hollow core;  
Beneath thy touch, my faith was swift betrayed,  
The garden’s bloom now choked with weeds of yore.  
Yet still, beneath the moon’s indifferent gaze,  
My soul recalls the music of thy voice,  
Its silver notes that set my mind ablaze,  
And left me captive, robbed of prudent choice.  
  Though trust be slain and innocence undone,  
  From ashes, wisdom’s bitter flowers are won.

327

When thou didst plant thy vows in vernal spring,  
The serpent’s tongue lay hidden in thy bloom,  
So sweet the shade thy budding words did bring,  
I wandered blind, unheeding of my doom.  
Thy gentle hand did twine with mine in trust,  
A garden bower wrought of hope and dew,  
Yet underneath, the roots were sown with rust,  
And every petal masked a poison true.  
The morning sun beheld our union bright,  
Unknowing of the storm that night would send,  
For in thy gaze did flicker hints of blight,  
A secret winter longing to descend.  
  Yet though betrayed by thee, I hold thee dear,  
  For love once sprung may bloom despite the fear.

328

When midnight’s rose did wither in thy hand,  
Thou turned’st thy face as shadows swept the lea,  
The velvet hush obeyed thy soft command,  
And stilled the stars that watched so jealously.  
Upon the moor, the wind recalled thy name,  
Its sigh a secret carried on the night;  
The moon, in silver grief, confessed its shame  
For waning in the absence of thy light.  
Yet time, unkind, would draw the morning near,  
And steal the perfume from the faded bloom;  
But memories, like petals, linger here,  
Unfurling in the silent, haunted room.  
  So let the rose of midnight never die,  
  But blossom where thy dreams and heart comply.

329

Upon the stage where once thy vows were sworn,  
A serpent’s tongue hath turned my trust to thorn.  
Thy honeyed words, now bitter to the taste,  
Have left my budding hope in ruin lorn.  
The moon bears witness to our union waste,  
As shadows creep where golden sunlight shone.  
A tapestry of dreams is rent and chaste,  
Unraveling until the threads are gone.  
Yet in this grief, my spirit seeks the dawn,  
To find in loss the wisdom sorrow brings.  
Though love’s sweet rose is bruised and nearly gone,  
Its scent remains upon the wounded wings.  
  For broken faith, though sharp as winter’s wind,  
  May teach the heart anew to hope, and mend.

330

Upon the midnight stage my soul did play,  
A shadowed actor wandering astray,  
The distant stars a silent, watching crowd,  
Whose silver gaze unveiled my heart unbowed.  
The velvet drapes of night concealed my fears,  
Yet hope’s faint lantern flickered in the gloom,  
It drew the curtain back on vanished years,  
And bathed my spirit in a gentle bloom.  
Each whispered line, rehearsed within my chest,  
Did tremble on the edge of longing’s breath,  
While memory in haunted costume dressed,  
Stalked soft across the floor of life and death.  
  Yet as the dawn announced its golden cue,  
  My soul bowed low, and dreamed of scenes anew.

331

Within the vault where secret sorrows dwell,  
My soul, a wounded dove, doth bleed unseen,  
The heavy silence tolled by midnight’s bell  
Enshrouds my heart in shadows deep and keen.  
The candle’s flame, a sentinel of pain,  
Casts trembling ghosts upon the weary stone,  
Its flicker paints my memories again  
With faces lost and hopes that died alone.  
Yet in this chamber, sorrow’s bitter root  
Conceals a seed of solace, faint but true;  
Each tear distilled is sorrow’s final fruit,  
A draught of strength for trials I must rue.  
  So from this darkness, quietly I rise,  
  And greet the dawn with newly opened eyes.

332

My soul, a vessel cracked by faithless hands,  
Doth bleed the golden trust that once it bore,  
The tides of hope recede from silent sands,  
And leave a barren longing on the shore.  
The whisper of a promise, cold and spent,  
Like autumn leaves that spiral to their rest,  
Reminds me how the tender heart is bent  
By winter’s breath against a yearning breast.  
Yet in the hollow ache where faith has fled,  
A spark endures beneath the ashen gloom;  
Though shadows press, I do not count love dead,  
For seeds of dawn lie dreaming in the tomb.  
  So let the night its mourning vigil keep—  
  The soul shall rise anew from sorrow’s deep.

333

Upon the altar of my trust thou lied’st,  
A serpent coiled within my yearning soul,  
Thy honeyed words in venom were disguised,  
And shattered hope no gentle hand made whole.  
Thine eyes, once lanterns in my midnight gloom,  
Now flicker cold as frosts upon the moor,  
Where blossoms fair were destined not to bloom,  
And love’s sweet promise vanished evermore.  
Yet in the ashes of my faith betrayed,  
A fiercer fire within my heart is born;  
Though tears like silent rain have oft obeyed,  
I rise anew upon the break of morn.  
  For though thy treason wove my soul with pain,  
  My trust, once lost, shall ne’er be thine again.

334

Upon the altar of my trust thou lied’st,  
A serpent’s whisper clothed in angel’s guise,  
Thy honeyed tongue in silken treason tried’st  
To weave deceit's fine net before mine eyes.  
Yet, blinded by the radiance of thy smile,  
I knelt and bared my heart without defense—  
Unknowing of the venom all the while  
That flowed in every word and every sense.  
Thy promises, like morning dew, did fade,  
Leaving the barren truth of love’s decay.  
My soul, betrayed, in shadow now is laid,  
Yet from this gloom, I seek a brighter day.  
  For though thy falsehood brought me to my knees,  
  I rise, and walk unbound by thy disease.

335

Upon the treacherous winds my soul did sail,  
Yet found no harbor in thy fickle heart,  
Each hope I cast became a tattered veil  
That could not shield me from love’s biting dart.  
Thy promises, like foam upon the sea,  
Did sparkle bright, then vanish from my sight.  
What trust I held dissolved so easily,  
As morning mists retreat from rising light.  
Yet still I wander shores where dreams have died,  
Collecting fragments left by faithless tide;  
The gulls above, in vagrant circles wide,  
Echo my longing none can quite deride.  
  Though tempests rage and all the stars depart,  
  Still drifts my soul, unanchored, from thy heart.

336

Upon the stage of trust I played my part,  
Yet shadows crept and nestled in my breast,  
The light of hope did flicker in my heart,  
While doubts, like silent thieves, denied me rest.  
I spoke in earnest, offered up my soul,  
Believing words could bridge the aching space,  
But fragile dreams slipped swiftly through the hole  
That time and sorrow carved upon my face.  
Yet still I stand, though shaken by the storm,  
Resolved to seek the sun behind the rain,  
For broken faith, though battered from its form,  
May rise anew, unshackled from its pain.  
  So let the wounds of treachery be known:  
  From fractured trust, a stronger love is grown.

337

Within my breast, a serpent coils in woe,  
Its venom drips where erstwhile trust did grow.  
The whispered lies like shadowed rivers flow,  
Unseen, they taint the roots of love below.  
A garden once adorned with hope’s fair bloom  
Now wilts beneath the weight of secret blight;  
Despair’s cold fingers weave a silent loom  
And shroud the dawn in endless, starless night.  
Yet still, my heart in trembling silence yearns  
For balm to heal the wounds deceit hath made,  
To see the truth as gentle daylight burns  
And pierce the gloom where innocence did fade.  
  O, let forgiveness like the morning rise,  
  And banish all the poison with its skies.

338

Upon the stage of trust my shadow fell,  
Betrayed by whispers woven in mine heart,  
The silvered tongue that tolls a secret bell  
Did rend the fragile threads that once did start.  
Where once was sunlight, now the clouds convene,  
And doubts, like winter’s frost, upon me grow.  
What once was certain now lies hid, unseen,  
As silent rivers bear the weight of woe.  
Yet in the breach, a lesson softly gleams:  
That faith, once broken, seeks a patient hand—  
New hope is kindled in the dust of dreams,  
And from the shards, a braver soul may stand.  
  For though the trustless night may tarry long,  
  The dawn restores the heart betrayed by wrong.

339

Upon the stage of trust, thou played’st thy part,  
Yet left’st my soul a garden choked with frost,  
Where once sweet blooms did flourish at the start,  
Now silent thorns recall what I have lost.  
Thy honeyed words, like gentle April rain,  
Did promise summer’s warmth and golden light,  
But vanished swift, and left a wounding stain  
That blights the day and haunts the cloak of night.  
Still do I tend the memory of thee,  
And water hope with tears I dare not show;  
For though thy love is but a withered tree,  
Its shadow lingers everywhere I go.  
  Yet from this winter, I shall rise anew,  
  And plant a trust that springs not wrought by you.

340

Upon the field where trust's fair blossoms grew,  
I wander lone, my heart in shadow cast,  
The golden hopes I once so dearly knew  
Now wither silent in the autumn blast.  
Beneath the sighing wind, the petals pale  
Lie scattered, bruised by time’s relentless hand,  
Their fragrance lost upon the wandering gale,  
Their promise faded from this weary land.  
Yet in the hush, faint whispers reach my ear,  
A memory of laughter, light, and song;  
Though night doth shroud the path I once held dear,  
The roots of faith in secret earth grow strong.  
  So shall I tend the garden of the mind,  
  And in new dawn, fresh blooms of trust shall find.

341

Within my breast, a serpent coils its shame,  
That once did bask in love’s illusive light,  
It murmurs softly, whispering my name,  
And strikes my heart with venom born of night.  
I wore affection’s mask, a fragile guise,  
To shield the wounds that longing left behind;  
Yet every hope unveiled a host of lies,  
And truth itself grew cruel and unkind.  
The memory of joy, a fleeting ghost,  
Now haunts the hollows time cannot repair;  
Desire devours what I cherished most,  
And leaves me clinging to the empty air.  
  Yet from this poison, wisdom may arise—  
  A phoenix forged from where the serpent lies.

342

When treason, cloaked in sweet affection's guise,  
Did whisper lies with honeyed, serpent tongue,  
The trust within my breast, to my surprise,  
Did falter first, and then from hope was wrung.  
False oaths did blossom, fair as April’s rose,  
Yet thorns unseen did pierce the tender heart;  
In shadowed words, deceitful fancy grows,  
And brightest bonds by subtle guile depart.  
O traitor love, with visage softly drawn,  
Whose gentle smile did mask a viper's bite,  
Thou turn’st the golden promise into dawn  
That fades, betrayed, before the full of light.  
  Yet from this fall, my soul shall rise anew:  
  For truth, once lost, is won by hearts most true.

343

When treachery did steal thy gentle breath,  
My heart, a wounded stag, did flee from death.  
The silent moon beheld my soul’s unrest,  
Its silver gaze a balm upon my chest.  
Within the hush where shadows softly creep,  
Thy memory lingers in the midnight air.  
I pace the wilds, denied the grace of sleep,  
And seek thy voice in dreams that end in prayer.  
The roses pale that once thy hand did tend  
Now bow their heads and weep with morning dew.  
No promise made can broken fate amend,  
Yet hope, though frail, is ever born anew.  
  Though night be long and sorrow’s path unkind,  
  In love’s bright dawn thy spirit still I find.

344

O treacherous Night, thou whisper’st in mine ear,  
Thy sable cloak hath veiled my trust in fear.  
With every hush, thy velvet shadows creep,  
And sow the seeds of doubt I cannot clear.  
Thy silent footfalls in the darkness deep  
Disturb the gentle dreams I long to keep,  
While moonlit secrets in the stillness lie,  
And stars like watchful eyes refuse to sleep.  
Yet in thy gloom, a trembling hope I spy,  
A promise that the dawn will soon be nigh;  
For though thy reign be fierce upon my soul,  
Thy fleeting hours in golden light shall die.  
  So let me brave thy whispers and thy wiles,  
  And greet the morn with undivided smiles.

345

When treachery did steal thy gentle guise,  
Sweet Trust in shadows weeps with silent art,  
The golden daylight dims in clouded skies,  
A frost of doubt encases tender heart.  
The whispered vows that once did softly bind  
Are scattered leaves upon a wayward breeze;  
I seek the lamp of truth, but naught I find,  
Save memories that haunt and never please.  
Yet from the ruins hope begins to rise,  
A fragile bloom within a field of stone;  
For though deceit's cold hand would dim my eyes,  
My soul recalls the warmth that once was known.  
  So let me mend the threads that fate did sever,  
  And trust, though scarred, may yet endure forever.

346

O treach’rous heart, thou weav’st thy silken snare,  
Whilst gentle trust lies bleeding on the ground,  
Thy honeyed words, like perfume in the air,  
Conceal the blade in velvet wrappings bound.  
Thou art the thief that steals the sleep from eyes,  
And plants pale doubt where once bright hope did bloom;  
A shadow passing ‘neath love’s summer skies,  
That leaves behind a chill of silent gloom.  
Yet, in thy grasp, sweet folly finds its voice,  
And yearning lingers, captive of thy art,  
For though betrayed, the soul makes mournful choice  
To seek the warmth that first undid the heart.  
  O faithless heart, thy power is bitter-sweet—  
  To fall again and rise on wounded feet.

347

When treachery in gentle visage dwelt,  
Fair Love herself did weep at broken truth,  
In honeyed words where poison's sting was felt,  
The bright-eyed hopes were slain in tender youth.  
A silver tongue did mask a heart of stone,  
And trust, so fragile, shattered in the night;  
The sweetest smiles concealed the seeds unsown,  
And day gave way to shadows void of light.  
Yet from the ruins springs a fiercer flame,  
For sorrow’s forge refines what once was pure;  
Though falsehood seeks to tarnish Love’s good name,  
Her steadfast soul endures and shall endure.  
  So let the traitor’s mask at last be torn—  
  True hearts, though bruised, are tempered and reborn.

348

When treachery did steal into my breast,  
My soul, betrayed, now weareth Sorrow's face,  
The gentle peace within me found no rest,  
But fled in silence from its hallowed place.  
The trust I nurtured, delicate as dew,  
Was dashed upon the rocks of faithless schemes;  
The dawn that once with golden promise grew  
Now hides behind the shroud of broken dreams.  
Yet in the dark, a stubborn hope remains,  
A spark unquenched by tempests of despair;  
Though bitter are the lingering, silent pains,  
My heart endures, and learns anew to care.  
  For though deceit's cold shadow touched my core,  
  I'll rise, and trust in gentle truth once more.

349

O treacherous Night, thou whisper’st in my soul,  
A serpent’s hiss doth mar my heart’s delight,  
Thy sable shroud attempts to make me whole,  
Yet steals the golden memory of light.  
Upon thy breast, the silent shadows creep,  
And dreams, like phantoms, flutter out of reach,  
While lonely hours in mournful silence weep,  
And hope lies stranded, speechless on the beach.  
Thou weav’st illusions with thy sable thread,  
Enticing weary minds to slip and stray,  
Awak’ning fears that long ago were dead,  
And painting all the stars in ghostly gray.  
  Yet when the dawn breaks free from thy control,  
  I find the sun hath mended all thou stole.

350

When treachery doth whisper in my soul,  
Her silken tongue unravels all my peace,  
She weaves a shadow where my hopes were whole,  
And bids the honest light of trust to cease.  
Upon the silent midnight’s trembling air,  
Her phantom fingers pluck the strings of doubt,  
While memory and reason both despair,  
And every pledge is poisoned by her rout.  
Yet in the storm, my heart, though battered, stands,  
Against the tempest, stubborn as the stone,  
Resolving not to yield to her commands,  
To claim again the sunlight as my own.  
  For though deceit may bruise and break my trust,  
  In loyal love I’ll rise again from dust.

351

My soul did court the night with trembling hands,  
Yet dawn betrayed her vows with silent spite;  
She scattered hope like dust on barren lands,  
And stole the velvet comfort of the night.  
The moon, in mourning, veiled her silver gaze,  
While stars retreated, shy behind the shade;  
The world, unkind, unmoved by lover’s praise,  
Watched dreams dissolve as waking’s debts were paid.  
Yet in the hush where shadowed longing lies,  
A whisper lingers, soft as lover’s breath—  
A promise kept beneath the sullen skies  
That night returns to rescue me from death.  
  So though the dawn may wound with fleeting light,  
  My soul shall rise and seek the arms of night.

352

O faithless night, thou steal’st my tender trust,  
And leav’st my soul to weep in silent rue,  
Thy sable veil doth shroud my hope in dust,  
And veers my thoughts from joys I once well knew.  
The moon, pale witness to my weary plight,  
Doth peer through clouds that hide her gentle face,  
While stars, cold sentinels within the night,  
Gaze down unmoved upon my lone disgrace.  
Yet in thy shadowed breast, a lesson lies:  
The dawn will follow, banishing thy reign.  
Though tears may stain the corners of mine eyes,  
Sweet morning’s breath can heal the deepest pain.  
  So though my trust is lost within thy shade,  
  I wait the sun, and hope shall be repaid.

353

My soul, a trembling guest in love's domain,  
Did trust her hopes to falsehood's velvet tongue,  
And found her joy exchanged for bitter pain,  
A song of trust by siren voices sung.  
The dawn, once golden, paled to ashen gray,  
As faith's bright bloom did wither on the vine;  
Yet in the night, hope would not flee away,  
Her embered light in shadowed halls did shine.  
Though promises like autumn leaves did fall  
And winds of doubt did chill the weary heart,  
I rise anew, despite each siren call,  
Resolved that truth and love shall not depart.  
  For though deceit's cold hand hath left its scar,  
  The soul remembers still what true loves are.

354

My soul, thou turn’st a dagger in thy breast,  
For Truth, once fair, now wears a traitor’s face,  
Her honeyed words now sour at my behest,  
And trust, once sworn, retreats in cold disgrace.  
The sun that rose with hope now hides in gloom,  
Each golden ray eclipsed by doubt’s dark hand,  
Where laughter danced, now silence fills the room,  
And joy’s bright banners fall upon the sand.  
Yet in this night, a stubborn ember glows,  
A spark defies the shadow’s grim parade;  
Though sorrow’s storm in bitter fury blows,  
Still courage stands, though battered and betrayed.  
  For in the heart, though pierced by pain anew,  
  Resides a strength that falsehood cannot rue.

355

O treacherous heart, why dost thou wound thine own,  
When trust, thy faithful shadow, turn'd away?  
Within thy breast, a garden overgrown,  
Where hope once bloomed now tangled thorns hold sway.  
Thou art both jailer and the prisoner made,  
Condemned to pace the borders of thy grief;  
A traitor's mask upon thy visage laid,  
Betrayal's hand steals comfort like a thief.  
Yet gentle reason pleads with thee to heal,  
To mend the cracks where whispered doubts reside;  
For time bestows the balm that wounds conceal,  
And patience leads thee where true loves abide.  
  O heart, forgive thyself and banish pain,  
  Let loyal trust within thy walls remain.

356

False Friendship, thou hast worn a gentle face,  
Yet mid my breast dost plant thy secret thorn;  
With honeyed words thou didst my heart enlace,  
But left its tender trust betrayed and torn.  
Thy laughter, once a balm, now rings untrue,  
A hollow echo in a faithless night;  
The golden promises I did pursue  
Lie tarnished by thy ever-fading light.  
Oh, how thou didst thy shifting masque compose,  
A seeming friend, yet harboring deceit;  
The bloom of kindness wilted as it grows,  
Its petals trampled by thy false retreat.  
  Yet from this wound, new wisdom I shall find:  
  True hearts are gold, but rare and hard to bind.

357

When Loyalty, sweet maid, did turn her face,  
And Hope in shadows crept from out mine eye,  
The world grew pale, bereft of gentle grace,  
And every whispered promise proved a lie.  
Yet still within my breast a faint refrain  
Of trust’s old song did stubbornly remain,  
Though tempests swept and broke the boughs of May,  
And left my heart to wander, lost, astray.  
The night was long, and faith seemed naught but dream,  
While doubt’s cold hand did clutch my trembling soul;  
Yet through the darkness shone a golden gleam—  
A memory of love that made me whole.  
  Thus, though deserted by the world’s delight,  
  Hope’s embers kindle in the blackest night.

358

When Trust, once robed in gold, did turn away,  
My heart, a faithful hound, was left to stray.  
The silent halls, where laughter used to play,  
Now echo hollow truths I can’t allay.  
A tapestry of words, once finely spun,  
Unravels thread by thread in midnight’s gloom,  
Revealing all the secrets come undone,  
And painting shadows deep within my room.  
Yet hope, though bruised, still flickers in the night,  
A candle trembling against fickle breeze,  
It gathers up the shards of broken light,  
And weaves a gentle promise with its ease.  
  For trust, though lost, may find its golden hue,  
  If hearts forgive, and dare to start anew.

359

Deceitful Night hath whispered in mine ear,  
That Love, my sovereign, turns a stranger's face;  
The stars conspire with shadows, drawing near  
To cloak familiar joys in dark embrace.  
Yet hope, a lantern in the velvet gloom,  
Doth flicker faint against the breath of lies,  
And bids my heart resist impending doom  
Despite the storm that gathers in the skies.  
O fickle Night, thy counsel is but smoke,  
A fleeting phantom dancing on the breeze;  
For though thou wouldst my constancy revoke,  
My love endures, unbowed by treacheries.  
  When dawn dispels the falsehoods thou hast spun,  
  My steadfast heart shall greet the waking sun.

360

When trust, once gold, is turned to leaden shame,  
My heart wars gentle love with bitter spite,  
The sun that rose on joy departs in blame,  
And hope’s bright tapestry is torn by night.  
The honeyed words that once did sweetly flow  
Now curdle on the tongue with silent dread;  
Where once did gleam the promise’s soft glow,  
Now shadowed doubts and heavy steps are led.  
Yet still, amid the ruins of my faith,  
A stubborn ember flickers in the gloom,  
Defying all that treachery bequeath,  
And breathing fragile life where sorrows loom.  
  Though trust is bruised, my heart endures the cost:  
  True love, though battered, never shall be lost.

361

When trust, once gold, doth turn to leaden stone,  
My heart, divided 'twixt delight and pain,  
Finds shadows where the light of love had shone,  
And seeks in silence what I seek in vain.  
The gentle vows that danced upon the air  
Now echo hollow, distant as the moon;  
A whispered promise fading to despair,  
A summer rose that withers far too soon.  
Yet hope, that stubborn root, defies the frost,  
And draws its breath from memories now past;  
Though innocence, like morning dew, is lost,  
Forgiveness bids a gentler shadow cast.  
  So let these wounds in time be healed anew,  
  For hearts, though broken, learn to trust and true.

362

When trust’s fair flower is bruised by thy deceit,  
Yet still my heart, divided, loves and mourns.  
The honeyed words that once did taste so sweet  
Lie bitter now, as time’s slow healing scorns.  
My soul, in tempest tossed, by hope betrayed,  
Seeks gentle refuge in remembered light,  
But shadows linger where thy hand hath strayed,  
And day is haunted by the shade of night.  
Yet though my reason bids me turn away,  
Affection clings like ivy to the stone—  
For bonds once forged in gold will not decay,  
Though all their luster to the dark hath flown.  
  Thus torn, I dwell between despair and grace,  
  A lover lost, yet yearning for thy face.

363

When trust in thee was gold, I found it lead,  
Thy honeyed tongue dealt gall where sweetness fed;  
Thy eyes, twin lanterns, promised hope ahead,  
Yet drew me wandering where angels dread.  
A gentle hand concealed a sharpened thorn,  
Thy laughter rang, but shadows danced behind.  
The sunniest of days was marked by scorn,  
The fairest words a snare to chain the mind.  
Yet in thy falsehoods did I learn to see  
The fragile beauty trust alone bestows—  
How faith, once shattered, sets the spirit free  
From golden lies whose shine but darkness grows.  
  Though love in ashes lies, my soul is wise:  
  From leaden heart, new hope and truth arise.

364

When truth did wear the mask of gentle grace,  
Thy tender vows concealed a serpent’s tongue,  
A honeyed smile adorned thy guarded face,  
Yet from thy lips the seeds of discord sprung.  
Beneath the silver moon, our hearts did meet,  
While shadows danced upon the fragile trust,  
Thy whispered words, so soft, so bittersweet,  
Did turn my golden dreams to ash and dust.  
Yet in the garden where betrayal grew,  
And silent tears did water thorny lies,  
I found the strength to bid my heart adieu,  
And let my soul from fettered hope arise.  
  For though thy artful guile did pierce me deep,  
  I wake, and leave thee to thy poisoned sleep.

365

When night betrays the promise of the day,  
My heart in hope's attire doth pine in vain,  
The silver moon obscured by clouds of gray,  
Each star a witness to my quiet pain.  
The gentle breeze that once bespoke delight  
Now whispers secrets, cold as winter's breath,  
And shadows crowd the corners of the night,  
Foretelling dreams entombed in silent death.  
Yet from despair, a trembling dawn is born,  
Its golden fingers brush my sorrowed brow;  
The petals of a rose conceal a thorn,  
But even wounded, I must rise somehow.  
  For though the night usurps the sun's domain,  
  The day returns, and hope shall bloom again.

366

Between the light of love and shade of lies,  
My soul doth wage its war 'twixt trust and doubt.  
Each word thou speak’st, a sunbeam in disguise,  
Yet shadows linger when the truth is out.  
Thy gentle hand extends, I flinch away,  
Uncertain if its warmth is false or true.  
The dawn of promise yields to dusk of grey,  
And hope’s sweet bloom is touched by sorrow’s dew.  
But oh, in fleeting moments, faith takes flight,  
And I believe thy heart to mirror mine.  
A fragile peace is forged beneath the night,  
Where dreams and fears in tangled threads entwine.  
  If love be light, let truth its beacon be,
