  Dispel the dark, and set my spirit free.

367

When faith didst wear the semblance of a friend,  
My soul didst trust where shadows softly grew,  
Believing light and promise would not end,  
Yet found the dawn concealed by mist anew.  
A gentle voice did whisper of the day,  
And hope’s fair blossoms opened in my chest,  
But silence crept and stole the words away,  
Leaving the heart’s frail longing unconfessed.  
The golden mask of trust began to fade,  
Revealing doubts beneath its painted smile;  
Where once the warmth of certainty was laid,  
Now colder winds of caution rise the while.  
  Yet though deceived by faith’s enchanting guise,  
  I seek the truth that waits behind all lies.

368

Within my soul two warring tempests vie,  
One whispers truth, the other weaves a lie.  
A gentle breeze would grant my mind reprieve,  
Yet storms of doubt and hope in shadows thrive.  
The silver tongue of longing tempts my ear,  
While reason's anchor bids my heart to stay;  
A thousand doubts in midnight's hush appear,  
Yet dawn returns to chase the dark away.  
In silence, both contenders stake their claim,  
And wrestle for the scepter of my will.  
The victor veiled, I know not whom to blame—  
Desires unspoken, or the voice grown still.  
  Yet through this strife, my soul in time may find  
  A peace that tempests leave in calm behind.

369

Within my breast, where hope and sorrow dwell,  
A traitor's whisper chills my faithful soul,  
Like midnight bells that toll a somber knell,  
Yet urge me on, though darkness takes its toll.  
The candle’s flame, beset by winds of doubt,  
Still dances in the shelter of my hand,  
While silent fears, unspoken, swirl about,  
As shifting tides reshape the steadfast sand.  
Yet in the hush where shadowed secrets lie,  
A gentle promise glimmers through the night,  
Reminding me that though I ache and sigh,  
The dawn will come and bathe the world in light.  
  For hearts that weather storms and silent pain  
  Shall find, in hope’s embrace, their strength again.

370

When trust, once sworn, falls silent in the gloom,  
My soul’s bright fire is quenched by icy lies;  
The golden rose that in my heart did bloom  
Now withers 'neath the shadowed, clouded skies.  
What once was light now trembles into night,  
Each whispered word a ghost upon the air.  
Yet still I search for embers lost to sight,  
And hope that dawn might banish cold despair.  
The hours creep with sorrow’s silent tread,  
Regret, a specter haunting every thought.  
I mourn the broken vows, the tears we’ve shed,  
The battles for forgiveness dearly fought.  
  Yet from these ashes, let my spirit rise—  
  For truth may bloom again where falsehood dies.

371

When faith did bloom, my soul was sweetly fed,  
Yet thorns of falsehood drew my heart to bleed,  
The golden promises from lips once wed  
Now lie in shadows, stripped of hope and creed.  
The morning’s hymns once lifted by the dove  
Are lost to winds that whisper doubts instead,  
While trust, once girded strong with cords of love,  
Unravels gently, by suspicion led.  
Yet in the midnight hush where sorrows grow,  
A fragile bud of faith begins anew—  
For even in the soil where grief seeds woe,  
The sun may coax a blossom through the dew.  
  So let me tend the garden of my heart,  
  And find again the faith false words depart.

372

When trust, once golden, turns to ashen lies,  
My heart debates 'twixt hope and deep despise,  
The sun, once bright, retreats behind a veil,  
Its warmth now lost to cold and clouded skies.  
I search the silent dusk for truth’s detail,  
Yet shadows lengthen where belief did dwell.  
The gentle vows that once could never fail  
Are echoes now within a hollow shell.  
Yet still, fair memory tempts my soul to yearn,  
Recalling laughter shared in softer light,  
But wounds by treachery refuse to burn  
And leave me restless through the watchful night.  
  Though trust be broken, time may heal the cost—  
  From ashen ruins, gold is sometimes lost.

373

Within my breast, a serpent coils in trust,  
Yet outward smiles belie the inward rust.  
A gilded mask conceals the creeping dust  
That settles deep where silent fears combust.  
The world beholds my laughter’s golden hue,  
But knows not of the shadows I contain;  
Each gentle word, a veil I must renew,  
Lest truth emerge and shatter my domain.  
While sunlight paints affection on my face,  
Night’s subtle whisper stirs the hidden ache.  
I play the part, adorned with borrowed grace,  
Though in the dark, my trembling heart may break.  
  Yet hope persists, that time may set me free  
  From serpents’ coils, to live in honesty.

374

When trust, once gold, doth turn to leaden weight,  
My heart debates 'twixt wrath and mercy's hand,  
The silver cords of kinship strained by fate,  
Unravel swift as footprints in the sand.  
Each gentle word now bears a sharpened edge,  
And glances, once like sunlight, coldly gleam;  
Where once we walked united, pledge by pledge,  
Now distance grows and love is but a dream.  
Yet in the hollows where resentment grows,  
A feeble hope, like violets in the snow,  
Persists despite the wounds that silence shows,  
And bids forgiveness root where sorrows go.  
  So shall I choose, though bitter tears be shed,  
  To mend the golden trust, though it lies dead.

375

When trust, once clad in gold, doth turn to rust,  
My heart in hope’s embrace is left betrayed,  
The gentle light of faith consumed by dust,  
And dreams, like autumn leaves, begin to fade.  
The whispered vows that once adorned the air  
Now echo hollow in the silent gloom;  
A fragile bond dissolved by cold despair  
Leaves only sorrow’s shadow in the room.  
Yet still I search for solace in the night,  
A flicker faint behind the veil of pain,  
Believing dawn may yield a gentler light  
To heal the wounds that fall like summer rain.  
  For though my trust be scarred by broken art,  
  Love’s ember lives, unyielding, in my heart.

376

Within my breast doth loyalty reside,  
Yet venom’d words have made my trust decay;  
The tides of doubt, relentless as the tide,  
Erode the steadfast stone of faith away.  
Once golden bonds are tarnished by a lie,  
And gentle hopes, like petals, fall to dust;  
Where once I soared, my soul now fears to fly,  
Distracted by the weight of broken trust.  
Yet still I seek some solace in the night,  
A flicker faint, a promise not yet spent;  
For though the world may cast my heart in blight,  
It cannot mar the love that’s truly meant.  
  So let them wound, and let suspicion reign—  
  True loyalty shall rise and heal the pain.

377

When trust did bloom, thy falsehood took its root,  
My heart’s fair orchard, spoiled by venom’s sting;  
What once was sweet, now bears a bitter fruit,  
Deceitful shadows darken hope’s young spring.  
The gentle sun that kissed our budding days  
Now hides behind the clouds thy lies have drawn,  
And in the garden’s hush, no bird now stays,  
For every song of joy, alas, is gone.  
Yet still I walk amid the ruined flowers,  
And mourn the petals trampled by thy guile;  
Their fragrance lingers through the weeping hours,  
A ghost of love that faded for a while.  
  Though roots are cut and blossoms lost to pain,  
  New trust may rise when sunlight falls again.

378

You were the lantern guiding through my night,  
Yet now your flame has burned my trust to ash;  
The gentle gleam that led me to the right  
Is but a spark consumed in sudden crash.  
I wandered once by certainty made bold,  
But shadows lengthen where your promise failed;  
The warmth of words you spun is now grown cold,  
Their silver threads by silent doubts assailed.  
Your laughter, once a beacon on the air,  
Resounds as echoes in an empty hall;  
What once was solace now is cold despair,  
A memory behind a crumbling wall.  
  Yet from these ruins, I will find my way—  
  A dawn must come to chase the dark away.

379

Your shadow lingers where my trust once lay,  
A garden wounded by your silent frost.  
The bloom of hope, once bright, now fades to gray,  
Its petals scattered, innocence the cost.  
I watched the sun retreat behind your eyes,  
A chill unspoken settled on the land;  
Yet still, my heart in silent longing tries  
To nurture seeds that tremble in my hand.  
The ghost of laughter haunts the empty air,  
And memory weeps dew upon the ground.  
The rose’s blush is paling in despair,  
Its fragrance lost where bitter roots are found.  
  Yet from this sorrow, strength may yet arise—  
  For broken earth gives birth to new sunrise.

380

You were the lantern guiding through my night,  
Yet now your flame has burned my trust to ash,  
Once gentle beams have turned to searing light,  
And every memory yields a painful flash.  
Where once your words were balm upon my soul,  
They now are daggers veiled in honeyed tone,  
The wounds you carved have left a bitter toll,  
A love undone, its sweetness overthrown.  
I wander shadows searching for relief,  
But echoes of your laughter haunt my mind,  
Each step I take is burdened by my grief,  
No solace in the comfort left behind.  
  Yet in this darkness, hope begins to gleam—  
  From ashes rise the embers of my dream.

381

You wore my trust as if a borrowed cloak,  
Then left it tattered, drifting in the rain.  
Sweet promises dissolved in words you spoke,  
Their golden luster tarnished by your gain.  
A fool am I to wish for what was lost,  
Yet memory clings as ivy to the wall.  
Each gentle lie exacts a bitter cost,  
And hope, once bright, lies shattered from its fall.  
Yet in the quiet dusk, I mend the seam  
Of faith undone, and gather what remains.  
Though love departs, I cradle still the dream,  
And wash my wounds in softly falling rains.  
  For from these threads, anew my heart shall weave—  
  A cloak of trust no storm shall make me leave.

382

You were the summer sun that kissed my skin,  
Yet left me shivering when light grew thin.  
A golden warmth that lingered deep within,  
Then vanished swift as autumn winds begin.  
Beneath your gaze, the world was painted bright,  
Each leaf and shadow trembled with your fire.  
But shadows lengthen with the waning light,  
And day departs, surrendering desire.  
I held the memory of your embrace,  
A ghost of laughter echoing at dusk,  
Yet in the twilight’s hush, I search your face  
And find but whispers fading into musk.  
  O blazing star that set too soon for me,  
  I chase your glow across eternity.

383

You were the sun I worshipped from afar,  
Yet shadows grew where once your radiance shone,  
Your golden gaze, a promise, now a scar  
Upon the fields my heart had called its own.  
The dawn you kindled faded into gray,  
And silence filled the places you would light.  
Where once I turned to you to guide my way,  
Now twilight falls and day dissolves to night.  
Yet in this dusk, a gentle lesson gleams—  
That worship built on distance turns to wane.  
No star endures the burden of our dreams  
When longing feeds but never soothes the pain.  
  Still, though your warmth has vanished from my view,  
  I thank the dawn that once I loved in you.

384

My soul’s a house where shadows softly creep,  
Betrayal’s whispers haunt the rooms I keep.  
By candle’s flicker, memories are cast  
Upon the walls, pale ghosts of love’s defeat.  
Each echoed footfall stirs the silent past,  
Where laughter’s gold is tarnished, bittersweet.  
The window panes confess the midnight rain,  
Their trembling glass recalls a last goodbye.  
Yet hope, a stubborn guest, defies the pain,  
And kindles embers time cannot deny.  
Frail curtains flutter with a gentle breeze,  
Inviting dreams to dance upon the floor.  
  Though haunted still, my spirit seeks its peace—  
  Unlocks the doors and lets the dawn restore.

385

My soul, a fortress breached by trusted hands,  
Now wanders deserts sown with seeds of doubt,  
Where once stood faith as tall as ancient sands,  
Now shadows creep and silent voices shout.  
The stars that watched me dream have lost their light,  
The moon’s pale witness dims behind a veil;  
The gentle winds that soothed me in the night  
Now howl with secrets borne on bitter gale.  
Yet from this ruin, hope begins to rise,  
A stubborn ember glowing in the ash;  
It kindles fire within my weary eyes,  
Defies the storm, withstands the thunder’s crash.  
  For though betrayed, my heart will mend anew—  
  Its strength reborn from all it has been through.

386

My soul, a garden once in gentle bloom,  
Now bears the frost of trust betrayed by night,  
Where petals curled succumb to silent gloom,  
And shadows feast where once was golden light.  
The trellis vines that twined with hope’s embrace  
Hang heavy, burdened by the weight of grief;

Is lost amidst the withered autumn leaf.  
Yet still beneath the frozen, bitter earth  
A seed of longing waits to rise anew,  
Dreaming of sunlight, warmth, and gentle mirth,  
And rain to cleanse the soil with crystal dew.  
  For though betrayed, the heart dares to forgive—  
  In spring’s sweet promise, gardens learn to live.

387

My soul, a fortress breached by whispered lies,  
Stands ruined where your shadow softly fell,  
The moon observes the darkness in my eyes,  
And weaves its silver threads about my cell.  
Each echo of your voice invades my sleep,  
A gentle thief that plunders peace and time,  
While memories like faithful phantoms creep  
Through haunted halls, in cadence and in rhyme.  
I search for dawn beyond the splintered gate,  
Yet night with velvet hands restrains my plea,  
Desire and grief entwine and orchestrate  
A silent dirge composed for you and me.  
  Yet from these ruins hope may rise anew—  
  A fortress built of dreams restored by you.

388

My soul, a lantern dimmed by faithless flame,  
Still flickers hope despite the night you bring.  
Your shadowed words, though etched with bitter blame,  
Cannot suppress the voice that longs to sing.  
The silent hours weigh heavy on my chest,  
Yet dawn’s pale promise glimmers in the gloom.  
Though love lay dormant, never fully rest,  
It stirs in embers swept about the room.  
Your absence carves a hollow in the day,  
A vacant throne where laughter once held court.  
Yet even voids can shape the light’s new way,  
And emptiness may be its own resort.  
  So let me bear the dark for what’s to be—  
  The morning waits beyond your memory.

389

My soul’s a garden razed by winter’s hand,  
Where seeds of trust lay buried in the frost,  
The branches ache beneath a silent band  
Of snow, remembering the warmth they lost.  
Yet in the hush, a stubborn hope remains,  
A root that clings beneath the frozen ground;  
It drinks the memory of gentle rains  
And dreams of sun in silence so profound.  
Though tempests rend the petals from their place  
And shadows prowl where once the jasmine grew,  
Still time’s own breath may gentle and embrace  
These battered beds, and grant the earth its due.  
  For even in the coldest, darkest hours,  
  The soul recalls the promise of its flowers.

390

Within my chest a serpent softly coils,  
Its venom whispers trust was but a lie,  
It winds about my heart in secret toils,  
And stains the dawn with shadows as they fly.  
Each pulse betrays a hope I dare not show,  
Yet memory clings like dew upon the thorn;  
The silent ache that only I may know  
Blooms nightly, where my innocence was torn.  
O treacherous guest, why linger in my breast,  
When gentle dreams would bid thee to depart?  
Thou feed’st upon the tender and the blest,  
And choke the budding promise of my heart.  
  Yet from this pain, some wisdom may be spun—  
  A soul grown wise from all that is undone.

391

Within my chest, a serpent coils unseen,  
Its whispered lies unravel who I've been.  
Each gentle beat becomes a sharpened keen,  
And doubts like thorns press cruelly from within.  
The shadows stretch along my fragile mind,  
Their sable fingers weaving webs of fear,  
In silent hours, solace hard to find,  
While venom drips and stains the hope held dear.  
Yet in the gloom, a spark begins to grow,  
Defiant in the serpent's jealous gaze;  
A stubborn flame that will not cease to glow,  
It dares to challenge darkness with its blaze.  
  Though serpents dwell where trembling hearts reside,  
  Still, light survives—unyielding, undenied.

392

Within my chest, a serpent coils in trust,  
Its silver tongue now hisses in my mind,  
It weaves through veins with whispers dark and just,  
A secret that my soul has not defined.  
It flickers soft behind my quiet breath,  
A promise made in shadows, sharp and sly,  
Yet bids me taste the fruit of subtle death,  
And tempts my heart to question every lie.  
Its scales reflect the moon’s uncertain gleam,  
A glimmer caught in webs of old desire,  
It haunts the threshold of each haunted dream,  
And kindles thought, a cold, forbidden fire.  
  Though coiled within, its power I confess—  
  The serpent’s truth, my burden and my guest.

393

I wore your promise like a borrowed skin,  
Yet found it shedding truths I feared within.  
Each vow a shadow brushing on my heart,  
A silken thread unraveling apart.  
Your words, once golden, now grow faint and thin,  
Their radiance dulled by doubts that softly start.  
The hope I clung to whispers frail and slight,  
A candle trembling in the hush of night.  
I sought your gaze for shelter from the storm,  
But saw reflections I could not transform.  
What warmth I claimed has vanished with the day,  
And left me clothed in memories that stay.  
  Yet from this shedding, I may rise anew—  
  A skin grown stronger, having shed for you.

394

My heart, a fortress razed by trust betrayed,  
Now shelters ghosts where once your laughter stayed.  
The sun that bathed our vows in golden light  
Has fled, and left these ramparts cold as stone.  
Where promise bloomed, now shadows breed the night,  
And echo hollow footsteps, mine alone.  
Yet still I pace the corridors of pain,  
Each memory a torch that burns anew,  
Recalling whispered oaths and gentle rain  
That fell like mercy when our love was true.  
But faith, once shattered, cannot be restored  
By tears or time or dreams of yesteryear.  
I mourn the peace our union once implored,  
A citadel undone by hope and fear.  
Yet from these ruins, I will rise and see—  
A heart rebuilt, though scarred, and wholly free.

395

A serpent slept within my open hand,  
Its whispered trust a blade I could not see,  
It coiled in silence, silk upon the sand,  
And pressed its cool devotion close to me.  
I watched its eyes, twin lanterns in the shade,  
Reflect the secrets that the night had spun;  
Their gleaming promise, soft and unafraid,  
Spoke comfort sweet as twilight nearly done.  
Yet trust that slumbers deep is quick to wake,  
And in its stirrings, venom finds its tongue;  
The gentle grip becomes a grasp that takes,  
Unraveling the faith to which I clung.  
  Thus, in the hush where golden lies expand,  
  I learn the cost of holding hope in hand.

396

My heart, a silent witness to your lies,  
Now wears the mask that trust itself denies.  
Each whispered vow that flickered in the dark  
Has faded, leaving only cold remark.  
The laughter spun like gold in summer’s air  
Is tarnished now by doubt’s unyielding hand.  
Where once was faith, a shadow lingers there,  
A garden turned to desolated sand.  
Yet still your eyes, like stars, attempt to plead,  
To mend the rift your falsehoods have unspun.  
I turn away, refusing to concede  
The battles lost in wars that can’t be won.  
  Yet in this silence, hope and sorrow blend:  
  A heart betrayed, uncertain how to mend.

397

When midnight’s trust lay sleeping in your eyes,  
The dawn betrayed with whispers draped in lies,  
A silver hush that trembled on your lips  
Did with the fleeting dark in silence vie.  
The timid stars withdrew in slow eclipse,  
And hope, like dew, dissolved as sunlight dries.  
Yet in the hush where breathless longing slips,  
A promise lingered, veiled in soft disguise.  
What dreams we sowed beneath the secret shade  
Now scatter, petals blown on faithless air;  
Still, shadows linger where your memory played,  
And echoes of your laughter haunt me there.  
  Yet as the night yields to the morning’s gold,  
  In loss, a love endures—unclaimed, yet bold.

398

My trust, a fragile bird, took flight in vain,  
Betrayal’s whisper chills the blood within.  
Once sunlit skies now thunderbolt with rain,  
A tempest churns beneath my trembling skin.  
The honeyed words that lulled my heart to sleep  
Now echo hollow, empty as the night.  
In shadows long, a wounded soul must weep,  
Distrust, a shroud that stifles hope’s faint light.  
Yet still, amid the ruins of my grace,  
A stubborn spark ignites against the gloom.  
I search for kindness in a stranger’s face,  
And coax new blossoms where old sorrows loom.  
  For though deceit may bruise, it shan’t define—  
  From broken faith, a stronger heart is mine.

399

The midnight wind confides your secret sin,  
While trust, once bright, now shivers in the cold.  
Upon your lips, repentant shadows spin  
And whisper tales that never should be told.  
The moon observes, her silver gaze austere,  
As guilt’s pale ghost attends your every breath.  
The silence gathers all you dread to hear,  
And weaves a shroud of doubt as close as death.  
In corridors where memory takes flight,  
Regret and longing dance a weary round.  
The hours stretch thin beneath the waning light,  
Where hope is lost and solace seldom found.  
  Yet, in the dark, forgiveness waits unseen—  
  A dawn to cleanse the night of what has been.

400

The night betrays me with your silent eyes,  
While hope, a liar, whispers gentle lies.  
The moon conceals her face behind the shroud  
Of drifting clouds that linger, pale and proud.  
Each shadow stretches long across the floor,  
A memory of footsteps lost before.  
The hush of midnight presses on my skin,  
Recalling laughter where your voice has been.  
Yet time, relentless, dulls what once was bright,  
And scatters dreams like stars beyond my sight.  
The sorrow lingers, cold upon my chest,  
As longing steals the solace of my rest.  
  Still, in the dark, your presence softly stays—  
  A ghost of love that haunts my waking days.

401

When Trust, once bright, now hides behind a mask,  
And Hope, betrayed, recoils from your embrace,

What shadows crept to steal its sacred place.  
The gentle words that used to calm the storm  
Are echoes lost in corridors of doubt,  
Affection’s fire no longer keeps me warm,  
Its fading embers flicker, nearly out.

In search of light that once adorned your eyes,  
And strain to hear amid the silence calls

  But time, relentless, teaches love to see—  
  That trust, once gone, returns most warily.

402

My soul, a silent witness to your lies,

The rifts where once our whispered dreams were told.  
Night’s hush reveals the secrets daylight hides,

  Yet through the ache, my soul endures its part,  
  And learns the quiet courage of the heart.

  A gentler dawn shall rise and rule again.

404

My soul, a silent witness to the crime,

  Though wrong’s cold hand would turn all trust to stone,

405

My soul, betrayed, now wanders through the dark,  
While hope, once bright, now hides its wounded face,  
The silent night extinguishes its spark,  
And shadows weave despair in every place.

Like autumn leaves abandoned to the wind,

Reminds me of the trust I once had pinned.

It gathers strength from sorrow, doubt, and tears,  
And yearns to guide me back toward the light.  
  Though faith is frail and wearied by the storm,  
  Within my heart, hope’s ember shall stay warm.

406

My soul, betrayed, now wanders through the night,  
A shadow mourning love that will not wake,  
The moon’s pale beams reveal my silent plight,  
Each step I take, my heart begins to break.  
The whispered winds recall what once was said,  
In secret vows beneath the trembling stars;

And sorrow’s touch reopens faded scars.  
Yet time moves on, indifferent to my pain,  
The dawn approaches, cold and unforgiving,

  Yet in this darkness, I remain undone,  
  A lover lost before the rising sun.

407

My soul, deceived by hope, now walks alone,

  Though hope may falter, yet I journey on,  
  For dawn may break when darkest night is gone.

408

Betrayal wears my shadow like a cloak,  
And whispers secrets only I should know.  
It steals my trust with every word it spoke,  
And plants in fertile doubt the seeds of woe.  
A friend once dear now haunts the edge of night,  
His laughter echoes hollow in my chest.  
Where candor bloomed now grows a withered blight,  
And loyalty is stripped, its roots unblessed.

From ashes of deceit I rise anew.  
Though promises are scattered, lost, and torn,  
My heart learns strength from what it must pass through.  
  So let the shadows stretch and secrets fall—  
  I step into the light, outshining all.

409

Tonight my trust, a wounded beast, retreats,  
Its silent howl echoing through my chest,  
The shadowed corners where suspicion meets  
The flicker of a faith once manifest.  
In moonlit hush, my soul recounts the cost  
Of broken vows and promises betrayed,

That haunts the fragile bonds we once had laid.  
Yet still, beneath the ruins of despair,

Might coax the beast to trust in morning’s light.  
  For though the wounds of doubt are slow to heal,  
  Love’s patient hand can teach the heart to feel.

410

When Trust, a fragile bird, forsakes my hand,  
It weeps within the cage my heart has spun,

Afraid to greet the rising of the sun.

A mournful hush attends each silent breath;

Betrayal wears my shadow like a cloak,

412

Pursued by memory’s scent, forlorn and just.

  O, heart ensnared by loyalty so blind,

Betrayal wears my shadow like a mask,

Divides itself between the truth and lie,  
Where shadows flicker, silent voices call,  
And hopes like tattered banners faintly fly.  
The golden days, now whispered in the dust,  
Are swept away by winds of cold regret;  
Each stone of faith eroded by mistrust,  
Each dream surrendered, longing to forget.  
Yet in the ruins, fragile seeds remain,  
A memory of sunlight in the rain.  
Though haunted halls echo with ancient pain,  
A gentle promise stirs beneath the strain.  
  From brokenness, a quiet strength may start—  
  New walls are built with mercy in the heart.

422

My soul, once fortress, now a breach concealed,  
By trust betrayed, yet loyalty revealed.  
The walls I raised with care now stand in vain,  
Their ramparts crumbled by a whispered lie.  
Within these ruins echo silent pain,  
Yet hope persists where bitter shadows lie.  
The dawn that breaks upon my weary gaze  
Unfolds a promise forged by trembling hands;  
Though faith lies wounded, scattered through the haze,  
Its embers glow beneath the ashen lands.  
Time’s gentle balm may mend the riven seam,  
Restoring strength where doubt and sorrow dwell,  
And teach the heart to trust its trembling dream,  
To rise anew, though once in darkness fell.  
For from betrayal’s night new dawn is drawn,  
And loyalty, though tried, endures the dawn.

423

My soul, once bright, now shivers in the shade,  
Where trust was sown, betrayal’s roots have grown.  
The golden dawn that hope and love once made  
Is veiled by clouds of sorrow, cold and lone.  
A whisper lost beneath the willow’s weep,  
A promise broken by a careless hand,  
In midnight hours, I wander, robbed of sleep,  
A castaway upon a faithless strand.  
Yet in the gloom, a trembling ember lies,  
A memory that flickers, faint but true;  
It paints forgiveness in my weary eyes,  
And dares my heart to dream of light anew.  
  Though shadows press, I rise from what remains—  
  For dawn is born from night’s most bitter pains.

424

My soul once soared where hope and longing meet,  
Now fractured, cold, beneath your absent gaze,  
The golden dawn retreats on silent feet,  
And shadows gather in these hollow days.  
Your laughter, once the sun upon my skin,  
Now lingers faint as ghosts at eventide;  
I search for warmth, but winter seeps within,  
A frost no gentle promise can deride.  
Yet still my heart, defiant in its ache,  
Persists to dream where memory ignites—  
A fragile flame no storm of grief can break,  
That kindles day from sorrow’s longest nights.  
  Though love now falters, wounded, yet I see  
  Its echo echoing eternally.

425

Within my soul, affection fights with pain,  
Where love once bloomed, now bitterness remains.  
The gentle dawn is shadowed by the rain,  
As hope retreats, imprisoned by its chains.  
Sweet memories drift by on silent air,  
Their golden hues now tinged with sorrow’s gray.  
I search for comfort, finding none is there,  
And watch the joy of yesteryears decay.  
Yet in this storm, a flicker still survives,  
A stubborn spark that will not be denied.  
For though my heart in loneliness contrives,  
It beats with dreams that will not be defied.  
  From ache and longing, strength is often spun:  
  The soul remembers love when hope is done.

426

I loved the hand that set my soul aflame,  
Yet now that touch delivers only cold.  
What once was joy is now a whispered name  
That haunts the silent corridors of old.  
The warmth we built dissolves within the mist,  
A fleeting echo lost to changing skies;  
Sweet laughter’s ghost, in memory, persists,  
Yet sorrow lingers where the future lies.  
The gentle dawns we shared are overcast,  
Their golden promise darkened by regret;  
The dreams we held have faded into past,  
Like distant stars the morning makes forget.  
  Yet in my heart, your ember’s ash remains—  
  A love that sings in silence through my veins.

427

Within myself, a war of trust and doubt,  
A friend’s soft voice now echoes sharp and cold,  
Once warmth and laughter wrapped my heart about,  
Now brittle words and silences take hold.  
The garden of our closeness, choked with weeds,  
Where once the sun through gentle branches played,  
Now shadow falls, and every promise bleeds  
Upon the ground where truest hopes are laid.  
Yet memory’s flame will flutter in the dark,  
A trembling light that will not be denied,  
It flickers still, a solitary spark,  
Though storms of anger threaten from inside.  
  If faith survives this night, the dawn may show  
  A bond renewed from all that we let go.

428

Within my chest, a war of trust and doubt,  
My heart betrays the truth my mind defends,  
A tempest stirs, unspoken and devout,  
Where reason’s wall against belief contends.  
The shadows stretch and whisper in the gloom,  
While hope’s frail lantern flickers in the night;  
Uncertain footsteps pace around the room,  
Desire and fear wage battles out of sight.  
Each promise made is weighed with trembling care,  
Suspicion’s seed takes root in fertile thought,  
Yet longing presses on through thick despair,  
And seeks the peace that certainty has brought.  
  O, faith and reason, ever intertwined,  
  Grant me the strength to reconcile my mind.

429

Within my heart, a war of trust and doubt,  
Your gentle words concealed a sharpened blade.  
I searched for truths your silence kept without,  
Yet found in shadows honesty decayed.  
The moonlight paints your face with borrowed peace,  
But in your smile, I sense the tempest near.  
Each promise spoken bids suspicion cease,  
Yet whispers linger, echoing in fear.  
The garden where our golden vows were sown  
Now blooms with questions tangled in the vine.  
I reap unease from seeds I should have known,  
And taste the bitter fruit of love’s design.  
  Yet still I yearn, though reason bids me flee,  
  For faith in you wars hard with memory.

430

Within my chest, a hope and shadow dwell,  
Your faithless kiss both curse and fleeting cure,  
A silent bell that tolls a secret knell,  
Yet draws me near with longing to endure.  
I wander through the garden of your eyes,  
Where petals close against the dawn’s regret,  
Each glance a sun that sets but never dies,  
Each word a rain that bids my heart forget.  
You sowed your promises in borrowed spring,  
And reaped the silence of a summer’s end;  
I wear the ache that autumn’s tempests bring,  
Yet in the frost, I dream you might amend.  
  Though night may claim the warmth we used to share,  
  Within my chest, your ghost remains, still fair.

431

When trust, once gold, is tarnished into rust,  
The heart learns hope and heartbreak intertwine,  
Fair promises dissolve in heedless dust,  
Yet still we seek the tender, frail design.  
A glance betrays what careful words conceal,  
While shadows stretch across the lover’s vow.  
What once was firm begins to softly reel,  
And doubt takes root where faith allowed its bough.  
Forgiveness stands upon a trembling stage,  
Uncertain if the curtain will descend,  
While memory, both solace and a cage,  
Reminds the soul how easily hearts bend.  
  Yet from the ashes, trust may rise anew—  
  For love endures what time and trial undo.

432

Thou serpent hid within a flower’s embrace,  
Didst strike my trust whilst smiling in mine eyes,  
Fair-seeming mask upon thy venom’d face,  
Thy dulcet words but cloak’d in shrouded lies.  
Soft petals curl’d about thy poison’d tongue,  
With honey’d speech thou didst my soul beguile,  
Whilst in mine heart the seeds of doubt were flung,  
And love lay bleeding, slain by subtle guile.  
Yet still the rose retains its crimson hue,  
Though thorns have drawn the weeping of my skin,  
Remembrance lingers in the morning dew  
Of what was lost when innocence gave in.  
  For serpents thrive where sweetest blooms appear,  
  And trust once broken, ne’er again grows clear.

433

Thou serpent fair, enwreathed about my heart,  
Thy honeyed tongue hath made my soul depart.  
With eyes that gleam as moonlight on the wave,  
Thou draw’st me in, thy willing, witless slave.  
A silver coil, thy whispered promise sings  
Of velvet nights and cool, beguiling spring.  
Yet venom hides in every tender smile,  
And tempests gather, masked by gentle guile.  
Thy beauty’s art a web of sweet deceit,  
Where hope and reason falter, both defeat.  
I drink thy draught and dream of paradise,  
Unmindful of the poison in the spice.  
  O cruel enchantment, let thy bonds be broke,  
  Or else I perish on the lies I spoke.

434

Thou art the serpent coiled within my breast,  
Whose honeyed tongue hath soured all my trust.  
With every whispered vow, my soul’s oppressed,  
And golden dreams lie trampled in the dust.  
Thy eyes, twin lanterns flick’ring in the night,  
Did lead me through the labyrinths of woe,  
Their seeming warmth concealed the chill of spite,  
A fire veiled in winter’s brittle snow.  
Yet still I cling to memories once sweet,  
Though venom drips from every word we share;  
The echo of thy laughter in retreat  
Reminds me love is often marked by care.  
  And though thy poison stains the love I knew,  
  My heart, bewitched, cannot uncoil from you.

435

Thou art the serpent coiled within my breast,  
Thy honeyed tongue hath turned my heart to stone;  
Each whispered word, a venomous bequest,  
Invades my dreams and claims my soul as throne.  
Thine eyes, twin torches burning in the dark,  
Illuminate the shadows of my mind;  
With every glance I bear thy secret mark—  
A subtle wound that reason cannot bind.  
Yet still I crave the poison of thy kiss,  
And yearn to taste the apples of thy sin;  
I barter peace for moments fraught with bliss,  
Though knowing well the war I wage within.  
  So bound am I to thee, my sweet undoer,  
  That love and ruin grow the more unsure.

436

Thou art the serpent coiled within my breast,  
Whose honeyed tongue did promise love, then fled.  
Thy whispered vows, once gentle, brought me rest,  
Now shed like skin, remembered words now dead.  
In shadows deep, thy memory does creep,  
A silent hiss that haunts my waking mind.  
I nurse the wound, though time forbade to weep,  
Yet still thy poison in my veins I find.  
What once was Eden now is thorn and stone,  
A garden overgrown with grief and rue.  
I wander paths where once we walked alone,  
Each blossom marked by what I lost with you.  
  Yet though thy venom works its subtle art,  
  Still beats thy absence fiercely in my heart.

437

Thou art a serpent coiled within my breast,  
Whose venom’d kiss betrays my soul to grief;  
Thy silver tongue with honeyed words hath pressed  
Upon my heart, and robbed me of belief.  
Thy shadow slithers through my waking mind,  
A whisper twined with promises and lies.  
The warmth I sought in thee I cannot find,  
For every glance conceals what truth belies.  
Yet still I clutch thee, trembling, in my arms,  
Though reason bids my foolish hands to flee;  
Enchanted by thy ever-changing charms,  
I yield my will and bind myself to thee.  
  O cruel delight, thy poison I embrace,  
  For love and ruin wear the selfsame face.

438

My soul, a fortress breached by traitor's art,  
Hath bled beneath the serpent's whispered jest,  
The shadows coil and slither 'round my heart,  
Their venom bidding hope's bright pulse to rest.  
What once was trust, now lies in shards of glass,  
A garden razed by hands I called my kin,  
The sweetest fruit now bitter as it was,  
A harvest sown by falsehood deep within.  
Yet in the dusk, a stubborn flame persists,  
Defiant 'gainst the night that seeks to reign;  
Though wounds may throb beneath the traitor's kiss,  
Resilience in sorrow I retain.  
  For from this breach, my spirit shall arise,  
  And build anew beneath unclouded skies.

439

My soul, a fortress stormed by faithless hands,  
Now lies in ruin, salt upon the scarred sands.  
Once proud, its banners flew in golden light,  
Defiant ramparts shining through the night.  
But whispered doubts like ivy crept within,  
And jealous winds conspired to wear me thin.  
Each vow betrayed became a toppled stone,  
Each promise broken left me more alone.  
Yet in this wreckage, wildflowers arise,  
Their fragile petals bright beneath dark skies.  
From shattered arches, hope begins to sing,  
A trembling lark that waits the dawn of spring.  
  Though walls may crumble, spirit dares to grow—  
  From dust and grief, new strength the heart shall know.

440

Within my breast a serpent coils unseen,  
Its venom seeping slow through trust once bright,  
It winds around the hope where joy had been,  
And whispers doubts that creep upon the night.  
A silver tongue it slides between my dreams,  
Distilling fears with every silent kiss,  
Each promise wilted, torn apart at seams,  
Replaced by memories of gentler bliss.  
Yet still I bear its winding, cold embrace,  
And search the dusk for what I once believed;  
Though poison weaves its shadows through my grace,  
The heart resists, unwilling to be grieved.  
  For though this serpent feeds on love’s decay,  
  I guard a spark it cannot steal away.

441

My soul, a ship, was moored in trustful bay,  
Yet tempests brewed within thy silent eyes;  
Thy glance, a gale, swept all my peace away,  
And turned sweet harbor into stormy skies.  
I braved the surge, yet found no guiding star,  
For hope’s faint lantern flickered in the mist.  
Each whispered promise vanished from afar,  
By winds of doubt and longing still unkissed.  
The moon, once kind, now hides behind the shroud,  
Its silver beams consumed by brooding night.  
My vessel drifts, by memories endowed,  
Bereft of anchor, wandering from light.  
  Yet though I roam where shadowed waters roll,  
  Thy love’s the compass ever steers my soul.

442

When thou didst steal the daylight from my soul,  
A serpent’s kiss did wound what once was whole,  
The silent hours grew long with bitter night,  
And hope’s fair visage vanished from my sight.  
Thy absence wove a shroud of shadowed fears,  
Where laughter died and left me naught but tears.  
Yet in the darkness, longing found its art,  
A flicker born from ashes of my heart.  
For though thy cruelty did pierce me deep,  
And sowed a barren field where dreams should sleep,  
Still from the thorn there bloomed a crimson rose,  
A stubborn grace that neither fades nor goes.  
  Thus pain, transformed by love’s unyielding fire,  
  Makes loss itself my reason to aspire.

443

My soul, a fortress razed by traitor's hand,  
Lies barren now, where once love's banners flew,  
The echoing silence haunts this broken land,  
And paints the hopeful dawn in ashen hue.  
Where laughter danced in halls of gilded light,  
Now shadowed corridors conceal my woe;  
The stars that crowned our vows have fled the night,  
And left me wandering where cold winds blow.  
Yet from these stones where ruin claims its prize,  
A stubborn root clings tight to shattered earth;  
In grief’s dark soil, a quiet promise lies—  
That spring may yet return to grant rebirth.  
  Though trust be slain and dreams in dust are cast,  
  Still hearts rebuild, and rise from pain at last.

444

Within this breast, a serpent coils and waits,  
Its venom born of trust that turned to bane,  
It slithers silent through the shadowed gates,  
And fills the hollow heart with subtle pain.  
Its tongue recalls the honeyed words once sweet,  
Now fangs that pierce the memory with scorn.  
Where laughter dwelled, now bitterness repeats,  
A garden choked with weeds, and hope forsworn.  
Yet still I nurse the creature’s wicked art,  
And feed it with the fruits of broken days,  
For wounds unhealed become the beating heart,  
A drum that marks betrayal’s endless phase.  
  But if the serpent sleeps, may dawn restore  
  A breast unbound, and trust to bloom once more.

445

A serpent’s kiss did poison once my trust,  
And left my soul a garden choked with weeds;  
The golden boughs all blackened into dust,  
Sweet fruits replaced by bitterness of seeds.  
Beneath pale moon I wander, lost and lone,  
My heart a graveyard for forsaken dreams;  
The gentle sun, once warm, now chills my bone,  
While hope dissolves in sorrow’s silent streams.  
Yet time, relentless, tills the ruined earth,  
And rain bestows her tears on barren ground;  
From pain and loss, new shoots are given birth—  
A fragile green where faith again is found.  
  Thus from the venom, healing’s roots arise;  
  The garden blooms anew beneath clear skies.

446

Within my breast a serpent coils in wait,  
Its venom drips, betraying trust to pain,  
With every breath it stirs the threads of fate,  
And whispers doubts that echo in my brain.  
It winds around the secrets that I keep,  
Its tongue a forked reminder of my fears,  
It haunts the fragile borderlands of sleep,  
And stains the dawn with silent, salted tears.  
Yet in its eyes I sense a wilder fire,  
A spark of knowledge twined with agony,  
For suffering may teach and thus inspire,  
And forge from wounds a deeper clarity.  
  So let the serpent slumber in my breast,  
  Its bite may harm, but wisdom is its guest.

447

Thou art the serpent coiled within my breast,  
Whose venom’d kiss hath robbed my soul of rest.  
Thy silver tongue doth hiss with sugared art,  
And in thy gaze, a thousand tempests start.  
Thou wind’st about my thoughts with subtle grace,  
Each whispered promise binds me ever near,  
Yet leave’st a shadow lingering on my face,  
A haunting echo only I can hear.  
I drink thy poison, sweet as summer’s wine,  
And in the draught, forget what once was pure;  
For every wound, I swear the fault is mine,  
And bear the scars that only love procure.  
  Though bound by thee, I dare not break thy spell—  
  For in thy arms, I make my private hell.

448

A serpent wrapped in velvet words did lie,  
And left my honest heart to bleed and pine;  
Its silver tongue did gild the midnight sky,  
Yet poisoned well the sweetest vintage wine.  
I trusted in the honey of its speech,  
Believed the promises that softly fell,  
But found the fruit of longing out of reach,  
And wandered lost within a gilded hell.  
The mask it wore shone bright with borrowed light,  
Concealing fangs beneath a gentle smile;  
I danced with shadows deep into the night,  
Bewitched and blinded by its cunning wile.  
  Yet let this wound be lesson to my soul:  
  Not every shining word can make one whole.

449

A serpent’s tongue did pierce my trusting breast,  
Whilst honeyed words concealed a viper’s art,  
Her silken voice did soothe each old unrest,  
Yet venom pooled beneath her gentle heart.  
She danced in shadows, cloaked in golden light,  
With laughter sweet as summer’s blooming rose;  
But whispers flickered, born of hidden spite,  
And thorns lay tangled where affection grows.  
Her smiles, like moonbeams, masked the midnight rain,  
Her promises dissolved in morning dew;  
I wore her love as chains of silken pain,  
And found my faith betrayed and born anew.  
  Yet from this wound, a wiser soul takes wing—  
  Beware the song that softer serpents sing.

450

O thou, whose tongue did kiss me with deceit,  
Dost Night herself in shadowed silence teach.  
Upon thy lips both honeyed words and sweet  
Did twist to poison, ever out of reach.  
Thy gaze, once gentle, now a veiled disguise,  
Conceals the tempest brewing in thine eyes;  
Yet still my heart, though warned by reason wise,  
Returns to thee, where all my solace lies.  
The moon observes our dance with silver gleam,  
Her witness cold to promises betrayed;  
While stars, indifferent, scatter every dream  
That in thy arms I foolishly have laid.  
  Yet though betrayed, my soul remains unfree—  
  For even falsehood binds my love to thee.

451

Thou serpent, Love, in borrowed visage fair,  
Didst whisper poison’d trust unto mine ear,  
With honey’d words that wove deceitful care,  
And masked thy fangs with glimmering, gentle cheer.  
Thy silken coils did wrap about my heart,  
Constraining hope with soft and subtle art;  
Yet in thy gaze, a thousand daggers start,  
And peace is rent, as faith and doubt depart.  
I drank thy promise, sweet as summer rain,  
Unknowing of the storm that brewed beneath,  
And found thy pleasures shadowed all by pain,  
Thy roses hiding ever-thorny wreath.  
  Yet here I stand, enthrall’d by cruel delight—  
  A willing captive to thy endless night.

452

When treachery, in silken guise, did creep,  
And Night herself did learn to feign her tears,  
The stars, in trembling watch, forgot to sleep,  
And whispered counsel to the doubting spheres.  
A serpent’s tongue did twine around the trust  
That once had blossomed in the garden fair,  
Now petals wilted, stifled by the dust,  
And hope hung heavy in the scented air.  
Yet in the hush, where broken promise lies,  
A single rose defied the poison’s art—  
Its crimson bloom an oath that never dies,  
An emblem of the undiminished heart.  
  For though deceit may shadow every dawn,  
  True faith endures when falsehood’s mask is gone.

453

Thou Falsehood, dressed in thy beloved's guise,  
Hast kissed my trust and fed it secret lies.  
Thy honey’d words, like velvet on the air,  
Conceal the serpent lurking in thy eyes.  
Thy promises, like morning dew, seem fair,  
Yet vanish swift beneath the noonday sun.  
I built my hope upon thy tender care,  
Unknowing all thy gentle work undone.  
Like midnight shadows dancing on the wall,  
Thy whispers paint illusions in my mind.  
Too late I see the mask begin to fall  
And false devotion left with truth behind.  
  Yet from this wound, a wiser heart I gain—  
  For love once fooled shall not be fooled again.

454

O treachery, thy serpent tongue hath stung,  
For thou, my love, didst court false Hope with glee;  
Thy honeyed words upon my spirit hung,  
Yet veiled beneath thy gaze, deceit I see.  
Sweet promises like blossoms in the spring  
Were plucked and scattered by thy wanton hand,  
The vows thou gav’st were but a borrowed ring,  
A fleeting mark upon the shifting sand.  
Didst thou not swear that constancy was thine,  
That fortune’s winds would ne’er our course divide?  
Yet now I dwell where bitter shadows pine,  
Bereft of truth, my heart’s last faith denied.  
  O, may thy conscience, restless, haunt thy sleep,  
  Whilst I in silence, love’s cold harvest reap.

455

False Love, thou serpent masked in Beauty’s bloom,  
Didst plant thy poison deep within my breast,  
With honeyed words thou didst my heart entomb,  
And in thy promise, gave my soul no rest.  
Thy glances, gentle as the morning dew,  
Concealed the fangs that waited in disguise;  
I drank thy sweetness, thinking all was true,  
Yet bitter truth lay hidden in thine eyes.  
Thy lips professed a passion pure and bright,  
Yet shadows crept where sunlight should have been;  
The vows thou whispered vanished with the night,  
And left me haunted by what might have been.  
  O False Love, from thy roses thorns did rise—  
  A lesson writ in tears, not paradise.

456

My soul, that once did court the truth in light,  
Now writhes, betwixt false shadows and despair,  
The sun that crowned my brow has fled the night,  
And left me haunted by the empty air.  
Where once the silver hope did softly gleam,  
A sable mist now veils my weary eyes;  
I chase the hollow echoes of a dream  
And grasp at phantoms cloaked in whispered lies.  
Yet in this murk, a stubborn ember glows,  
A promise that the dawn is not yet slain;  
For though the thorns of sorrow round me close,  
Some seed of comfort nestles in the pain.  
  Thus, battered by the tempests of the mind,  
  I seek the light my soul was born to find.

457

O treach’rous soul, thou serpent in my breast,  
Thou feign’st thy peace whilst plotting my unrest,  
In silken words my wary thoughts are dressed,  
Yet daggers hide beneath thy softest jest.  
Thy whispers curl like smoke about my mind,  
A subtle venom seeps in every sigh,  
What honeyed promise do I hope to find  
When truth behind thy gentle mask doth lie?  
Each glance thou cast’st is edged in secret spite,  
A shadow dancing in the noon of trust,  
Within thy gaze, the spark of furtive night  
Consumes the bond that love had forged from dust.  
  Yet though betrayed by thee, my heart withstands,  
  For strength is born when fate divides our hands.

458

O treacherous Soul, thou serpent in my breast,  
Thou lead’st me smiling to a thorn-clad doom,  
In honeyed words my wary thoughts dost rest,  
Yet plantest silent seeds of subtle gloom.  
Where once I strove with purpose, clear and bright,  
Thy whispers weave a mist upon my mind,  
And turn my morning gold to ashen night,  
Convincing hope its home I shall not find.  
With every promise, thou dost softly bind  
My heart in fetters forged of my desire,  
And feed the fevered shadows, so unkind,  
That set my dreams and waking hours afire.  
  Yet still I rise, and, knowing all thy guile,  
  Defy thee with my truth, and dare to smile.

459

O falseest soul, why dost thou court my trust,  
When love’s pale ghost in silent shadow fled?  
Thy honeyed words are veiled with cunning dust,  
Yet bitter thorns do line the path we tread.  
Thine eyes, once lamps that lit my midnight skies,  
Now flicker cold with secrets never told;  
For every vow that trembles from thy lies  
Is forged in frost and counterfeit as gold.  
I gave thee faith—a garden sweet and wide—  
Thou sowed’st the seeds of doubt in gentle ground;  
Where once devotion’s blooming rose did bide,  
Now weeds of falsehood choke the roots unbound.  
  Yet, though betrayed, my foolish heart remains—  
  Still seeks the sun through treacherous, sweet rains.

460

O treacherous soul, why dost thou shift thy face,  
When love’s sweet plea falls dead upon thine ear?  
What secret shadows linger in this space,  
Where once thy vows did warm and draw me near?  
Hast thou forgotten all our whispered dreams,  
Those tender hours beneath the silver moon?  
Now silence chills the hope that ever gleams,  
And time betrays the heart betrayed too soon.  
Yet in thy glance, a fleeting sorrow shows,  
A ghost of truth behind thy guarded eyes;  
Would that my words could soften all thy woes,  
Or bid thy spirit cease from wearing guise.  
  Yet love, though wounded, lingers in its pain:  
  I mourn thy loss, yet would not love in vain.

461

O treach’rous Soul, thou serpent in my breast,  
Dost woo my heart and scorn my peace to keep,  
With honeyed words my wary mind dost wrest,  
And plant false dreams within the fields of sleep.  
Thou dost betray me with a lover’s art,  
Inviting hope, then dashing it with guile;  
Thy whispers, gentle poison to my heart,  
Do lull my reason, beguile me the while.  
Yet though I know thy tricks and shadowed ways,  
Still am I drawn to trust each subtle plea,  
Entranced by flattery’s insistent praise,  
And blind to lies that turn and govern me.  
  O soul, my foe and friend, what fate is mine—  
  To live thy captive or at last resign?

462

O treachery, thou serpent in my breast,  
With poisoned tongue dost whisper me unrest,  
Thy hissing words deny me gentle rest,  
And fill my mind with shadows unconfessed.  
Thou changeling friend, thou mask of honeyed lies,  
Who weav’st thy snare in moments dark and fair,  
I know thee by the flicker in thine eyes,  
By furtive glance and soft, beguiling air.  
Yet still I clasp thee close, as lovers do,  
Mistaking venom for a lover’s grace,  
And drink the draught that thou alone dost brew,  
Though bitter tears may stain my trusting face.  
  O treachery, thy chains are forged so fine—  
  Yet in their hold, this captive heart is mine.

463

When Trust, that subtle sprite, did steal my breath,  
She left my soul to lie in bitter shade.  
Her whispered vows, once shield against all death,  
Now echo hollow where my hopes are laid.  
A golden thread I clutched with blinded will,  
Unravelled swift by hands I thought were true;  
The morning’s promise, gentle, bright, and still,  
Did fade to dusk and sorrow’s deeper hue.  
Yet in that dusk, a lesson softly grew:  
That hearts, though bruised, may find their pulse anew,  
And though the night does shroud the honest view,  
The dawn reveals what shadows cannot rue.  
  For trust, once lost, may seed a wiser spring,  
  And teach the soul what faith and time may bring.

464

When Trust, that gilded wraith, did pierce my breast,  
Her honeyed tongue bespoke a viper’s jest.  
She clothed her lies in silks of golden thread,  
And played sweet melodies to mask unrest.  
Her footsteps echoed softly where I bled,  
While in her eyes, a serpent’s promise slept.  
She crowned my hopes, then left them cold and dead,  
And from my dreams, the seeds of sorrow reapt.  
Yet in the shadowed garden of regret,  
I find her whispers tangled in the dew;  
The bloom of faith, though battered, lingers yet,  
And dares to seek the sun with roots anew.  
  So let her ghost depart—my soul forgives,  
  For in love’s ashes, hope defiant lives.

465

O treachery, thou serpent in my breast,  
With venom'd tongue, my hope thou didst arrest.  
Thy silken words, in shadows deftly dressed,  
Did sow fair trust, then render me oppressed.  
Like winter's frost upon the budding rose,  
Thou crept unseen, and chilled my gentle heart;  
In friendship's guise, thy lurking malice grows,  
To pierce with icy fangs love's tender part.  
Yet from thy bite, a wiser soul I gain,  
For scars remember what the eyes forget;  
I rise anew, though marked by secret pain,  
Resolved that truth shall triumph over threat.  
  Though serpents slither close with hearts untrue,  
  My spirit stands, and learns to start anew.

466

False Friendship’s dagger, sharpened by thy smile,  
Hath carved my trust and left me lone, reviled.  
The honeyed words thou spokest all the while  
In secret bitter roots of harm compiled.  
Thy laughter, sweet as April’s gentle rain,  
Concealed the storm that brewed behind thine eyes;  
A mask of warmth to hide thy cold disdain,  
A sunlit veil o’er ever-darkening skies.  
Yet I, beguiled, did clasp thee to my breast,  
Blind to the serpent coiled within thy art,  
Till venom dripped and truth was manifest,  
Its poison wrung the daylight from my heart.  
  Now wiser grown, I mourn what was betrayed—  
  Trust’s fragile bloom by treachery decayed.

467

O treacherous Heart, thou serpent in my breast,  
Thou feign’st a friend yet wound’st me like the rest.  
With honeyed words thy cruel intent is dressed,  
And in thy chamber, peace is dispossessed.  
I court thee softly, hoping thou art true,  
But find thy oath but vapor on the air.  
Thy fickle pulse doth ever misconstrue  
The loyal vows I whisper in despair.  
Yet still I serve thee, captive and undone,  
A willing thrall within thy tangled snare.  
For though thy winter chills me from the sun,  
I cling to shadows, haunted by thy care.  
  O Heart, be kind; let mercy now impart—  
  Undo the venom sleeping in my heart.

468

Thou honey-tonguèd serpent, sweet and vile,  
Didst kiss with warmth, yet froze my trust with guile;  
Thy velvet words, arrayed in gentle style,  
Conceal a dagger’s edge beneath thy smile.  
Thine eyes, twin lanterns in the treacherous dark,  
Did draw me close with promises so fair,  
Yet left upon my soul a scalding mark  
And made my heart a castle of despair.  
What cruel delight to tangle love with lies,  
To pour ambrosia laced with bitter rue!  
Thy beauty masks the venom it belies,  
Thy rose-red lips betray what they pursue.  
  Yet, serpent, know: thy poison cannot stay—  
  My wounded heart shall rise and break the day.

469

Thou art the poison mingled in my wine,  
Yet once my heart proclaimed thy love was pure;  
Thy whispers, sweet as honey, did entwine  
Around my soul, and bade my trust endure.  
But shadows crept where sunlight should have stayed,  
And tempests brewed within thy gentle eyes;  
The vows we spoke beneath the willow swayed,  
Now echo back as hollow, faded lies.  
Yet still, thy visage haunts my nightly rest,  
A phantom wreathed in memory’s embrace;  
Though reason warns, my folly is confessed—  
I seek the warmth that once adorned thy face.  
  So bitter-sweet, this draught I drink for thee:  
  A love that wounds, yet will not set me free.

470

Thou smiling serpent, clothed in angel’s guise,  
Didst pierce my trust whilst swearing faith anew,  
With honeyed words thou masked thy heart of lies,  
And veiled thy venom in a lover’s hue.  
A sunlit glance beguiled my wary mind,  
As shadows wove their secrets through thy speech;  
My loyal hopes, to treachery resigned,  
Lay vanquished, far beyond my hand’s beseech.  
Yet still thy beauty haunts my waking dream,  
And whispers solace where no rest is found;  
I chase the phantom of what once did seem,  
While silent tears fall soft without a sound.  
  O false enchantment, cruelest art thou be—  
  To wound with love, and leave me naught but thee.

471

Thou smiling serpent, masked in honeyed grace,  
Did'st plant cold daggers where thy kisses lay;  
Thy velvet words, like silk, my heart encase,  
Yet weave a shroud that dims the golden day.  
In laughter's lilt thy venom finds its course,  
A gentle hand that draws the shadow near,  
Dost charm the lamb, then lead it to remorse,  
And stain the joy with ever-bleeding fear.  
Oft have I danced within thy tangled eyes,  
Mistaking poison for the sweetest wine;  
I wore thy chains, enchanted by thy lies,  
And called thy withered touch almost divine.  
  Yet from this coil of falsehood I arise,  
  And see thy love a serpent's thin design.

472

Thou art the dawn that promised golden light,  
Yet left me shivering in endless night.  
Thy whispered vows, like dew, did softly gleam,  
Yet vanished swift as shadows flee a dream.  
Thy smile, once bright, now haunts my weary eyes,  
A ghostly ember lost to hope’s demise.  
Where once thy laughter danced upon the air,  
Now silence reigns, and sorrow settles there.  
I traced thy footsteps in the morning dew,  
But found them lost where chilling darkness grew.  
The promise of thy touch, a fleeting flame,  
Is but a memory I cannot name.  
  Yet still I wait, though reason bids me flee,  
  For love, once kind, still binds my soul to thee.

473

When honey'd words from treach'rous lips do fall,  
Yet cruel frost attends what should be spring,  
The sweetest songs are marred by bitter gall,  
And hope is strangled ere it learns to sing.  
A mask of kindness veils a serpent's spite,  
Soft hands conceal the dagger's sharpened gleam;  
What seems to glow with warm and gentle light  
Is but the moon's cold image in a stream.  
O trust not eyes that sparkle false delight,  
Nor chase the shade that mimics morning's beam;  
For fairer faces oft do hearts indict,  
And silken speech may hide a darker scheme.  
  Yet from deceit, let wisdom's fruit be won:  
  To know true gold, one must have touched the dun.

474

Within my breast, a serpent’s whisper dwells,  
Yet outward smiles my tongue with honeyed art;  
In shadowed silence, secret longing swells,  
While painted joy adorns my wary heart.  
The mask I wear is forged of sweet deceit,  
Its golden edge conceals the bitter core;  
Yet in the night, my restless pulses beat  
With dreams that dare to hope for something more.  
The world applauds the laughter on my lips,  
Yet knows not of the tempest in my frame;  
Each gentle word my careful spirit sips,  
Lest careless truth ignite forbidden flame.  
  O, let me shed this borrowed, brittle skin,  
  And show the world the storm I hold within.

475

My soul, once bright, now shrouds itself in night,  
Where trust was sown, the serpent’s tooth I feel,  
The stars bear witness to my silent plight,  
While hope’s faint embers flicker, lost and pale.  
The laughter once that danced upon the breeze  
Is swallowed now by shadows’ cold embrace;  
Beneath the weight of sorrow, knees do seize,  
And joy, like fleeting mist, forsakes this place.  
Yet in the darkness, memory’s gentle spark  
Reminds me love once bloomed where ruin lies;  
I seek the dawn to banish hours so stark,  
And wipe the bitter dew from weary eyes.  
  Though night be long, I’ll trust the coming day—  
  For even broken hearts may find their way.

476

When trust’s bright flame did warm my trembling soul,  
Now icy daggers rend my heart in twain,  
The gentle light that made my spirit whole  
Is swallowed by the shadow’s cold domain.  
Once laughter danced upon the summer air,  
And promises, like blossoms, sweetly grew,  
But now suspicion’s poison lingers there,  
And every word feels heavy, harsh, untrue.  
Yet hope, though battered, will not yield to night,  
It flickers faint within this storm of pain,  
For time may mend what sorrow would indict  
And guide the lost to gentleness again.  
  O trust, return and calm this tempest wild,  
  Restore the faith that grief has thus exiled.

477

When soul did trust where falsehood did reside,  
Sweet faith betrayed by lips that softly lied,  
A gentle hope within my breast had died,  
Yet still I mourn the love that fate denied.  
The moon looks on with pale, unpitying face,  
Its silver beams a mockery of pain.  
In dreams I wander back to that embrace,  
To taste the honeyed words that fell like rain.  
O, cruel heart, why dost thou feign regret,  
And cloak thy guile in garments fair and bright?  
The memories, like shadows, linger yet,  
And haunt the silence of the lonely night.  
  Yet from this woe, my soul shall rise anew,  
  For truth, though wounded, learns to love what's true.

478

When falsehood’s kiss did stain my trusting soul,  
Yet truth, unbid, did haunt where love should reign,  
A shadow crept where once was sweet control,  
And joy’s bright tapestry was rent in twain.  
The silver tongue that whispered hope to me  
Did mask the viper’s venom in its art,  
And blinded by affection’s revelry,  
I let deception settle in my heart.  
Yet dawn, relentless, peeled the night away,  
Exposing all the secrets time had kept;  
No longer fooled by honeyed words’ decay,  
I mourned the innocence my spirit wept.  
  From ashes rises wisdom, forged by pain—  
  And truth, though late, restores my soul again.

479

My soul, a trembling leaf in storm’s disdain,  
Doth bleed for love that knows me not in kind,  
The midnight weeps upon my windowpane,  
And hope, like mist, dissolves within my mind.  
Yet still, I chase the echo of thy voice,  
A distant lark that sings beyond my reach,  
My heart, ensnared by fate’s unyielding choice,  
Finds comfort in the lessons sorrow teach.  
Thy shadow dances softly in my dreams,  
A wraith of longing drifting through the night,  
While passion, muffled, muffles all my screams,  
And longing starves in absence of thy light.  
  Yet if my love unheeded must remain,  
  I’ll wear my ache as roses bear their pain.

480

Within my breast, a serpent coils unseen,  
Yet once I wore the visage of the dove,  
A gentle mask, where tranquil hope had been,  
Now shrouded by the venom thereof.  
The silent whisper winds about my heart,  
Its silver tongue distills the midnight fears,  
Unraveling the strands that held each part  
Of innocence entwined with vanished years.  
Yet still I long for wings to lift my soul,  
To rise above the shadows that confine,  
And mend the seams that venom sought to stole,  
Restoring light to chambers once divine.  
  If I may shed this skin of subtle guile,  
  Perhaps the dove may yet return awhile.

481

O falsehood, thou dost nest within my breast,  
Yet truth’s sharp blade would fain my soul defend;  
A shadowed whisper bids my heart unrest,  
While reason’s light would see such darkness end.  
Thy honeyed words do twine about my mind,  
And gentle mask the sorrow they conceal,  
Yet honest thought, though oftentimes unkind,  
Commands the wounds that only truth can heal.  
For every lie that stains the silent night  
A candle’s flicker trembles in the gloom,  
And hope, though battered, seeks the dawn’s new light  
To chase away deceit’s encroaching doom.  
  Though falsehood weaves its web with artful care,  
  The soul finds peace where truth breathes open air.

482

Within my breast two warring tempests rage,  
One seeks for peace, the other feeds on spite;  
Their thunder cracks within my private cage,  
And reason shudders in the waking night.  
Soft whispers plead for solace, balm, and rest,  
Yet sharper tongues provoke the storms anew;  
My trembling heart is pulled at love’s behest—  
Then spurned by doubts that cloud my every view.  
Oft have I begged the winds to grant me ease,  
To hush the quarrels tearing soul from soul,  
But every breath the warring factions seize,  
And neither grants relinquishment nor goal.  
  Yet in this strife, a strength I come to find:  
  The storm, though fierce, reveals my truer mind.

483

In faithless night, thy oath did turn to lies,  
Yet in my breast, true hope refus’d to die.  
The silver moon beheld thy false disguise,  
While silent stars bore witness to my cry.  
Though shadows crept where once thy promise gleamed,  
And gentle words grew cold upon thy tongue,  
I nursed the embered dream of what we dreamed,  
Still hearing songs that in our spring were sung.  
No bitter wind could chill my steadfast heart,  
Nor could the dawn unmake what love had sown;  
Though distance scorns to keep our souls apart,  
My constancy remains, though I’m alone.  
  For in the ruins of a broken trust,  
  I find my love endures, as all loves must.

484

When honeyed words did mask thy serpent’s heart,  
My trust, a fortress, fell to ruin’s tide;  
Thy gentle smile, a most deceptive art,  
Did cloak the venom coiling deep inside.  
Sweet vows once whispered in the silken night  
Now echo hollow through the haunted halls;  
Thy touch, which kindled stars in lover’s sight,  
Now chills my soul as faith and comfort falls.  
Yet from these ashes, wisdom’s wings arise,  
A phoenix born of sorrow’s cruel flame;  
No longer blinded by thy painted guise,  
I cast aside the burden of thy name.  
  For treachery, though bitter, sets me free—  
  From honeyed lies, I claim my liberty.

485

Thou false of heart, whose honeyed tongue did feign,  
Hast made my loyal breast thy wanton's grave;  
Thy whispered vows, like gentle April rain,  
Did promise roses none but tempests gave.  
My faith did bloom where shadows softly grew,  
Unseen, the serpent nestled in thy gaze;  
Each golden word, a lie adorned as true,  
Did paint my trust with hope's deceptive haze.  
Why dost thou smile beneath a borrowed light,  
When midnight's mask conceals thy fleeting flame?  
My soul, once blind, now wakes to bitter sight,  
And shuns the echo of thy cherished name.  
  Yet from this grief, my wounded heart shall rise—  
  For truth is born where falsehood surely dies.

486

You were the knife disguised within the rose,  
A sweetness sharp that bled me as it healed.  
Your gentle touch concealed a thorn that grows  
Beneath the velvet petals unrevealed.  
I drank your perfume, drunk on fragrant lies,  
Yet tasted steel within your honeyed breath.  
In beauty’s grasp, I learned how love belies  
The tender hand that leads me to my death.  
I would not trade the wounding for release,  
Nor wish the blossom’s edge be dulled or tamed.  
For pain and pleasure in your arms found peace,  
And by your hurt, my heart was soft unclaimed.  
  So let me bleed where your fair flower goes—  
  The knife I loved, forever in the rose.

487

You were the flame that promised gentle light,  
Yet left me stranded in a world of ash.  
I sought your warmth to banish endless night,  
But found my hopes consumed in tempests rash.  
Your words, like silken threads, would softly bind,  
Yet soon unravelled by a colder breeze;  
I searched for solace, but in you would find  
A fleeting shadow, never meant to please.  
The garden that we planted, now decays,  
Its blossoms wilt beneath remembering skies;  
And though I beg the sun to mend my days,  
Its golden promise fades and quickly dies.  
  Yet from these ruins, something new may start—  
  A wilder, braver beating of the heart.

488

You poured your words like honey in my ear,  
Yet left me poisoned, drowning in your lies.  
Your gentle voice disguised what I should fear,  
A serpent’s charm concealed behind your eyes.  
Sweet promises like petals soft and fair  
Did wilt and rot beneath your fleeting touch.  
I wandered lost, entangled in your snare,  
Believing solace, trusting far too much.  
Yet dawn reveals the shadows you have cast,  
And shards of truth illuminate my pain.  
The gilded hopes you offered could not last,  
Their golden hue now tarnished by disdain.  
  From bitter seeds of falsehood, wisdom grows—  
  I rise anew, though sorrow sharply flows.

489

You wore my trust like armor, bright and cold,  
Yet pierced it deftly, smiling as you lied.  
A gallant mask, with stories left untold,  
Concealed the shadows whispering inside.  
You pledged your heart with honeyed, silver tongue,  
And drew me close with promises of spring;  
Yet as the hopeful morning bells were rung,  
You vanished softly, stealing what they bring.  
The echoes of your laughter haunt the air,  
A phantom touch that lingers on my skin.  
I search for answers, finding none but care  
In memories of what we might have been.  
  Yet through deception, one bright truth I see:  
  The wounds you gave have set my spirit free.

490

Your love was but a dagger in disguise,  
A shadow slipping silent through my trust,  
It glimmered sweetly in your honeyed eyes  
Yet left my hopeful heart to rot and rust.  
The velvet words you spun were poison-laced,  
A charm that drew me close yet cut me deep;  
Your gentle smile so artfully misplaced,  
A promise made that you would never keep.  
I wore the wounds, invisible but real,  
And wandered through the night you made of day,  
Each whispered lie a thread my soul could feel,  
Unraveling the faith you took away.  
  Yet from this pain, a strength in me was born—  
  A rose that blossoms brighter for each thorn.

491

You were the sun that promised endless day,  
Yet left me shivering in midnight's shade.  
Your golden vows dissolved like morning’s ray  
When hope, like dew, in silent sorrow laid.  
The gardens bloomed beneath your warming gaze,  
Each petal opening to whispered gleam,  
But soon the light departed in a haze,  
And love withdrew as if it were a dream.  
I wander now through shadows cast by you,  
The echoes of your laughter haunt the air.  
The sky recalls a brilliance once it knew,  
Now empty, cold, and aching with despair.  
  Yet in the darkness, memory’s fire burns—  
  A lesson learned the heart must not unlearn.

492

My soul, a fortress breached by whispered lies,  
Now staggers blind beneath your shadowed art;  
The moon conceals the sorrow in my eyes,  
While hope retreats, besieged within my heart.  
With every breath, remembrance softly weeps  
And paints your absence on the trembling air;  
A garden ravaged, love no longer keeps  
Its gentle bloom within the ruins there.  
Yet still I haunt the threshold of your name,  
A ghost that clings to embers left behind;  
Desire and doubt, inextricably the same,  
Conspire to chain my will and fret my mind.  
  Though darkness spreads and solace seems untrue,  
  My soul endures the night in dreams of you.

493

My soul’s a garden razed by one false friend,  
Where trust, once seed, lies trampled at the gate,  
The gentle bloom of hope did meet its end  
By shadowed hands that bore the weight of hate.  
Each petal’s blush is blanched by bitter rain,  
As whispers coil like thorns about the stem;  
Sweet sun is barred, all warmth consumed by pain,  
And memories grow wild—I cannot hem.  
Yet in this ruin, roots refuse to die;  
A stubborn green persists beneath the scar,  
Defying all the poison of your lie  
And reaching still for light that waits afar.  
  Though friendships fall and faith may break in two,  
  My heart will sow anew, and gardens bloom anew.

494

My soul, a garden razed by secret knives,  
Now bleeds in silence where your shadow thrives.  
The petals fall on memories unspoken,  
Each bloom a promise lost, each root a token.  
Beneath the moon’s pale gaze, my longing grows,  
Entwined with thorns that longing never chose.  
I walk the haunted rows where laughter died,  
Where summer’s golden hush was crucified.  
Yet in the dark, a distant fragrance calls—  
The ghost of hope that waits beyond these walls.  
O gentle wind, revive my withered trust,  
Restore the earth from sorrow’s heavy dust.  
  For though your absence blights my garden fair,  
  Love’s seeds endure, and dream you might return.

495

My soul, a locked room, left cold by your retreat,  
You promised warmth, but winter took your place.  
The silent frost now creeps along my feet,  
And memory shivers in this hollow space.  
Once golden light would spill across the floor,  
Your laughter kindled hearths within my chest;  
But shadows gather where you come no more,  
And solitude is now my only guest.  
I search for echoes of your vanished voice,  
Each corner holds a ghost your love had made.  
The world outside moves on—its careless noise  
Mocks all the vows that time cannot persuade.  
  Yet in this chill, a hope begins to bloom:  
  The heart remembers spring, despite the gloom.

496

My soul, a fortress breached by guileful hands,  
Now crumbles slow beneath your silent gaze,  
The stones unmoored by time’s relentless sands,  
Foundations lost within your shadowed maze.  
Where once the banners of my hope unfurled,  
Now tattered flags in mournful breezes fall,  
The heralds of a bright and stalwart world  
Lie mute within these melancholy walls.  
Yet in the ruins, gentle seeds appear,  
And from the cracks, a tender green ascends;  
Your eyes, though cold, have watered roots of fear  
That now, transformed, to quiet strength portends.  
  Though breached and worn, my soul shall rise anew,  
  And build a fortress stronger, shaped by you.

497

My soul, a garden razed by whispered lies,  
Now withers slow beneath your faithless gaze.  
Where once hope bloomed beneath forgiving skies,  
Now tangled bramble chokes the gentle maze.  
The sun that crowned my heart with golden fire  
Is hidden now behind a sullen shroud;  
Each blossom falls, undone by false desire,  
Its petals dashed upon the frozen ground.  
Yet in this ruin, memory persists—  
A scent of summers lost, of laughter flown,  
While phantom hands recall forgotten trysts  
And trace the ghostly seeds I once had sown.  
  Though love's bright Eden crumbles into dust,  
  Still, from the ashes, hope may rise, and must.

498

I carved your name in stone upon my trust,  
But found the rock was salt that wept to dust.  
The morning sun, with gentle, careless gust,  
Did scatter all the letters as it must.  
What vows I built with hope’s unsteady hand  
Were castles drawn too near the hungry tide;  
The waves erased the lines upon the sand,  
And dreams dissolved as soon as they were tried.  
Yet still I trace your shadow in the air,  
Though memory’s chisel falters on the truth,  
For love’s inscription lingers faintly there,  
A fleeting echo of unyielding youth.  
  Though stones may fail, and salted trust may part,  
  Your name endures, engraved upon my heart.

499

My trust was glass, and you the stone that fell,  
I taste the shards within my bitter chest,  
A silent shatter none but I can tell,  
The ruins echo where my heart once rest.  
Your gentle words were veils for sharpened lies,  
A melody that hid the sound of break;  
I searched for solace in your fleeting eyes,  
Yet found the dawn was nothing but a fake.  
What sweetness lingered now is tinged with pain,  
Each memory a splinter in my mind,  
The lessons learned from love’s unkind domain,  
Are written in the tears I left behind.  
  Yet from the fragments, I shall rise anew,  
  For broken glass reflects a brighter view.

500

A serpent in my garden wore my face,  
Its hiss disguised as counsel in my ear,  
With silvered tongue it slithered into place,  
And sowed within my heart a subtle fear.  
It coiled around my hopes with velvet grace,  
And shed its scales upon each trembling year.  
It whispered secrets time cannot erase,  
Then vanished, leaving shadows lingering near.  
I plucked the tainted fruit with trembling hand,  
Believing wisdom sweet as summer wine,  
Yet found the taste of ash upon the land,  
A blight upon the root, the leaf, the vine.  
  Yet now I see the serpent was but me:  
  My own desires undone by vanity.

501

I wore my trust like armor, cold and bright,  
Yet found your kiss a dagger in disguise.  
Beneath your gaze, the world was swathed in light,  
But shadows danced behind your honeyed eyes.  
I charted stars upon your whispered vow,  
Unraveled secrets woven in your hair,  
And pledged my heart though reason counseled how  
The sweetest fruit oft hides a poisoned snare.  
Your laughter, sparkling, masked the rising storm,  
Each gentle word a thread within your net.  
A touch, a promise—warmth that would transform  
To chilling winds of longing and regret.  
  Yet from this wound, new strength my soul shall glean:  
  Betrayal taught me love is seldom seen.

502

I watered seeds of trust within your shade,  
Yet found my garden choked by roots of lies,  
Your whispered sun, a fickle serenade,  
Beguiled my hopes with ever-changing skies.  
Where once the gentle promise softly grew  
In fertile earth of hearts' unspoken pact,  
Now brambles rise, and honest blooms eschew  
The tangled truth your silences have lacked.  
Yet still I tend these furrows, bruised and worn,  
My hands stained dark with disappointment's dew,  
For faith, once sown, is not so lightly torn,  
Though seasons shift and bitter winds accrue.  
  If love be harvest, let the reaper find  
  A yield of strength where trust has been refined.

503

You poured your lies like poison in my veins,  
A garden burned by frost of faithless hands,
