Where once the sun bestowed its golden gains  
Now shadows root in wilted, broken lands.  
The promises you wove were threads of mist,  
They vanished with the dawn, a pale deceit;  
Your honeyed words, my trusting lips they kissed,  
But left me haunted by their bitter sweet.  
I built my hope on shores of shifting sand,  
While tides of guile swept every dream away.  
Yet from these ruins, I shall bravely stand,  
And greet the rising of another day.  
  For though you sought to bind me with your art,  
  You cannot chain the courage of my heart.

504

Betrayal tiptoes, cloaked in your perfume’s trace,  
It whispers lies behind your smiling guise,  
In moonlit rooms where shadows interlace,  
A silent tempest gathers in your eyes.  
Your laughter rings with echoes sharp and cold,  
Yet softer than the silk upon your skin;  
A thousand secret stories left untold  
Now shimmer on the edge of what has been.  
I taste the night’s deceit upon your breath,  
As honeyed words unravel into rue,  
And in your arms I feel the threat of death  
That slumbers in the love I thought I knew.  
  For trust, once cracked, no gentle hand may mend—  
  So ends our play, with truth its bitter end.

505

Betrayal wears your face, yet speaks my name,  
A serpent clothed in silks of whispered trust,  
You smiled and drew me closer to the flame,  
While shadows gathered quietly in dust.  
Your honeyed words, like springtime on the breeze,  
Did mask the venom coiled behind your eyes,  
Yet every tender touch served but to tease,  
And every promise hid a dozen lies.  
I held your hand, unknowing of your art,  
Believing love could keep the darkness far,  
Yet now I find a dagger in my heart,  
Its mark as cold and certain as a scar.  
  Though faith is lost, and I am left betrayed,  
  From ashes, wisdom blossoms unafraid.

506

When Trust, that gentle ghost, turned sharp and cold,  
You wore Deceit like jewelry, proud and bold.  
The silver words you spoke became my chains,  
While shadows danced upon your honeyed tongue.  
A garden once we grew now yields but stains,  
Its petals bruised, its sweetest fruit unsung.  
You smiled, and in that smile the poison lay,  
A serpent twined in ribbons of delight.  
I searched the dawn to drive your lies away,  
But found the sun had fled before the night.  
Yet from these shards of faith, a lesson gleams:  
To treasure truth and guard the heart from schemes.  
  For though your mask was fair, the soul beneath  
  Could never match the beauty of belief.

507

Betrayal slipped into your gentle smile,  
A serpent cloaked within a lover’s face,  
Its venom masked by laughter’s artful guile,  
And tenderness transformed to sly disgrace.  
The honeyed words that once did soothe and please  
Now ring with hollow echoes in my ear,  
Affections wilt, as frost will fell the trees,  
And trust, once firm, grows brittle, frail, and sheer.  
Yet still I search your eyes for some reprieve,  
Some spark of truth amid the tangled lies—  
But every hope I nurture you bereave  
With every sweet deception you devise.  
  Thus love, undone by shadows you impart,  
  Leaves poisoned roots entangled in my heart.

508

Betrayal wears your face, a borrowed mask,  
And Love, your servant once, now turns to flee,  
In silence, shadows gather to unmask  
The truths that night would hide from memory.  
I trusted in the gentle words you spoke,  
As dew trusts dawn to keep the world in light;  
Yet every promise, fragile, fell and broke,  
And hope, once gold, now tarnished into blight.  
The hours we shared dissolve like mist at morn,  
Their echoes haunt the corners of my mind.  
What once was cherished stands neglected, worn,  
A garden choked by weeds you left behind.  
  Yet from this pain, new wisdom shall arise—  
  For hearts betrayed may see through sweeter lies.

509

False Hope, my foe, has slipped into your eyes,  
And whispers poison sweet as honeyed lies.  
She paints the dawn with colors never true,  
And stirs the longing heart with golden skies.  
Her silken words, a web of gentle rue,  
Entwine the soul until it cannot see  
The certain dusk that waits when day is through,  
The shadow veiled beneath her reverie.  
She bids thee dance upon uncertain ground,  
Promising stars that tumble from the night;  
Yet when the morning breaks, she's never found—  
A faded memory of borrowed light.  
  Beware her touch, though soft as summer rain:  
  For Hope, once false, begets the truest pain.

510

My soul, that once wore trust as morning's gown,  
Now sharpens doubts like knives behind my eyes,  
The sunlit faith I wore has fallen down  
And left me cloaked in shadowed, gray disguise.  
Each whispered vow that promised endless spring  
Dissolves to frost upon a trembling tongue;  
What once did hope like birds aloft would sing  
Now weeps in silence, songs unsweetly sung.  
Yet still the memory of warmth remains—  
A fleeting ghost that brushes at my cheek,  
Reminding me, through heart’s familiar pains,  
That trust, though wounded, never grows so weak.  
  For though the night be long and full of fears,  
  The dawn may rise and dry the bitter tears.

511

My soul, a serpent, coils against my heart,  
It hisses lies of trust that fell apart.  
Its scales are memories that gleam with rue,  
Each glint a shard of what I thought I knew.  
Around my chest it weaves a cold embrace,  
A silent whisper winding through my veins,  
Its ancient eyes reflect my doubting face,  
And in their depths, a catalogue of pains.  
Yet still I breathe beneath its jealous hold,  
Defiant hope alive despite the pain;  
For though its poison words are sharp and bold,  
My beating heart withstands the strangling chain.  
  From broken faith, I gather strength anew—  
  The serpent fades, and I am born anew.

512

My soul, that trusted daylight’s gentle hand,  
Now finds its mirror shattered by deceit;  
Once golden fields turn to a barren land,  
Where hope lay blooming, now lies pale defeat.  
The laughter of the dawn is tinged with pain,  
The lark’s sweet song is choked by bitter air;  
What once was balm now only falls as rain  
Upon the wounds that treachery laid bare.  
Yet, in this gloom, a stubborn ember glows,  
Defiant ‘gainst the shadow’s cold embrace.  
Though trust is bruised and every comfort goes,  
Resilience carves new courage in its place.  
  For though the day may falter, night may fall,  
  The soul endures and rises over all.

513

My soul, deceived, now wears your shadow’s face,  
It weeps in corners where your promise died,  
The silent hallows echo your disgrace,  
While hope, once bright, in sorrow’s cloak is tied.  
You whispered dawn, then vanished with the night,  
And left me stranded in a web of pain,  
Your memory clings, a ghost that shuns the light,  
A haunting chill upon my windowpane.  
Yet still I search the dusk for signs of you,  
Each fleeting shape a wish that you return,  
Though reason warns what dreams cannot undo,  
My heart, unheeding, lets the embers burn.  
  So here I dwell, where loss and longing meet—  
  Your absence turns my solace to defeat.

514

My soul, betrayed, now wears a stranger’s face,  
It haunts my chest, a ghost denied embrace.  
Once golden trust now shadows every room,  
Its echoes whisper doubt in silent gloom.  
The mirror’s gaze reflects what is not mine,  
A borrowed mask, a sorrowed, fractured line;  
Where laughter dwelled, suspicion takes its place,  
And hope recedes before this bitter chase.  
Yet through the cracks, one ember faintly gleams—  
A memory of gentler, brighter schemes.  
Shall I reclaim the self that once I knew,  
Or wander lost, unsure of what is true?  
  Though night is long, and faith is slow to grow,  
  The dawn may bring the face I used to know.

515

My soul, betrayed, now whispers lies to me,  
It wears your name, yet shuns your memory.  
The shadows spin your laughter through the night,  
A ghostly echo glimpsed in candle’s gleam.  
I chase the fragments of a vanished light  
That flickers faint upon a distant dream.  
Thy absence weaves a shroud about my heart,  
Each thread entwined with hope’s unravelled thread.  
The silence speaks where once thy voice took part,  
And in its hush, I mourn the words unsaid.  
Yet still, my longing clings to what is gone,  
As ivy climbs upon a crumbling stone.  
  Though faith be false, its ache is never done—  
  I grieve the loss and grieve to be alone.

516

Betrayal sits beside me in my bed,  
Her whispers burn the trust I thought I knew.  
She weaves her poison threads within my head,  
And stains the world I once beheld as true.  
The gentle dawn now casts a colder light,  
Revealing shadows where her secrets hide;  
The comfort of the dark becomes my plight,  
With every sigh, I mourn what’s cast aside.  
Yet in her gaze, a lesson lies concealed,  
For hearts betrayed may learn to steel their core.  
Though wounds are deep, in time they may be healed,  
And scarred, I rise more wary than before.  
  The bed where loyalty was softly slain  
  Becomes the forge where I outgrow my pain.

517

Betrayal wears my shadow like a skin,  
It whispers lies beneath my hopeful breath,  
A serpent coiled in trust, it waits within,  
Unseen, it plants the seeds of silent death.  
Once laughter bloomed where now suspicion grows,  
The garden of my heart, in ruin, lies.  
Each promise wilted by what darkness sows,  
And tears reflect the truth behind your eyes.  
I mourn the innocence that once I knew,  
A fragile glass now shattered on the floor.  
Yet in this loss, a bitter strength I drew—  
My soul resolved to trust that heart no more.  
  Though night surrounds, I rise to greet the sun,  
  For from your treachery, my dawn’s begun.

518

Betrayal wears my shadow like a skin,  
She laughs within my chest, a hollow sound.  
Her whispers thread through every vein within,  
And root my trust like weeds into the ground.  
She dances in the corners of my mind,  
A specter clothed in memories once bright;  
The warmth of friendship now grown cold and blind,  
Replaced by frost that shivers through the night.  
Yet in her wake, I gather shards of me,  
And count the lessons hidden in the pain.  
The mask she wore is gone; at last I see  
The shape of truth that rises from the stain.  
  Though grief may stain the morning’s gentle hue,  
  I mend my heart and find the dawn anew.

519

Betrayal wears my shadow like a skin,  
Each heartbeat mocks the trust I let you win.  
The whispered vows, now dust upon the air,  
Lie tangled where your promises have been.  
A serpent’s grace concealed behind your stare—  
So sweet your words, so venomous within—  
Did love not bloom, then wither in despair,  
Its petals torn by every hidden sin?  
Yet still I haunt the garden we designed,  
Where memories, like ghosts, refuse to part.  
I sift through ashes, desperate to find  
Some ember left unblackened in my heart.  
  Though time may heal the wounds your lies have made,  
  The shadow lingers, never quite to fade.

520

Betrayal wears my name upon her tongue,  
She dances in my shadow, cold and sly,  
Her laughter curdles songs I once had sung,  
And weaves her poison truths with every lie.  
She moves through halls where trust and hope reside,  
Unseen, she plucks the petals of belief,  
Her silver whispers twist what I confide,  
And turn my moments bright to bitter grief.  
I watch her shape dissolve behind my eyes,  
A phantom echo walking in my stead,  
She gathers all the dreams that daylight buys,  
And leaves me counting promises long dead.  
  Yet from this wound, a stronger heart may grow,  
  For light is born where darkest shadows go.

521

Deceit now wears my shadow as its cloak,  
Her whispers haunt the silence where I dwell,  
She weaves her threads as softly as she spoke,  
And crafts from light a most beguiling shell.  
Her gentle hands have shaped my every doubt,  
She paints with lies the windows of my mind,  
Yet in her gaze, no honest truth breaks out,  
But cunning glances, artful and unkind.  
I watch her dance upon my weary face,  
A masquerade I cannot yet forsake,  
Her laughter echoes in this hollow space,  
And wakes the fears I feign for reason’s sake.  
  Yet though she reigns and shadows all I see,  
  I dream of dawn when I at last break free.

522

You wore the face of truth yet spoke in lies,  
A gentle touch that masked a sharpened blade,  
With honeyed words that cloaked your foul disguise,  
And promises that wilted as they swayed.  
In moonlit halls, your laugh became my chain,  
A siren’s song that led my heart astray;  
I drank the sweetest nectar laced with pain,  
And watched the brightest noon dissolve to gray.  
Yet in your eyes, the storm began to clear—  
A fleeting glimpse of sorrow, raw and true—  
For in your heart, regret was sharp and near,  
A shadow trailing all that you would do.  
  Though trust is lost, I mourn what might have been,  
  A love betrayed by falsehoods deep within.

523

You wore your faith like armor, bright and bold,  
Yet hid a serpent’s grin beneath your gaze,  
A tale of virtue glimmered, deftly told,  
But tangled truth lay veiled within the haze.  
Your honeyed words, a shield against my doubt,  
Did paint your faults as shadows in the night,  
Yet whispered silences would edge them out,  
Revealing cracks that shattered painted light.  
Upon your breastplate shone a righteous crest,  
But in the quiet hours, masks would slip,  
And all your gentle vows stood sorely pressed  
By venom coiled behind your trembling lip.  
  Oh, faith that gleams yet crumbles with a kiss—  
  How bravely worn, how treacherous it is.

524

You wore your vows as masks, not shields, my dear,  
Yet kissed me warm while plotting winter's cold.  
Your whispered truths were veiled so I might hear  
A sweeter song than honesty had told.  
With every glance, you conjured summer skies,  
While frost lay hid beneath your gentle gaze.  
You painted love with ever-changing dyes,  
A shifting art that melted in the haze.  
I wore my trust as armor, thin and bright,  
Believing spring could banish all the snow.  
But now I see the day succumb to night,  
And understand the seeds you chose to sow.  
  Yet from this thaw, a wiser heart I claim,  
  And hold your shadow gently in its frame.

525

You swore your heart was tethered close to mine,  
Yet found in stranger arms your sweetest rest,  
I traced the silent fault along the line  
Where vows were inked and love was manifest.  
The moon bore witness to your fleeting gaze,  
While I, in shadowed corners, held my breath,  
A captive of remembrance and the haze  
Of whispered promises betrayed by stealth.  
Though dawn revealed the emptiness you left,  
I gather shards of trust that once were whole,  
And mend the tattered vestige of the theft  
That robbed the gentle solace from my soul.  
  Yet still I rise, though wounded by your art—  
  For time, not you, shall be the keeper of my heart.

526

You smiled with warmth, yet ice behind your eyes,  
A gentle touch that masked a cold goodbye.  
Your laughter’s music cloaked in sweet disguise,  
A fleeting summer’s breeze that passed me by.  
I reached for hope within your painted jest,  
Believing kindness lived behind your mask,  
But found a heart that would not grant me rest,  
And answers silent to the truths I ask.  
The moonlight shimmered on your distant face,  
Its silver glow concealing what you hide.  
I chased illusions time cannot erase,  
Yet sorrow lingers, standing by my side.  
  Though frost may linger where your fondness lay,  
  Love’s lesson taught me how to walk away.

527

You smiled with honeyed lips and daggered eyes,  
Professing love while weaving subtle lies.  
The moon bore witness to your whispered vow,  
Yet shadows danced behind your furrowed brow.  
A gentle hand concealed a sharpened blade  
That traced my trust, then softly split the seam;  
Beneath your laughter, silent bargains laid,  
Each promise fractured like a shattered dream.  
With every word you drew my spirit near,  
Then led it wandering through mist and doubt;  
Your velvet voice transformed my hope to fear,  
Unraveling the love we spoke about.  
  Yet in your gaze, both sweet and treacherous,  
  I learned the cost of loving dangerous.

528

My soul, once fortress, now a crumbling wall,  
Betrayed by whispers echoing my trust,  
Within these stones, I hear the nightbird’s call,  
And watch my hope dissolve in bitter dust.  
The moonlight paints my ruin with regret,  
As ivy climbs the remnants of my pride;  
Old scars, like shadows, haunt what I forget,  
And all my dreams in broken corners hide.  
Yet from the weeds, a stubborn flower grows,  
Defiant in the silence of my fear;  
Its petals bright against the gloom it knows,  
A promise that the dawn may yet appear.  
  Though battered, bruised, my heart endures its fall—  
  From rubble, love can build another wall.

529

My soul, once open, locked its doors to trust,  
For bitter truth in honeyed words is thrust.  
Fair promises, like dew, dissolve to dust,  
And gentle laughter trembles into rust.  
The silver notes of hope, now faint and thin,  
Are muffled by the weight of silent fears;  
Yet faintly through the gloom they strive within,  
To mend the tapestry of vanished years.  
I wander through the shadows of my mind,  
Where memories like autumn leaves descend;  
Though scars remain, some solace I may find,  
If time and tender grace will help me mend.  
  Yet still, my soul awaits the dawn anew,  
  When trust returns, and light breaks gently through.

530

My soul, once fortress, now a traitor’s gate,  
Where trust and treason war in shadowed halls,  
Each whispered oath is twisted by its fate,  
And hope, beleaguered, bruised, behind me falls.  
The moonlight streams through iron-latticed pain,  
Its silver fingers reaching for my heart,  
Yet love’s bright banner, tattered in the rain,  
Is trampled underfoot and torn apart.  
Within these chambers echo voices past,  
Their promises now changed to bitter lies,  
And every joy, like fleeting shadows cast,  
Dissolves before the dawn can clear my eyes.  
  Yet from this dark betrayal, I shall rise—  
  A soul remade, and wise where ruin lies.

531

My soul, once kindled by your gentle fire,  
Now shivers cold beneath your silent spite.  
Once hope did lift my heart and draw me higher,  
But now falls low in never-ending night.  
Each word unspoken hangs in frosted air,  
A shadowed echo of our vanished spring;  
The laughter lost, a ghost that lingers there,  
Reminds me of the warmth you used to bring.  
Yet still I search the silence for your face,  
And dream your kindness blooming once again,  
While memory pursues me in its chase,  
A bittersweet and ever-clinging pain.  
  If time could thaw the winter of your heart,  
  We’d mend the world that sorrow tore apart.

532

You swore your heart would never turn from mine,  
Yet left me cold, where once your warmth was true.  
The stars that watched our vows no longer shine,  
Their silver gaze now blind to me and you.  
A promise, like a rose, with petals torn,  
Lies scattered, crimson, on the heedless ground.  
The dawn that once caressed the hopeful morn  
Now hides behind the clouds, without a sound.  
Yet in this silence, memories remain—  
Sweet echoes of a laughter lost to time—  
Though love has faded, still persists the pain,  
A hollow ache that reason cannot rhyme.  
  Yet, though betrayed, my heart recalls the fire—  
  The ghost of joy, and sorrow’s soft attire.

533

You promised warmth, yet left my soul in frost,  
A beacon bright now shadowed by your lie,  
The summer’s gold by winter’s fingers tossed,  
And laughter drowned beneath a silent sky.  
I traced your vows through gardens overgrown,  
Where petals droop from words that would not stay,  
Each memory a seed that’s never sown,  
Each hope a dawn that cannot find its day.  
Yet still my heart recalls your gentle fire,  
The phantom touch that kindled secret light;  
In absence, longing sharpens its desire,  
And dreams, once sweet, grow bitter in the night.  
  But though you vanished, cold as morning dew,  
  I rise, and from this frost, begin anew.

534

In shadows cast by friendship’s sunlit face,  
I nurse the wound your loyalty betrayed,  
A trust once firm, now shifting, out of place,  
Its golden promise tarnished, dulled, and frayed.  
The laughter shared now echoes, bittersweet,  
As memory’s lantern flickers in the gloom,  
Where whispered doubts and silent sorrows meet,  
And petals of affection fail to bloom.  
Yet through the ache, forgiveness stirs anew,  
Its gentle hand extends despite the pain,  
Reminding me of bonds that once I knew,  
Desiring hope might blossom once again.  
  Though faith be bruised, the heart endures the test—  
  For even broken ties can mend and rest.

535

I trusted light, yet shadows wore your face,  
Within my heart, your kindness turned to lies,  
A gentle hand became a cruel embrace,  
And hope was masked by sorrow’s thin disguise.  
Your laughter echoed like a distant bell  
That tolled for dreams abandoned in the night;  
The sweetest words became a cryptic spell  
That led my soul away from warmth and light.  
Beneath the moon, I searched for truth in vain,  
Each promise crumbled softly into dust.  
The memory of joy was tinged with pain,  
And every silent glance betrayed my trust.  
  Yet from this grief, I rise and learn to see—  
  True light begins where shadows cease to be.

536

I loved with fire, yet ice was in your eyes,  
My trust stood tall while yours was built on lies.  
The hopeful dawn I cherished turned to grey,  
As shadows claimed the promise of our day.  
You sang of love, but silence filled your tune,  
And every gentle word was edged with fear.  
I reached for warmth, but found the chill of June,  
A summer lost that once had blossomed here.  
Yet in the ashes, wisdom softly grows,  
My heart, though bruised, beats braver than before.  
The pain of winter yields to spring’s repose,  
And from the ruins, hope will build once more.  
  Though love’s bright flame was smothered by disguise,  
  A truer fire within my soul shall rise.

537

When trust was wine, you drank my hope to dregs,  
Yet pledged me love with lips as cold as stone,  
The vows you spun were threads on brittle legs,  
A tapestry of warmth I’d never known.  
Your eyes, twin lanterns shuttered to the night,  
Refused me passage to the soul within;  
Each word you tossed, a feather in its flight,  
Would flutter down, unanchored by the skin.  
I traced the frost that lingered on your hands,  
Mistaking diamond chill for diamond fire;  
Still, through the winter’s court I made my stand,  
A fool enthralled by echoes of desire.  
  Yet now I see love’s vintage was but pain:  
  Your empty cup, and all my trust was vain.

538

I gave my trust, yet found a sharpened blade,  
Your silent smile concealed the wound you made.  
Beneath soft words, a storm of daggers lay,  
And every gentle touch did thus betray.  
The sun that lit our path now hides its face,  
A shadow falls where once our laughter grew.  
My heart recalls the warmth of your embrace,  
Yet trembles now at everything you do.  
You wore your kindness like a silken mask,  
A veil that hid the venom in your eyes.  
I pondered love, but dared not truly ask  
If truth could dwell where constant falsehood lies.  
  Yet from these ashes, stronger shall I rise,  
  And see at last through all your sweet disguise.

539

You promised truth, but left me clothed in lies,  
My heart’s devotion met your cold disdain,  
The gentle dawn concealed your midnight guise,  
And hope dissolved beneath a shadow’s stain.  
I traced your vows in stars that lost their gleam,  
Each whispered word, a ghost upon the air,  
The future vanished like a fleeting dream,  
Replaced by silence, hollow with despair.  
Yet still, my soul recalls those fervent days  
When laughter bloomed and love had seemed so sure.  
Now memory’s garden withers in the haze,  
Its petals bruised by wounds I must endure.  
  Yet from these ashes, I shall rise anew,  
  And seek a world untainted by untrue.

540

Sweet Judas, thou hast sown thy kiss in frost,  
Yet beg I mercy for the wound thou deal’st—  
A silver serpent’s tongue at such a cost  
That trust, once green, now withers unrepealed.  
Thy eyes, twin lanterns, flicker in the night,  
Dispel the dark but blind with borrowed flame;  
Thy hands, which pledged allegiance to the right,  
Have signed my sentence, cloaked my heart in shame.  
Yet in this garden tangled with deceit,  
I seek the rose that braves thy wintered breath,  
And though thy shadow chills the summer heat,  
Some mercy lingers, trembling before death.  
  For in thy kiss betrayal’s sorrow lies—  
  Yet love survives, though crucified by lies.

541

O treach’rous night that veils thy faithless deed,  
Why dost thou steal the bloom from love’s fair rose?  
Beneath thy shadow, hearts in silence bleed,  
And hope, once bright, in silent ashes grows.  
Thy sable cloak conceals the whispered lie  
That drips like poison from a honeyed tongue;  
The stars avert their gaze within the sky,  
Ashamed to see where trust and truth are hung.  
What boon hast thou to offer, cruel shade,  
But dreams that flicker, false as fleeting light?  
Thou leav’st the soul in solitude arrayed,  
Forsaken by the promise of the night.  
  Yet dawn shall break and strip thee of thy art,  
  Restoring faith and healing every heart.

542

If thou, my sun, dost set behind false cloud,  
Why must thy light in shadow be enshroud’d?  
Doth fickle fate decree thy beams be cowed,  
Or is my longing for thy warmth too proud?  
In absence, chill and silence fill the air,  
The world bereft of gold that thou hast spun,  
Yet still I seek thee, though I know not where—  
I pine for dawn though night is scarce begun.  
Perchance the darkness teaches me thy worth,  
And makes each slender ray more dearly prized;  
My heart, eclipsed, awaits thy gentle birth,  
And dreams of radiance not realized.  
  Return, sweet sun, and all my sorrows mend;  
  Let not false clouds our steadfast love forfend.

543

O, turn thine eyes from daggers forged in lies,  
And let not frost infest the bloom of trust;  
For bitter glances chill the clearest skies,  
And love decays when hearts surrender rust.  
If whispered venom stains the gentle ear,  
Then truth, like petals, withers in the shade;  
Yet hope may flourish, banishing all fear,  
If honest words in open light are laid.  
Let not suspicion choke the budding spring  
Nor shadowed doubts eclipse the golden day;  
For faith is gentle, borne on hopeful wing,  
And melts the ice that keeps fond souls at bay.  
  Let candor reign, and let thy spirit see:  
  Love thrives in truth, and dies in perfidy.

544

O faithless moon that guides my heart astray,  
Why steal’st thou light from stars that bid thee stay?  
Thy silver gaze, so cold upon my pain,  
Doth mock the vows that lovers would maintain.  
With borrowed fire thou dress’st the midnight blue,  
Yet veilest truth behind thy borrowed beams;  
Thy promises, like shadows, prove untrue,  
And haunt the corridors of broken dreams.  
Yet still I chase thy path across the skies,  
Entranced by hope that thou wilt turn and see  
The longing written deep within mine eyes,  
And grant thy borrowed light to only me.  
  But fickle orb, thou art no constant guide—  
  My heart, like night, is emptier inside.

545

O, wilt thou turn thy gentle face away,  
And cast my heart into the night’s cold sea?  
The moon, in grief, hides half her golden ray,  
While silent stars bear witness silently.  
The trembling leaves confess my secret fears,  
As every shadow whispers thy disdain;  
The garden’s breath is heavy with my tears,  
Its roses pale beneath a lover’s pain.  
Yet hope, like dawn, compels the darkness hence,  
And in the east a blush of comfort grows;  
Perchance thy heart relents at love’s expense,  
And mercy on my longing soul bestows.  
  If thou return, the night shall sweetly end—  
  For thou art both my torment and my friend.

546

O treach’rous night that wounds mine inward eye,  
Why dost thou weave such frost about my soul?  
Thy sable shroud blots out the gentle sky,  
And steals from me the stars that made me whole.  
Upon thy breath, cold whispers twist and creep,  
Disturbing dreams that once with hope were crowned;  
Thou sow’st unrest where quiet ought to sleep,  
And drown’st the heart in silence so profound.  
Yet in thy darkness blooms a secret fire,  
A flame that flickers, fragile, yet so bright,  
It stirs my courage, kindles new desire,  
And bids me trust the promise of the light.  
  So though thou wound’st, thou teachest me to see—  
  The dawn is sweetest born of night’s decree.

547

O treachery, thou serpent in my breast,  
Why dost thou gnaw the roots whence virtue springs?  
Within thy coils my better self is pressed,  
And silenced are the songs that honor sings.  
With honeyed tongue, thou whisper’st in my ear,  
Untruths that stain the mirror of my mind,  
And plant in fertile soil the seed of fear,  
Till all my noble thoughts are undermined.  
Yet, still I strive to cast thee from my heart,  
To loose thy grip with reason’s tempered sword,  
Though oft thy shadow plays a cunning part,  
And tempts my soul from paths it once adored.  
  But steadfast hope, though battered and betrayed,  
  Shall rise anew, and see thy venom fade.

548

O treach’rous night, why rend’st thou so my soul,  
That storms within like tempests 'gainst my will,  
With shadows thick, as ink in midnight’s bowl,  
And silence deep, my weary heart to chill?  
Thy sable veil conceals both hope and fear,  
As moonlit whispers dance upon the pane;  
Each hour drags on, relentless, cold, severe,  
While memories return to wound again.  
The clock’s dull chime doth echo through the gloom,  
And specters rise from corners of my mind;  
All joy lies buried in this stifling tomb,  
And peace, elusive, I may never find.  
  Yet dawn must break, and darkness yield to light—  
  Thus hope endures the tyranny of night.

549

O, rend not thus the lattice of my soul,  
Where trust, a tender vine, once sweetly grew,  
Thy words, like tempest winds beyond control,  
Have torn the gentle leaves with bitter rue.  
Within these chambers, hope’s faint embers lie,  
Their golden warmth eclipsed by shadows cold,  
Yet still I search the darkness for the sky,  
And cradle dreams thy silence would withhold.  
What folly, love, to build on shifting sand,  
When tides of doubt erode the steadfast shore?  
Yet, trembling, still I reach to grasp thy hand,  
And plead thy mercy, love, forevermore.  
  Restore, I pray, the faith thy deeds have slain,  
  Let trust, once rent, in gentler soil remain.

550

O faithless moon, thou turn’st thy gaze away,  
Whilst my poor soul in shadows doth implore—  
Thy silver light, once kind, now led astray,  
Abandons me upon this silent shore.  
The nightingales lament in mournful tune,  
Their aching notes adrift on bitter wind.  
The stars, in council with the cunning moon,  
Conspire to mock the love I left behind.  
What hope remains beneath thy fickle gleam,  
If even night’s companion proves untrue?  
I wander lost, the captive of a dream,  
Where every promise fades into the blue.  
  Yet still I wait, though hope be faint and slight:  
  Return, sweet moon, and grant me back thy light.

551

O treach’rous night, why steal’st thou my soul’s light,  
Thou turn’st my heart to shadow with thy guile,  
Each star that pricks the dark becomes my plight,  
And dreams, once sweet, now wander and defile.  
Within thy velvet shroud doth sorrow grow,  
As silent fears in corners softly creep,  
Thy chilly breath lays all my hopes so low,  
And robs the weary world of gentle sleep.  
Yet in thy depths a silver promise gleams,  
A whisper woven in the hush of gloom—  
That dawn shall rescue all my wayward dreams  
And gild the faded rose with golden bloom.  
  So though thou art a thief of warmth and sight,  
  I trust the day shall vanquish thee, O Night.

552

O faithless night, why dost thou steal my trust,  
And plant false lilies where my roses grew?  
Thy sable cloak conceals a traitor’s lust,  
And dims the gold of dawn I once did view.  
Thy whispers, silver-tongued, beguile mine ear,  
With promises embroidered soft and sweet,  
Yet in thy shadow, hope dissolves to fear,  
And footsteps falter on deceitful street.  
The moon’s pale witness shrouds her gentle face,  
Ashamed to shed her truth upon thy game,  
While stars, complicit, blink with fleeting grace,  
Unwilling to betray thy borrowed name.  
  Yet, come the morn, thy falsehood shrinks from sight—  
  My garden blooms anew, despite the night.

553

O treach’rous heart, why dost thou turn thy blade,  
And wound the breast where once thy promise lay?  
Did not our vows in golden hours persuade  
Thee to remain, and never flee away?  
Yet shadows creep where sunlight once abode,  
And trust, like petals, falls upon the ground.  
The gentle words we spoke are now bestowed  
To silence, where no sweeter songs are found.  
Beneath thy guise of love, deceit was sown,  
A garden choked with thistles, sharp and wild.  
What fruit remains from seeds so falsely thrown  
But bitter fare for hope, now unreconciled?  
  O faithless heart, repent thy secret art,  
  Restore the peace thou stole from mine own heart.

554

O, let not serpent’s whisper wrack my breast,  
For trust once sewn is now by daggers torn,  
The garden’s peace now withers, dispossessed,  
And hope’s sweet petals pale, betrayed, forlorn.  
Where once the gentle lark would greet the day,  
Suspicion’s shadows cloud the morning light;  
What golden bonds we wove are frayed away,  
Their silken threads unravel in the night.  
Yet in the hollow silence of my pain,  
Some stubborn root of faith remains unbowed,  
Defies the venom coursing through each vein,  
And calls for truth to break the serpent’s shroud.  
  Though trust may sleep, by treachery oppressed,  
  Love’s steadfast heart yet beats within my chest.

555

O wilt thou break the vessel of my trust,  
And spill my hopes like wine upon the dust?  
The trembling cup within thy gentle hand  
Doth fear the tempest of a heart unmanned.  
Shall faith, so fragile, shatter at thy breath,  
Or will thy kindness mend what time undoeth?  
Each whispered promise hovers near to death,  
Yet in thy gaze my soul its home pursueth.  
Thy silence, heavy as the midnight rain,  
Falls on my spirit, flooding every seam;  
Yet still I beg for sunlight after pain,  
And nurse the ember of a distant dream.  
  If thou wouldst keep thy word, my heart would prove  
  More strong than glass, and worthy of thy love.

556

If thou wilt turn thine eye from me, sweet sun,  
Let not my heart in shadowed silence run.  
For every dawn that steals thy gold away  
Leaves pallid ghosts to haunt the hopeful day.  
Yet if thy beams withhold their tender fire,  
My spirit’s warmth shall flicker and expire;  
The garden’s blush fades pale beneath thy frown,  
And petals close when thou art not in town.  
Return, bright sovereign, grant thy gentle gaze,  
And paint with light the corners of my maze;  
Restore the songbirds’ trills upon the air,  
And bid the world anew with colors fair.  
  For in thy sight, my soul finds morning’s birth—  
  Without thy glance, I am unmoored from earth.

557

O, wilt thou rend the bark from this frail tree,  
And leave mine heart to wither in the wind?  
Though roots reach deep in earth’s obscurity,  
Thy absence makes my summer days rescind.  
If night enfolds the boughs where hope once grew,  
And bitter frost doth creep along each vein,  
Then all that’s green shall fade from vibrant hue,  
And love, once bright, shall taste of autumn’s pain.  
Yet if thy gentle hand restoreth grace,  
And tend with care the wounds that thou hast made,  
This tree may rise with blossoms to embrace  
The golden dawn and cast away the shade.  
  Thus let not tempests part what fate entwines,  
  But graft thy soul to mine, as ivy twines.

558

O treachery, thou serpent in her breast,  
Why dost thou whisper doubts where love should dwell?  
Thy venom turns her quiet heart to rest,  
And fills with icy fears what once was well.  
A shadow falls upon her gentle eyes,  
Her laughter stilled by winds of sly deceit;  
What honeyed words her honest tongue belies,  
When trust and longing in her bosom meet.  
Yet still I hope one day thy coil will break,  
And leave her spirit bright as morning’s beam,  
Her faith restored, her soul no more to ache,  
Unshackled from the burden of thy scheme.  
  For love, though wounded, may its strength renew—  
  If truth, not treachery, her heart pursue.

559

O treacherous Night, why dost thou steal her trust,  
And clothe my gentle hope in garments dust?  
Thy sable cloak conceals the silvered gleam  
Of promise once alight within my breast.  
Now shadows creep where once did daylight stream,  
And all my whispered vows are dispossessed.  
The silent hours conspire to betray,  
Ensnaring peace with webs of cold dismay.  
Yet in the gloom, her memory soft and bright  
Outshines the stars that fret the lonely skies.  
Though faith be shaken by the wiles of night,  
Her spirit in my longing never dies.  
  So let the dawn disperse thy cruel art—  
  For hope, though buried, pulses in my heart.

560

O gentle heart, why turn’st thou thus from me,  
When Truth herself doth weep at thy disguise?  
Thy silence writes a chill upon the sea,  
And clouds of doubt obscure thy shining eyes.  
Did I offend with words too sharp or cold,  
Or trespass where my presence was unbid?  
The tale of love, too often left untold,  
Now hides within the shadowed thoughts thou hid.  
Yet hope, that stubborn guest, will not depart,  
But lingers near the ruin of my peace;  
It beats its wings against my battered heart,  
And prays thy distant kindness may increase.  
  Return, sweet soul, and let these sorrows cease—  
  Restore the sun, and grant my ache release.

561

O gentle Love, why dost thou turn thy face,  
And lend thy voice to Falsehood’s cruel song?  
What once was sweet is now a bitter chase,  
Where trust and hope are led in shadows long.  
Thy silver tongue, which promised summer’s day,  
Now weaves a winter’s chill about my heart.  
Were vows but dew to vanish and betray,  
Or dreams but dust that scatter and depart?  
Yet still I beg thee, Love, return anew,  
Restore the spring that wilted in thy flight.  
Let honest light dissolve this mournful hue,  
And banish spectres haunting out of sight.  
  If faith and truth in thee may dwell once more,  
  Then joy shall bloom as never known before.

562

O gentle heart, why dost thou turn from me,  
Whilst treason’d Night doth whisper in thine ear?  
The silent moon rides high above the lea,  
And shadows fall where once thy smile was near.  
Upon the breeze, thy distant laughter flies,  
Yet chill and faint it stirs my longing breast;  
A thousand stars behold my weary sighs,  
And mock the hope that will not let me rest.  
If ever dawn might chase this gloom away,  
Or sunlight mend the rift that night hath spun,  
Then might my soul in gentle comfort stay,  
And find its peace once all the dark is done.  
  But till that hour, I wander lost and true,  
  A shade that waits the turning back of you.

563

O gentle heart, why turn’st thou thus away,  
Whilst Hope, thy servant, weeps at Reason’s door?  
The golden morn dissolves in ashen gray,  
And every joy seems fainter than before.  
Where once the lark did herald dreams anew,  
Now silent boughs confess the weight of time;  
Thy gaze, once bright, is veiled in sullen hue,  
And hesitant thy step on hope’s incline.  
Yet stars still wait behind the shroud of night,  
And buds will dare to bloom despite the frost;  
For sorrow finds its end in morning’s light,  
And what seems vanished never can be lost.  
  Return, sweet heart, and let thy trust be shown—  
  No soul finds solace wandering alone.

564

O treach’rous soul, why dost thou turn from me,  
Thy whispers wound as daggers in the night;  
What once was trust now fades in uncertainty,  
A shadowed heart recoils from gentle light.  
The vows we spun unravel strand by strand,  
As autumn leaves from trembling branches fall;  
No tender look nor soft, forgiving hand  
Can mend the breach thy silence doth install.  
Yet memory clings, a bittersweet refrain,  
Of laughter lost and promises undone,  
While love, beset by sorrow’s cold domain,  
Still hopes to feel the warmth of morning sun.  
  Though faith be bruised and dreams in tempest tossed,  
  I mourn the soul I loved, and all I lost.

565

O gentle soul, why dost thou turn thy face,  
And yield thy trust to treachery’s embrace?  
The night conceals the sorrow in its lace,  
While hope lies hidden in a shadowed place.  
Yet morning’s blush may banish all thy grief,  
And light may mend what darkness has undone;  
For time bestows its balm, however brief,  
To heal the wounds that fate and men have spun.  
The fickle world may toss thee in its game,  
And fortune’s wheel may turn with cruel jest,  
But steadfast hearts, though battered, burn the same,  
And find their strength when put unto the test.  
  So rise, sweet soul, and cast thy fears aside—  
  Let faith and gentle courage be thy guide.

566

O gentle Soul, why dost thou turn thy face,  
And whisper secrets dark to faithless Night?  
Dost thou from hope or memory seek thy grace,  
Or shun the longing day’s persistent light?  
Thy silent prayers with shadowed breezes blend,  
While starlight weaves its silver in thy hair;  
The world asleep, and yet thou dost not mend  
Thy troubled heart, nor shed its weight of care.  
What visions haunt thee in the hush of gloom,  
What distant music stirs thy dreaming ear?  
Doth sorrow’s rose in midnight silence bloom,  
Or love’s lost echo bid thee linger here?  
  Yet dawn will find thee, gentle soul, at peace,  
  And from the Night, thy secret griefs release.

567

O soul, why dost thou turn thine eyes away,  
When love, thy suitor, kneels in rueful plight?  
Why shroud thy heart in dusk and close the day,  
When hope stands trembling, longing for thy light?  
The gentle winds do whisper in thy ear,  
Of promises that blossom in the spring,  
Yet thou dost shrink from all that draweth near,  
And silence falls where once the larks did sing.  
Oft have I watched thee drift in lonely seas,  
While golden shores await thy faintest call,  
If thou wouldst only trust the tender breeze,  
And let thy guarded citadel downfall.  
  For love, once spurned, may turn and walk away,  
  And leave thee lost in night that shuns the day.

568

O gentle Soul, why dost thou turn from me,  
Whilst Love, in chains, doth beg thee to remain?  
The moon doth pale to see thy constancy,  
Yet shadows in thine eyes conceal my pain.  
Upon the breeze thy whispered promise flies,  
A fleeting echo lost to silent night;  
I count the tears unshed within thine eyes,  
And seek the dawn to set our hearts aright.  
Dost thou remember vows in gardens fair,  
When summer’s blush adorned thy tender cheek?  
Or hast thou faded like the perfumed air,  
And left me here, thy ghosted form to seek?  
  Yet shouldst thou turn once more and softly speak,  
  My barren heart would bloom, no longer weak.

569

O gentle soul, why dost thou turn from me,  
When Love, thy suitor, kneels in tearful woe?  
Hast thou not heard the sighing of the sea,  
Or seen how roses in the moonlight grow?  
Each blossom bends as if to touch thy hand,  
The nightingale laments his lonely tune,  
Yet thou, unmoved, upon the threshold stand,  
While hope and longing fade beneath the moon.  
If every star were fashioned for thine eyes,  
And every breeze did whisper forth my plea,  
Wouldst thou remain unmoved by all my sighs,  
Or yield at last and gentle-hearted be?  
  O grant me but a word, a fleeting sign,  
  That I may hope thy love might yet be mine.

570

O Heart, why dost thou sharpen Night’s keen blade,  
And bid sweet Trust, thy cherished guest, to flee?  
What tempest in thy silent depths is made  
That turns thy gentle tides to storm-bound sea?  
The moon’s pale hand would calm thy restless ache,  
Yet shadows gather where her fingers pass.  
Dost thou for love thy tender vessel break,  
Or fear the truth that glimmers in the glass?  
No balm of dawn can heal what thou hast torn,  
Nor can the stars redeem thy faith’s retreat.  
Upon thy ramparts hope stands all forlorn,  
While Doubt, unbidden, claims the victor’s seat.  
  Yet, Heart, in darkness, courage finds its part—  
  And learns anew the sunrise in the heart.

571

O treachery, thou serpent in my breast,  
Why dost thou feast upon my gentle heart?  
Thy poison sleepeth not, nor granteth rest,  
But gnaws within as shadows cold impart.  
Thou whisperest in the dark where hopes reside,  
And sow’st suspicion where pure love had grown,  
With honeyed words my trust thou dost deride,  
Till faith and joy are withered to the bone.  
What mask dost wear, that I might know thy face?  
What cloak of reason hides thy fangs from me?  
Yet still I feel thy grasp, thy sly embrace,  
And struggle vainly, longing to be free.  
  Yet though I bleed, let virtue not depart—  
  I’ll heal the wounds thou leav’st upon my heart.

572

O treach’rous Heart, why dost thou rend mine own,  
And teach my soul to turn ‘gainst gentle trust?  
Within thy chambers seeds of doubt are sown,  
Where once did bloom affection pure and just.  
Thou art a fickle master of desire,  
Whose whispers kindle hope and then betray;  
Thy flames consume with ever-hung’ring fire,  
Yet leave me shiv’ring cold at break of day.  
How oft thy promises dissolve to mist,  
And leave behind the bitter taste of rue!  
The lips I kissed, the tender hands I kissed,  
Now haunt my dreams with memories untrue.  
  Yet still I yield, though reason bids me flee—  
  Such is the power thou hast, O heart, o’er me.

573

O treachery, thou serpent in my breast,  
Why dost thou haunt the chambers of my soul?  
With honeyed words thou dost my peace arrest,  
And make my reason yield to thy control.  
Thy shadow falls where once my trust was bright,  
And poisons hope with whispers sharp and sly;  
Thou turn’st the gentle day to fearful night,  
And sows suspicion where no cause doth lie.  
Yet art thou born of wounds not yet made whole,  
A ghost that rises from forgotten wrong;  
Within my heart thou play’st thy cunning role,  
And teachest weakness to be ever strong.  
  O serpent, flee! Restore what thou hast ta’en—  
  Let honest love and faith return again.

574

O treach’rous Heart, why dost thou strike me low,  
And teach my Trust to wear a traitor’s face?  
Thou whisper’st doubts where faith once used to grow,  
And steal’st the sun from out my spirit’s place.  
What poison’d promise didst thou swear to keep,  
That now dissolves in shadows cold and deep?  
Thy flutterings wake me from my gentle sleep  
And leave my hope upon the rocks to weep.  
Yet still I bear thee, Heart, though thou betray,  
For none can live untouched by love’s deceit;  
The sweetest rose must risk the thorn’s array,  
And every joy doth some affliction meet.  
  So teach me, Heart, to love though wracked with woe,  
  For in thy pain, my soul doth richer grow.

575

O treach’rous Night, why dost thou court my trust,  
And steal sweet Hope with false and fleeting breath?  
Thy sable cloak conceals all dreams unjust,  
Yet whispers solace in the arms of death.  
Thou draw’st the curtain on my waking fears,  
And bidst my soul in shadowed silence dwell,  
While Time, unseen, distills my joys to tears  
And weaves deceit within thy dusky spell.  
The moon, thy watchful bride, with silver gaze  
Attends my sorrow in her ghostly flight,  
Her pale embrace unmoved by plaint or praise,  
She marks my longing, captive of the night.  
  Yet when dawn breaks and banishes thy reign,  
  Hope rises new, released from Night’s disdain.

576

If thou wert false, then truth itself would die,  
Yet still I beg thee, swear thy heart is true.  
The stars would dim and darkness cloud the sky,  
Should faithless winds thy whispered vows undo.  
For what is love if not the soul’s pure light,  
A beacon burning through the storms of doubt?  
No tempest born of envy or of spite  
Could ever quench the fire we dream about.  
Yet shadows linger where suspicion grows,  
And silence breeds the seeds of aching fear.  
But in thine eyes, a fairer promise shows,  
A dawn that bids the night to disappear.  
  So let thy oath be sealed with honest breath,  
  For truth in love defies both time and death.

577

If love be false, then truth itself is vain,  
Yet still my soul doth yearn to trust thee more,  
Though hope hath tasted oft the dregs of pain,  
It seeks thy light as sailors seek the shore.  
Thy words, like gentle rain upon my heart,  
Restore the barren fields with tender grace,  
Where doubt and shadow threatened to impart  
A winter’s chill, thou bring’st a warmer place.  
If thou art false, then stars deceive the night,  
And every dawn gives birth to faith betrayed;  
Yet in thine eyes, I see a steadfast light  
That bids me cast aside the fears I’ve made.  
  So let me love, though reason bids me flee;  
  For truth and love are both made whole in thee.

578

Sweet love, thou art the sun and yet my shade,  
Thy gentle hand hath drawn a sharpened knife,  
Within thy smile my trembling heart is laid,  
Yet every breath doth threaten hope and life.  
A thousand roses blossom in thy gaze,  
But thorns are tangled deep within thy hair;  
My soul is captive, lost within thy maze,  
Yet gladly walks the path that leads to care.  
Thy laughter is the rain upon my field,  
Yet storms arise beneath thy whispered word.  
With every wound my truest self revealed,  
And in thy silence, aching dreams are stirred.  
  Though pleasure weds with sorrow, joy with pain,  
  I drink the draught of love, and love again.

579

If thou wouldst turn from truth to falsehood’s face,  
Yet let thy mercy linger in thy heart,  
Let not thy tongue with bitterness disgrace  
The gentle bonds that once did love impart.  
Though shadows fall between thy soul and mine,  
And trust lies shattered, scattered on the floor,  
Remember still the days when light did shine  
And sweet affection dwelled within thy door.  
Condemn me not with wrath that blinds thine eyes,  
But see the wounds that time and sorrow made.  
For in the mirror where reflection lies,  
Both blame and pardon in our hearts are weighed.  
  Forgiveness, like a rose from thorns, may grow—  
  And heal the wounds that parting’s hand bestow.

580

If faith be false, yet let thy eyes be true,  
And let thy lips deny what hearts construe;  
Though whispers roam where jealous doubts accrue,  
Yet in thy gaze, I find my hope renew.  
Let rumor’s shadow darken not thy face,  
Nor envious tongues disturb our gentle night,  
For in thy presence, all my fears erase,  
And banished sorrow yields to sweet delight.  
If words deceive, let silence be thy friend,  
And in thy touch, let honesty reside;  
Though fortunes change and fickle seasons end,  
Love’s quiet pulse shall evermore abide.  
  So if the world be false, and fates unkind,  
  In thee, my constant truth I still shall find.

581

If truth be thine, why dost thou choose to feign,  
Whilst faithless shadows cross thy gentle brow?  
What secret sorrow weaves this subtle chain  
That binds thy heart to silence even now?  
The silver moon doth witness nightly sighs,  
Her quiet beams confess what lips withhold;  
Yet in thine eyes, a deeper longing lies,  
A tale of love and treachery retold.  
Why mask thy soul in veils of pale disguise,  
And clothe thy words in garments not thine own?  
For honest light, though harsh, shall cleanse the skies,  
And mend the trust that time hath overthrown.  
  Unbar thy breast and let thy spirit free:  
  In truth’s embrace, thou art most dear to me.

582

O soul, that once didst wear my trust as gold,  
Now shiver’st cold, with treachery enrolled—  
Thy honeyed words, in velvet shadows told,  
Concealed the frost that in thy breast took hold.  
I wandered blind, enraptured by thy art,  
Whilst serpent lies did coil about my feet;  
The garden’s bloom was poison at its heart,  
And every promise proved a swift deceit.  
Yet in this winter’s chill, I find my fire;  
Betrayal’s blade hath tempered will anew.  
The ashes of my faith, in dark attire,  
Shall birth a dawn untainted, clear, and true.  
  Thus from thy false embrace my heart is freed,  
  And sows from sorrow’s ground a purer seed.

583

O soul, why dost thou wound thyself with doubt,  
When once in trust thy gentle wings did soar?  
Why cast thy gaze on shadow’s winding route,  
And bar the golden threshold of thy door?  
The dawn hath not yet wearied of its light,  
Nor have the stars withdrawn their silver care;  
Though tempests rage and threaten endless night,  
The steadfast heart may find true solace there.  
Remember how thy hope in youth would sing  
Of endless days and skies unmarked by fear;  
Recall the strength that gentle faith can bring,  
And let thy spirit rise on winds once clear.  
  For though the world may press with weight unkind,  
  Within thy trust, thy peace again shalt find.

584

O thou, my soul, divided 'twixt the night  
And day, betrayed by self yet seeking light,  
Within thy breast doth storm and silence fight,  
A tempest caged, a dove in earnest flight.  
The shadowed hours in whispered doubt conspire,  
Yet dawn’s soft hand with golden balm hath pressed  
Upon thy wounds, and bids thee rise the higher,  
To search the east where hope is manifest.  
How oft dost thou thy darker self lament,  
When truth, unmasked, would set thy spirit free?  
Yet from thy flaws are holy lessons sent—  
The stars must pierce the blackest canopy.  
  O soul, embrace the dusk as well as fire,  
  For both shall lead thee to thy heart’s desire.

585

O gentle soul, why turn thee thus away,  
Whilst mine in truth thy falsehood doth embrace?  
The morning weeps to see the night decay,  
Yet I remain, enraptured by thy face.  
What subtle art hath forged thy fleeting smile,  
That lures my hope upon so frail a thread?  
Thy vows, as shifting sands, beguile the while,  
And leave my heart to mourn the words unsaid.  
Yet, in the garden where our promise grew,  
I wander still, a pilgrim to thy shade,  
Recalling whispers, tender, sweet, and true,  
Now withered blooms beneath the vows we made.  
  Though shadows fall and distance doth increase,  
  I love thee still, and pray thy heart finds peace.

586

O, rend my soul not with thy silent scorn,  
For love’s sweet day is met by night forlorn.  
Thy eyes, once dawn, now cast a wintry shade,  
And joy’s bright banner bitter tears have torn.  
How oft in dreams thy gentle hand I’ve laid  
Upon my heart, to soothe its aching moan,  
But waking hours betray what night hath made—  
A fool who loves when all but hope has flown.  
Yet still I wait, as patient rivers do,  
Carving through stone in hope of fertile field.  
My words, unheeded, beg a sign from you,  
A glance, a touch, my sorrow’s balm to yield.  
  If silence be the answer thou dost send,  
  Then let me perish thus, and call it end.

587

O gentle soul, why turn’st thou thus from me,  
When faith and falsehood battle in mine eye?  
Is love so frail that shadowed doubts must be  
The veils through which our truer hearts must sigh?  
Thy silence lingers, heavy as the night,  
And paints the air with secrets left unsaid;  
Yet in thy gaze I see a flick’ring light  
Where hope and fear in trembling balance tread.  
If I have sinned in trusting words too kind,  
Or blinded reason with affection’s flame,  
Let mercy grant some solace to my mind,  
And let not pride our gentle bond defame.  
  For though mistrust may cloud the fairest day,  
  True hearts endure, and find a clearer way.

588

O heart, why dost thou war 'twixt truth and guile,  
And plead for faith where trust hath fled in shame?  
What tempests rage behind thy patient smile,  
What hope persists where love forgets thy name?  
The moon doth pale beneath thy silent ache,  
While midnight hours weigh heavy on thy breast.  
Each vow recalled becomes a wound awake,  
Each promise broken robs thee of thy rest.  
Yet still thou seek’st the dawn with trembling hands,  
And gather’st light from embers nearly cold;  
Thou build’st anew from time’s unyielding sands,  
And dreams of peace within thy grasp dost hold.  
  O heart, endure—let neither pain nor fear  
  Unhouse the truth thy soul was born to bear.

589

O, faithless heart, why dost thou turn from me,  
When loyalty and sin within contend?  
Dost thou not hear my soul’s impassioned plea,  
Nor see the tears that from my eyes descend?  
Thy promises, once sworn beneath the moon,  
Lie scattered now like petals on the ground;  
The honeyed oaths that vanished all too soon  
Have left my trust in silence coldly bound.  
Yet still I linger, tethered to thy gaze,  
And chase the shadowed hope thou mightst return;  
In memory’s halls I wander lost, amaze  
At how devotion’s fire can blindly burn.  
  O, heart untrue, though thou dost cause me pain,  
  I wish for naught but love’s embrace again.

590

If truth be false and falsehood wears the crown,  
Why must my soul with honesty be bound?  
When virtue walks in tatters through the town,  
And flattery in silken robes is found.  
The world adorns the liar’s gilded tongue,  
While silent truth is cast into the shade;  
The songs of fraud are caroled, sweetly sung,  
And trust is oft betrayed, then sorely flayed.  
Yet in my breast, a stubborn spark remains,  
A beacon faint, but steadfast in its glow—  
Though battered by the storm and marred by stains,  
It guides me where the surging tempests blow.  
  For crowns may fall and fleeting glories die,  
  But truth shall rise, though all the world deny.

591

If ever truth did dwell within my breast,  
Why must thy promise turn to bitter jest?  
The golden words thou spak’st, now dispossessed,  
Lie withered where my hopeful heart once pressed.  
Upon the moor of dreams my feet did stray,  
Led by the beacon of thine earnest vow,  
Yet morning’s light hath chased the stars away,  
And dull reality intrudes me now.  
I built a palace from thy fleeting sighs,  
Each stone a wish, each window framed with trust,  
But tempests rose, and under sullen skies  
My towers crumbled, fallen into dust.  
  Yet though I mourn what faithless lips betray,  
  Still in my breast, true love shall find its way.

592

If thou wouldst turn from me, yet let me stay,  
Though cold thy heart, my soul to thine would cleave.  
The silent dusk draws hope and light away,  
Yet in the dark, thy memory I weave.  
Though fortune’s fickle winds may shift thy mind,  
And time may steal the laughter from thine eyes,  
Still, in my breast thy gentle looks I find,  
A beacon bright beneath the sullen skies.  
Let not disdain’s sharp edge thy voice employ,  
Nor banish all the sweetness we have known;  
For in thy absence, love grants scanty joy,  
And leaves me wandering in dreams alone.  
  But if thy heart must from my presence flee,  
  Let love’s last echo softly dwell with me.

593

O, turn not hence thy once-belovèd eyes,  
For shadows fall where once did sunlight play.  
The gentle dawn that warmed our sweet replies  
Now yields its gold to sorrow’s coldest gray.  
Upon thy brow, the memory’s tender gleam  
Flickers and fades as hope begins to wane.  
What once was certain now dissolves in dream,  
And every joy is mingled close with pain.  
Yet, let us not forsake the garden fair  
Where laughter’s echo lingers in the dew;  
Though petals droop in melancholy air,  
Their fragrance whispers love that once we knew.  
  So, let thy gaze remain and softly prove  
  The sun endures within the heart of love.

594

Beneath your smile, I built my trembling trust,  
Yet now it crumbles, brittle glass in hand,  
A fragile hope dissolved by bitter gust,  
And scattered pieces shimmer where they land.  
The gentle words you spoke, once sweet and sure,  
Are echoes lost within a hollow hall,  
While shadows gather where we once were pure,  
And silence deepens as the curtains fall.  
Your laughter, once my summer’s brightest fire,  
Now chills me with a cold I cannot name;  
What once was solace now breeds new desire  
To flee the warmth that flickers into shame.  
  Yet from this shard of sorrow, I arise—  
  A wiser heart, unblinded by your eyes.

595

If love was gold, why did you turn it lead?  
My heart’s own sun eclipsed by shadows near,  
The promise bright that once our spirits fed  
Now lingers dim within a silent tear.  
You spoke of spring, yet winter claimed your voice,  
And petals fell in hush'd, unheeded pain,  
What once was fate now seems a fleeting choice,  
A garden lost beneath relentless rain.  
Yet still I haunt the memory of your smile,  
As hope resists the darkness you bequeath,  
And count the hours in sorrow's patient style,  
Beneath the withered rose and broken wreath.  
  But if you turned my gold to lead so true,  
  Know lead remembers all it once knew you.

596

If trust be glass, you’ve dropped it at my feet,  
Yet still I beg for shards that cut me deep.  
The echo of your promise, bittersweet,  
Returns in sleepless hours I cannot keep.  
Where once your words were gentle as the dawn,  
Now silence braids the shadows in my chest,  
And every sunlit hope I leaned upon  
Is lost beneath the weight of your bequest.  
Yet even as the splinters pierce my skin,  
I gather them as if they might restore  
The vessel that my faith once rested in,  
Still thirsting for the draught I knew before.  
  For love, though fractured, binds me to your name—  
  A fool, perhaps, but constant in my shame.

597

Why did you turn, my sun, to cast me cold,  
When once your light in gentle warmth did fall?  
The golden threads you wove have turned to old  
And brittle straw, unheeded by your call.  
Your radiance once danced upon my cheek,  
A blush of dawn that promised hope anew;  
But now the days grow wan, the nights grow bleak,  
And shadows stretch where once your glory grew.  
What fickle wind has blown your heart away,  
Or clouded o’er the promise in your eyes?  
Did I offend the morning, bid it stray,  
Or did your rising ever mean disguise?  
  Yet still I turn my face toward the sky,  
  And hunger for your warmth, though you pass by.

598

Why let your heart become a fortress cold,  
When I, the storm, would break upon your gate?  
Such ramparts built of caution, stone and old,  
Deny the dawn, and keep the night in wait.  
The thunder of my longing shakes the wall,  
Yet still you hide within your shadowed keep;  
Unheeding to the rain that dares to fall,  
You guard your dreams in silence, buried deep.  
But love, unbidden, finds a slender crack,  
A single window left without a pane,  
And through that breach, the light comes streaming back,  
Dispelling years of solitude and pain.  
  O yield, sweet heart—let not your fears enslave;  
  For peace is found not safe, but in the brave.

599

Why break the vessel where your promise lies,  
And spill the wine of love with silent hands?  
What shadow dims the sunlight in your eyes,  
And chills the heart that one warm word commands?  
Did time itself conspire to turn you cold,  
Or fickle winds persuade your soul to stray?  
The stories in your touch remain untold,  
Yet memory of hope will not decay.  
If lips forget the vows that once they swore,  
And trembling fingers loose the sacred thread,  
Still echoes of our laughter fill the floor,  
And haunt the silent chambers of my bed.  
  Yet should you seek to mend the world we knew,  
  Pour forth your promise, let love’s wine renew.

600

My soul, a garden, once you called your own,  
Now trembles as your shadows haunt the gate.  
Where laughter bloomed, the lonely weeds have grown,  
And petals pale beneath the weight of fate.  
The gentle paths we traced in youthful spring  
Lie overgrown with memories and thorn,  
While distant echoes of your voice still sing  
A ghostly tune that leaves my spirit worn.  
The sun departs, and twilight chills the air,  
Each dusk recalling vows we could not keep;  
Yet hope, a stubborn vine, persists to dare  
And climbs the ruins where my sorrows sleep.  
  Though seasons fade and all that’s sweet has flown,  
  Still, in my heart, your seeds of love are sown.

601

My soul, a castle breached by trusted hands,  
Now pleads for mercy from the shifting sands.  
The banners torn, the ramparts worn and weak,  
I search for solace words dare not to speak.  
The echo of betrayal haunts the halls,  
Where laughter once adorned the marble floor,  
And every shadow cast upon these walls  
Reminds me of the light that is no more.  
Yet hope, a stubborn seed, begins to stir  
Beneath the ruins, reaching for the sun,  
Believing gentle winds may yet confer  
A second spring when all seems lost and done.  
  Though faith is marred by scars that will not fade,  
  I rise anew, my trust but not betrayed.

602

My soul’s a fortress breached by traitor’s hand,  
Its banners torn by whispers in the night,  
Once stood I proud upon this guarded land,  
Now shadows steal the edges of my sight.  
The ramparts tremble with each silent doubt,  
As loyalty dissolves in shifting mist;  
Within these walls, the seeds of fear break out,  
A garden choked by serpents in its midst.  
Yet still I pace these ruined halls alone,  
In search of courage buried ‘neath the stones,  
My heart’s lament a low and hollow tone,  
Its echo weaving through the fractured bones.  
  But from these ashes, hope may yet arise—  
  A phoenix burning bright in faith’s disguise.

603

My soul, a garden scorched by secret lies,  
Still pleads for rain beneath your distant sun,  
Each petal droops with hope that slowly dies,  
While shadows mark the things I can’t outrun.  
The roots remember laughter once we shared,  
Now thirsting for the gentle dew you gave,  
Yet silence grows where sweet affection dared,  
And memory becomes a hollow grave.  
The wind repeats the stories we forgot,  
Unweaving dreams I planted in your name.  
Yet even now, my heart betrays its plot,  
And kindles light within this ashen frame.  
  If ever dawn should break these clouds apart,  
  Let love return to water my poor heart.

604

My soul’s a ship that sails on faithless seas,  
Yet you, my star, refuse to light my way.  
The tempest whispers secrets in the breeze,  
And hope’s faint lantern flickers into gray.  
Upon the waves, I search for signs of you,  
While darkness presses close with silent dread;  
The compass spins, unsure of what is true,  
My heart recalls the gentle words you said.  
A phantom beacon glimmers in the mist,  
But vanishes before I reach its glow.  
My trembling hands, by bitter longing kissed,  
Still steer towards where your vanished footsteps go.  
  Though night denies me comfort from above,  
  I sail this endless sea, compelled by love.

605

Why rend my soul, a garden left to blight,  
When once your sun did bid my roots to grow?  
The petals curled beneath the shroud of night  
Recall the warmth your glance was wont to show.  
What cruel eclipse hath stolen from my skies  
The golden touch that made my wild heart bloom?  
Now shadows cling where morning used to rise,  
And verdant hopes are tangled in their gloom.  
Yet still I crave the promise of your spring,  
The gentle rain that fell from words you spoke.  
Without your care, what fruit may seasons bring  
But bitter seed and branches split or broke?  
  Yet if you turn and tend my earth anew,  
  My soul shall flower once again for you.

606

Why did you turn the lantern in the storm,  
And leave me grasping shadows in my palm?  
Your sudden absence shapes a colder form  
Of night, that stirs the wind with silent qualm.  
The beacon’s glow now flickers on the wall,  
A trembling ghost that dances out of reach,  
While thunder cracks and restless raindrops fall,  
And distant hopes dissolve like salt on beach.  
Yet in the dark, I trace your vanished light,  
Remembering the warmth your flame bestowed;  
The memory endures beyond my sight,  
And guides me, though the path be overgrown.  
  For even as the tempest claims the skies,  
  Love’s ember in my heart shall never die.

607

Within my chest, a garden torn by frost,  
I beg my heart to trust, though trust is lost.  
The budding hopes, once bright beneath the sun,  
Lie shivering where winter’s work is done.  
Each petal dreams of warmth it cannot find,  
Yet roots persist in earth both hard and cold.  
The wind rehearses memories in my mind—  
Of summers green, of blossoms brave and bold.  
Yet now I tend the soil with trembling hand,  
Uncertain if the thaw will come again.  
The promise of a bloom that love had planned  
Seems faint beneath the shadow of my pain.  
  But still, I wait for spring to find its way,  
  And coax my heart to trust another day.

608

Within my chest, a serpent coils in shame,  
Its whispered lies have turned my heart to frost.  
It slithers through my thoughts and speaks my name,  
Reminding me of every hope I’ve lost.  
Its venom chills the blood beneath my skin,  
A shadow cast upon the light I knew.  
Regret and fear entangle deep within,  
And turn the brightest dawn to somber hue.  
Yet still I wage a battle, fierce and long,  
Against the poison winding through my soul.  
For though the serpent’s voice is cold and strong,  
There lingers yet a spark to make me whole.  
  If courage blooms where silent terrors dwell,  
  Perhaps I’ll break the serpent’s bitter spell.

609

Why must my heart be cast a sinking ship,  
While you, the storm, refuse to let me land?  
Each hope I cling to slips beneath the grip  
Of waves you conjure with a careless hand.  
The stars I charted vanish in your night,  
Your tempests steal the compass from my soul,  
And though I search for any saving light,  
Your thunder drowns the beacon from my goal.  
Yet, in this wreck, affection will not drown;  
The salt of longing seasons every wave.  
Though battered, still I will not let love down—  
My vessel’s will is stronger than the grave.  
  So, storm, if you must rage and toss my sea,  
  Know I remain, still seeking liberty.

610

If I am but a bridge you chose to burn,  
Why must my heart in ashes still remain?  
Did embers of your leaving never learn  
That flame once spent should not revive the pain?  
The smoke of yesteryears still haunts my breath,  
Its curling fingers cling to what you gave;  
Though fire consumes, it seldom grants me death,  
But leaves a soul that struggles to be brave.  
Why build anew, if sparks recall the past,  
And cinders whisper secrets in the dark?  
Yet hope, defiant, kindles love to last,  
Its glow surviving long beyond the spark.  
  Though bridges fall and lovers turn away,  
  My heart endures, and dreams of light from gray.

611

Why must you be the dagger in my trust,  
When all my heart was yours, untouched by rust?  
Did not my vows, as diamonds, prove robust,  
And every whispered promise more than dust?  
The midnight hours I kept with hope’s embrace,  
Now shiver in the chill of your retreat.  
I search your gaze and find a stranger’s face,  
Where once the dawn of tenderness was sweet.  
Oh, treacherous love, why do you steal away,  
Leaving a barren field where gardens grew?  
The bloom of truth, now choked by thorns of gray,  
Withers beneath the lies I never knew.  
  Yet from this wreck, my soul shall rise anew—  
  For trust once lost may still be found in true.

612

O Heart, why do you whisper lies at night,  
When daylight begs your truth to walk with me?  
Thou weav’st false hope within the waning light,  
Yet shrink’st from candor’s gaze so tenderly.  
The moon attends thy secrets, pale and shy,  
While morning’s golden hand would draw them forth.  
What fear commands thee, Heart, to thus deny  
The honest pulse that testifies thy worth?  
Art thou so frail, encased in trembling bone,  
That shadows serve as keepers of thy pain?  
For every gentle word remains unknown  
When silence is the monarch of thy reign.  
  Yet I implore thee—brave the breaking dawn,  
  And let thy truest song be built upon.

613

O dearest heart, why let the midnight steal  
The trust that morning’s gentle hands reveal?  
When shadows weave their secrets, dark and real,  
Yet dawn returns to heal what night would seal.  
Upon thy brow, the moonlight softly lays  
A crown of silver, wrought from silent fears,  
Yet in thine eyes, the hope of brighter days  
Outshines the sorrow born of midnight’s tears.  
Let not the hush of night thy spirit sway,  
Nor let its whispers chill thy steadfast fire;  
For every dusk prepares the world for day,  
And every star rekindles lost desire.  
  So rest thee, love, and let thy worries cease—  
  For morning brings thee trust, and gentle peace.

614

O dearest Heart, why turn thy face from mine,  
When Trust, our gentle guest, now weeps alone?  
Upon thy brow, a clouded sorrow’s sign  
Has cast a shadow where our joy had shone.  
Did whispers false invade our hallowed space,  
Or did I falter, blind, and miss thy plea?  
The moon looks on with pale and mournful grace,  
Recalling nights of sweet serenity.  
If Time could spin her golden threads anew  
And bind thy soul with hope’s unbroken thread,  
Then would I speak with words both bold and true,  
And banish from thy gaze all silent dread.  
  Yet here I stand, entreating from afar:  
  O turn again, and be my guiding star.

615

O Heart, why do you lock your doors to me,  
When Hope itself entreats you not to stray?  
Within your silent halls, what shadows be  
That chase the kindly light of love away?  
The rose that once adorned your gentle gate  
Now wilts unseen, bereft of sun and dew;  
You fear the storm, and yet invite your fate  
By shuttering the dawn that seeks you true.  
Yet, hark! The faithful robin sings anew,  
His song a plea to guide you through the night.  
Why cage the lark whose wings would carry you  
Beyond the reach of sorrow’s jealous blight?  
  O Heart, unbar your doors—let morning in,  
  For love denied is sorrow’s surest sin.

616

O gentle heart, why turn thy face away,  
When trust, my humble servant, calls thy name?  
The sunken stars still linger in the gray  
And whisper hope, though all else seems the same.  
If shadows haunt the corners of thy mind,  
Let not their coldness banish gentle light,  
For in thy gaze a kinder world I find,  
A home that renders every sorrow slight.  
Though tempests rise and doubts assail the soul,  
I’ll stand beside thee through the bitter rain;  
For love’s true flame, though battered, yet is whole,  
And in its warmth is healed the deepest pain.  
  So turn thee not, but let thy heart be shown—  
  For trust, once given, makes our fates its own.

617

O gentle love, why turn thy face from me,  
When trust, once bright, now walks in shadowed guise?  
Thy whisper’d vows now drift upon the sea,  
And hope’s fair bloom in withered silence lies.  
Where laughter danced, now heavy sorrows tread,  
The golden dawn eclipsed by twilight’s hand;  
Once-fervent words hang dull, their ardor fled,  
As distant dreams recede to barren land.  
Yet still I seek thy grace in starless night,  
And beg the fates to mend what time has torn;  
For though thy absence dims my soul’s delight,  
My heart endures, by longing ever worn.  
  Return, sweet love, and let our trust renew—  
  Let morning rise, and paint the world anew.

618

O soul, why turn your gentle face from me,  
When trust, our silent bond, begins to fray?  
The shadows gather where the light should be,  
And silence steals the golden words away.  
Once laughter danced upon the summer air,  
And hope did blossom in our woven speech;  
Now all is fraught with worry and despair,  
As distant shores our longing cannot reach.  
Yet still I seek thy spirit in the haze,  
To find the spark that once did brightly burn;  
Through sorrow’s night and memory’s winding maze,  
I search for signs that thou wilt yet return.  
  O let not fear upon our love descend,  
  But turn once more, and let our hearts amend.

619

O soul, my gentle friend, why turn away,  
Enticed by shadows whispering deceit?  
The dawn awaits to gild thy troubled day,  
Yet thou dost flee with silent, hasty feet.  
Upon the wind, the voices softly call,  
Promising solace in the arms of night;  
Yet false their comfort, hollow is their thrall,  
And dim the joy concealed from morning’s light.  
Wilt thou not tarry where the true heart sings,  
And let the sun’s embrace renew thy fire?  
For in thy breast the hope of summer springs,  
A balm to soothe the ache of lost desire.  
  Return, sweet soul, and let thy sorrow cease;  
  In truth’s warm gaze, thou shalt discover peace.

620

O soul, why turn thy gentle gaze away,  
When trust, thy friend, did plead with open hands?  
Upon thy brow the clouds of doubt do stay,  
And cast their shade upon thy fertile lands.  
The morning sun once danced within thine eyes,  
Its warmth a promise whispered in the breeze;  
Yet now the golden hope within thee lies  
Obscured by fear that will not grant thee ease.  
Recall the days when faith was thine to hold,  
When every word was silver, every vow  
A thread of light that wove through cloth of gold—  
Why let the darkness claim thy spirit now?  
  O gentle soul, turn not from love’s embrace;  
  Let trust return, and sorrow be effaced.

621

O soul, why do you turn your face from mine,  
And let cold silence wear your voice as skin?  
The sun once traced our laughter’s golden line,  
Now shadows gather where our hopes had been.  
Your distant gaze, a ship on foreign seas,  
Unanchored by the memory of shore,  
Leaves me to wander, begging with the breeze  
For tidings of the love we held before.  
Yet time, relentless, plucks each tender thread  
That bound our spirits in a woven whole,  
And sows in place the thorns of words unsaid,  
That pierce the fragile fabric of the soul.  
  If thou return and speak as once you knew,  
  My heart would find its voice and answer true.

622

My soul, a battered guest within your hall,  
Implores the silent night to hear its plea,  
In shadowed naves where weary echoes fall,  
It wanders seeking solace, lost and free.  
The moonlight paints regret upon the floor,  
A silver script of longing and of pain;  
Each breath recalls the joy that is no more,  
Yet hopes, through darkness, love may rise again.  
Your kindness once did shelter me from storm,  
A hearth where dreams would gather, softly fed;  
Now coldness creeps where once your heart was warm,  
And empty words hang heavy, left unsaid.  
  Yet still, I linger, captive of your thrall:  
  A soul in chains that will not leave your hall.

623

O soul, why turn thy face from mine in night,  
When all my hopes are begging for thy grace?  
The stars bear witness to thy silent flight,  
And shadows linger softly on thy face.  
Thou art the moon that draws my restless tide,  
Yet veiled in mist, thou dost elude my plea;  
Within thy silence, secret fears reside,  
And in thy absence, sorrow dwells with me.  
O gentle spirit, wilt thou not return,  
And set alight the darkness of my mind?  
For in thy warmth, forgotten fires burn,  
And solace in thy tender gaze I find.  
  If love be true, let not thy heart depart,  
  But grant its light unto my longing heart.

624

O Heart, why turn thy dagger on thine own,  
When trust, thy gentle guest, had made thee whole?  
Dost thou not feel the seeds of doubt are sown  
By shadows whispering upon thy soul?
