Why strike the hand that sought to tend thy pain,  
And cast aside the balm of tender grace?  
What joy is gained when faith is forged in vain,  
Or love made stranger in its rightful place?  
The light that once within thee softly grew  
Now flickers, battered by thine inward storm.  
Yet hope remains, if thou dost seek the true,  
And bid forgiveness in thy breast be warm.  
  Let not despair thy guiding star remain,  
  But bind thy wounds, and learn to trust again.

625

O Heart, why turn your gentle face away,  
When every hope in me still begs you stay?  
The dawn’s soft breath entreats you to remain,  
Yet shadows in your glance bid me dismay.  
I watch the trembling petals of the rose  
That blush and wilt beneath the morning sun—  
So do my spirits falter as you close  
The door on love before it’s yet begun.  
Did some unspoken sorrow paint your eyes,  
Or secret winds from distant longing blow?  
If fate must weave us threads in separate skies,  
Let memory be kind as we let go.  
  Yet if your mercy grant one final plea,  
  Turn, heart, and spend a gentle look on me.

626

O Heart, why turn thy dagger on thy kin,  
Why dress my trust in shadows, sharp and thin?  
What tempest whispers counsel to betray,  
And steals the gentle night from honest day?  
Each promise made, now trembling on thy tongue,  
Falls lifeless, like a song unsweetly sung.  
My soul, a garden laid with careful hand,  
Is trampled by the doubts at thy command.  
Yet still I seek the dawn within thine eyes,  
A fragile hope that treachery denies.  
For love, though oft assailed by cruel despair,  
Clings fast to dreams it cannot quite forswear.  
O let us mend these wounds with patient art,  
And bind the trust that bled from heart to heart.  
For mercy, like the spring, may softly start,  
And raise anew the faith thou didst depart.

627

O Heart, why do you whisper spiteful lies,  
And paint my hope with colors you devise?  
Thou dost betray me with thy cunning art,  
And turn sweet dreams to thorns that pierce my heart.  
When gentle dawn upon the hills appears  
And bathes the world in gold, I feel thy fears—  
A shadow creeping where there should be light,  
A trembling doubt that lingers out of sight.  
Yet still I trust thee, though thy words are sly,  
For in thy chambers truth and falsehood vie,  
And every beat resounds with joy and pain  
As sun and tempest battle in the rain.  
  O heart, be kind, and let thy whispers cease;  
  Restore my faith and grant my spirit peace.

628

O gentle heart, why turn thy face from me,  
When Night herself begs Day for company?  
The stars attend in silent sympathy,  
While shadows dance upon the trembling lea.  
Dost thou not see the sorrow in my eyes,  
A mirror to the longing in the skies?  
The moon doth pale to hear my lonely cries,  
Yet hope’s faint glimmer ever in me lies.  
If morning’s light could warm thy soul anew,  
And chase the chill that keeps thy love at bay,  
I’d gather all the dawns I ever knew  
To paint a golden path that leads thy way.  
  Return, sweet heart, and let my plea be heard:  
  Restore the song to silence long interred.

629

O Heart, why do you whisper him my dreams,  
While Trust, betrayed, still lingers at my door?  
The moonlight spills in soft, forgiving streams  
Yet shadows stretch where faith was kept before.  
His name, a secret sigh upon the night,  
Unbidden drifts upon my weary mind,  
A specter clothed in memory and light,  
Who leaves the wounds he promised to not find.  
I lock my doubts within a silent chest,  
Yet echoes of his laughter seek the key;  
My soul, unrested, yearns for gentle rest,  
But stirs with hope where hope should never be.  
  O Heart, be still and let the silence grow—  
  For love returns where it is least to go.

630

Why must your gentle hand deliver pain,  
While love’s soft whisper turns to sharpened steel?  
The rose once bloomed beneath a summer rain,  
Now wilts in shadows that your words reveal.  
Your eyes, which held the dawn in golden hue,  
Now flicker cold as winter’s distant star.  
The vows you spoke, once tender, sweet, and true,  
Lie broken shards between us, near and far.  
Yet, in this ache, my heart recalls the fire  
That lit the world when first I knew your grace;  
Desire’s ghost, undimmed, will not retire,  
Nor can I banish memory’s embrace.  
  Though sorrow weaves its threads through all I see,  
  I loved, and in that love, was truly free.

631

Though love once crowned thy name with golden light,  
Now shadows stretch where trust had made its claim,  
A garden bloomed within the gentle night,  
But thorns have risen, leaving only blame.  
The laughter lost lies silent on the breeze,  
And echoes faint where once our hearts would meet;  
The summer’s blush has faded from the trees,  
Replaced by autumn’s sigh beneath our feet.  
Yet memory, like dawn, breaks through the gloom,  
Reminding me of warmth that once was ours,  
And though the rose has lost its hopeful bloom,  
Its scent remains amidst the fallen hours.  
  So let the night recall what day denies:  
  Love’s ghost still lingers where its promise lies.

632

Though trust was once the fortress of my soul,  
Your whispered lies have torn its walls apart,  
The gentle faith that made my spirit whole  
Now crumbles, pierced by cunning of your art.  
A garden lush with hope, now overgrown  
With thorns of doubt that choke each tender stem,  
The seeds of truth I planted, overthrown  
By shadows you allowed to enter them.  
Yet in the ruins, daylight dares to gleam,  
A stubborn beam that will not be undone,  
For though I mourn the loss of cherished dream,  
The dawn proclaims the healing has begun.  
  From fractured trust, my strength shall rise anew,  
  And I’ll rebuild my heart, untouched by you.

633

If love is truth, then why do you deceive,  
And leave my honest heart to starve in lies?  
You promised dawn, yet hastened dark to weave,  
And clouded hope that once adorned these skies.  
I gave thee trust as rivers grant the sea,  
Believing tides would ever faithful stay;  
Yet found thy words were ships that turned from me,  
And vanished ghostlike at the break of day.  
Is love a mask that mocks the wearer's face,  
A fleeting spark to dazzle and betray?  
Or doth it live in moments we embrace,  
Yet falter when the world would pull away?  
  Though shadowed now, my heart recalls the sun—  
  For truth and love, I ache when they are one.

634

Why turn away when once your gaze was mine,  
Yet speak of love as though it never died?  
The stars that watched us waltz in sweet design  
Now pale, as if their golden hearts have lied.  
Your laughter lingers softly in the air,  
A ghost that haunts the corridors of night;  
I reach for shadows, finding nothing there—  
A memory that flickers out of sight.  
If time could mend the cracks that fate bestowed,  
Would you return, or wander ever far?  
The silent moon recalls the vows we owed,  
Her silver witness lost behind a star.  
  Yet in my heart, your warmth is still confined—  
  A flame that burns, though you have been unkind.

635

If love be true, why lies it in disguise,  
When trust denied, my soul in silence cries?  
The gentle light that once adorned thine eyes  
Now shrouds itself in doubt’s deceitful guise.  
What whispered oath did turn to empty air,  
While hope, once firm, now trembles on the brink?  
The petals fade upon the rose we share,  
And ink runs thin before I dare to think.  
Yet in the hush that falls when night is near,  
A memory’s touch recalls thy vanished grace;  
It fans the coals of longing, bright and clear,  
Restoring dreams no shadow can erase.  
  If love but wears the mask that pain designed,  
  Still let me seek its truth, and solace find.

636

O soul divided, torn by trust undone,  
Yet hope and heartbreak both within me vie;  
The shadowed chorus sings what I have won,  
While longing threads its sorrow through the sky.  
A fragile peace now weaves between my fears,  
Each promise fractured, echoing the past;  
The silent dawn still glimmers through my tears,  
Its golden hush uncertain, fading fast.  
Yet in this storm, a quiet strength is found,  
A stubborn bloom that rises in the rain;  
Though faith is wounded, dreams are loosely bound,  
They gather in the twilight of my pain.  
  For even ravaged trust can mend anew,  
  If love endures the night and breaks it through.

637

My soul, divided, pleads for peace in vain,  
Yet craves the truth that tore its trust apart;  
A silent war within me breeds disdain  
And shadows hope within a battered heart.  
The dawn betrays the solace night bestows,  
While dreams unravel, thread by thread, to dust.  
In mirrored eyes, a haunted sorrow grows,  
Reflections cracked by loss and broken trust.  
Yet still I wander through uncertain light,  
Each step a question, every breath a plea:  
Will time restore what vanished in the night,  
Or bind these wounds with gentle memory?  
  Though tempest-tossed, my spirit seeks release—  
  To find, at last, a truer, lasting peace.

638

My soul is torn between the truth and lie,  
A faithful heart betrayed by its own doubt,  
As restless winds disturb the placid sky,  
So do my thoughts in anxious storms break out.  
Beneath the mask of calm, a tempest brews,  
Each whispered word a shadow on my mind;  
The light of reason flickers and renews,  
Yet leaves the darkest mysteries behind.  
What comfort lies in certainty’s embrace,  
When every answer births another fear?  
The mirror shows a strangely haunted face,  
Reflected hopes too distant, never near.  
  Yet still I strive to hold to what is right—  
  And seek the dawn that follows after night.

639

My soul, divided, begs for what you spurn,  
Yet clings to hope while watching bridges burn.  
The silent night laments what cannot be,  
As longing drips from every memory.  
I plead with fate to grant my heart reprieve,  
Yet shadows dance where love was once believed.  
The dawn arrives, but still, I do not find  
Release from thoughts that echo in my mind.  
Each hour apart, the ache grows sharp and clear,  
A wound that time and reason cannot heal.  
Still, through the ashes, fragile wishes rise—  
A trembling flame that never truly dies.  
  If ever mercy softens your disdain,  
  My soul, made whole, shall love you once again.

640

O, let not shadows steal the soul I gave,  
Nor leave me lost where hopes and heartache blend.  
Let memory’s gentle hand reach forth to save  
The fragile light that time would seek to rend.  
Upon the hush of night, my vows remain,  
A whispered pledge against the coming shade;  
Though sorrow’s breath may chill with silent pain,  
Love’s ember deep within shall not be swayed.  
If fortune’s fickle winds do toss our days  
And scatter dreams like petals on the ground,  
Still, steadfast faith shall guide me through the maze,  
And by thy grace, my spirit shall be crowned.  
  So let not darkness claim what we have known,  
  For in thy light, my soul is not alone.

641

How can thy smile, so sweet, conceal thy knife,  
That cleaves my soul yet claims to give it life?  
Thy laughter rings as music in mine ear,  
Yet shrouds a storm that draws my spirit near.  
Thy gentle touch, a balm upon my skin,  
Doth hide the thorn that burrows deep within.  
Thy words, like honey, drip with soft deceit,  
They promise peace yet set my heart’s defeat.  
O fair deceiver, masked in beauty’s glow,  
Thy glances warm, yet chill me with their snow.  
I drink thy gaze as though it were pure wine,  
Unknowing poison mingles with the fine.  
Yet bound am I, ensnared by thy delight,  
To chase the sun that ever brings me night.  
For though thy knife hath carved my heart in twain,  
I’d die to taste thy tender smile again.

642

I beg the night to pardon what I’ve done,  
Yet curse the dawn for secrets I conceal,  
The silent sins I’ve whispered to no one  
Return with day, their shadows all too real.  
The moon, complicit, shields my furtive plea,  
While stars bear witness, cold and far removed.  
Each hour’s tick becomes a judge to me,  
Reciting faults I thought the dark had soothed.  
Yet morning’s blush betrays my veiled regret,  
Unraveling the mask I wore with pride.  
No gentle light absolves or lets forget  
The truths that in the hush of night I hide.  
  Still hope persists that mercy might be won—  
  If not from night or dawn, then from the sun.

643

How can my heart condemn what once it praised,  
When trust’s bright lantern now in shadows stays?  
The golden words, like autumn leaves, are razed,  
And silence stalks the halls where laughter plays.  
Each memory, a ghost with wistful eyes,  
Reminds me of the warmth that once was true,  
But hope, unmoored, drifts under sullen skies,  
And doubts, like thorns, grow wild where roses grew.  
Yet can a heart unlearn the songs it knew,  
Or banish echoes from its longing breast?  
The gentle dawn that broke our night in two  
Now hides behind the clouds of lost unrest.  
  Though pain may reign, my love cannot depart—  
  Its root runs deep within my wounded heart.

644

O heart, why leap to trust when trust is pain,  
And crave the sun yet shiver in the shade?  
Why chase the gentle breeze through storm and strain,  
And barter joy for fears that never fade?  
Thou fool, to drink of hope’s uncertain wine,  
And squander peace for dreams that may betray.  
Yet in thy folly, some faint stars still shine,  
And promise dawn though night outlasts the day.  
If wounds have marked thine ever-aching core,  
Let wisdom’s salve be drawn from sorrow’s well.  
Yet still, thou seekest love and yearn for more,  
Though oft the sweetest tale is grief to tell.  
  So, heart, persist, though trials be thy cost—  
  For hope is best when all but hope is lost.

645

If love be truth, then why does falsehood reign,  
Within my chest where hope and sorrow fight,  
A gentle pulse that soothes yet breeds me pain,  
And blinds my reason with deceiving light.  
Sweet words like honey drip from lips untrue,  
Yet in their warmth, my doubting heart is fed;  
I chase the ghost of joy I never knew,  
And lay my weary dreams upon this bed.  
The stars bear witness to my restless plea,  
Their silent vigil keeps my longing still,  
For in the shadowed dusk, I yearn to see  
A sign that love obeys a purer will.  
  Yet though the night conspires to dim my view,  
  I trust the dawn shall show what love is true.

646

If truth once dwelled in vows you swore to me,  
Why does your heart now flee where once it stayed?  
What fickle winds have tossed you out to sea,  
And left our promises to shrink and fade?  
Did dawn reveal some flaw that night concealed,  
Or did my trust grow heavy in your hands?  
The tender faith that once our love annealed  
Now cracks like glass, undone by shifting sands.  
Yet still I search the silence for your voice,  
Some echo of the oaths you once professed—  
Is love a thing that withers by its choice,  
Or was our union never truly blessed?  
  Though hope now dims, I mourn what used to be,  
  And haunt the shade where love has ceased to see.

647

When trust, once sacred, crumbles into dust,  
Yet still I cling to hope, though love's unjust.  
The golden days, now tarnished by distrust,  
Are ghosts that haunt the chambers of my lust.  
The vows we whispered, frail as autumn leaves,  
Lie scattered where the bitter tempests blew;  
My heart, a silent house that nightly grieves,  
Still holds a candle for the thought of you.  
Though shadows stretch across our broken way,  
And memory sharpens every silent pain,  
I search the stars for signs of budding day,  
Believing morning may eclipse the rain.  
  For though the past is lost, my soul endures—  
  In hope that time may heal what love obscures.

648

Within the furnace of my longing breast,  
Thou art the flame that feeds yet brings unrest.  
Thy presence kindles hope and sweet despair,  
A torch that burns the midnight of my care.  
Thy voice, a gentle wind on fevered skin,  
Doth fan my thoughts to blaze with fierce delight.  
Yet absence chills the fire that dwells within,  
And turns the day to shadow-cloaked midnight.  
O cruel joy, to ache and yet adore,  
To yearn for thee and tremble at thy name!  
What alchemy can heal what I endure,  
Or quench the heart that ever feeds its flame?  
  Yet in this fire, my spirit finds its home—  
  For love is ash and ember, spark and loam.

649

Within mine breast a flame of longing lies,  
Yet shadows veil the light that in thee dwells.  
A silent ache beneath these yearning skies  
Doth bind my tongue in sorrow’s secret spells.  
Oft do I watch thee move, a distant star,  
Unknowing of the fires thou hast made.  
Thy laughter reaches gentle, from afar,  
Yet leaves my soul in silent masquerade.  
Each night I dream thy hand within mine own,  
And wake to find the morning cold and bare.  
Desire, a seed in silent darkness sown,  
Doth blossom hope, yet harvests naught but air.  
  But if thy heart should e’er respond to mine,  
  All heaven’s joy within my breast would shine.

650

Within the shadowed chamber of my breast,  
Desire’s flame consumes all gentle rest.  
The silent choir of dreams, by longing pressed,  
Erupts in sighs that leave my soul distressed.  
The moon in pallid grace through curtains peers,  
And paints my hope upon the trembling wall;  
Her borrowed silver light dissolves my fears,  
Yet stirs in me a hunger I recall.  
Oh, would that dawn might cool this fevered ache,  
And with its blush persuade my heart to peace—  
But every morn, new embers would awake,  
And bind my spirit’s yearning with caprice.  
  Thus chained to passion’s wheel, I burn anew,  
  A captive lost, and yet, I burn for you.

651

Within the shadows of my longing heart,  
Thy visage burns—a star I cannot claim,  
Each night anew, desire’s subtle art  
Draws forth thy name within my silent flame.  
Though distance weaves a tapestry of pain,  
Hope’s gentle hand still strokes thy absent face,  
And in my dreams, thy echoes yet remain,  
A fleeting waltz beyond both time and place.  
The moonlight spills confession on the floor,  
While sighs, like whispers, tremble in the air;  
I trace thy absence—what I can’t restore—  
And court the memory I yet must bear.  
  O, cruel fate, that holds thee out of reach,  
  Yet feeds my soul with lessons thou dost teach.

652

Within my breast doth burn a mournful fire,  
That feeds on shadows cast by thy disdain;  
Each hope, once bright, now dressed in funeral attire,  
Is haunted by remembrance of thy reign.  
The silent moon doth witness nightly woe,  
As dreams of thee like phantoms drift and fade;  
A garden withered, where no blossoms grow,  
Reflects the sorrow thy cold heart hath made.  
Yet in the ashes, stubborn embers gleam,  
Defying winds that urge my love to cease,  
And in the midnight hush, I dare to dream  
Of kinder days when longing brought me peace.  
  Thus bound by grief, I nurse love’s dying spark,  
  And seek for dawn to banish memory's dark.

653

Within the shadow’d hall of longing’s fire,  
My soul doth pine, a moth to thine desire.  
Each whispered hope, a candle in the gloom,  
Awakes pale dreams that blossom and expire.  
Thy visage haunts the corners of my room,  
A phantom sweet, whose absence breeds my doom.  
Through midnight’s hush, I trace thy tender name  
Upon the air, and bid my heart resume  
Its fruitless chase, enchanted by the same  
Soft spell that set my quiet blood aflame.  
Unseen, I dwell where secret sorrows gleam,  
And count the hours lost to love’s cruel game,  
Yet in this ache, a fragile joy I deem:  
For even pain, when born of thee, is sweet,  
And makes each breath with hope and anguish meet.

654

Within the prison'd vaults of longing's fire,  
My soul doth wrestle 'twixt delight and woe,  
A captive heart, ensnared by sweet desire,  
Yet chained by fears it dares not fully show.  
The silent echoes haunt the midnight air,  
Each shadow stretched across the stony floor,  
And hope, a candle trembling in despair,  
Flickers, then fades, then bravely burns once more.  
O, absence carves its hollows in my chest,  
While memory, like ivy, climbs the walls,  
Each heartbeat thunders, yearning for the rest  
That only in thy gentle presence falls.  
  Yet still I wait, as patient as the grave,  
  For love to free the heart it longs to save.

655

My soul, a moth, doth circle flame of thee,  
Yet burns with longing’s ash, denied its flight.  
In midnight’s hush, thy glow consumes my plea,  
And draws my trembling wings into the night.  
Each pulse, a hush of hope not yet confessed,  
While shadows paint thy visage on the wall,  
Desire’s ember hidden in my breast,  
Afraid to rise, yet powerless to fall.  
Thy voice, a breeze that stirs the silent air,  
Unfurls my heart, though fear would have it close.  
I chase thy light, though blinded by the glare,  
And bear the wound that only longing knows.  
  Yet, should I burn, let it be thus for thee—  
  To die in flame is sweetest agony.

656

My soul, a lantern hung in midnight's keep,  
Doth flicker 'twixt the longing and the loss,  
Within its flame, the silent shadows creep,  
And mark the time with footsteps soft across.  
The hush of dreams, like petals in the air,  
Descends upon the embers of my thought,  
While hope and dread in spectral forms declare  
The battles that my heart has nightly fought.  
Yet in this gloom, a gentle promise grows,  
A whisper spun from silver-threaded light,  
That even sorrow’s thorned and withered rose  
May blossom in the shelter of the night.  
  So let my lantern burn against the gloom,  
  And guide me softly through the world’s perfume.

657

My soul, a moth, doth yearn for candle’s flame,  
Though wings are singed by longing’s cruel delight,  
I circle ever closer, void of shame,  
Drawn helpless by the promise of its light.  
The golden glow, it dances in my eyes,  
A beacon sweet, yet fraught with subtle pain;  
For every touch, another hope belies,  
And every spark delivers loss again.  
Yet still I flutter, captive to the fire,  
Entranced by warmth I know will be my doom;  
Desire weaves a shroud of soft attire,  
A silken veil spun in the candle’s womb.  
  So let me burn, if but to feel its gleam—  
  For love’s bright agony outshines the dream.

658

My soul, a moth, doth circle round thy flame,  
Yet finds no rest within thy heart’s domain.  
It flutters near, enchanted by thy name,  
And scorched with longing, seeks thee still in vain.  
Thy light, so bright, doth pierce the shrouded night,  
Unveiling dreams that tremble on the air,  
While hope and fear in silent, trembling fight  
Entangle wings within their subtle snare.  
Each gentle glance doth pull me ever near,  
Though every touch, a spark that bids me flee.  
I dwell in shadow, haunted by my fear,  
Yet ever drawn to what consumes in thee.  
  So let me burn, if burning be my part—  
  For in thy fire, I find my restless heart.

659

My soul, a moth, doth flutter near thy flame,  
Yet burns in silence, longing for thy light,  
Entranced by hope, though reason counsels shame,  
I dwell in shadow, dreaming of thy sight.  
Thy eyes, twin lanterns, pierce my shrouded night,  
Their gentle fire doth all my fears disarm;  
No distant star, nor moonbeam half so bright  
Could e’er bestow so warm and sweet a charm.  
I trace thy name in whispers on the air,  
Each syllable a prayer I dare not speak,  
For love, alas, is frail and faint as prayer  
When lips are mute and hearts remain so meek.  
  Yet if thy gaze should turn and fall on me,  
  My soul would soar, unfettered, wild, and free.

660

Within this breast, a hungered fire doth dwell,  
Yet feeds upon the ashes of my will,  
A silent blaze no roaring tongue can tell,  
Whose embers pulse though all the world is still.  
It yearns for light yet shuns the open day,  
And in the shadows finds its secret home.  
It whispers dreams that never fade away,  
And burns for joys it dares not call its own.  
Oft have I sought to quench its fierce desire,  
To bury longing deep in silent dust,  
But hope, rekindled, fans the stubborn fire,  
And tempts my heart again to dare and trust.  
  So let it burn, though reason bids me part—  
  For living flame is better than a heart.

661

Within the furnace of my longing breast,  
Desire’s phantom weaves a restless shade,  
Each sigh ignites a hope I dare not rest,  
And dreams in embers smolder undismayed.  
The night bestows no peace upon my mind,  
For every star recalls thy distant face;  
In silent hours thy absence makes me blind,  
And yearning grows where once was gentle grace.  
Yet in this ache, sweet visions softly rise,  
Thy whispered name is balm upon my pain;  
Though fate condemns my heart to fruitless sighs,  
Affection’s fire within shall yet remain.  
  So let me love, though shadows fill my view—  
  My soul’s bright flame endures, because of you.

662

Within my breast a smoldering hunger lies,  
A flame that feeds on dreams my soul denies.  
It whispers secrets woven through the night,  
And stirs the shadows veiled from mortal eyes.  
Its longing stretches past the faltering light,  
Each hope it kindles, cautious yet so bright,  
Yet every dawn, the hunger grows anew,  
Unquenched by reason, fanned by wistful sight.  
The stars above in silent witness view  
This restless ache that time can scarce subdue,  
Their distant gleam a promise faint but true—  
That though my heart to longing is resigned,  
  In patient wait, desire may find its due,  
  And peace descend upon my troubled mind.

663

Within my breast a silent fire doth grow,  
A candle’s flame that burns yet yields no light;  
Its embered passion flickers soft and slow,  
Unseen by day, more fervent in the night.  
It whispers secrets no ear dares to hear,  
A longing bound in shadows, veiled and deep,  
And in its warmth are woven threads of fear,  
Desires awakened only as I sleep.  
This hidden spark consumes with sweet unrest,  
It feeds upon the hours that slip away;  
In secret chambers, longing is my guest,  
Who stirs my soul and paints my dreams in gray.  
  Yet hope, like dawn, may cast its golden ray,  
  And bid my silent fire to blaze as day.

664

Within the prison of my longing breast,  
Desire’s flame doth burn but finds no guest.  
These silent walls contain my soul’s unrest,  
A captive hope that will not be suppressed.  
Each night I pace the corridors of thought,  
Where shadows dance with memories of thee;  
The keys to freedom, vainly have I sought,  
Yet love remains my sweetest enemy.  
O time, relentless warden of my heart,  
Release me from these fetters forged in pain!  
Or grant the grace that we may never part,  
And let affection’s sunlight fall again.  
  Yet if my yearning must in darkness dwell,  
  It clings to dreams no prison bars can quell.

665

Within the prison of my longing breast,  
Desire doth burn, a flame no balm can quell,  
Each silent night denies my heart its rest,  
And with each dawn, renews the aching spell.  
Thy image haunts the chambers of my mind,  
A ghost that lingers, sweet yet full of pain,  
In every whispered breeze thy voice I find,  
And dream of moments never lived in vain.  
Oh, would that time could turn its ruthless tide,  
And grant my soul the solace it implores,  
Or let me near thee, ever at thy side,  
Unshackled from these solitary shores.  
  Yet hope persists, though reason bids it flee—  
  In dreams, at least, thy heart belongs to me.

666

Desire, that thief, doth haunt my midnight cell,  
And weeps in shadows where my wishes dwell,  
It tiptoes softly through the hush of gloom,  
And stirs the embers in my silent room.  
Its whisper trembles on the candle’s flame,  
A secret longing that I dare not name,  
It paints my dreams with colors rich and deep,  
Yet leaves me restless when I yearn to sleep.  
The stars bear witness to my aching heart,  
As hope and fear in trembling measure part;  
I chase the phantom footsteps on the floor,  
Yet find but echoes vanishing once more.  
  O cruel desire, thy sweet torment give—  
  For in thy chains, I know I truly live.

667

Desire, thou art a ghost that haunts my breast,  
With sighs thou steal’st the quiet from my night;  
Thy footsteps stir the chambers of my rest,  
And fill the silent dark with longing’s light.  
Thou art the flame that flickers in my core,  
A gentle tyrant ruling all within,  
And though I bolt the windows and the door,  
Still dost thou slip, unbidden, ‘neath my skin.  
In daylight hours thou dost in shadow creep,  
A whisper shaping dreams I dare not name,  
And in the hush where waking meets with sleep,  
Thou weav’st thy golden promise, sweet as shame.  
  Yet, though thy chains are forged of mist and fire,  
  I live and die, thy captive to desire.

668

Desire, thou art a restless, burning guest,  
That in my breast dost make a withered nest,  
Thou flit’st like flame that will not be at rest,  
And robb’st my nights and days alike of zest.  
No gentle hand can soothe thy fevered fire,  
Nor reason’s counsel bid thee to depart,  
For thou art fanged and clothed in hot attire,  
A serpent coiled about my trembling heart.  
Yet in thy thrall I find a strange delight,  
A sweetness tangled with a secret pain,  
For even as I curse thy constant blight,  
I beg thee, never leave my soul again.  
  Thus am I bound, and gladly wear my chain,  
  For love and longing ever shall remain.

669

O Desire, thou restless guest within my breast,  
Dost haunt the silent chambers of my night,  
Thy whispered yearnings rob me of my rest,  
And turn sweet dreams to phantoms of delight.  
In waking hours thou dost my thoughts ensnare,  
With visions bright that dance beyond my reach,  
Each hope a star that flickers in the air,  
Each longing lesson that my heart must teach.  
Yet though thy fires consume my peace of mind,  
I would not trade thee for a colder heart;  
For in thy flames some spark of life I find,  
A secret joy that sorrow can't impart.  
  O restless guest, remain within my soul—  
  For thou art ache and ecstasy made whole.

670

Desire, thou restless specter of the night,  
Dost haunt my breast with whispers cold and deep,  
Thy fingers trace my dreams with aching light,  
And rob my weary heart the gift of sleep.  
Thou bid’st me chase the moon’s elusive gleam,  
And drown in honeyed visions yet unseen,  
Each longing sigh dissolves a sweeter dream,  
Yet leaves behind a hunger sharp and keen.  
Thou art a flame that dances in the gloom,  
A shadow’s lover, ever just outstretched,  
Thy presence turns the quiet air to bloom,  
Yet in thy grasp, my soul is never fetched.  
  Still, let me burn, though reason should retire—  
  For life is naught but ashes of desire.

671

O Desire, thou restless thief that haunts my breast,  
Thy shadowed steps do mar my nights with woe,  
Upon my heart thou set’st a ceaseless quest,  
And in thy fire, my reason comes and goes.  
Thou art the midnight whisper on the wind,  
A hunger veiled in silk and perfumed air,  
With every hope, my soul is disciplined,  
Yet tumbles headlong in thy subtle snare.  
I bargain with the dawn to grant me peace,  
Yet morning brings thy longing ever near,  
I beg my wayward pulses for release,  
But thou art ever master of my fear.  
  Yet would I trade thee for a tranquil mind?  
  Nay—better haunted, than to love resigned.

672

My soul, a famished guest at longing's feast,  
Doth pine for shadows love hath long released,  
The banquet cleared, the revelry hath ceased,  
Yet echoes of thy laughter have not ceased.  
Upon the chill and hollow boards I tread,  
Where memories like spectral dancers twine,  
Each ghostly touch recalls the words once said,  
A whispered hope that time will not consign.  
The dawn creeps in, its gold upon my floor,  
Yet finds me lost within a midnight hue;  
No radiance can heal what went before,  
Nor paint my dreams with any shade but blue.  
  Yet still I wait, where silent sorrows meet,  
  For love’s return to make my soul complete.

673

Desire, thou restless guest within my soul,  
Dost haunt mine hours with sighs that none may see,  
Thy whispers, soft, do chide my self-control,  
And paint fair dreams of what may never be.  
Thou art the flame that flickers in the night,  
That shadows reason, leading thought astray,  
With longing's ache thou giv’st my heart its plight,  
And steal’st the peace that once adorned my day.  
Yet though I curse thy ever-burning fire,  
And strive to cast thee out with stoic art,  
I find my will undone by thy desire,  
A willing captive of my wayward heart.  
  So let me wear thy chains, sweet agony,  
  For life is dull without thy tyranny.

674

My soul, a restless guest, doth pine for more,  
Though chained by yearning’s hand to longing’s shore.  
The silent moon attends my midnight plea,  
Its silver gaze unfastening the door  
To secret hopes that drift as waves at sea,  
Each breath a wish the dawn may yet restore.  
Desires, like shadows, lengthen on the wall,  
As dreams, half-chased, retreat with every sun.  
Yet in the hush, I hear the nightbird’s call—  
A promise that the morrow is begun.  
If fate be kind, and fortune heed my cry,  
Then wings of purpose lift my heart anew.  
  For though I wander, hope shall never die,  
  And from these chains, my soul shall break on through.

675

My soul, a pilgrim, wanders through the night,  
Desire doth whisper secrets none requite,  
The moon, pale guardian, veils her borrowed light,  
While silent hopes in shadowed corners bide.  
Upon the wind, lost memories take flight,  
In tangled woods where longing dares to hide;  
Each star above ignites a distant fight  
Between the heart’s unrest and weary pride.  
Yet time, relentless, presses on its way,  
And sorrow weaves its thread through every dream,  
I chase the dawn and pray for break of day  
While truth dissolves within the silver gleam.  
  Still, in the dark, my pilgrim soul aspires—  
  To find the dawn that wakes forgotten fires.

676

My soul, a restless guest, doth pine for thee,  
Yet shadows answer where thy presence fled,  
The silent moon hangs watchful o’er the lea,  
And dreams of golden days now cold and dead.  
I wander through the gardens we once knew,  
Each rose recalls the blush upon thy cheek,  
The trembling leaves repeat thy last adieu,  
Their whispers haunt me when the night is bleak.  
The hours grow long, and hope becomes a knave,  
Unfaithful guide that leads me on in vain;  
The stars bear witness to the wish I crave,  
Yet none can heal this ever-aching pain.  
  Return, sweet spirit, ere my longing cease—  
  And grant my weary heart its longed-for peace.

677

My soul, a prisoner to longing’s chain,  
Doth wander weeping through the silent night,  
Enshrouded deep in melancholy’s reign,  
Bereft of hope, and far from morning’s light.  
Each shadowed hour whispereth thy name,  
A ghostly echo drifting on the breeze,  
While dreams of thee ignite a restless flame  
That leaves my heart with neither rest nor ease.  
The moon, pale witness to my secret woe,  
Surveys my tears with sympathetic gaze,  
And stars, like scattered embers, softly glow  
To gild my sorrow with their ancient blaze.  
  Yet in this darkness, love’s sweet promise gleams—  
  A dawn awaiting at the edge of dreams.

678

Desire, thou ghost that haunt’st my silent breast,  
Dost stir my soul to ache for what I shun,  
In midnight hours, by longing dispossessed,  
Thy whispered winds undo what day has done.  
Thou art the flame that scorches hope’s repose,  
Yet in thy fire I seek a sweeter pain;  
Thy shadow lingers where the true heart goes,  
And binds me fast with soft, enchanted chain.  
I flee thee, yet thy presence ever near  
Is woven through the tapestry of thought.  
I plead for peace, but find thee ever here,  
A specter with the happiness I sought.  
  Yet though I strive to banish thee in vain,  
  Desire, thou art my pleasure and my bane.

679

Desire, thou restless guest within my breast,  
Dost haunt my sleep with sighs of aching flame,  
Thy whispers rob my weary soul of rest,  
And every thought doth tremble at thy name.  
Thou art the shadow cast by midnight’s gloom,  
A golden promise just beyond my grasp;  
Thy fragrant hope doth fill the silent room,  
Yet vanishes whene’er I strive to clasp.  
Why must thou stir the longing in my core,  
And paint with fire the edges of my dream?  
Thou lead’st me ever onward, seeking more,  
To chase the phantom glimmer of thy gleam.  
  Yet though I burn with longing’s sweet unrest,  
  I cherish thee, thou tempest in my chest.

680

Desire, thou restless monarch of my breast,  
With sable wings dost haunt my waking thought,  
Thy midnight whispers rob my soul of rest,  
And leave me tangled in the webs thou’st wrought.  
Thou art the fire that dances in mine eye,  
A fevered thirst no draught can e’er appease,  
A siren’s song beneath a moonlit sky,  
A tempest’s call that will not let me ease.  
Yet sweet thou art, though oft thy touch be pain,  
For in thy grasp life’s colors brighter seem;  
Thy golden promise glimmers through the rain,  
And bids me chase the shadows of a dream.  
  O tyrant fair, whose rule I must obey,  
  Lead on—my heart is thine, come night or day.

681

Desire, thou ghost that haunt’st my hollow breast,  
Doth woo my soul whilst peace refuseth stay,  
Thy burning whispers rob mine hours of rest,  
And chase the gentle gods of thought away.  
In midnight’s hush, thy shadow draweth near,  
A specter clothed in longing’s silver thread;  
Thy honeyed voice, both music sweet and drear,  
Doth stir the embers hope believed was dead.  
Yet in thy flame, I find both woe and bliss,  
For thine embrace is rapture laced with rue;  
The cup thou offer’st brims with poison’s kiss,  
Still, parched with yearning, I thy draught pursue.  
  So ever bound, my captive heart dost pine,  
  For freedom lost, and fevered dreams of thine.

682

Desire, thou restless ghost within my breast,  
Dost haunt my thoughts with shadows of thy will,  
Thy whispered longings rob me of my rest,  
And fill my quiet hours with aching thrill.  
Thou art the flame that burns yet gives no light,  
A hunger growing sharper by the day,  
Thy presence turns the gentle dawn to night,  
And leads my wandering reason far astray.  
I chase thee through the corridors of mind,  
Yet never grasp thy form, elusive shade;  
For every joy in thee I hope to find  
Is lost as soon as dreams begin to fade.  
  Still, though thou leave’st me ruined and undone,  
  I would not part from thee till life is run.

683

Desire, thou tyrant, dost within me dwell,  
And whisper’st dreams my waking heart doth rue;  
Thy honeyed tongue doth ring a golden knell  
That summons hope, then bids it bid adieu.  
Thou paint’st the moon with longing’s silver fire,  
And dress’st the night in robes of fevered thought;  
Each star, a spark to kindle fresh desire,  
Each breath, a chain by which my soul is caught.  
Unyielding lord, thou dost my peace betray,  
And set’st my reason in tumultuous flight;  
Yet in thy thrall, I curse thee and obey,  
A willing captive, trembling with delight.  
  For though thy reign be fraught with joy and pain,  
  Without thy yoke, what heart or love would reign?

684

Oft do I yearn, yet curb my longing soul,  
Desire’s fire chilled by reason’s icy hand,  
My heart, a captive, strains against control,  
While thoughts like restless tides invade the sand.  
A glance, a word, and hope begins to bloom,  
Yet caution weaves a veil before my eyes;  
Within my breast, both fear and joy find room,  
Each whispering their own uncertain lies.  
Sweet dreams by daylight’s sober truth undone,  
I mask the ache with laughter’s fleeting art,  
Yet midnight finds me yearning for the sun,  
And shadows cradle this divided heart.  
  Oft do I yearn, though sense would have me cease—  
  For love, not reason, grants the soul its peace.

685

Within my breast, fierce longing doth contend,  
Yet reason’s chill would bid that longing end.  
Desire’s sharp flame consumes what sense would mend,  
While cautious thought its icy force would send.  
Each night, my restless hopes in darkness bloom,  
Their petals trembling on the edge of sleep,  
As shadows whisper secrets to my gloom,  
And memories, like haunted rivers, sweep.  
Oft have I tried to douse this inner fire,  
To chain my heart with wisdom’s tempered hand,  
But still I yearn, enslaved by pure desire,  
Adrift upon a sea I cannot land.  
  So passion wars with reason, fierce and deep—  
  And in this strife, my soul its vigil keeps.

686

Desire, thou art a flame in wintry shade,  
That warms my soul, yet leaves my joy unmade.  
Thou whisper’st sweet of summer in the frost,  
Yet every touch reminds me what is lost.  
In midnight’s hush, thy embers softly gleam,  
A golden ghost that dances through my dream.  
Though hope would have me chase thy fleeting spark,  
I wander blind, and stumble through the dark.  
Thy promise lingers, honeyed in the air,  
But vanishes when I would hold thee near.  
Such longing weaves its thorns around my heart,  
And makes of solace yet a deeper smart.  
  Yet still I cherish thee, consuming fire,  
  For life is ash without thee, my desire.

687

When longing’s fire consumes my trembling breast,  
Thy cold regard doth bid my hope decay,  
My heart, once bold, now falters with the rest  
Of dreams that dawned and perished in thy sway.  
The moon’s pale gaze reproves my silent grief,  
While midnight winds repeat thy distant name;  
No gentle word bestows my soul relief—  
Desire’s bright torch is spent, yet burns the same.  
Upon the edge of night I plead in vain,  
Enchanted by the shadow of thy smile,  
The bitter sweetness of remembered pain  
Compels my wandering thoughts to thee awhile.  
  Yet if thy heart would turn and glance my way,  
  All sorrow’s night would vanish into day.

688

Thou art the flame to which my soul aspires,  
Yet cold neglect is all my heart acquires.  
Thy presence kindles hope in wintry gloom,  
Yet absence weaves a silence in my room.  
Upon thy glance, the morning’s gold is cast,  
While shadows from thy silence grow and spread;  
Desires like restless rivers running fast  
Are dammed by words unspoken, left for dead.  
If but thy voice would call me from despair,  
And grant the balm my weary heart desires,  
Then would I soar as light upon the air,  
Enthralled and lifted by those gentle fires.  
  Yet still I wait, within this aching night,  
  For love to turn my darkness into light.

689

Thou art the flame that warms my winter’s night,  
Yet chillest hope with shadows of disdain;  
Thy gentle gaze doth set my soul alight,  
Yet oft it turns to frost and leaves me pain.  
Thy voice, a lark that calls the morning sun,  
Doth bid my heart to leap and soar anew,  
Yet ere the day’s bright course is fully run,  
Thy silence falls and dims the golden hue.  
O paradox, my comfort and my woe,  
How swift thy favor melts and then departs!  
A fickle breeze that bids the blossoms grow,  
Yet steals the fragrance from my budding heart.  
  Still, though thy love be both my balm and blight,  
  I’d brave the storm for but a glimpse of light.

690

My soul, a prisoner of hope and dread,  
Doth yearn for fire whilst shiv’ring in the cold;  
Desires for dawn do wrestle in my head  
With shadows inked in memories grown old.  
The golden day breaks promise in the east,  
Yet night’s pale hands still linger on my brow;  
Each hopeful whisper, by a doubt, released,  
Doth weigh upon the heart that would endow  
Its trust to winds that shift as fortune wills,  
Or rivers winding ever out of sight.  
Thus, chained between the valleys and the hills,  
I seek the sun, yet tremble at the night.  
  O let me find, within this sweet unrest,  
  The peace where hope and fear alike may rest.

691

My soul doth hunger, yet it shuns the feast,  
In longing’s fire, cold reason maketh moan,  
Desire’s wild tempest, by restraint increased,  
Doth bid my heart to wander far alone.  
The banquet set with dreams I dare not taste,  
For caution’s hand doth pull my gaze away;  
In shadows deep, my silent hopes are placed,  
While golden joys retreat and fade to gray.  
Yet still within the quiet of my chest,  
A spark endures, defying winter’s chill,  
It whispers promises of gentle rest,  
And kindles faith when all the world is still.  
  O, may this yearning find its peace at last,  
  And turn from future fears to heal the past.

692

My soul doth yearn, yet trembles at its fire,  
In longing bound, yet fettered by disdain,  
Each hope doth spark anew my heart’s desire,  
Yet reason’s voice entreats I should refrain.  
The moon doth witness secrets left unspoke,  
Night’s gentle shroud conceals my hidden ache,  
Within the hush, old dreams are slow to choke,  
And dawn reveals the truths I dare not wake.  
O fickle fate, why dost thou twist my path,  
To lead me near, then bid me turn away?  
Thy whispers fan my joy, then stoke my wrath,  
And taunt my soul with visions frail as day.  
  Yet still I love, though wisdom counsels no,  
  For hearts must dance where star-lit passions glow.

693

Within my breast doth longing wage its war,  
Yet cold indifference reigns where flames should be,  
Desire’s bright embers smothered from afar,  
By reason’s chill and stern austerity.  
The heart, imprisoned, beats against its cage,  
And dreams of summer in a winter’s night,  
While thoughts of passion struggle to engage  
The frost that turns all yearning into blight.  
Yet hope persists, a spark beneath the snow,  
And whispers sweet rebellion to my soul,  
That love, though buried deep, shall someday grow  
And break these chains to render me more whole.  
  So let this silent duel within me cease—  
  And may warm longing rise and grant me peace.

694

My soul, a prisoner of longing's flame,  
Yet chained by silence, dares not voice thy name.  
Each night I pace the corridors of thought,  
Where shadowed hopes and memories are wrought.  
The moonlight weeps upon my windowpane,  
A silent witness to my heart's refrain.  
Thy visage haunts the chambers of my mind,  
In every dream thy gentle eyes I find.  
The hours bleed slow, each longing breath a plea,  
For sweet release, or kind return from thee.  
Yet pride, my gaoler, guards the words I crave,  
And fear of scorn forbids my heart be brave.  
Still, deep within, affection brightly glows,  
A secret garden only passion knows.  
If ever fate should grant my wish be true,  
My soul would speak at last its love for you.

695

My soul doth burn, yet cold despair it keeps,  
Desire's bright flame in shadowed silence dies,  
Within the hush where hope in darkness sleeps,  
The day retreats behind uncertain skies.  
A trembling heart, by secret longing pressed,  
Would chase the sun, but finds no light to guide;  
In sorrow’s grasp, my spirit is undressed,  
Each joy that once was known, now cast aside.  
Yet in the weight of night, I seek thy face,  
A beacon lost amidst the winter’s gloom,  
Thy memory doth haunt this empty space,  
And in its echo, faded roses bloom.  
  Still, let thy gentle voice my grief allay—  
  For love’s true fire shall never fade away.

696

Within my breast doth longing wage its war,  
Yet chained by reason's hand, I dare not soar.  
Bright fancy paints the sky with dreams afar,  
But caution bars the ever-open door.  
Desire’s flame within, so softly burns,  
While shadowed doubt its silent vigil keeps;  
For every hope, a tide of fear returns,  
And tempests stir the calm wherein it sleeps.  
Yet in the night, when reason's chains grow weak,  
The heart will whisper songs it dares not sing,  
And lips, though still, the silent truth will speak,  
Unfurling secret yearnings on the wing.  
  O, let me break these bonds that hold me fast,  
  And find the courage to be free at last.

697

Within my breast doth longing wage its war,  
Yet reason’s chain would fetter every fire;  
Desire’s bright spark, though bound, burns ever more,  
Imprisoned yet unwilling to expire.  
A thousand nights I conjure up thy face,  
Soft as the dawn that gilds the waking earth;  
Yet caution bids my fervor to erase  
The hope that seeks in dreams a second birth.  
Though silence veils the tempest of my thought,  
And duty mutes the whispers of my heart,  
Still, in the hush, thy memory is wrought,  
A tapestry from which I cannot part.  
  So reason wars with passion’s sweet command,  
  While longing’s armies ever make their stand.

698

Within my breast doth longing wage its war,  
Yet reason’s frost denies what passions crave;  
Desire’s bright banners glimmer from afar,  
But caution’s chains my beating heart enslave.  
The moonlight weaves illusions in the night,  
While hope, uncertain, trembles at the gate;  
My soul, divided, shuns both dark and light,  
Afraid to love, yet powerless to hate.  
O, tempests rise with every whispered thought,  
And silence grows where once sweet music played;  
I barter peace for dreams so dearly bought,  
And in the dawn, behold my courage fade.  
  Yet still I yearn, though wisdom bids me cease—  
  For longing is both torment and release.

699

Upon the edge of longing stands my soul,  
Yet reason’s chill doth bid my heart be still;  
Desire, like tides, against these rocks doth roll,  
While wisdom weaves a net against its will.  
The moonlight dances on the silent sea,  
Its silver fingers tracing dreams denied;  
Each star a memory of what could be,  
Yet hope, like morning, cannot be defied.  
The nightingale laments her lonesome tune,  
Her notes entwined with secrets left unsaid;  
The world lies hushed beneath the watchful moon,  
While restless thoughts keep vigil in my head.  
  Though reason reigns, my yearning won’t depart—  
  For love’s true fire still smolders in my heart.

700

Within my breast doth longing wage its war,  
Yet cold disdain encircles all my fire;  
Desire’s bright spark, enshrouded by the scar  
Of hope denied, yet kindles new desire.  
Each glance, a wound; each silence, sharp and deep,  
My heart entombed in frost of thy neglect.  
Yet still, love’s vow within my soul I keep,  
Though reason calls affection to defect.  
O, would thy gaze but soften as the dawn  
That melts the icy hush of winter’s reign,  
Then might my spirit, long in darkness drawn,  
Arise and taste the warmth for which I strain.  
  But should thy heart remain as cold as stone,  
  Still shall I love, though loving be alone.

701

Desire in shadowed silence doth abide,  
Yet burns within, where coldest thoughts reside.  
A subtle flame that trembles in its cage,  
Unseen, unspoken, yet it will not fade.  
It tiptoes soft through corridors of mind,  
In midnight hours, its whispers intertwine  
With dreams that dare not waken in the light,  
But haunt the hush between the day and night.  
Oft do I mask its fervor with a smile,  
And turn away its longing for a while,  
Yet still it lingers, aching to be known,  
A secret seed in barren silence sown.  
  For though I strive to keep its fire at bay,  
  Desire consumes the heart that must obey.

702

My heart’s a lantern lost within your night,  
It burns for you, yet trembles at the flame;  
Each flicker feeds the darkness with its light,  
Desire and doubt entwined, yet both the same.  
Your shadow dances softly on my wall,  
A silent specter whispering your name.  
I chase the echo down the empty hall,  
Where love and fear together play their game.  
The hush between your words becomes my song,  
A melody of longing, sweet and low.  
Within your absence, all my days grow long,  
Yet in your gaze, the stars themselves do glow.  
  O, let me be the torch that guides you home,  
  A beacon burning bright, for you alone.

703

My heart’s a lantern starved of gentle light,  
It burns for you, yet flickers in the gloom,  
Each hope, a fragile moth that braves the night,  
And circles longing’s flame within my room.  
The hush of midnight presses on my chest,  
With shadows sculpting absence in your stead,  
Yet memory, defiant, will not rest—  
It kindles golden embers where you tread.  
Your voice, a distant bell across the moor,  
Entreats my soul to wander from its pain,  
But longing’s tide returns me to the shore  
Where dreams, like driftwood, gather in the rain.  
  Still, should your love ignite my soul anew,  
  My lantern’s glow would blaze its path to you.

704

Desire, a distant star I cannot reach,  
Yet burns within my chest, a silent plea,  
It glimmers just beyond all prudent speech,  
A beacon cast across a restless sea.  
It tempts me forth with whispers in the night,  
And fills my dreams with visions yet unclaimed,  
It shapes my shadow, veils my soul in light,  
And leaves my heart unguarded and untamed.  
Though reason bids me turn my gaze away,  
And cautions hope to slumber in its bed,  
This longing grows anew with every day,  
And paints forbidden wishes in my head.  
  What folly, yet what sweetness in the fire—  
  To live and breathe beneath the star of desire.

705

Your eyes, the lanterns lost to distant shores,  
Illuminate my nights yet grant no day.  
Their haunted gleam the quiet dusk implores  
To draw the shadowed longing far away.  
Within their depths, I seek a gentle fire  
That flickers hope in storms I cannot quell,  
And though my heart aspires to climb up higher,  
It dwells beneath a softly woven spell.  
Your gaze, a tide that pulls and will not break,  
Leaves footprints in the sands of memory.  
I watch the golden morning never wake,  
Content to sail where dreams and darkness be.  
  Yet in this twilight, love’s uncertain art  
  Still kindles secret dawns within my heart.

706

My heart’s a lantern starved of gentle flame,  
It flickers hope where shadows whisper doubt,  
In every hush, it softly calls your name,  
Yet fear’s cold breath threatens to snuff it out.  
I pace the midnight chambers of my mind,  
Each echo draped in memories of you,  
Desire and dread in equal chains combined,  
To bind my thoughts in longing’s sombre hue.  
Where once your laughter kindled summer’s light,  
Now winter weaves its frost upon my chest,  
Yet in the gloom, I dream of sweetest sight—  
Love’s ember waiting, patient for the rest.  
  Should morning break and grace my soul anew,  
  My lantern’s flame will burn for only you.

707

My heart’s a lantern starved of your warm light,  
It burns alone, a moth without its flame,  
The hours stretch long in sorrow’s endless night,  
Each shadow etched with echoes of your name.  
Upon the silent air, your laughter’s trace  
Still lingers, haunting corners of my mind,  
And every dream recalls your vanished face,  
A phantom love that memory can’t unwind.  
I pace the garden where our whispers grew,  
Now tangled brambles choke the blooming rose,  
The moonlight weeps for all I lost with you,  
And mourns the sweetness only absence knows.  
  Yet hope endures, though reason bids it part—  
  Your distant sun still kindles in my heart.

708

My soul, a lantern starved of hopeful flame,  
Desires the dawn yet dreads what light became.  
Night’s velvet shroud enfolds me in its ache,  
Yet whispers promise newness when I wake.  
The silent hours with longing softly burn,  
Each shadowed wish for comfort I discern.  
O gentle winds that stir the sleeping leaves,  
Bear forth my sorrow, hush the heart that grieves.  
If morning’s blush could mend the fractured dark,  
Or heal the wounds that memory leaves stark,  
I’d lift my gaze to where the heavens part  
And beg forgiveness for my trembling heart.  
  Yet time, relentless, draws the night away—  
  And bids my soul to hope for one more day.

709

My soul, a lantern longing for its spark,  
Wanders through corridors of silent ache,  
Each shadow whispering a secret dark,  
Where memories like restless spirits wake.  
The moonlight weaves a tapestry of fears  
Upon the walls my trembling hopes have drawn,  
And time, relentless, brushes back the years  
To nights when dreams, unsullied, softly shone.  
Yet in this gloom, a flicker dares arise—  
A gentle promise kindled in the gloom—  
That every dawn returns to stubborn skies  
And scatters seeds of solace yet to bloom.  
  So, let my lantern burn against the night,  
  And guide me gently toward the coming light.

710

My soul, a lantern starved of fire’s embrace,  
Is haunted by the echo of its ache,  
It wanders lonely through the silent space,  
Entwined with shadows longing to awake.  
A flicker’s hope within the shrouded gloom  
Persists despite the weight of endless night,  
Yet sorrow hangs, a shroud, within my room,  
And memory’s embers faintly yield their light.  
Still, through the cracks, a gentle promise gleams,  
That dawn may spill her gold upon my chest,  
And kindle once again those distant dreams  
Where love’s bright flame in darkness finds its nest.  
  So let my soul, though weary, rise and burn—  
  For in the seeking, lost hearts may return.

711

My soul, a lantern longing in the dusk,  
Burns bright for you, yet flickers in the shade.  
Your gentle voice, a zephyr’s subtle musk,  
Restores the hope that daylight’s hours have frayed.  
Each silent wish becomes a trembling star  
That dances on the edge of midnight’s veil,  
While memories, like petals scattered far,  
Conspire with dreams too delicate to pale.  
The silver hush that gathers in your eyes  
Reveals the dawn that slumbers in my chest,  
And though the world in shadowed slumber lies,  
Your love ignites the east, and I am blessed.  
  So let me be the flame that will not die,  
  A beacon drawn forever to your sky.

712

My soul, a moth, pursues your distant flame,  
Yet finds but ash where once your promise burned.  
Each night I whisper softly to your name,  
And ache for tender lessons never learned.  
The moon recalls the silver of your eyes,  
A radiance that dawn could not eclipse.  
Within the hush, a thousand silent sighs  
Escape the prison of my longing lips.  
The world, it turns, indifferent to our ache,  
While shadows stretch their fingers through the room;  
Yet hope anew each morning dares to wake,  
And scatters petals in the face of gloom.  
  Though love’s bright fire may flicker, fade, or fall,  
  Still to your ember’s glow I yield my all.

713

My soul becomes a lantern in the night,  
Its flame compelled to long for distant stars,  
It burns within, yet yearns for further light,  
And seeks its solace past the prison bars.  
The darkness whispers secrets to my mind,  
As shadows dance upon the trembling wall,  
Yet hope, like dawn, is never far behind,  
And softly breaks despair’s encircling thrall.  
The wind’s lament is music for my heart,  
Its trembling notes a balm for weary pain,  
Each breath a promise that we shall not part,  
Though distance grows and dreams may wax or wane.  
  So let me shine, though midnight shroud the sky—  
  A beacon for the love that cannot die.

714

Within my chest, a furnace softly glows,  
Its yearning smoke disguises all I lack;  
Each secret ember flickers as it grows,  
And shadows curl along my weary back.  
The night enfolds me in its velvet sweep,  
Yet dreams, like moths, are drawn toward the flame;  
I cradle hopes too delicate to keep,  
And whisper prayers too fragile now to name.  
My heart, a bell that tolls for distant shores,  
Repeats its longing in the hush of dawn.  
Desire drifts in through half-forgotten doors,  
While yesterday’s illusions linger on.  
  Still, in this warmth where silent wishes dwell,  
  I forge anew the tales I dare not tell.

715

A hunger coils within, a silent flame,  
That burns for more than ashes I contain.  
It whispers secrets cloaked in starless shame,  
Yet feeds my marrow, pulsing through each vein.  
The moon observes my restless, sleepless eyes,  
Her silver gaze compels my soul to yearn,  
For distant shores where hope and promise rise,  
And from this want, I neither flee nor learn.  
Desire, a tempest threading through my chest,  
Disturbs the peace my reason seeks to sow,  
It will not let my weary spirit rest,  
But kindles dreams where wildest wishes grow.  
  Though hunger haunts with shadows dark and deep,  
  It stirs the life within I vow to keep.

716

Within my chest a silent flame takes hold,  
Yet drowns beneath the tides of doubt and fear,  
A golden light that shivers, wan and cold,  
Its brilliance dimmed whenever night draws near.  
I pace the shadowed halls of my unrest,  
My thoughts, like phantoms, flicker in retreat,  
Each hope that rises swiftly is suppressed,  
And courage falters, trembling and discreet.  
Yet in the hush before the morning breaks,  
When darkness bends and softens into gray,  
The ember stirs, and silently awakes,  
Defiant of the dreams that fade away.  
  O let this flame, though battered by the sea,  
  Burn bright enough to set my spirit free.

717

Desire is ash that stains my waking breath,  
A silent flame that burns yet fears the day.  
It creeps in shadows, bound by hope and death,  
A restless longing kept so far at bay.  
Its whispers haunt the corners of my mind,  
Enticing dreams with honeyed, bitter tongue.  
Yet reason’s chains around my heart are twined,  
And still its song is sweetly, softly sung.  
It lingers in the hush before the dawn,  
A ghostly ember glowing in the gloom.  
With every sigh, another wish is drawn,  
A secret garden tangled into bloom.  
  Thus, in the quiet, yearning makes its plea:  
  Release my soul, and let my spirit be.

718

Desire is rain that stains my barren skin,  
A thirst that grows where hope has never been.  
It falls in secret hours, soft and thin,  
Yet carves a river, deepening within.  
Unbidden, longing drifts upon the air,  
A whisper threading through the silent room,  
It tangles fingers in my loosened hair  
And paints the walls with silhouettes of gloom.  
Each night I hunger for a distant star,  
Yet morning finds the emptiness remains.  
No touch or word can heal what aches afar,  
Nor wash away the memory of pains.  
  Yet still I wait, heart open to the sky—  
  Desire, the only reason I ask why.

719

Desire’s ember smolders in my chest,  
A silent flame that shadows every day,  
Its longing whispers keep my soul unrest,  
And lead my thoughts along a secret way.  
The moonlight paints its silver on my skin,  
While midnight winds confess what I conceal,  
My heart entangled, tethered deep within,  
Afraid to speak the truths I truly feel.  
Yet hope, relentless, fans the quiet spark,  
Imagined futures glimmer in my mind,  
A beacon rising gently through the dark,  
That dreams might gather what I yearn to find.  
  If courage wakes and dares to voice this fire,  
  Perhaps the world will answer my desire.

720

Desire knocks nightly on my weary chest,  
It wears your face and will not let me rest.  
The moonlight spills your shadow on my wall,  
And in the hush, I hear your silent call.  
My heart, a prisoner, paces in its cell,  
Remembering the hours before we fell.  
Your laughter haunts the corners of the night,  
A phantom melody that chills delight.  
The stars conspire to paint you in the skies,  
Their silver ink betrays my longing eyes.  
I chase your echo through the darkened air,  
Each breath a promise, weighted with despair.  
  Yet hope persists where reason bids it flee—  
  Desire, in dreams, restores you back to me.

721

Desire, that thief, invades my quiet mind,  
It stirs the dusk where reason used to reign,  
With silken words it leaves my thoughts confined,  
And sets a fever burning through my brain.  
It paints the world in shades of want and fire,  
Transforming every shadow into gleam,  
Each whispered longing kindles fresh desire,  
And blurs the boundary of truth and dream.  
Yet in its grasp, sweet agony I find,  
A pleasure sharpened by a trace of pain—  
For though it binds the senses, heart, and mind,  
It teaches joy that otherwise were vain.  
  O restless thief, though peace you oft betray,  
  I would not have you wholly turn away.

722

Desire, that thief, invades my restless mind,  
It weaves your shadow through my sleepless nights,  
A longing in my soul I cannot bind,  
Nor quell the fire that in my bosom lights.  
Your laughter lingers long when you are gone,  
Its echo dances softly on the air,  
A golden thread that I must grasp upon,  
Though absence sharpens want beyond compare.  
The moon, pale witness to my silent ache,  
Draws silver secrets from my trembling heart,  
While hope and fear in equal measure wake,  
Entwined within a web they dare not part.  
  Yet still I yearn, a captive to your face,  
  Desire’s willing prisoner, lost in grace.

723

Desire sits beside me in the dark,  
Whispering secrets I dare not impart,  
Her breath a flame that leaves a trembling mark  
Upon the trembling chambers of my heart.  
She weaves her longing through the midnight air,  
A tapestry of wishes left unsaid,  
Her fingers linger lightly in my hair,  
Igniting embers thought forever dead.  
Her eyes, twin lanterns, lead me far astray,  
Beyond the bounds of reason and of fear,  
Where shadows dance and all the world is fey,  
And every whispered want becomes sincere.  
  Yet when the dawn dispels her velvet art,  
  Desire’s echo lingers in my heart.

724

Desire knocks gently at your guarded door,  
A silent guest whom you refuse to see.  
Its shadow dances faintly on the floor,  
A wisp of longing borne on memory.  
You turn your face, pretending not to hear  
The music rising softly in the night,  
Yet every whispered hope grows sharp and clear,  
A flame that bathes your solitude in light.  
Why build such walls to cage the restless heart,  
When dreams slip through the cracks with tender grace?  
No lock can keep the wanting thoughts apart,  
Nor banish secret yearning from its place.  
  So let the gentle guest within at last,  
  And find the joy in what you once held fast.

725

Desire, that ghost who haunts my empty chest,  
Whispers your name to every sleepless night,  
It stirs the coals where longing will not rest,  
And paints your shadow in the failing light.  
The moon laments its distance from the dawn,  
As I lament the distance of your eyes;  
Each breath recalls the touch that now is gone,  
A dream dissolved beneath indifferent skies.  
Yet hope, that ancient alchemist of pain,  
Distills my ache into a sweeter fire,  
And binds my heart with an unceasing chain  
To memories that never shall expire.  
  O, let desire be both wound and balm—  
  For in its ache, I find my sweetest calm.

726

Desire, that ghost, resides within my chest,  
It stirs my soul and will not let me rest.  
It whispers secrets in the midnight gloom,  
And paints my dreams with longing’s fevered crest.  
By day it haunts the corners of each room,  
A silent shadow trailing where I go,  
It hangs upon the air, a sweet perfume,  
A memory that time cannot outgrow.  
I chase its form through corridors of thought,  
Yet grasp but mist, elusive and unkind.  
Each hope I hold, by hungered yearning caught,  
Leaves only ash and embers in my mind.  
  Yet still I crave that fire, though it may cost—  
  For life is dullest where desire is lost.

727

Desire creeps softly through my hollow chest,  
A shadowed guest that will not let me sleep,  
It winds through midnight’s corridors, a quest  
For dreams it sows and secrets it will keep.  
Its fingers trace the outline of my hope,  
A silent whisper shivering my bones,  
Entwined with longing’s ever-tightening rope,  
It sings in dusky, melancholy tones.  
No gentle plea can bid this ache depart,  
Nor reason’s hand dispel its smoldering fire,  
For in the quiet chambers of my heart  
It plants the seeds of unfulfilled desire.  
  Yet in this restless yearning I remain,  
  A soul by passion shaped, by want made plain.

728

My soul, a restless tide, implores the night,  
Desire’s whisper burns against my will,  
The moon’s pale gaze attends my silent plight,  
While longing’s shadow lingers, cold and still.  
Each star, a distant witness to my ache,  
Unfolds a dream that dances out of reach;  
The hush of darkness bids my heart to break,  
Yet hope, elusive, hides beyond my speech.  
My thoughts, like waves, are tossed on fate’s command,  
They crash and scatter on the rocky shore;  
Unanswered prayers slip through my trembling hand,  
Yet yearning’s tide returns forevermore.  
  O night, be gentle—softly draw me near,  
  And soothe the restless winds of doubt and fear.

729

My soul, a restless guest within my chest,  
Whispers for love that never turns its head,  
It seeks a haven where the heart may rest,  
And finds but shadows on the path it’s led.  
The night’s embrace is cold without thy fire,  
Each star a distant hope that will not stay,  
Desire’s echo, longing ever higher,  
Yet dawn removes the fragile dream of day.  
I wander ‘midst the ruins of my thought,  
Where memories like faded petals lie,  
And wrestle with the peace I have not sought,  
While silent tears confess what words deny.  
  Yet still, within the hush of dark’s domain,  
  My spirit yearns for love, and yearns in vain.

730

My soul, a vagrant wind, pursues your fire,  
Yet finds no rest within the arms of night;  
It wanders ‘neath the moon, in hope, entire,  
To taste the dawn that follows from your light.  
The stars conspire to whisper of your grace,  
And every shadow stretches in your name;  
The silent hours linger in this chase,  
While longing fans the ever-hungry flame.  
Your laughter haunts the corridors of sleep,  
A gentle echo dancing through my dreams;  
Within my breast, your memory I keep,  
A river swelling with relentless streams.  
  O, let my spirit find its home in thee,  
  And rest at last where love and solace be.

731

My soul, a restless moth, seeks flame in vain,  
It burns for love that shadows can't sustain.  
Yet in the hush of dusk, I yearn anew,  
Ensnared by dreams that tremble in the rain.  
The night unfolds its velvet, somber hue,  
And whispers secrets only hearts construe.  
Unbidden longings flutter through my chest,  
As hope and sorrow wage their rendezvous.  
I chase the glint of joy that will not rest,  
Each fleeting spark a promise unpossessed.  
Though singed by wishes never to ignite,  
I circle ever closer to the light.  
  For in the dance 'twixt passion and despair,  
  My soul persists, and finds its solace there.

732

Desire sits heavy in my hollow chest,  
It weaves my thoughts with longing, sharp and sly,  
A silent thief that robs my heart of rest,  
And paints false daylight in my midnight sky.  
It whispers honeyed hopes into my ear,  
And bids me chase the phantoms of a dream,  
Each promise sweet, yet never drawing near,  
Each shadow dancing just beyond the gleam.  
I pace the floor, my pulse a frantic drum,  
Imprisoned by the ache I cannot name,  
While reason’s voice grows feeble, deaf, and dumb,  
And passion’s fire devours me like a flame.  
  Yet in this torment, life more brightly glows—  
  For what is joy, if not the thorned rose?

733

Desire whispers softly in my chest at night,  
A restless guest that shadows every thought,  
It weaves its golden thread through black and white,  
And kindles flames of longing never caught.  
The moonlight spills upon my sleepless bed,  
Illuminating dreams I dare not name,  
While silent wishes tangle in my head,  
And every hope burns brighter than a flame.  
Yet reason stands, a sentinel of frost,  
To guard against the folly of my heart,  
But battles with the yearning I have lost,  
For passion tears all careful plans apart.  
  So in this hush, desire’s voice I hear—  
  A song of want that draws my soul so near.

734

Desire knocks softly at my battered door,  
A shadowed guest I cannot bid to leave,  
Its whispers coil along my chamber floor,  
And tangle dreams the night would oft reprieve.  
It weaves its longing through my waking hours,  
A bloom that wilts and blossoms in my chest,  
Both thorn and rose entwined with secret powers,  
A fevered ache that grants my soul no rest.  
I plead with reason, yet the heart is blind,  
The distant moon conspires with the tide,  
While hope and fear are warring in my mind,  
Their voices fused where passions dare confide.  
  Though peace may flee when yearning is so strong,  
  Desire endures and winds my will along.

735

Desire knocks nightly on my hollow chest,

And paints with fever dreams my weary head.

Its silver tongue enticing hope anew,  
Yet leaves me thirsting, aching, craving more—  
A wish that never asks if it is true.

Finds solace in the hush of distant stars,  
While dawn arrives to bid my hunger wake,  
Dispelling mist and healing midnight’s scars.  
  Yet still, when darkness falls, I yield again,  
  To longing’s gentle touch and whispered pain.

736

Desire knocks nightly at my lonely door,  
Her restless hands leave shadows on my skin,  
She weaves her longing through my dreams once more,  
A silent tempest raging deep within.  
She paints her wishes on my windowpane,  
With every breath, her heat ignites the air,  
The hush of midnight amplifies my pain,  
A hunger born of hope and dark despair.  
My heart, a captive, trembles at her call,  
Each whispered promise pulls me to the flame,  
Yet dawn arrives, and silence covers all,  
The echo of her touch and whispered name.  
  Yet still I wait, though reason bids me flee,  
  For night returns, and brings her back to me.

737

Desire sits restless, whispering in my chest,  
A shadow longing for what won’t be mine,  
It weaves through every moment, never rest,  
A silent ache that grows with passing time.  
It paints the dawn with colors never seen,  
And haunts the dusk with sighs of failed intent,  
It calls me forth where I have never been,  
And spends my hope as if it’s cheaply spent.  
Though reason bids me chain these wayward dreams,  
Yet still they slip and flutter past control,  
They shimmer bright in moon’s enchanting beams,  
And kindle wildest tempests in my soul.  
  So let desire’s shadow in me stay,  
  For hope, though fleeting, lights my heart’s array.

738

I crave your touch, yet shun the fire it brings,  
Your nearness soothes, but leaves my spirit torn.  
Desire, like wind, unsettles tender wings,  
And seeds of longing in my heart are sown.  
A gaze, a word, and all my senses reel,  
Yet caution weaves its tether round my mind.  
The taste of hope is balanced on a keel  
Of doubt, whose shadows leave my trust confined.  
I dream of nights where distance is undone,  
Of whispered vows that dare the breaking dawn.  
But fear, relentless, quells what has begun,  
And bids me hide the ache I dwell upon.  
  Yet still, between the longing and the ache,  
  I love thee more than any risk I take.

739

Within my chest, fierce longing stirs and sighs,  
Yet reason’s chill denies what heart desires.  
A silent war is waged behind my eyes,  
My passion smolders, bound by frosted wires.  
The moonlight weeps upon a restless bed,  
While shadows stretch and tangle in the gloom.  
Though hope’s pale flame within me has not fled,  
It flickers low, oppressed by silent doom.  
O, if my soul were free of reason’s chain,  
I’d fly upon the wings of fevered dreams,  
And taste the honeyed balm that soothes my pain,  
Unfettered by the cold, relentless schemes.  
  Yet still I wait, where longing dares not roam—  
  A captive heart, in winter’s silent home.

740

My longing fights against the peace I crave,  
Desire’s flame consumes what reason saves;  
Within my breast, a storm I cannot stave,  
A prisoner bound by what the heart enslaves.  
The silent night, a witness to my plight,  
Pale moonlight weaves its solace on my skin,  
Yet shadows deepen with the waning light,  
And restless dreams betray the calm within.  
I seek retreat in thought, but passion grows,  
Each hope defies the quiet I pursue;  
The gentle wind, it whispers as it blows  
Of wishes lost, and promises untrue.  
  Yet still I yearn, though wisdom bids me cease—  
  For longing is the cost of stolen peace.

741

I crave the warmth your distant eyes deny,  
Desire burns bright where cold indifference lies.  
My hopes, like trembling stars, adorn the sky,  
Yet darkness veils the light that in me dies.  
I speak in sighs, my words but shadows cast,  
Upon the marble silence of your face.  
Each moment aches, remembering the past,  
When laughter danced with love in sweet embrace.  
The frost that settles round your guarded heart  
Chills every spark that dares within me grow,  
And though I plead, the heavens stand apart,  
Impassive to the longing that I show.  
  Yet still, I seek your gaze to end my night,  
  And beg the dawn to grant my soul respite.

742

Desire burns cold within this longing chest,  
Yet in your warmth my heart finds winter’s chill,  
A fire’s ache that will not let me rest,  
But ever seeks your light, my soul to fill.  
The moon’s pale gaze reflects my silent hope,  
While shadows stretch between our distant eyes;  
I stumble through the dark, yet blindly grope  
For whispered dreams that dawn may realize.  
Your laughter rings like bells on frosted air,  
Each note a promise spring will soon return.  
I trace your memory, fragile and fair,  
And through the storm, for your embrace I yearn.  
  Though seasons turn and stars may fade above,  
  No frost can quell the root of longing love.

743

My yearning heart pursues what flees my hand,  
While shadows bloom where once your promise lay,  
The golden hour forgets what we had planned,  
And morning steals the colors from the day.  
The echo of your voice in silence grows,  
A haunting song that will not be denied;  
In empty halls, the memory still glows,  
Yet hope and sorrow walk here side by side.  
I trace the light that lingers on your face,  
A ghostly sun that warms the bitter night;  
Though time conspires to steal away all grace,  
Desire endures beyond its failing light.  
  If love be lost where all my wishes dwell,  
  Then let my heart remember, and rebel.

744

My soul is split between a wish and woe,  
Desire’s flame burns cold in shadowed light,  
The silent ache no gentle winds can know,  
A longing lost betwixt the day and night.  
Within my chest, a tempest gently weeps,  
For dreams that falter, trembling to depart,  
While hope, like fragile blossom, softly sleeps  
And doubts take root within my wary heart.  
Yet in the dusk, a whisper stirs anew,  
A subtle promise carried on the breeze,  
That sorrow’s shade might soften into hue,  
And grant these restless thoughts their needed ease.  
  Thus torn I stand, ‘twixt yearning and regret,  
  In hope that dawn and solace may be met.

745

Within the hollow chambers of my chest,  
Desire wars with silence, peace with ache,  
A tempest stirs where hope is dispossessed,  
And longing wakes as every dream I make.  
The night enfolds my thoughts in sable shade,  
Yet still the embers of my wishes burn,  
Each sigh a secret vow I once had made,

  Love’s fragile seed breaks bravely through the ground.

746

My soul, a flame that burns yet shuns the fire,  
Longs for the light yet trembles in the shade,  
It strains for heights that ever rise up higher,  
Yet falters where its inner doubts are laid.  
Desire and dread in silent conflict strive,  
As hope and fear wage wars within my breast;  
From restless dreams my weary thoughts derive,  
No haven found, no gentle place of rest.  
Yet still I chase the dawn on trembling feet,  
Though shadows cling and whisper of despair,  
Each promise of the sun both cold and sweet,  
Its golden touch both burden and repair.  
  O grant me strength to let the darkness part,  
  And kindle steadfast light within my heart.

747
