Desires warmth from hearts that shun its light;  
It braves the wind, enduring silent pain,  
And flickers faint against the pressing night.  
Yet hope persists beneath the sodden gloom,  
A golden ember sheltered in my chest,  
That dreams of spring, of lilacs yet to bloom,  
And finds in longing's ache a strange bequest.  
For every drop that falls upon my flame  
Is but the world’s attempt to quench my fire,  
Yet still I blaze, refusing to grow tame,  
My spirit kindled by its own desire.  
  So let the storm about my lantern swirl—  
  Within, I hold the summer of the world.

748

My longing soul is haunted by your name,  
Yet shadows fall where once hope’s sunlight lay,  
The silent night ignites the hidden flame  
That aches within my breast till break of day.

Each whispered word recalls a sweeter time,  
When laughter wove our hearts in bright array,

  O, if thy heart should answer to my plea,  
  Let longing’s ghost at last be set free.

749

My soul, a captive aching for release,

And midnight’s chill invades the trembling air;

As silent echoes answer every prayer.

Promising roses where now only doom  
Paints with gray brush what once the morning knew.  
  O, may love’s gentle hand unlock this door,  
  And teach my soul to wander free once more.

750

Desire burns bright, yet leaves my spirit cold,  
I chase what’s near, but grasp what lies afar,  
The silver dreams I covet turn to gold  
Within the forge of each internal scar.  
A whisper tempts me through the silent night,  
While shadows dance upon my chamber wall;  
My longing’s flame devours each flickering light  
And draws me deeper in its restless thrall.  
What solace dwells within the heart’s unrest?  
What peace can bloom from seeds of discontent?  
I trade my comfort for this aching quest,  
And wonder where my hopeful yearning went.  
  Yet in this fire, my soul is wrought anew—  
  For all I seek, at last, I find in you.

Within my chest, a fire both sweet and dire,

And scatters peace like footprints in the sand.

That bids my soul to cast its caution out.  
  Though reason’s frost may grip my trembling core,  
  Desire’s flame will burn forevermore.

752

Desire burns within my chest, yet chills my soul,

And bids me reach for hands I cannot hold.

  Yet from this ache, a sweeter truth is spun—  
  The fire of longing proves my heart is one.

753

Within my chest, a fire coldly burns,  
Desire’s bright sun eclipsed by shadowed doubt,

And hope’s green shoots are choked and withered out.

  And wait the dawn to banish all the dark.

756

Within my breast doth smolder fierce desire,

757

Thou art the flame that burns my soul to dust,  
Yet in thy warmth, I find my ruin sown.  
Thy glance ignites a longing fierce and just,  
And leaves me lost within a world unknown.  
A tender spark that dances in thine eyes,  
Doth set ablaze the fortress of my heart.  
In every whispered vow and soft disguise,  
I see the fire and feel its aching start.  
Though ashes gather where thy passion fell,  
Still hope arises from the embers’ gleam;  
For in thy presence, silent secrets swell,  
And every sigh becomes a lover’s dream.  
  So let me burn, consumed by love’s delight—  
  A willing moth that yearns to chase thy light.

758

Thou art the flame that feeds my soul with frost,  
Desire’s cruel fire, in longing lost.  
Thy gaze, a midnight star that pulls me near,  
Yet leaves me wandering in shadows tossed.  
My heart, a captive bird, sings out in fear,  
Its wings entangled by thy whispered cost.  
What sweet affliction dost thy presence bear,  
That joy and sorrow equally accost?  
I seek thee in the hush of silver dawn,  
Where hope and memory entwine as one;  
Yet every step toward thee is withdrawn,  
As fleeting as the light when day is done.  
  So let me burn upon this wintry pyre:  
  For love is both the frost and cruelest fire.

759

Thou art the flame that mocks my withered fire,  
A star that laughs whilst I in darkness pine;  
Thy visage wakes forgotten, lost desire,  
While I am chained to sorrow’s slow decline.  
Around thee spring’s bright garlands ever bloom,  
Yet winter’s frost encases all my days.  
Thy laughter banishes the midnight gloom,  
But I must wander through a shadow’s maze.  
A single glance from thee would warm my night,  
Restore the glory youth and hope once gave;  
Without thy sun, my world admits no light,  
My heart is but a solitary grave.  
  Yet still I burn, a moth caught in thy flame,  
  And love thee, hopeless, though thou know’st my name.

760

Desire’s flame doth scorch my withered core,  
Whilst thou, cold moon, refus’st to grant me light,  
In shadowed silence, hope exists no more,  
Yet still I yearn for solace in the night.  
Thy distant gleam, aloof and pale above,  
Mocks every plea my trembling lips confess;  
I wander lost, denied the balm of love,  
Bereft of comfort, tangled in distress.  
Though time doth steal the blush from youthful skin,  
My longing lingers, stubborn as the tide.  
Each empty promise wrestles hope within,  
And leaves my soul by heartbreak’s hand defied.  
  But if thy heart, once chill, should pity find,  
  Desire’s flame anew thy light may bind.

761

Thou art the flame that scorches hope to dust,  
Yet bids my frostbitten devotion burn.  
Within thy gaze, my trembling heart I trust,  
And from thy voice, my wav’ring spirits learn.  
A tempest stirs beneath thy silken skin,  
Where longing’s embers kindle secret fire;  
I, captive to the fever locked within,  
Am drawn to ruin by my own desire.  
The night enfolds my pleas in velvet gloom,  
Yet still thy memory kindles aching light.  
Each hour apart is but a silent tomb,  
Each whispered dream a moth consumed by night.  
  Yet if thy warmth would grace my soul anew,  
  From ash and ice, I’d rise and live for you.

762

My soul, a famished wolf, doth gnaw within,  
Desire’s sharp fangs do pierce my hollow breast,  
It prowls the moonlit wastes of hope and sin,  
And finds in barren dreams no place to rest.  
The scent of longing rides upon the air,  
A phantom feast forever out of reach;  
Each step is haunted by the hungered snare,  
Each shadow whispers what it cannot teach.  
I chase the fleeting echoes of delight,  
Yet stumble o’er the bones of vanished days;  
Night’s silvered silence deepens my affright,  
While memory flickers through the ashen haze.  
  Yet still I howl beneath the watching skies,  
  In search of solace love alone supplies.

763

My soul, a moth, doth burn upon thy flame,  
Yet curses light that draws me to thy pyre;  
Enchanted by the heat I cannot tame,  
I flutter closer, scorched by my desire.  
Thy eyes, twin lanterns, beckon in the night,  
While shadows dance and whisper secret fears.  
I chase thy glow and shun the prudent fright,  
Though every step but draws me near to tears.  
O cruel delight, that lures and then betrays,  
Thy beauty is my rapture and my bane.  
Yet still I circle, lost within thy blaze,  
A captive bound in love’s consuming chain.  
  So let me perish, drawn by fatal gleam—  
  For dying thus, I live within my dream.

764

My soul, a furnace stoked with longing's fire,  
Consumes itself upon the coals of need,  
Each breath a bellows, fanning hot desire,  
My heart, an anvil where all hopes are freed.  
Within the forge of night, my wishes bend,  
As iron shapes to will beneath the flame;  
Old dreams are hammered, broken, made to mend,  
Yet still they rise, persistent in their claim.  
The shadows on the wall are not my own,  
They flicker, dance with memories long past,  
And leave me stranded, weary and alone,  
A ghost of yearning tethered to the last.  
  Yet in these embers, hope and pain conspire—  
  From ashes, love may yet again aspire.

765

My soul, a moth that burns upon thy flame,  
Doth flutter close, yet finds thee cold as stone;  
In hope, I circle, whispering thy name,  
While shadowed silence claims thee for its own.  
The night, complicit, veils thy distant gaze,  
And draws a curtain soft about thy heart;  
Yet still I wander through this misty maze,  
Ensnared by longing, doomed to play my part.  
The hours drip slow as wax in candle’s light,  
Each moment stretched upon a rack of pain;  
Yet in thy absence, dreams take sudden flight,  
And in their wings thy phantom touch remain.  
  Though scorched and lost, I seek thee evermore,  
  For love, once kindled, dares not flee the door.

766

My soul, a moth, doth flit to flame's cruel lure,  
Yet burns in shadows cast by thy disdain,  
Each longing wing beats hope that is unsure,  
While love’s bright torch doth kindle secret pain.  
I chase the light that dances from thy eyes,  
And court the warmth thy distant smile bestows,  
Yet find my fate where silent darkness lies,  
For mercy from thy lips, the night foregoes.  
If I could, like the dawn, dispel thy gloom,  
Or teach the stars to whisper in thy ear,  
Then might my heart, unburdened, find its bloom,  
And in thy gaze, my purpose reappear.  
  Yet still I yearn, though reason bids me flee—  
  A moth undone by love’s fierce alchemy.

767

My soul, a moth, doth scorch upon thy flame,  
Yet thou, cold star, refusest me thy fire;  
I flutter close, bewildered by thy name,  
And singe my wings upon thy bright desire.  
Thy silence chills the marrow of my thought,  
Yet still I circle, drawn by hope's deceit,  
For every spark my fevered longing sought  
Is lost amid the frost beneath thy feet.  
If love be winter, let me bear the snow,  
And perish gladly in thy distant light;  
Yet if a single glance thou wouldst bestow,  
My darkness would transform to purest white.  
  O let me burn, or else release my soul—  
  For cold or fire, I yield to thy control.

768

Desire, thou serpent coiled within my breast,  
Dost whisper poison where my hopes should grow,  
Thy silver tongue disturbs my tranquil rest  
And stirs a fire that reason cannot know.  
With velvet scales, thou wind’st through every thought,  
Enticing dreams to blossom then decay,  
A honeyed trap with bitter lessons fraught,  
That leads the faithful heart so far astray.  
Yet in thy clutch, the world is bright and keen,  
Each longing breath a sweet, forbidden wine,  
Though oft thy gifts are seldom what they seem,  
Still do I chase the shadows that are thine.  
  Oh, serpent, wrap me gently or depart—  
  For peace is exiled when thou hold’st my heart.

769

Desire, thou serpent coiled within my breast,  
Thy venom taints the honey of my will,  
With whispered wants that steal away my rest,  
And lure my reason down the shadowed hill.  
Thou paint’st forbidden fruits with colors bright,  
And set’st them just beyond my trembling hand,  
So every hour is vexed with inward fight,  
And hope is lost upon thy shifting sand.  
Yet in thy fire some secret sweetness lies,  
A spark that kindles all my soul’s delight;  
For though I curse the ache thy art supplies,  
I long to taste the darkness and the light.  
  O, cruel friend, whose chains I wear unseen,  
  Thou art the wound—and thou alone the keen.

770

Desire, thou serpent coiled within my breast,  
Thy venom taints the dreams I once did keep,  
With whispered wants that grant my soul no rest,  
And rob the gentle quiet from my sleep.  
Thou art the fire that burns yet gives no light,  
A shadowed flame that flickers in the gloom,  
Temptation’s hand that leads me through the night,  
To chambers hung with tapestries of doom.  
Yet, in thy gaze, a promise lies concealed,  
A taste of rapture laced with bitter rue;  
Thy silken coils would have my fate revealed,  
And bind me fast, though I would break from you.  
  Still, in thy thrall I linger, lost, undone—  
  For I am night, and thou my rising sun.

771

Desire, thou art a serpent in my breast,  
Whose fangs of longing poison all my peace,  
In midnight hours, thou robb’st me of my rest,  
And bid’st the golden fires of reason cease.  
Thy whispers curl like smoke about my mind,  
Enticing dreams with honeyed, fleeting breath;  
Thou mak’st me chase what I shall never find,  
And lead’st me dancing to the brink of death.  
Sweet tormentor, thy promise ever near,  
Yet vanishing like dew before the sun;  
Each hope, a phantom, fashioned from my fear,  
Each joy, a shadow, lost ere it’s begun.  
  Yet, though I suffer by thy subtle art,  
  I would not pluck thee from my aching heart.

772

Desire, thou serpent coiled within my breast,  
Dost gnaw with fangs that never taste of gain,  
Thy whisper turns my quiet hours to unrest,  
And fills the sweetest joy with secret pain.  
Thou art the fire that burns but gives no light,  
A shadow stretched across the gleaming day,  
A haunting voice that calls me through the night  
And leads my willing heart astray.  
Yet in thy grip, I find a living spark,  
A pulse that stirs the blood and wakes the mind,  
A yearning flame that kindles in the dark  
And leaves the drowsy bonds of peace behind.  
  Though oft I curse thy name with weary breath,  
  I know I’ll love thee true until my death.

773

Desire, thou serpent coiled within my breast,  
Dost bite the hand that feeds thee, leaving pain,  
Yet whisper’st sweet that I am surely blessed  
To taste the fruit forbidden to the sane.  
Thou draw’st my senses forth with honeyed speech,  
And paint’st illusions on the dust of mind,  
A paradise forever out of reach,  
A phantom hope that reason cannot bind.  
Each night thou wind’st about my dreaming heart,  
And plant’st thy venom deeper than before;  
My will, thy prey, is torn and pulled apart,  
Yet hungers still for what thou dost implore.  
  O, cruel flame that burns and does not slay,  
  Thy longing leads me ever more astray.

774

Desire, thou serpent coiled within my breast,  
Doth whisper poison where my hopes should dwell,  
Thy silver tongue denies my heart its rest,  
And weaves enchantments with a secret spell.  
Thou draw’st me forth to gardens overgrown  
Where roses bloom and brambles twist the way,  
With every step, the path becomes thine own,  
Though reason bids my trembling feet to stay.  
Thine eyes are torches burning in the night,  
Yet oft they blind me to the coming dawn,  
I chase the promise glimmering in their light,  
While innocence and peace are swiftly gone.  
  Yet still, I hold thee close, my sweetest pain—  
  For without thee, what songs or dreams remain?

775

Desire, thou serpent coiled within my breast,  
Thou whisper’st poison where my reason sleeps,  
With honeyed tongue my waking thoughts dost wrest,  
And sow’st a garden wild that darkness reaps.  
Thy velvet scales conceal a burning fire,  
A fevered longing none but thee can sate,  
Yet every balm thou grant’st fans new desire,  
And turns my peace to ashes of debate.  
I chase thy shadow through the haunted gloom,  
Each step enwinds me deeper in thy snare,  
Thy perfumed promise blossoms into doom,  
Yet still I breathe the incense of the air.  
  O tyrant sweet, my heart is still thy prey—  
  I love the chains I cannot cast away.

776

O thou, whose beauty kindles fiends within,  
Desire, that serpent, coils about my breast,  
Its venom sweet as honey on my skin,  
Its whispers rob my nightly dreams of rest.  
I chase thy shadow through the haunted lane,  
Where moonlight paints thy visage on the dew;  
Each star conspires in my fevered brain  
To shape thy lips in every shade of blue.  
Yet virtue wars against my heart’s command,  
And reason, pale and trembling, seeks to flee;  
For in thy gaze, the world dissolves like sand,  
And all my sense is lost in thoughts of thee.  
  O grant me peace, or fan these flames anew—  
  For I am naught but ash, if not for you.

777

O thou, whose eyes did kindle hope in vain,  
Desire, that wretch, doth haunt my soul’s domain.  
Thy gentle voice, a balm for every pain,  
Now echoes hollow, deep as autumn rain.  
The night doth stretch, and in its shadowed fold  
I trace the ghost of laughter once so sweet.  
The warmth of yesteryears grows faint and cold,  
Yet mem’ry lingers where our spirits meet.  
What cruel star hath drawn our fates apart,  
And torn thy promise from my trembling hand?  
Still, longing plants its roots within my heart,  
Defying reason’s harsh and barren land.  
  Though hope be fled, my love shall still remain,  
  A silent flame that burns despite the rain.

778

Desire, thou cruel tyrant of my breast,  
Dost haunt mine hours and rob mine eyes of rest,  
With silent whispers bidding peace be gone,  
And painting dreams with yearnings unconfessed.  
Thou art the fire that sears when day is done,  
A shadow in the light of reason’s sun,  
Thou draw’st my thoughts as tides are pulled by moon,  
And in thy grasp, all sober will is spun.  
I chase thee as Apollo chaseth dawn,  
Yet find thee fleeting as the morning dew,  
The heart’s deep ache that lingers though withdrawn,  
A wound unseen, forever fresh and new.  
  Yet, though thou art a torment and a thief,  
  Without thy sting, what joy would crown belief?

779

O thou, Desire, that feed’st upon my pain,  
Thy banquet is my hope, thy wine my tears;  
With every longing sigh thy feast doth gain,  
And in my restless heart thy court appears.  
Thou draw’st the moon to witness my unrest,  
And bid’st the silent stars to watch me pine;  
Thy whispers rob my weary soul of rest,  
Yet sweetly promise all I crave be mine.  
Thou art the fire that kindles in the night,  
The shadow dancing just beyond my reach;  
A phantom clad in ever-changing light,  
A lesson love and sorrow both may teach.  
  Yet though thy chains do wound, I would not flee—  
  For in thy bondage, I am most set free.

780

Desire, thou serpent, coil’st about my soul,  
And sows in fertile thought thy brackish seed,  
With velvet whisperings that take their toll,  
And make my reason bow to wanton need.  
Thy honeyed promise drips from every tongue,  
Yet leaves a bitterness upon the lips;  
From Eden’s root thy twisted vine was sprung,  
Entwined around the heart as daylight slips.  
Thou art both jailer and the key to flight,  
A fire that warms and threatens to consume,  
Within thy shadows, day dissolves to night,  
And roses bloom with thorns that spell out doom.  
  Yet though I know thy touch may be my bane,  
  Still, willingly, I wear thy golden chain.

781

Desire, thou art a serpent in my breast,  
That gnaws my soul and robs my heart of rest.  
Thy whispers curl like smoke within my mind,  
And bind my reason, leaving sense behind.  
With honeyed tongue, thou dost my will undo,  
And turn my brightest day to midnight hue.  
I flee thy grasp, yet run where thou dost lead,  
A moth enchanted by the flame’s cruel creed.  
In dreams thou dost appear with siren’s grace,  
And haunt the hollow chambers of my sleep.  
No chain or prayer can slow thy fevered pace,  
Nor can the watchful heart its vigil keep.  
  Yet though thou art the cause of joy and pain,  
  Still, in thy thrall, I wear thy golden chain.

782

My soul, a famish'd hound, doth gnaw within,  
Desire its master, cruel, cold, and sly,  
It prowls the shadowed corridors of sin,  
And bays beneath a pale and distant sky.  
The moonlight pours on pavements old and bare,  
Yet hunger deeper than the bones remains.  
I chase the phantom hope that flits the air,  
And drag along the rattle of my chains.  
Oh, wretched beast, why dost thou haunt my breast,  
And feast upon the marrow of my peace?  
Will time not grant thy ravening heart some rest,  
Or mercy bid thy constant gnawing cease?  
  Yet from this torment, wisdom oft is grown:  
  The hungriest hearts may make the world their own.

783

Desire, thou serpent coiled within my breast,  
Dost mock my soul with dreams it ne’er shall taste;  
Thy hissing whispers banish gentle rest,  
And turn to bitter gall all honey chased.  
With velvet tongue thou paints the fairest lies  
That shimmer far beyond my mortal reach,  
Yet when I clutch, the fleeting vision flies,  
And leaves me hollowed by the truths they teach.  
Yet still I court thee, knowing thou art bane,  
For hope, though poisoned, sweetens every pain;  
Thou art the sun that scorches through the rain,  
The longing shadow I pursue in vain.  
  Though thou dost wound, I cannot bid thee go—  
  For life is naught without thy fervent glow.

784

O thou, my soul, dost pine in fevered chase,  
Whilst Desire mocks thee with his wanton art,  
Thy restless longing paints a blushing face,  
Yet shadows linger deep within thy heart.  
The moon doth watch thy footsteps, silver-bright,  
Her silent gaze reflects thy secret ache,  
And in the hush of every passing night  
Thy hopes, like trembling leaves, begin to quake.  
What balm can cool this fire that doth consume,  
Or still the tempest whirling in thy breast?  
Each fragrant dawn dispels the midnight gloom,  
Yet leaves thee yearning, ever dispossessed.  
  O soul, be patient, let thy trial cease—  
  For time alone bestows the gift of peace.

785

Desire, thou tyrant, dost my soul enthrall,  
Yet mock’st her pleas with icy, scornful breath;  
Thou bidst me rise, then cast me to my fall,  
A fickle lord who weaves both life and death.  
Thy golden promise glimmers in my mind,  
Yet slips through grasping fingers, fleet as air;  
In midnight’s hush thy whispers keep me blind,  
And haunt my waking hours with sweet despair.  
Thou draw’st me forth on paths I dare not tread,  
And fan’st the coals of hope to searing flame;  
Yet when I near the prize for which I bled,  
Thou turn’st away, and leave’st me naught but shame.  
  Still, in thy chains, my heart would dwell with thee,  
  For in thy bondage lies my liberty.

786

Desire, thou serpent coiled within my breast,  
Dost poison hope with venom sweet yet dire,  
Thy hissing whispers rob my soul of rest,  
And kindle longing’s ever-hungry fire.  
Thou art the shadow stretched upon the day,  
A phantom hand that beckons from afar,  
Enticing reason’s faithful heart astray,  
And branding dreams with unattainèd scar.  
Oft have I warred against thy subtle art,  
And strove to bind thee in a cage of will,  
Yet still thou steal’st the scepter from my heart,  
And bend’st my purpose to thy wanton thrill.  
  Yet, serpent, though thy bite be laced with pain,  
  Without thy sting, what sweetness could remain?

787

Desire, thou serpent coiled within my breast,  
Dost gnaw my peace and mock my vain command,  
With velvet tongue, my tranquil hours infest,  
And whisper dreams the soul dares not withstand.  
Thou paint’st the world with hues of crimson fire,  
And fill’st the night with restless, longing sighs;  
The heart, ensnared, becomes thine own attire,  
Bound fast by threads unseen to mortal eyes.  
I chase thy shadow down the gilded lane,  
Though reason calls and bids me turn away;  
Yet every step is yoked to pleasure’s chain,  
And hope’s sweet lantern guides my soul astray.  
  O, cruel muse, in tempest and in calm,  
  Thy poison stings e’en as it brings its balm.

788

Desire, thou serpent coiled within my breast,  
Thy venom turns my sweetest thoughts to gall,  
With silken words thou robb’st me of my rest,  
Yet in thy chains, I gladly yield my all.  
Thou art a fire that feeds upon my will,  
A shadow cast upon the noonday light,  
A whisper soft that bids the world be still,  
Yet rouses storms to trouble calmest night.  
How oft I curse thy ever-burning flame,  
Yet seek thy touch when thou art fled from me;  
My reason’s foe, yet love’s enduring name,  
Thy yoke is pain, but pain I welcome free.  
  For though thy poison steals my peace away,  
  Without thee, life were but a shade of day.

789

Desire, thou serpent, coil’st about my breast,  
With venom’d whispers haunting every thought,  
Thy silver tongue denies my soul its rest,  
And feeds on dreams thy treachery hath wrought.  
A fire thou kindlest where cold reason lies,  
Consuming wary senses in its flame,  
Thine emerald gaze doth ever hypnotize,  
And softly calls my virtue by its name.  
I wrestle with thy shadow in the night,  
Yet find thee, sly, entwined about my will,  
Thou changeling mist that turns the day to blight,  
And leaves the trembling heart with longing still.  
  Yet, serpent sweet, I yield unto thy art—  
  For life is naught when thou depart’st my heart.

790

Desire, thou serpent coiled within my breast,  
With venom’d tongue dost mock my yearning soul,  
Thou whisper’st dreams that grant me no true rest,  
Yet stoke my fires beyond my wise control.  
Thy golden promises, they gleam and fade,  
Like distant stars that vanish with the dawn;  
Each hope, by thine alluring hand betrayed,  
Is naught but mist the wind hath swiftly drawn.  
Oft do I wrestle with thy cunning art,  
And curse the chains thy subtle coils devise,  
Yet still thou wind’st thy way into my heart,  
And cloud’st my reason with thy honeyed lies.  
  Though reason bids me cast thy shade aside,  
  Still, in thy grip, my frail resolves abide.

791

Desire, thou serpent, wound’st my soul anew,  
Thy venom'd whispers mock what hope I crave,  
Thine eyes, twin fires, burn my reason through,  
And in their light, my will becomes thy slave.  
Thou mask’st thy fangs with honeyed words and sighs,  
Yet each sweet promise breeds a deeper ache.  
Thou draw’st me forth with ever-changing guise,  
Then leave’st me drowning, lost within thy wake.  
Upon the midnight’s hush thy breath I feel,  
A silken coil that binds my heart in dread,  
Yet still I seek thy kiss, thy wound to heal,  
Though every cure by thee is poison fed.  
  O cruel Desire, my bane and my delight,  
  In longing’s chains I perish every night.

792

Desire, thou art a serpent in my breast,  
Yet poison'd sweetness bids my soul adore;  
Thy silver tongue persuades my heart to rest  
Upon the very edge of longing’s shore.  
Thou dost with gentle fire my reason bend,  
And whisper dreams that daylight must abjure,  
A fevered hope thy promises portend,  
Though wisdom's voice grows faint and insecure.  
By night I walk the labyrinth of thought,  
Each step a folly fashioned in thy name;  
The fruit thou offer’st is with peril fraught,  
Yet still I reach, ensnared by subtle flame.  
  Though bitter thorns attend the path I tread,  
  I follow thee, by longing ever led.

793

O cruel want, that binds my soul in fire,  
Yet chills my heart with frost of thy disdain,  
Thou art the thief of every sweet desire,  
Who sows in hope, but reaps a crop of pain.  
Upon the dawn I cast my longing gaze,  
Yet morning brings no balm my ache to still;  
Each hour is wrapped in melancholy's haze,  
My thoughts thy captive, fettered to thy will.  
The richest banquet turns to dust and stone  
When hunger for thy grace is all I feel;  
No golden sun can warm this heart alone,  
Nor gentle breeze my weary spirit heal.  
  Yet in my want, I love thee all the more,  
  For absence makes thee dearer than before.

794

When fervent longing wars with wisdom’s chain,  
My heart’s delight doth breed my reason’s woe;  
Desire’s tempest sweeps across the plain  
Where cautious seeds of prudence strive to grow.  
Yet hope, a miser, hoards its golden gleam  
While doubt, like shadows, haunts the shining day.  
Each yearning thought constructs a fragile dream  
That reason’s voice, unkind, would sweep away.  
Oft have I weighed the sweetness of the fire  
Against the sullen counsel of restraint,  
And found my soul divided by desire,  
Half angel-bright and half a passioned saint.  
  Thus torn between what is and what should be,  
  I find my freedom bound, my longing free.

795

How sweet thy glance, yet bitter is its dart,  
Desire’s feast denied my famished heart,  
Thy smiling lips conceal a silent smart,  
A gentle wound that tears my soul apart.  
Fair Venus weaves her snares with subtle art,  
And every sigh doth deeper ache impart;  
Thy shadow lingers where my day would start,  
Yet leaves me captive, chained by Cupid’s chart.  
The blush that blooms upon thy cheek is fire,  
And in its warmth, my hope and dread conspire;  
The nightingale laments my lost desire,  
His mournful tune becomes my funeral choir.  
  Yet, in thy gaze, I find both pain and glee—  
  For though love slays, it lives eternally.

796

Thou art the flame that chills my longing breast,  
Yet coldest ice within thy glances burns;  
In shadowed gloom, my restless heart finds rest,  
Yet in thy light, my wandering soul returns.  
Thy voice, a gentle wind through silent trees,  
Disturbs the peace that night would fain restore;  
Yet in its hush, I find a sweeter ease,  
A music wrought from silence I adore.  
Thus doth thy presence both enkindle fear  
And grant a courage born of pure delight;  
For every step that draws thee ever near  
Unfolds the day and deepens still the night.  
  O paradox, in thee my fate is spun:  
  Both lost and found, in thee I am undone.

797

Thou art the flame that chills my longing breast,  
Desire’s cruel guest that bids my heart unrest,  
Thy eyes, twin stars, deny my soul its sleep,  
Yet in their gaze, a secret darkness keep.  
Soft whispers haunt the corridors of night,  
Where shadows dance upon my fevered mind.  
In dreams I chase thy ever-fleeting light,  
And wake to find thy absence intertwined.  
O, time conspires to stretch the aching hours,  
Each tick a blade that shaves my hope away;  
Yet hope persists, as spring revives the flowers,  
And bids me trust the promise of the day.  
  So bound am I within thy subtle snare,  
  That joy and torment both thy name declare.

798

My soul, a field where longing’s poison grows,  
Yet starves within the plenty of its need,  
The silent wind through barren furrows blows  
And whispers secrets only roots can heed.  
Each dawn arrives, adorned in golden lace,  
Yet shadows clutch the corners of my dream;  
Desire’s bloom is set in thorn’s embrace,  
Its velvet petals lost upon the stream.  
I sow with hope, but reap with trembling hand,  
For harvests in this heart are never sure—  
The rain of yearning softens barren land,  
Yet cannot coax what time will not procure.  
  O let some gentle sun break through my gloom,  
  And draw a rose to blossom from my tomb.

799

My soul, a prisoner of hungry fire,  
Doth writhe 'twixt icy scorn and fevered need,  
Each pulse consumed by ever-bright desire,  
Yet starved for gentle words my heart to feed.  
The night enfolds me in its sable shroud,  
While memory’s lantern flickers, faint and cold;  
Long-lost ambitions whisper soft and proud,  
Yet falter ‘neath the weight of dreams grown old.  
Hope, like a moth, circles the dying flame,  
Her wings grown tattered in the smoky gloom;  
Regret, pale specter, calls my secret name,  
And shadows gather silent in my room.  
  Yet from these ashes, new resolve may start:  
  Love’s ember glows, unquenched, within my heart.

800

My soul, a furnace hung with frosted chains,  
Desires the flame that scorches yet restrains,  
Within this wintry cage, my longing grows,  
As embers dream beneath their veil of snows.  
O fickle fire, both comforter and threat,  
Thou art the sun my shadow dare not seek;  
I yearn for warmth my heart cannot forget,  
Though every touch may render spirit weak.  
The ice that binds me glimmers in the night,  
Reflecting ghosts of passions long since fled,  
Yet hope persists, a solitary light,  
That dances where the coldest fears have spread.  
  Release me, fire, from these frozen bands—  
  Consume, transform, and free my captive hands.

801

What cruel delight doth wound my longing soul,  
That feeds on hope yet starves in sharp disdain,  
A spark illumed by absence, not made whole,  
Each whispered wish but deepens secret pain.  
Thine eyes, twin beacons, haunt my waking hours,  
Their silent song compels my heart to break;  
Desire, a garden choked by frosted flowers,  
Still blooms in dreams I tremble to forsake.  
Thy laughter’s ghost attends me in the night,  
A melody that mocks my fruitless quest,  
And though I flee the torment of delight,  
My thoughts return, as birds unto their nest.  
  Yet if my sorrow gives thee cause to shine,  
  Let love’s cruel sweetness evermore be mine.

802

My soul, a captive to thy cold disdain,  
Yet burns with fire that thou wilt not requite,  
In winter’s clutch, I nurse a summer’s pain,  
And dream of dawns that never greet my sight.  
Thy silence weaves a shroud about my heart,  
Each word unsaid a stone upon my chest;  
Yet hope persists, unwilling to depart,  
A stubborn guest that will not be suppressed.  
Thy shadow haunts the corners of my mind,  
Thy memory, a ghost that will not fade;  
In longing’s chains, my freedom is confined,  
By love’s decree, my liberty betrayed.  
  If thou but smiled, my suffering were sweet,  
  And all my sorrow rendered incomplete.

803

O longing soul, thou art a burning frost,  
In fervent chase, yet ever more thou’rt lost.  
Thy wings of yearning beat against the night,  
Yet shadows thieve the promise from thy sight.  
Desire’s lantern flickers in the gloom,  
A trembling star within an empty room.  
Dawn’s gentle hand doth stroke thy weary face,  
But light cannot unmake the dark’s embrace.  
Thou wanderest through fields of vanished dreams,  
Where hope’s bright river runs to silent streams.  
Still dost thou call for what thou canst not hold,  
A warmth remembered, but a touch too cold.  
  Yet in this ache, thy spirit learns to rise,  
  For longing births the fire that never dies.

804

O cruel fire that warms yet leaves me cold,  
Desire’s sweet fruit, within, doth turn to gall,  
Thy honeyed words, like promises of old,  
Do stir the heart, yet swiftly let it fall.  
A glance exchanged ignites a hopeful spark,  
But shadows gather ere the morning breaks;  
Each whispered vow is swallowed by the dark,  
And trust succumbs to lies the daylight wakes.  
Yet still I chase thee through the endless night,  
A moth bewitched by ever-distant flame,  
For in thy glow all sorrow seems so light,  
Though reason weeps, my soul recalls thy name.  
  Thus bound, I burn, and in thy fire remain,  
  A willing captive to both joy and pain.

805

Desire, thou serpent coiled within my breast,  
Dost wound me sweetly with thy poisoned art,  
Thy silver tongue persuades me to unrest,  
And stirs a tempest raging in my heart.  
Thou lead’st my reason captive to thy will,  
With honeyed whispers veiled in silken lies,  
While shadows in my soul grow darker still  
And longing paints false dawns in faded skies.  
What cruel delight to hunger and to ache,  
To chase a phantom hope that will not stay,  
Each trembling sigh, a risk I vainly take,  
Yet cannot turn my hopeless gaze away.  
  Though oft I curse the chains thou dost employ,  
  Without thy pain, I know not how to joy.

806

O bitter flame that feeds upon my core,  
Desire’s sweet bane dost chain me evermore,  
Thy whisper’d heat doth haunt my midnight door,  
And leaves my peace in ashes on the floor.  
Thou art the sun that will not let me rest,  
Yet cold in longing’s shadow must I lie,  
A moth compelled to seek thy burning breast,  
Though knowing well thy kiss may bid me die.  
Each hope I plant within thy fertile gaze  
Unfolds to bloom, then withers in thy frown,  
My thoughts, ensnared within thy tangled maze,  
Climb high for light, yet find their ladders down.  
  O cruel delight that will not let me be,  
  I wear my heart in chains, and call it free.

807

What cruel delight, that feeds yet starves my will,  
Desire’s sweet fire, that chills my marrow so,  
A feast of longing served with bitter skill,  
A garden’s bloom beneath a veiled woe.  
Each glance, a spark that sets my soul aflame,  
Yet leaves the ash of yearning in its wake;  
A whispered promise bearing Cupid’s name,  
But every vow, a shadowed, silent ache.  
Thy nearness tempts me with forbidden fruit,  
Yet distance wraps my heart in colder chains.  
Sweet music played upon a broken lute,  
Where joy’s refrain dissolves in mournful strains.  
  So love, I live, both famished and fulfilled,  
  By hunger’s hand, with thy enchantment stilled.

808

O cruel fire that feeds upon my breast,  
Yet leaves me chilled where once my spirit burned,  
Thy embered fingers grant me little rest,  
For all my pleas for solace are returned.  
A shadowed hunger stalks my waking hours,  
And haunts my sleep with visions sharp and bright;  
Desire, like tangled thorns among the flowers,  
Ensnarls my heart in longing’s thorny blight.  
The sun, indifferent, climbs the distant sky,  
While I, undone, am captive to my pain.  
No gentle breeze, nor songbird’s hopeful cry,  
Can soothe the ache that waxes in my vein.  
  Yet in this pyre, some hope may yet be pressed:  
  That love, once kindled, never is at rest.

809

Desire, thou serpent coiled within my breast,  
Dost feed on hope, yet leave my spirit lean,  
With whispered dreams that will not let me rest,  
And visions bright where naught but lack is seen.  
Thy golden promises, though sweet they seem,  
Are poisoned fruits that dangle just beyond,  
A fleeting glimpse—a half-remembered dream—  
That draws me forth, unwilling, yet so fond.  
I chase thy shadow through the misty gloom,  
And in pursuit, my reason seems to fade;  
For every flower plucked, a thorn doth bloom,  
Each pleasure won is lost in sorrow's shade.  
  Yet still, I serve thee, captive to thy fire,  
  Both blessed and cursed, my ever-burning desire.

810

My longing burns, a cigarette half-spent,  
Each drag a wish upon your distant skin,  
The night inhales the secrets that I’ve sent,  
While moonlight carves your absence on my chin.  
The embers glow with memories we made,  
And ashes fall like hours we let go by,  
My trembling hand recalls your touch that stayed  
When dawn was just a rumor in the sky.  
The silence curls around me, thick and deep,  
A smoke that blurs the edges of your name,  
Yet in this haze, your promises still seep—  
Desire’s ghost, persistent as a flame.  
  O, if the wind would carry you to me,  
  I’d quit the night, and set my longing free.

811

Desire coils within, a viper’s ache,  
It strikes my heart each time I see your face,  
A breathless hunger reason cannot slake,  
My thoughts consumed by longing’s fierce embrace.  
The moonlight spills its secrets on your hair,  
Soft silver whispering what words conceal,  
While shadows dance and tempt me to declare  
The depth of aching truths I must reveal.  
Your laughter lingers, haunting midnight hours,  
Its echo trembling on the silent air,  
A scent of summer, sharp as jasmine flowers,  
Awakening a hope too sweet to bear.  
  Yet still I linger, captive to desire,  
  A moth enchanted by forbidden fire.

812

Desire’s ember scorches where you stand,  
A poison root that twists within my chest,  
It winds unseen, yet burns at your command,  
My waking hours and dreams alike possessed.  
Your voice, a siren’s call across the night,  
Unmoors my reason, sets my spirit free,  
Yet binds my will with threads of pure delight,  
A captive heart that yearns for liberty.  
I chase your shadow through the haunted dark,  
My longing echoes in the moon’s pale gaze,  
Each whispered word ignites a secret spark,  
A hope that dances through unending maze.  
  Though scorched and tangled in this sweet unrest,  
  I live and die by passion’s bold behest.

813

You are the flame that scorches through my veins,  
Yet leave me shivering in midnight’s frost,  
A summer’s blaze that dances through my pains,  
Then in your shadow, all my warmth is lost.  
Your laughter rings as bells in distant halls,  
But echoes fade when silence fills the room;  
A golden light that paints the ancient walls,  
Yet slips away, devoured by the gloom.  
I chase your glow as moths pursue the fire,  
And in pursuit, I’m tangled in the night;  
Desire’s wings are singed by my desire,  
Yet hope returns anew with morning’s light.  
  Though burned and chilled, for you my heart remains,  
  Bound by the bliss, the frost, and fiery chains.

814

You are the flame that scorches but won’t warm,  
A fevered thirst denied the rain’s embrace,  
A tempest wrapped in beauty’s fragile form,  
A riddle carved upon a lover’s face.  
I chase the shadow cast by longing’s fire,  
Yet find but ashes clinging to the day,  
A songbird wounded by its own desire,  
Still singing hope as twilight slips away.  
Your laughter dances just beyond my reach,  
Like sunlight tangled in the jealous mist,  
And time, that cruel and unrelenting teacher,  
Records the moments lost and chances missed.  
  Yet though my heart is marked by love’s cruel art,  
  It beats for thee, a captive from the start.

815

You are the flame that scorches in my chest,  
Yet leave me cold, a shadow at your door.  
Your laughter’s light, yet I am dispossessed,  
And crave the warmth I tasted once before.  
I wander halls where silent echoes dwell,  
Each memory a ghost that will not part;  
Your gentle voice, a lingering, distant bell,  
Resounds in caverns carved within my heart.  
The world is bright, yet dusk within my gaze,  
For absence paints the sun in ashen hue.  
I chase your phantom through unending days,  
And dream of embers flaring bright and true.  
  Yet still, my love, though hope at last be gone,  
  Your fire remains, and I must carry on.

816

My soul’s a furnace stoked by longing’s hand,  
Yet every ember brands me with regret.  
Desires like shadows flicker where I stand,  
Their shapes recalled in dreams I can’t forget.  
The night consumes the hope that morning gave,  
While ashes of old wishes cloud the air.  
I pace the borders of what I still crave,  
Afraid to find a hollowness in there.  
The clockwork stars revolve without a sound,  
Indifferent to the flames within my chest.  
My heart, a smithy’s anvil, world-renowned  
For forging chains from yearning unconfessed.  
  Yet as the dawn ignites the sky anew,  
  I find my longing tempered—still, but true.

817

My soul’s a furnace fed with longing’s flame,  
Desire’s ash choking every breath I take.  
Each midnight hour, I whisper out your name,  
Yet dawn arrives with ache that will not break.  
The shadows crowd the corners of my mind,  
And smother hope beneath their sable wing.  
I seek some gentle peace I cannot find,  
For every thought of you is suffering.  
Yet in this pain, a strange, sweet ember glows,  
A promise kindled from the loss and rue;  
Though absence with its icy current flows,  
My heart’s devotion only grows more true.  
  Thus, let these flames consume me, fierce and bright—  
  If love be pyre, I burn for you each night.

818

My soul’s a furnace craving what it burns,  
Desire’s ash choking the breath it earns.  
Each night I stoke the embers with my sighs,  
Yet morning finds me cold beneath the skies.  
The more I feed the flames, the more they claim,  
Consuming hope and leaving only blame.  
I chase the shadows dancing on the wall,  
Their fleeting shapes forever out of thrall.  
In passion’s crucible my heart is cast,  
Transformed and scarred by conflagrations past.  
Yet from these ruins longing’s phoenix grew,  
A hunger kindled ever fresh and new.  
  Though scorched, I rise to seek what fire discerns—  
  My soul’s a furnace craving what it burns.

819

My soul’s a thirsting well that draws no rain,  
Desire’s sharp teeth gnaw hope to brittle bone,  
Each dawn arrives to echo night’s refrain,  
And seeds of joy in barren ground are sown.  
The sun, indulgent, turns a blinded eye  
While shadows coil about my aching chest;  
Upon the wind, unheard, I loose my cry,  
Yet silence, cold and constant, is my guest.  
What ghostly balm can soothe a heart so dry?  
What gentle hand can mend the rent unknown?  
Yet still I grope for stars within the sky,  
And gather light where only dark has grown.  
  Though thirst may parch and sorrow’s grip remain,  
  I rise, and hope for gentle, healing rain.

820

My soul, a starving wolf, pines at your gate,  
Denied the feast of love it thought was fate.  
Your distant eyes reflect the moon’s cold light,  
And cast my longing heart in endless night.  
I prowl the borderlands of your regard,  
A shadow stretched where dreams and doubts collide;  
Yet hope, though wounded, keeps its watch and guard,  
And hungers still for solace you denied.  
How sharp the ache that howls beneath my skin,  
Each heartbeat echoes with a silent plea.  
Would that your mercy let my wildness in,  
And grant my spirit’s hunger liberty.  
  But if I’m doomed to wander, lone and late,  
  I’ll worship still the threshold of your gate.

821

My soul’s a moth that burns upon your flame,  
Yet finds no warmth within your cold disdain.  
I flutter close, yet you remain the same,  
A star aloof, immune to love or pain.  
I chase the light that dances from your eyes,  
A hope that flickers, trembling and slight.  
Your silence weaves a shroud of thin disguise,  
And veils my longing in the cloak of night.  
Each whispered dream dissolves into despair,  
Yet still I circle, helpless to depart.  
Your indifference is more than I can bear,  
Yet still I yield the embers of my heart.  
  For though your heart with icy armor gleams,  
  My soul ignites, a prisoner of dreams.

822

Desire is acid poured upon my will,  
It eats the steel of reason, leaves me raw;  
Its fingers pluck my purpose, cold and still,  
And bend my heart beneath its hungered claw.  
A fire unbidden flickers in my chest,  
Consuming sense with every fevered breath;  
It turns my quiet hours to unrest,  
And shadows every joy with whispered death.  
Yet in this tempest, strangely, I am whole,  
For longing carves the shape of who I am;  
It writes its longing deeply on my soul,  
And brands my skin with its consuming flame.  
  Though reason shatters, I remain its slave—  
  Desire’s captive, neither free nor brave.

823

Desire’s flame, a serpent in my chest,  
It coils and hisses, never granting rest.  
Its golden tongue entwines my waking thought,  
And every dream within its scales is caught.  
I pace the midnight halls with fevered pace,  
Each shadow whispers longings unconfessed.  
A thousand wishes flicker on my face,  
Yet none can cool the ardor in my breast.  
The fruit I crave hangs just beyond my hand,  
Its sweetness glimmers in the distant sun.  
I chase, but never catch, the promised land,  
And find the race anew when night is done.  
  O, let this serpent’s fire at last abate,  
  Or teach my heart to find a gentler state.

824

Desire’s fire gnaws within my hollow chest,  
A vulture feasting where my hopes once lay,  
Its talons tear the peace I once possessed,  
And night devours the gold of gentle day.  
My soul, a field where longing’s shadows grow,  
Is tilled by hands of ache that will not tire,  
Each breath a burden, weighted down with woe,  
Yet fanned to flame by secret, fierce desire.  
I chase the ghost of joy through tangled thought,  
Yet find my arms embrace but empty air;  
The dreams I covet ever come to naught,  
And fortune turns her ever-fickle stare.  
  Yet from these ashes, yearning’s voice is spun:  
  To love, to lose, and rise—forever one.

825

Desire is acid burning through my veins,  
A feast of longing served on empty plates,  
It whispers hope beneath my silent chains,  
Yet mocks the hourglass as it hesitates.  
A phantom touch that dances on my skin,  
The shadowed flicker of a distant flame,  
It paints forbidden gardens deep within,  
And carves my heartbeat with a lover’s name.  
It steals the moonlight from my sleepless night,  
And plants wild roses in forgotten fields,  
It turns my certainties to frail delight,  
And asks what strength a yearning spirit yields.  
  So let me drink the ache I cannot flee—  
  For longing is the price of being free.

826

Desire is ash that smolders in my chest,  
A feast denied, though hunger never dies.  
It haunts the hollow hours without rest,  
And paints with longing all my waking skies.  
The flame once bright is now a muted glow,  
Yet still it breathes beneath a fragile crust,  
A secret ember no cold wind can know,  
Defying time, untouched by sleep or dust.  
I walk through days with shadows at my heel,  
Each moment tinged with memory's perfume;  
The heart recalls what reason would conceal,  
And gardens bloom where only ashes loom.  
  So, though the fire is spent and hope seems thin,  
  Desire endures, and burns me from within.

827

Desire is acid poured upon my soul,  
A hunger gnawing where no feast is laid,  
It carves in silence, makes the broken whole,  
And stains the daylight with its restless shade.  
It haunts the quiet chamber of my mind,  
A specter trailing footsteps on the floor,  
In every glance, its meaning I would find,  
Each whispered hope more urgent than before.  
It blooms in darkness, thorned and overgrown,  
A fire that withers reason’s careful seed,  
It calls me forth to places yet unknown,  
Where longing is a wound that will not bleed.  
  Yet still I crave, as night devours the day,  
  For dreams that passion’s acid cannot slay.

828

Desire, that thief, has stolen sleep from me,  
It whispers poison where my thoughts should be.  
With silken words it tempts my restless mind,  
And paints illusions hope would never find.  
It stirs the shadows lurking in the night,  
Disturbs the peace that dreams are meant to give,  
And bathes my soul in ever-hungry light,  
Demanding more than mortal hearts can live.  
Each hour I count with longing’s fevered gaze,  
While silence deepens as the moon ascends,  
Imprisoned by the want that will not raze,  
A torment sweet that neither breaks nor mends.  
  Yet in this ache, my spirit learns to see—  
  Desire’s fire forges what I am to be.

829

Desire, that thief, has robbed my restful mind,  
And left me wandering where hope is blind.  
It steals with gentle hands, yet leaves behind  
A storm of longing reason cannot bind.  
By moon’s pale gaze, I pace the haunted floor,  
Each shadow shaped by wishes unfulfilled,  
A captive heart behind a bolted door,  
With dreams by silent midnight’s hunger stilled.  
The golden fruit hangs just beyond my reach,  
Its sweetness taunts my soul with fragrant air;  
All wisdom falters when my senses teach  
That yearning grows the more I am aware.  
  Yet in this ache, I find my spirit’s fire:  
  For what is life without the spark—desire?

830

Desire, that serpent coiled within my chest,  
Feeds nightly on the hope I tried to kill,  
It wakes with dawn, refusing me my rest,  
And bends my will anew against my will.  
It whispers honeyed lies beneath my skin  
And paints false heavens just beyond my sight,  
A fevered ache that burns away my sin,  
Yet leaves me trembling, hollowed by the night.  
Oft have I cursed its venom-laden tongue,  
Yet crave the poison that it pours in me;  
From longing’s well my heart was ever sprung,  
And thirsts for dreams that never let me be.  
  So let me suffer sweetly, though I fall—  
  For in desire’s chains, I am enthralled.

831

Desire knocks sharp upon my hollow chest,  
While you, my love, pretend you do not hear,  
A fevered plea that will not grant me rest,  
Yet you avert your gaze and draw not near.  
The moonlight falls, a witness to my ache,  
Its silver tongue recites my secret woe,  
While shadows gather promises you break,  
And silent winds confess what I forego.  
Still hope, that traitor, lingers in my veins,  
And paints your absence with a tender hue,  
Despite the salt and sorrow of these stains,  
My heart still thrums a melody for you.  
  For though you turn from what I feel inside,  
  Desire endures, and will not be denied.

832

Desire, that serpent, coils around my mind,  
It hisses truths my silence cannot cage,  
Its emerald scales in hidden shadows wind,  
Temptation’s whisper inked on every page.  
In midnight’s hush it slithers through my thought,  
With golden eyes that pierce the mask I wear;  
Entwined with longing, all my peace is caught,  
Each heartbeat tangled in its hungry snare.  
I dare not flee, nor yet invite its kiss,  
For venomed sweetness lingers on my tongue;  
A paradise concealed within abyss,  
Where hope and peril endlessly are sung.  
  Yet still I gaze into its blazing eyes,  
  And find in longing what the soul denies.

833

Desire, that serpent, coils around my sleep,  
It hisses truths my wounded heart must keep.  
Its velvet scales caress my midnight mind,  
A whispered longing tangled and unkind.  
It weaves through shadows cast by flickering light,  
A hunger clothed in hope, yet edged with fear.  
It tempts with visions burning soft and bright,  
Then leaves me lost when morning draws too near.  
No gentle hand can soothe its secret claim,  
No reason banish what the soul would crave.  
Its fire consumes both innocence and shame,  
And makes the beggar dream he may be brave.  
  Still, I embrace the serpent in my breast,  
  For in its ache I find my heart confessed.

834

Desire claws nightly at my weary soul,  
It whispers poison, sweetly taking hold.  
Each dream it barters bears a hidden toll,  
And promises of gold grow pale and cold.  
Within the hush, its shadowed fingers creep,  
Entwining hope with longing’s tangled thread,  
Disturbing quiet hours I ought to keep,  
Awakening old yearnings long since dead.  
Yet still I chase its ever-fading light,  
A moth ensnared by flame I cannot claim,  
For every step draws pleasure out of sight  
And leaves me scorched and haunted by its name.  
  O, would desire release me from its art—  
  Or else consume the hunger in my heart.

835

My soul, a hungry beast, devours my peace,  
It prowls through longing’s halls, refusing rest,  
It bays with ancient grief that will not cease,  
And turns my quiet dreams to troubled quest.  
At dawn’s pale edge it stalks the fragile light,  
Its shadow trailing memories unkind,  
It whispers promises in sleepless night,  
And feasts upon the doubts it leaves behind.  
No gentle word nor wisdom’s balm can tame  
This creature prowling deep within my chest,  
It calls my secret fears by secret name,  
And leaves my weary spirit dispossessed.  
  Yet through this hunger, love may still be found—  
  A rose that blooms where haunted hearts are bound.

836

Desire knocks fierce upon my soul’s locked door,  
It whispers lies and leaves me wanting more.  
A shadow cast upon my waking thought,  
It weaves its silken threads I can’t ignore.  
With every breath my trembling heart is caught  
Between the pull of hope and aching sore.  
Sweet tempter, dressed in golden, fleeting light,  
You promise joy but barter peace for pain.  
Like restless flame, you flicker in the night,  
Yet in your warmth, I find my spirit wane.  
What strength have I to keep you from my sight,  
When all resistance seems to be in vain?  
  Yet, in the battle twixt my will and thee,  
  I learn the cost of longing’s tyranny.

837

Desire claws nightly at my restless soul,  
A hungry beast denied its wanted feast,  
It paces shadows in my mind’s control,  
Its growling hunger never once released.  
The moon doth pale beneath my fevered gaze,  
Her silver tears ignite forbidden fire,  
Each hour stains the dark with longing’s haze,  
Each breath becomes a spark of old desire.  
Yet hope, elusive, dances just ahead,  
Her footsteps echo softly on the floor,  
She leaves a trail of dreams I fear to tread,  
And locks behind her every secret door.  
  But still I chase her through the endless night,  
  A moth enchanted by the cruelest light.

838

Desire knocks nightly at my hollow chest,  
My soul, her suitor, spurned by cruel disdain,  
She wanders restless, longing for her rest,  
Yet finds no shelter from this subtle pain.  
In silent hours, shadows court the mind,  
And whisper secrets veiled by moon’s embrace,  
Old hopes, like ghosts, return to be unkind,  
Their tender hands caress my longing face.  
O fickle heart, why chase what will not yield?  
Why plant thy seeds on barren, frosted ground?  
The sweetest fruits are kept in others’ field,  
And thirst grows deeper when no springs are found.  
  Yet still I dream, though hope and sense depart:  
  Desire, thou art the tyrant of my heart.

839

My soul, a beggar knocking at your gate,  
Finds hunger where your absent mercy lies,  
It waits beneath the moon in mournful state,  
And feeds on hope that flickers, starved and wise.  
Your silence weaves a shroud about my heart,  
Each echo thins the marrow of my trust,  
Yet still I linger, loath to drift apart,  
My longing blooming stubborn from the dust.  
Oft have I dreamt your kindness at my door,  
Imagined warmth that never crossed the sill,  
Yet daybreak finds me poorer than before,  
My cup unfilled, my craving deeper still.  
  Yet should you grant a glance, or let me in,  
  My soul would feast, forgiven of its sin.

840

Desire sits grinning, gnawing at my bones,  
A tyrant king who mocks my every plea,  
He weaves his whispers into silent moans  
And plants his flag where once my will was free.  
His golden promises, all sharp and sweet,  
Lead hope like lambs along a tangled path,  
Yet every victory tastes of defeat,  
And every gift conceals the threat of wrath.  
He gilds the moon, he paints the distant shore  
With visions fair that vanish in the light,  
His hunger ever asks for something more,  
A shadow stalking joy with constant blight.  
  Yet still I court him, though he brings me pain,  
  For longing is the pulse that makes me sane.

841

Desire, that serpent coiled within my chest,  
Whispers of hunger clothed in honeyed lies,  
It stirs my soul to yearning unconfessed,  
And kindles longing deep behind my eyes.  
It weaves its silver tongue about my will,  
And bids me chase the shadows of the night,  
A fevered pulse that time cannot distill,  
Nor reason’s counsel cool with gentle light.  
Yet in its fire I sense both joy and woe,  
A double-edged delight I dare not shun,  
For though it leads where wiser men forego,  
Its promise shines as bright as any sun.  
  Thus bound, I follow, though the path be dire,  
  A moth enchanted by forbidden fire.

842

Desire, you thief, you haunt my sleepless mind,  
You dress in hope, then leave me cold, unkind.  
You whisper sweet illusions in the dark,  
A candle’s flame that scorches with its spark.  
With every sigh, you promise distant bliss,  
Yet every touch dissolves to empty air.  
I chase your shadow, longing for a kiss,  
But grasp at nothing, tangled in despair.  
You paint with gold the edges of my dreams,  
Then vanish with the breaking of the day.  
Your laughter echoes softly in my seams,  
And leaves my heart to wander and to stray.  
  Yet though you wound, I beg you never part—  
  For longing is the life-blood of my heart.

843

Desire, that sly imp, claws beneath my skin,  
It whispers lies of love I’ll never win.  
It paints her eyes with fire and velvet night,  
And tempts my soul to dare forbidden sin.  
The moonlight teases dreams beyond my sight,  
Her laughter haunts the hours I begin  
To hope—then falter, lost within the blight  
Of longing’s ache, condemned to dwell within.  
Yet still I chase her shadow through the gloom,  
A phantom touch that burns me as I yearn;  
The roses bloom and wither, scent of doom,  
Yet still for her, my heart will twist and burn.  
  Desire, cruel master, grant me sweet release,  
  Or bind me evermore without surcease.

844

Desire stalks my shadow, sharp and cold,  
A thief that steals my peace yet leaves me whole.  
It whispers secrets, brazen yet untold,  
And draws my soul toward its hidden goal.  
In midnight’s hush, it weaves a silver thread  
That binds my heart to longing’s restless fire;  
Each pulse and breath by silent hunger led,  
A dance compelled by ever-climbing dire.  
Yet in its grasp, a trembling hope unfurls,  
A fragile bloom that trembles in the night—  
It paints with yearning’s brush a world of pearls,  
Transfigured by the ache of distant light.  
  So let it haunt, this ghost I cannot flee,  
  For in desire’s hold, at last I’m free.

845

Desire, that serpent, coils about my chest,  
It whispers lies while feeding on my pain,  
It stirs my dreams and robs me of my rest,  
A phantom’s touch that lingers in my brain.  
Its gilded promises, so sweet, so sly,  
Entangle reason, turning clear to haze;  
Its golden tongue persuades my soul to try  
To chase the mirage through a fevered daze.  
Yet every step it leads is fraught with fire,  
A path of thorns disguised as silken thread,  
Each longing glance but fans the flames higher,  
And leaves me yearning for the words unsaid.  
  Still, in its grip, my heart refuses flight:  
  For what is life, untouched by keen delight?

846

I hunger for the warmth your absence chills,  
Yet crave the ice that numbs my longing’s fire,  
My heart, a captive bird, against its will  
Beats hope and dread in tangled, sweet attire.  
The hours stretch thin, as winter’s silent breath  
Consumes the sun and shadows all my days;  
Yet in the frost, I taste a hint of death  
That stirs my soul and sets my mind ablaze.  
Each memory, a spark upon the snow,  
Each whispered word, a thaw within my chest—  
But distance weaves a veil I cannot throw,  
And time denies my aching heart its rest.  
  Still, in the cold, your ghost brings bittersweet—  
  Absence and yearning twined where warmth and chill meet.

847

My heart craves fire, yet finds itself in frost,  
Desire’s gain forever weighed by loss.  
A silent ache attends what passion cost,  
The warmth I seek entombed in icy gloss.  
Each breath, a ghost, recalls what once was bright,  
Now veiled beneath the hush of winter’s hand.  
I chase the sun that flees the shroud of night,  
And dream of embers buried in the sand.  
Yet hope persists, a stubborn crimson thread  
That binds my spirit though the world is cold.  
Within the chill, a fever softly spread,  
A secret blaze that never will be told.  
  For though I burn where snow and shadow meet,  
  This frozen longing makes my fire sweet.

848

Desire claws at reason’s fragile thread,  
Yet love, cold-wrought, lies silent in my chest,  
The ghost of warmth that flits about my bed,  
A shadowed ache that steals away my rest.  
In midnight’s hush, I chase what once was fire,  
But find instead the embers of regret;  
A longing spun from folly and desire,  
That memory and mercy can’t forget.  
O cruel delight, that beckons and betrays,  
Your honeyed lips do poison every thought;  
I wander lost within your fevered maze,  
My heart ensnared, resisting as it ought.  
  Yet in this torment, hope begins to bloom—  
  A rose that dares to pierce the winter’s gloom.

849

Your glance ignites a fire I cannot hold,  
Yet leaves my hopeful heart to freeze in vain;  
A summer’s warmth that flickers into cold,  
A gentle balm that deepens every pain.  
I chase the ghost of laughter in your eyes,  
Yet shadows swallow light before it grows.  
Your voice, a fleeting wind that softly sighs,  
Awakens dreams that no dawn ever knows.  
In silent rooms your memory appears,  
A whisper tangled in the midnight air;  
It stirs the well of unacknowledged fears,  
And seeds a longing far too fierce to bear.  
  Yet, in this ache, my soul is newly born—  
  For love is sweetest when by absence torn.

850

Desire, like poison, courses through my veins,  
Yet you remain untouched, immune, apart.  
Each longing glance, a prisoner in chains,  
Finds no reflection in thy frozen heart.  
My pleas, like whispers lost upon the breeze,  
Dissolve to silence ere they meet thine ear.  
No tender word nor gentle touch shall ease  
This fevered ache that draws thee ever near.  
Night after night, my dreams are writ with fire,  
A tapestry of hopes that cannot be.  
Yet daylight burns away each sweet desire,  
Restoring sense and cold tranquility.  
  O cruel immunity, thy gift and bane—  
  To love so fierce, yet ever love in vain.

851

I crave your light, yet darkness fills my days,  
Desire burns cold where hope has lost its spark,  
My weary soul is tangled in a maze  
Of silent fears that flourish in the dark.  
I reach for warmth, but shadows hold me tight,  
Their jealous fingers clutch my trembling mind,  
And every hour denies the promised light  
That once, in happier seasons, I would find.  
Yet in the gloom your memory remains,  
A beacon faint, but steadfast through the night;  
It soothes my wounds and gently dulls my pains,  
Reminding me that dawn will chase this blight.  
  O grant me strength until the morning’s due—  
  For in the sun, my heart may live anew.

852

My soul desires what reason would deny,  
Yet yearns for frost beneath a summer sky.  
The heart in secret shadows dares to tread,  
While logic weaves its web with measured sigh.  
A thousand stars descend where hopes have fled,  
Still, burning dreams ignite what doubt would quell;  
The mind, a wary lantern, dims with dread,  
But passion’s flame persists, refusing spell.  
In silent nights, my wishes softly creep,  
Past borders reason guards with iron bars;  
Through tangled thoughts, emotions waking leap,  
Unfurling wings to reach forbidden stars.  
  Though wisdom warns the soul to stay confined,  
  Desire breaks the chains that reason bind.

853

My soul is split by longing’s cruel design,  
Desire burns bright where hope itself declines.  
Within the hollow chambers of my mind  
A shadowed ache, in silent sorrow, pines.  
The midnight whispers secrets to my heart,  
And paints thy absent face in silver hue,  
Though distance keeps our bodies far apart,  
My dreams revive the love I once thought true.  
Each dawn returns with promises unmet,  
The sun ascends, yet darkness holds me still,  
For memories, like dew, make eyelids wet,  
And time obeys a slow, unyielding will.  
  Yet in this grief, my spirit seeks release—  
  If longing be my torment, love my peace.

854

My soul, a battlefield of want and woe,  
Desires burn bright where hope is laid to waste,  
Each longing arrow, drawn and loosed in woe,  
Finds not its mark, but bitterly is chased.  
Within the silent trenches of my mind,  
Regret and yearning wage their endless fight,  
And dreams, like fallen banners, lie entwined  
With shadows stretching far beyond the night.  
Yet still, some stubborn embers softly glow,  
Defiant 'gainst the darkness in my breast,  
And whisper, gentle, what I used to know—  
That peace may come if longing is confessed.  
  So let my heart, though battered, rise above,  
  And seek its solace in the light of love.

855

My soul is drunk on thirst it cannot slake,  
Desire’s flame chills every dream I make,  
Each hope I taste becomes a phantom ache,  
And joy, once sweet, now sours for sorrow’s sake.  
The moon bestows her silver on my brow,  
Yet shadows gather where her blessing falls.  
I beg for dawn, but night is all I know,  
Her silence echoing in empty halls.  
This longing weaves its spell through every thought,  
A restless wind that stirs my heart to roam.  
I chase illusions, never what I sought,  
Forever far from peace, forever home.  
  Yet in this fevered thirst, I still remain,  
  A soul in love with longing, drunk on pain.

856

My soul, a furnace craving winter’s frost,  
Desires what’s gained is ever dearly lost.  
The fire within me longs for icy breath,  
Yet finds its comfort in the threat of death.  
What warmth I harbor, coldness soon betrays,  
And longing’s ashes softly fill my chest.  
The fleeting embers faintly light my days,  
A glow that yearns for chill to grant it rest.  
But in the hush of snow, my heart persists,  
A paradox of hunger and retreat;  
The heat of absence in the cold exists,  
And shadows gather where the fires meet.  
  So let me blaze and freeze in equal part,  
  For loss and longing dwell within my heart.

857

My soul, a furnace fueled by hope’s decay,  
Burns cold for love that never warms my skin;  
The dawn arrives and yet the night will stay,  
For dreams depart before they can begin.  
A phantom presence haunts the silent room,  
The echoes of your laughter fade to mist.  
In petals bruised by sorrow’s heavy bloom,  
I search for solace in the lips I’ve missed.  
Yet time, relentless, claims its bitter due,  
And paints with frost the corners of my mind.  
What once was bright now dims to ashen hue,  
As memories grow distant, undefined.  
  Yet still I stand, though winter chills my bone,  
  A soul ablaze, yet evermore alone.

858

Desire’s flame, a poison in my veins,  
Yet colder grows my heart with every year,  
A winter spreads where once were summer rains,  
And hope, once bright, now trembles, frail with fear.  
The golden dreams of youth have turned to dust,  
Their shining promise withered by regret;  
I walk through shadows, learning not to trust  
The whispered vows the midnight stars beget.  
Yet still, beneath the ice, a spark remains,  
A secret ember smoldering unseen,  
That, kindled by a glance, might break my chains  
And wake a soul from slumber cold and keen.  
  O love, return before my fire is spent—  
  Unthaw this heart, and teach me to relent.

859

I crave the flame that leaves my spirit cold,  
Desire’s feast, yet hunger’s hands I bind;  
A silver longing, tarnished, pale and old,  
Within the velvet chambers of my mind.  
The moon conspires with shadows on the wall,  
To paint my dreams with embers never spent,  
And in the hush I hear the darkness call,  
A whispered ache that reason can’t prevent.  
Yet hope endures where icy passions sleep,  
And kindles warmth in winter’s stern embrace;  
The ashes stir, and from their silence leap  
The ghosts of fires I cannot quite replace.  
  So let me yearn, though yearning be in vain—  
  For in the wanting, love and loss remain.

860

I crave the fire that leaves my spirit cold,  
Desire’s bright lie outshines the truths I own;  
A fevered wish that withers, swift and bold,  
Yet seeds of longing in my heart are sown.  
The hollow flame consumes what reason builds,  
In shadows cast by hope’s elusive gleam;  
A tapestry of yearning, deftly quilted,  
Unravels quick beneath the morning’s beam.  
Reason’s quiet voice, a distant shore,  
Calls out through mists of passion’s veiling night;  
Yet still I grasp for embers, seeking more,  
Mistaking ashes for a beacon’s light.  
  So burns the want that leaves me pale and old,  
  A fire that chills, a hunger never told.

861

Desire gnaws within, a hungry beast denied,  
Yet love, untouched, retreats where shadows hide.  
My heart, a silent drum, forgets its tune,  
While hope’s faint ember dims beneath the moon.  
I pace the midnight halls of restless thought,  
Each step recalling dreams that came to naught.  
A whispered name dissolves upon the air,  
And longing’s ghost is all that lingers there.  
What cruel alchemy turns want to ache,  
And weaves a prison none but I can make?  
Yet in the stillness, yearning softly grows  
Like fragile petals opening to woes.  
  O grant me courage, fortune, or release,  
  To find in love at last some gentle peace.

862

Desire gnaws within, a hungry flame,  
Yet cold indifference answers every plea,  
My hopes, like moths, are scorched and put to shame,  
While shadows stretch where love should warmly be.  
I pace the midnight halls of silent doubt,  
Each echo bearing dreams I dare not name,  
The moon bears witness as I wander out,  
In search of kindled eyes to spark the same.  
But frost adorns the petals of my heart,  
And longing's fire is met with winter's breath,  
No tender glance, no word to play its part,  
Just emptiness that chills me close to death.  
  Yet still I burn, though night denies me light—  
  A candle clings to hope against the night.

863

Desire, a fire that chills me to the bone,  
I chase a ghost whose eyes will never see.  
Its whisper threads through nights I walk alone,  
A haunting song that binds and tortures me.  
The shadows stretch with every longing sigh,  
Their fingers cold caress my fevered skin.  
No dawn can banish phantoms I decry,  
For hope and hunger wage their war within.  
I drink the air she left with every breath,  
Yet thirst remains, unquenched by time or tears.  
Her absence stalks me, faithful as my death,  
A specter fed on all my hidden fears.  
  O cruel desire, sweet torment of my heart,  
  You forge my chains and tear my soul apart.

864

O wilt thou quench the fire within my breast,  
That burns for thee as stars for dawning year?  
Thy absence makes my waking soul unblest,  
And fills each night with longing's silent tear.  
The moon in silver beams doth mock my pain,  
Yet hope, like morning light, doth gently rise.  
Thy name, a whisper in the summer rain,  
Doth echo soft beneath the velvet skies.  
Oh, let thy gaze alight upon my face,  
And grant a balm to soothe this fevered heart.  
For in thine eyes I find my spirit’s grace,  
A world reborn, where sorrows may depart.  
  If thou but speak, my joy shall know no end—  
  For love, through thee, the fates themselves would bend.

865

O thou, whose light doth set my soul aflame,  
Why dost thou flee when I thy warmth implore?  
The dawn, once bright, now hides its golden name,  
And shadows stretch where sunbeams danced before.  
Fair hope, thou fragile wisp, dost thou retreat  
When longing hearts reach upward from the night?  
Must yearning eyes their bitter tears repeat  
And search in vain for comfort in thy light?  
Yet in thy absence, memory holds thee near,  
A phantom glow that lingers on my skin.  
Though silence reigns, thy voice I seem to hear,  
A whispered promise borne on gentle wind.  
  Return, sweet flame, and banish all my fears—  
  For in thy glow, my soul’s true self appears.

866

O gentle flame that burns within my breast,  
Why dost thou scorn the shelter of my plea?  
Thy restless glow denies my heart its rest,  
And feeds upon the hope that dwells in me.  
Each night I court thee with a trembling hand,  
Entreating solace in thy golden light,  
Yet still thou wander’st, wild, across the land,  
A furtive spark that dances out of sight.  
In shadows deep, thy whispering embers call,  
Awakening the silent ache of pain;  
I follow blindly, fearing I may fall,  
Yet bound to thee by love’s unyielding chain.  
  O flame, be kind—let not my spirit yearn,  
  But grant the peace for which my soul doth burn.

867

O let my longing be the wind that sighs,  
And thou the distant flame it seeks in vain,  
Across the moors where silent midnight lies,  
And shadows dance upon the dew-wet plain.  
Let every breath that wanders from my chest  
Be drawn to thee, as rivers to the sea,  
And in the hush where weary hearts find rest,  
Let all my restless hopes return to me.  
For though the stars may mock my fevered quest,  
And time may steal the color from my days,  
No force can cool this fire within my breast,  
Nor blind these eyes that hunger for thy gaze.  
  So long as wind and flame in longing meet,  
  My soul shall strive, though fate decrees defeat.

868

O let me be the candle to thy night,  
Whose flame doth yearn yet quails before thy shade,  
A trembling spark that dreams of lending light  
Though in thy vast eclipse it grows afraid.  
Yet still I burn, and in my longing stay,  
To keep the darkness gentle for thy sleep;  
My waxen soul melts slow in warm decay,  
And trails of hope in golden silence weep.  
If but one flicker dances on thy cheek,  
Let it be proof of all my heart confides;  
For though the dawn doth make my purpose weak,  
I glow anew each time the dusk abides.  
  So let me perish, faithful to thy gloom,  
  A candle spent, yet hallowed by thy room.

869

O thou, whose light doth set my soul aflame,  
Yet scorn’st the fire that in my breast doth grow,  
Thy glance, a spark that knows not yet my name,  
Ignites a longing I dare not bestow.  
My silent heart beats time to thy disdain,  
While hope and fear in warring tempests vie.  
Each word thou speak’st is music to my pain,  
A melody that lifts my spirit high.  
Yet, like the sun that warms but does not stay,  
Thy favor flees ere I can hold it fast.  
I chase thy shadow through the waning day,  
And mourn the golden hours forever past.  
  Still, let me love, though love be but a dream—  
  For in thy light, all other lights do seem.

870

O restless flame that in my breast dost yearn,  
Consume my soul and leave but ash to mourn,  
Thou art the torch for which my senses burn,  
And by thy heat, my night is turned to morn.  
No gentle breeze thy rising fire can quell,  
Nor tears, nor pleas can urge thee to depart;  
Within thy grasp, I am but bound to dwell,  
A willing captive of thy hungry art.  
Thy crimson tongues do dance upon my will,  
With every beat, my heart becomes thy pyre;  
Yet in thy pain, a strange and tender thrill  
Awakes my hope and fans the frail desire.  
  O flame, endure till naught of me remains,  
  And in my ruin, let thy glory reigns.

871

O burning star that lights my soul's unrest,  
Why dost thou flee when I pursue thee most?  
Thy golden fire within my trembling breast  
Doth haunt my dreams, a phantom or a ghost.  
Upon the eve, thou climb’st the dusky sky,  
And tossest down thy beams with careless pride,  
Yet when I reach to claim thee, thou art shy,  
And hid’st thy face behind the night’s dark tide.  
Perchance thy flight is but a lover’s game—  
To draw me forth from shadows into light.  
Or else I chase a vision none can tame,  
Condemned to long for what eludes my sight.  
  Still, hope compels my heart to upward soar,  
  For love but burns the brighter when ignored.

872

O longing soul, thou art a famished flame,  
That hungers still for what it may not claim,  
Within thy breast, desire hath writ its name,  
And every hope is shadowed by its shame.  
Thou wander’st nightly through the silent air,  
In search of solace that the dark denies,  
A pilgrim bound by dreams and deep despair,  
Who nightly weeps beneath unpitying skies.  
Yet in thine ache, a quiet beauty grows,  
A trembling light that will not be concealed;  
The rose that through the frosted winter shows,  
And in its thorn-clad heart, remains unhealed.  
  Take comfort, soul, for longing is not loss—  
  The fire within thee is its own bright cross.

873

My soul, a lantern lost to midnight's plea,  
Doth yearn for thine, yet finds no answering flame.  
The silent stars bear witness unto me,  
And whisper low the echo of thy name.  
Upon the moors where haunted zephyrs sigh,  
I wander, fettered by the moon’s pale gaze,  
While shadows paint thy memory in the sky,  
And time dissolves our laughter into haze.  
Each hour drips slow, a drop of candle’s grief,  
Consuming hope with every waxing night;  
Yet in my heart persists a fragile leaf,  
A promise trembling toward returning light.  
  If dawn shall break and find thee by my side,  
  My soul, once lost, in love’s embrace shall bide.

874

O gentle soul, imprisoned in thy fire,  
Why dost thou yearn where hope may ne'er aspire?  
Thy silken dreams, like shadows, wander late,  
Yet find no rest within their golden spire.  
The moonlight weeps upon thy heavy fate,  
And silent stars bewail thy secret ache.  
Thou seek’st in night the dawn to recreate,  
But morning shuns the path thou fear’st to take.  
Still, courage stirs beneath thy trembling breast,  
And whispers soft of joys not yet begun;  
Let not despair thy gentle heart arrest,  
For every darkened eve must greet the sun.  
  Unfurl thy wings, release thy captive song—  
  For hope, though frail, will bear thee swift and strong.

875

My soul, a moth, doth flutter toward thy flame,  
Yet burns in silence, yearning for thy light.  
Entranced by hope that whispers forth thy name,  
I linger on the edges of the night.  
The gentle glow of thee, my secret sun,  
Invites my wings to risk the fatal heat;  
Though shadows warn that I should turn and run,  
Desire compels my heart to not retreat.  
O fickle fate, dost thou not pity me,  
Who lives between the ember and the ash?
