For every glance thou grant'st, a jubilee,  
Yet every absence strikes a sorrow brash.  
  Still, drawn to thee, I circle in my pain—  
  A moth who lives for love, though love be bane.

876

Within this breast, a furnace doth implore,  
Its embers fed by dreams I dare not name,  
They smolder deep, and yet they hunger more,  
Concealed beneath the quiet flesh and frame.  
By night, the silent coals in secret glow,  
Aflame with hope the daylight would deny;  
Yet still, amidst the ashes, embers grow,  
And billow smoke that stains the inward sky.  
Oft have I sought to quench their fervent fire,  
To douse the spark with reason’s steady rain,  
But passion’s heat surpasses cold desire,  
And logic’s chains dissolve within the flame.  
  Thus in this heart, these yearnings ever war—  
  A blaze within that burns, but shuts no door.

877

O burning star that lights my soul’s unrest,  
Why dost thou haunt the chambers of my will?  
Thy golden gaze denies my mind its rest,  
A ceaseless flood my reason cannot still.  
Each night, thy fire invades my silent dreams,  
A beacon cast on longing’s stormy sea;  
Thy distant spark is nearer than it seems,  
It draws my heart where shadows cease to be.  
Though time’s swift hand would bid thy lustre fade,  
Yet ever bright, thy memory remains;  
In hope and doubt, thy presence undismayed  
Ignites anew the pulse within my veins.  
  O star, relent—release my captive mind,  
  Or grant the peace I seek, yet cannot find.

878

O burning star within my breast confined,  
Why dost thou scorch the garden of my thought?  
Thy fervent rays outshine the gentle mind,  
And leave my reason wilted, overwrought.  
Where once cool shade and gentle blossoms grew,  
Now parched desire cracks the fertile ground;  
The tender buds of hope thy heat undo,  
And in their place are withered petals found.  
Yet still I bear thy fire, fierce and bright,  
A captive sun that will not be denied;  
My sleepless heart endures the endless night,  
Awaiting peace thy fury doth deride.  
  But if thy blaze such passion must impart,  
  Let love, not ashes, flourish in my heart.

879

O, let my soul be tinder to thy flame,  
Though cold neglect doth freeze me where I stand,  
Thy distant light still calls me by my name,  
And draws me forth from winter’s bitter land.  
If hope be frail, yet hope I must embrace,  
For every night doth yield unto the dawn;  
The memory of laughter, love’s bright trace,  
Outlives the hour when comfort’s warmth is gone.  
Yet if thy heart, like marble, will not melt,  
And silent shadows settle on thy brow,  
Still let me cherish every joy I felt,  
And wear my longing as a sacred vow.  
  For love endures, though fortune wax and wane,  
  A steadfast fire that doth all loss sustain.

880

O wilt thou quench the fire within my breast,  
That burns unseen, yet makes my spirit plead?  
Thy absence turns my waking hours to rest,  
Yet in my dreams, thy visage plants a seed.  
The tender night doth shroud my silent ache,  
While hope, like dawn, creeps softly through the gloom.  
For every sigh, a thousand thoughts awake,  
And yearning hearts with secret love consume.  
If but thy voice could grace my longing ear,  
Or gentle hand allay my fevered pain,  
The world would seem less cold, the dark less drear,  
And joy, long exiled, might return again.  
  Yet till thy mercy bids my torment cease,  
  I dwell in flames, and pray for gentle peace.

881

O burning star, thou keep’st thy warmth from me,  
Yet still I chase thy light through endless night,  
Thy golden crown enthroned in mystery,  
Refusing mortal touch, yet gifting sight.  
Thy distant beams do paint the world anew,  
And bid the frozen fields to dance and wake;  
Yet I, denied thy fire, must still pursue  
The hope that dawn and daybreak ever make.  
For shadows flee before thy rising eye,  
And all the darkness quails beneath thy gaze;  
Yet in thy brilliance, I am left to sigh,  
A lover lost amidst the morning’s blaze.  
  O star, though far, thy promise ever gleams—  
  I live and love sustained by borrowed dreams.

882

O yearning Heart, why dost thou haunt mine eyes,  
And pull my soul toward her distant light?  
Each morn I wake beneath unquiet skies,  
Yet dream of her with every fall of night.  
Her laughter lingers on the breath of spring,  
A gentle ghost that dances through my mind,  
Her absence weaves a sorrowed, silver string  
That binds me fast to memories unkind.  
I wander fields where once her footsteps lay,  
The flowers bow, remembering her grace;  
No time nor tide can draw my pain away,  
Nor gentle winds erase her vanished face.  
  Yet hope endures, though fate keep us apart:  
  Love’s flame burns bright within my captive heart.

883

O gentle heart, why dost thou fly my plea,  
Desire, unbridled, wars within my breast,  
As restless winds compel the tumbling sea,  
So stirs my soul, denied of hope or rest.  
Thy eyes, twin lanterns, guide me through the gloom,  
Yet turn away when I would seek their light;  
In absence grows the shadow of my doom,  
Each hour apart transforms the day to night.  
Yet still I chase the phantom of thy grace,  
And fashion dreams from whispers of thy name.  
My longing paints thy memory on my face,  
While reason falters, passion fans the flame.  
  O grant me peace, or let my heart be free;  
  For love in fetters knows no liberty.

884

O gentle Love, why dost thou thus torment,  
And bind my soul in fetters of thy will?  
Thy whispered vows are but a sweet lament,  
Yet all my reason bends to suffer still.  
Thou art the sun that wakes my sleeping heart,  
And yet the storm that shakes my trembling mind;  
With every glance, thou draw’st my world apart,  
And leave’st but longing, desperate and blind.  
Upon thy altar, hope and fear entwine,  
A sacrifice to passion’s fickle flame;  
Desire and doubt in endless war combine,  
And peace is but the echo of thy name.  
  Yet, gentle Love, though sorrow be thy art,  
  I would not trade thee for a calmer heart.

885

O gentle Love, thou tyrant of my breast,  
Why dost thou mock me with her distant eyes?  
Thy arrows pierce my peace, deny me rest,  
And turn my daylight into dark disguise.  
Her voice, a melody the night wind bears,  
Is sweet yet cruel, for it stirs my pain;  
Each whispered hope dissolves in silent airs,  
And longing's fruit proves bitter once again.  
I seek her shadow in the crowded room,  
Yet shadows flee before the waking dawn.  
Within my heart, desire finds its tomb,  
A grave where dreams and memories are drawn.  
  Yet still, O Love, I yield unto thy will:  
  For though thou wound’st, I love and love thee still.

886

O gentle heart, why dost thou flee my call,  
Whilst longing’s hand doth knock at reason’s door?  
Upon thy silence shadows softly fall,  
And hope’s bright lantern flickers evermore.  
The nightingale in secret weeps for thee,  
Her song, a mirror to my hidden pain;  
Each star, a witness to my misery,  
Glows coldly distant through the window pane.  
Yet if thy grace would grant my soul reprieve,  
And turn thy gaze to where devotion lies,  
No longer would my trembling spirit grieve,  
Nor would the dawn conceal these weary sighs.  
  For love, though chased by doubt and midnight’s hue,  
  Finds courage still when hearts believe in true.

887

O gentle Love, why dost thou flee my side,  
When every hour I plead with longing’s breath?  
Thy shadow slips beneath the moon’s pale tide,  
Unmoved by tears or threats of living death.  
I cast my hopes like petals on the breeze,  
Yet every blossom finds a barren ground;  
No whispered vow nor earnest prayer can please  
The silent ear where all my dreams are drowned.  
What charm or token may thy favor win?  
What star must fall to kindle in thine eye?  
My heart, a captive, beats its cage within,  
Awaiting but a glance or soft reply.  
  Yet still I chase thee through the waking morn,  
  For hearts, though scorned, are ever hope-forlorn.

888

O restless Soul, why dost thou pine and yearn,  
Within thy silent cage dost nightly burn,  
The moonlight weaves thy shadow on the wall,  
And whispers soft of dreams that cannot turn.  
Thou seek’st the dawn, yet tremblest at her call,  
A prisoner of hope and ancient fear,  
While time’s slow river hears thy secret thrall,  
And mocks thy longing with each passing year.  
Yet in the hush, a golden promise gleams,  
A subtle breeze that stirs thy slumbering fire;  
Through midnight’s gloom, the heart recalls its dreams,  
And bids thee rise above thy low desire.  
  O soul, take wing! Let sorrow’s night be brief—  
  The world awaits thee, blossoming beneath.

889

O restless soul, thou dost within me yearn,  
In midnight’s grasp thy silent flames do burn,  
The shadows twist thy secrets to discern,  
While time, relentless, counts what I must learn.  
Upon the winds thy whisper drifts and sighs,  
A haunting echo lost in fate’s domain,  
Yet hope’s sweet ember glimmers in thine eyes,  
Defiant still against the weight of pain.  
Oh, weary spirit, wilt thou never rest?  
The dawn arrives, yet peace eludes thy flight.  
In dreams thou seekest solace, ever pressed  
By longing’s hand that holds thee through the night.  
  But gentle morning calls—be still, be free,  
  And find thy peace at last, return to me.

890

O restless soul, thy hunger speaks in sighs,  
Thou knock’st on longing’s door with weary hand;  
The silent moon attends thy plaintive cries,  
And shadows stretch to heed thy mute command.  
Within the hollow chambers of thy breast  
A tempest stirs, unseen by mortal eyes,  
Desire’s fever grants thee little rest,  
Yet hope, like dawn, across the darkness flies.  
What distant star doth guide thee through the night  
When patience fades and yearnings multiply?  
Art thou condemned to wander out of sight,  
Or wilt thou find the peace for which you vie?  
  Let gentle time thy troubled heart appease—  
  For even hunger, sated, finds its ease.

891

O restless Soul, thou knock’st at Love’s closed gate,  
In hope Desire might deign to ease thy pain,  
Yet silent stands the threshold, sealed by Fate,  
And longing’s echo answers thee in vain.  
Thy heart, a ship adrift on moonless seas,  
Seeks haven in the promise of a star,  
But shadows stretch where light should bring thee ease,  
And distance mocks how near thy wishes are.  
Still, gentle hope doth flutter in thy breast,  
A fragile bird that sings despite the storm,  
And dreams of peace that bid thee onward quest,  
Though tempests try to break thy yearning form.  
  So, wait and watch as dawn begins to rise—  
  Love’s gate may open with the morning skies.

892

O gentle Soul, why dost thou pine for flame  
That will not warm thee, nor return thy name?  
Upon the silent night thou call’st in vain,  
Thy words dissolve like dew before the rain.  
The phantom hope that flickers in thine eye  
Doth lead thee dancing on a shadow’s thread;  
Yet still thy heart aspires to mount the sky,  
Though weary wings would rather rest instead.  
Why court the sun that burns and will not yield  
A gentle touch, but scorches as it flies?  
Why sow thy longing in a barren field,  
And water dreams the waking day denies?  
  Yet soft, dear Soul; in longing thou art true—  
  For love unreturned makes the heavens blue.

893

O gentle Soul, thou wander’st night and day,  
In longing’s chains, thy liberty denied,  
Upon the winds thy silent hopes do stray,  
With every breath thy secret pangs confide.  
The silver moon doth watch thee from afar,  
And weeps in pale reflection of thy plight;  
The distant stars, like lanterns, faintly spar  
To guide thee through the corridors of night.  
Yet still thy heart, though fettered, seeks release,  
And dreams of dawn that breaks with golden fire;  
In whispered prayers thou findest fleeting peace,  
A balm to soothe thy ever-burning lyre.  
  Take comfort, Soul, for time’s unyielding art  
  Shall melt thy bonds and mend thy yearning heart.

894

O burning Want, thou tyrant of my breast,  
Why dost thou haunt me in my longing night,  
Thy fevered whisper steals away my rest,  
And blinds my reason with thy hungry light.  
In silence deep, thy shadow stalks my mind,  
A bitter guest that will not be denied;  
I seek for solace, none but thee I find,  
And all contentment in thy flames hath died.  
Yet, in thy torment, I am most alive,  
Each pulse a testament to thy command;  
Without thy fire, how could I strive or thrive,  
Or dream of treasures just beyond my hand?  
  O Want, thou art both prison and release,  
  My heart’s unrest, and yet its sweetest peace.

895

O, restless Want, thou tyrant of mine breast,  
Why dost thou haunt my peace with longing’s fire?  
Thy shadow falls upon my nightly rest,  
And stirs my soul with ever-new desire.  
Thou art the wind that bends my trembling bough,  
The tide that sweeps my anchored hopes away;  
No promise calms the fever of my brow,  
Nor golden dawns can turn thy night to day.  
I chase thy ghost through gardens of delight,  
Yet find thee hid in every fragrant bloom;  
Thy voice is echoed in the hush of night,  
And whispers sweetly in the silent gloom.  
  Yet though I tire and falter in thy chase,  
  I live, O Want, enchanted by thy trace.

896

O thou, Desire, that dost my breast inflame,  
Why dost thou wrest my soul in restless fight?  
Thy embered touch doth set my thoughts to shame,  
And turn the gentle day to fevered night.  
Thou art a shadow cast by golden dreams,  
A whisper'd promise fleeing from my grasp,  
A crystal hope that cracks with morning beams,  
A fleeting joy too delicate to clasp.  
Within thy snare I thrash and plead in vain,  
Yet sweeter grows my hunger for thy face;  
Thy honeyed poison courses through each vein,  
And leaves my reason stranded in thy chase.  
  Yet though I burn, I would not see thee gone—  
  For life without thy fire were all undone.

897

O Desire, thou tyrant of my breast,  
Why dost thou haunt where hope is laid to rest?  
Thy flames arise when reason bids me cease,  
And rob my soul of long-deserved peace.  
In midnight’s hush thou whisper’st wild and low,  
Painting sweet visions time cannot renew,  
Yet every promise thou art swift to sow  
Doth wither ere the morning breaks anew.  
How oft thy shadow danceth on my mind,  
Turning my wisdom into wayward flight?  
I seek for chains thy cunning cannot bind,  
Yet find thee strongest in the dead of night.  
  O, leave me now, and let my spirit be—  
  For I am weary of desiring thee.

898

O Desire, thou tyrant in my breast dost reign,  
With chains of longing forged in midnight’s fire,  
Thy whispers fill my waking hours with pain,  
And drive my secret thoughts to dark aspire.  
Thou art the tempest raging through my veins,  
A fevered wind that will not let me sleep,  
A shadowed specter haunting windowpanes,  
Who bids my silent soul to yearn and weep.  
Yet in thy thrall, I find a bitter bliss,  
A sweetness tainted by a drop of rue;  
For every kiss is bartered for abyss,  
And every hope doth wear a shade of blue.  
  Still, would I cast thee out? Nay, let thee stay—  
  For life is naught if longing fades away.

899

O gentle Desire, why dost thou haunt my breast,  
And bend my will to shadows not possessed?  
Thou art the subtle thief that steals my rest,  
And leaves my soul in longing unconfessed.  
Within the hush of night thy whispers grow,  
A flame that dances just beyond my hand,  
So fair thy form, so distant doth it go,  
Like mist that slips away upon the sand.  
Thou paint’st with dreams the paleness of my cheek,  
And draw’st me forth to chase what may not be,  
Yet in thy chains my heart is rendered weak,  
A willing captive to thy tyranny.  
  O gentle Desire, both torment and delight,  
  Thou art the fire that kindles hope’s faint light.

900

O gentle heart, why dost thou flee my cry,  
When every wish within would have thee stay?  
The silver moon attends my lonely sigh,  
As shadows steal the golden light away.  
If hope be weak, yet longing will not cease,  
For in thy glance a thousand dreams reside.  
The restless winds implore thee grant me peace,  
And bear my soul’s confession where I hide.  
Dost thou not hear the trembling of my voice,  
Or see the vow that lingers in mine eyes?  
For love hath left me little other choice  
Than offer up my truth without disguise.  
  Yet if thou goest, let mercy guide thy part—  
  And leave some gentle memory in my heart.

901

O, let me not deny the fire I feel,  
Yet reason bids my heart its flames conceal;  
Desire, like summer’s sun, wouldst me reveal,  
But prudence weaves a mask I must anneal.  
When night descends and shadows stretch their hands,  
My secret longing dances in the gloom;  
Yet daylight’s gaze demands that I withstand  
The urge to let my soul’s true blossom bloom.  
I walk in silence, fettered by my mind,  
Each glance, a spark that lights forbidden coals;  
Yet hope persists, no fetter can it bind,  
And stirs a gentle riot in our souls.  
  Though duty’s chain would keep my passion weak,  
  My eyes betray the words I dare not speak.

902

O, let me burn with longing’s gentle fire,  
Yet quench my soul lest hope consume my heart;  
Desire’s flame doth climb forever higher,  
Yet wisdom bids me from such dreams depart.  
A single glance can set the night aflame,  
Yet shadows gather where bright passions dwell;  
The sweetest song oft bears the bitter name  
Of love concealed in silence none may tell.  
Thy beauty haunts the corners of my mind,  
A ghost that lingers long beyond the dawn.  
In waking hours thy memory I find,  
Though reason bids my foolishness be gone.  
  Yet still I yearn, though yearning bring me woe—  
  For what is love if not the ache I know?

903

O sweet tormentor, dost thou turn away,  
Whilst flames within my breast beseech thy light?  
Thy silence weaves the shadows of the day,  
And steals the gentle comfort from the night.  
My longing soul is fettered by thy gaze,  
Yet boundless hope doth flutter on the wing;  
Within thine eyes, the cruelest of mazes,  
Where dreams do perish ere they learn to sing.  
If but a word from thee could mercy show,  
I’d trade a thousand dawns for one reply;  
Yet pride forbids my heart to let thee know  
How oft thy name becomes my trembling sigh.  
  Thus in this silent war, my love I hide—  
  A prisoner of hope, and naught beside.

904

If thou wouldst grant the longing of my heart,  
Yet turn’st away and bid’st my hopes depart,  
A shadow falls where once the sunlight lay,  
And mournful winds through empty branches start.  
My soul entreats thee with a silent plea,  
While night descends and paints the roses pale;  
I seek thy face in dreams that cannot be,  
And trace thy name upon the midnight veil.  
O, let not cold indifference be thy guise,  
Nor steel thy gentle voice against my cry,  
For every tear that trembles in mine eyes  
Doth mark the measure of my silent sigh.  
  Yet if thy love be gone, my heart shall keep  
  A vigil for thee still, though doomed to weep.

905

O turn thine eyes, thou bright and distant star,  
For absence burns whilst presence healeth naught;  
Thy silver beams, though scattered wide and far,  
Recall the gentle comfort thou once brought.  
The night, in silent longing, wears thy face,  
While shadows stretch and yearn to touch thy light.  
Each breath I draw is haunted by thy grace,  
A memory that lingers past the night.  
If hope could bridge the gulf 'twixt thee and me,  
I'd scale the vault of heaven for thy gaze;  
Yet chained am I by cruel mortality,  
Condemned to worship thee through mist and haze.  
  Still, let my soul in whispered prayers impart  
  The constancy of love that fills my heart.

906

O restless soul, why dost thou yearn for fire,  
Yet shiver cold within thy cloistered breast?  
Dost hope consume thee with a fierce desire,  
Or doth thy fear deny thee gentle rest?  
The world without is bright with burning flame,  
While shadows linger deep within thine eyes;  
Thy silent longing, thou dar’st not to name,  
Is masked by cautious words and guarded sighs.  
Yet still the embers smolder in thy core,  
A secret heat that pleads to be set free.  
What holds thee fast behind thy chamber door?  
What chains thee here, and keeps thy spirit’s plea?  
  O break thy bonds—a phoenix be, arise!  
  And greet the dawn with flame that never dies.

907

O restless soul, imprisoned by thy fire,  
Yet bound to ice, thou tremblest for thy will,  
Desire’s tempest, tangled in the mire,  
Wouldst climb the heights, yet fears the silent chill.  
Thy hopes, like shadows dancing on the wall,  
Confess their longing, fluttering to be free,  
Yet shackled by the doubts that softly call  
And haunt the heart with pale uncertainty.  
Oh, bid the winter’s frost release its hold,  
And let the summer’s breath thy spirit warm;  
Cast off the chains, be daring, fierce, and bold,  
Defy the storm and rise above the storm.  
  For though the soul is tossed by fate’s command,  
  Its blaze and thaw lie always in thy hand.

908

My soul, divided, longs yet doth protest,  
Desire's fierce fire wars with reason's frost,  
Within my breast, a storm that grants no rest,  
Each hope and fear by equal tempests tossed.  
The morning’s golden light calls forth my dreams,  
But evening’s shadow bids my heart refrain;  
My will is torn asunder at the seams,  
Entrapped between delight and gentle pain.  
Oh, how the heart pursues what mind denies,  
And seeks the sun while shunning morning’s truth!  
Yet reason’s chains cannot restrain the skies  
Of longing’s flight, nor cage the wings of youth.  
  Thus in this strife, my soul must ever be—  
  A captive yearning sore to wander free.

909

O, turn thine eye, lest I be lost to night,  
For every glance withheld doth scorch my soul,  
Thy absence dims the stars’ resplendent light,  
And longing rends my heart beyond control.  
The moon in envy veils her silver fire,  
When thou art gone, the world is naught but shade;  
Each moment stretched upon the rack, entire,  
Till hope, like fragile glass, begins to fade.  
Yet if thy gaze should fall on me anew,  
The sun itself would pale before thy grace,  
A thousand blooms would blush in every hue,  
And joy, unbridled, dance across my face.  
  So let thine eyes deliver me from woe,  
  For in their light, my soul’s true self I know.

910

O soul that burns yet dwelleth lone in night,  
Dost thou not crave the sun, yet flee its fire?  
Within thy breast, a war ‘twixt hope and fright,  
A trembling string, both yearning and desire.  
The dawn doth bid thee rise from shadow’s keep,  
But comfort hides within the silent shade;  
Thou dream’st of light that rouses thee from sleep,  
Yet quails before the brilliance it hath made.  
How oft thy longing eyes survey the morn,  
And in its golden promise find thy pain—  
For every gleam doth leave thee more forlorn,  
A captive soul, enamored of its chain.  
  Yet courage, heart: for even night must yield,  
  And love, like day, awaits beyond thy field.

911

My soul in longing doth thy absence chide,  
Yet chained by hope, in hopelessness I dwell,  
Each hour without thee stretches far and wide,  
A silent bell that marks a lover’s hell.  
The sun doth pale where once thy laughter shone,  
And shadows creep where once thy light would play;  
The night is colder now that thou art gone,  
And dreams, once sweet, in sorrow waste away.  
Yet in my breast thy memory burns bright,  
A beacon fierce against the tide of pain;  
I conjure visions soft as wings of night,  
And whisper prayers to see thee once again.  
  Though fate may part us with its cruel art,  
  Love’s steadfast flame shall never leave my heart.

912

O burning wish, that wars against my peace,  
Yet craves the flame that bids my longing cease,  
A captive heart that trembles at release,  
Still hoping love might grant its own surcease.  
Thou art the midnight star that haunts my sky,  
A silver wound that will not heal nor fade,  
For every breath I take, I breathe a sigh,  
And in thy shadow, all my joys are laid.  
Why must desire in secret chambers dwell,  
And make a brief delight of constant pain?  
Each stolen glance is but a tolling knell,  
Reminding me such yearning is in vain.  
  Yet hope endures, unfading and unwise,  
  That I might live and die within thine eyes.

913

Within my breast doth burn a restless fire,  
And yet cold reason bids my heart retire;  
Desire and doubt in silent combat meet,  
While hope and fear wage war upon my seat.  
The moon doth pale in witness to my plight,  
As stars bear secrets folded in the night.  
A gentle sigh escapes my lips unbidden,  
For dreams must dwell in chambers closely hidden.  
Yet visions fair do haunt my waking eyes,  
And whisper truths that sober thought denies.  
Oft have I sought to quench this fervent flame,  
Yet find my efforts fruitless, all the same.  
  Thus bound am I, both prisoner and king,  
  Enthralled by love, yet powerless to sing.

914

O burning wish that wars with reason’s frost,  
My heart’s wild flame by sober thought is tossed,  
Desire, a tempest veiled in midnight’s hue,  
Consumes the stars that caution once embossed.  
Yet reason’s chill, in silent counsel true,  
Attempts to cage the fire that passion cost.  
The mind debates, yet cannot quite subdue  
The sweet unrest that leaves me tempest-tossed.  
Within this storm, both hope and fear conspire,  
Each hour entwined with longing’s subtle thread.  
No gentle peace can calm the secret fire  
That rages on where angels fear to tread.  
  Yet if to love is folly’s noble art,  
  Then let wild longing ever rule my heart.

915

O cruel flame that bids my heart to yearn,  
Yet chills my soul with every hope denied,  
Within thy glow my secret wounds still burn,  
And yet I wear my longing as my pride.  
Thy fickle light, it dances on the wall,  
A shadowed promise flickering and fleet;  
It rises high, then lets my spirits fall,  
And scorches lips that taste its honeyed heat.  
I chase thee through the corridors of night,  
A moth bewitched by dreams I cannot keep;  
Thy warmth eludes my grasp though close in sight,  
And leaves me restless in the arms of sleep.  
  Yet, though thy torment bids my peace depart,  
  I cherish still the embers in my heart.

916

O burning wish that chills my trembling frame,  
Thou art both balm and wound within my breast,  
A flickering torch that neither dies nor tame,  
Yet bids my restless soul for thee unrest.  
Thy whispers haunt the silence of the night,  
They paint thy visage on the moon’s pale face,  
And in the hush before the breaking light,  
I chase thy shadow through the starry space.  
Desire’s fevered hand doth stroke my heart,  
While reason’s voice entreats me to forswear,  
Yet neither wit nor will can make thee part,  
For hope and longing linger in the air.  
  O let me burn, if burning be my fate,  
  For in thy fire, my life is consecrate.

917

O let my longing not in darkness dwell,  
Nor yet in silence choke my fervent plea,  
But let the moon my secret sorrow tell  
Upon the restless whisper of the sea.  
For every night does draw thy face anew  
In silver lines upon the midnight air,  
And hope, like rising dawn, doth chase the blue  
That shadows cast where once I found thee fair.  
Thy absence weaves a tapestry of pain,  
Yet memory clings to fragrance left behind;  
Within my heart, thy name, a soft refrain,  
Doth echo sweet, though fate be most unkind.  
  Yet in my dreams thou art not far from me—  
  There love, unbroken, walks eternally.

918

I am a moth that circles round your flame,  
Yet fear the fire that draws me closer still,  
Desire and dread within my heart proclaim  
A war between my caution and my will.  
Your light, alluring, dances in the night,  
It promises a warmth I dare not trust;  
My trembling wings are caught between delight  
And ashes left for longing hearts to dust.  
I hover in the shadows near your glow,  
Entranced by every flicker, every spark,  
Unsure if I should stay or if I go—  
The dawn is far, the night is deep and dark.  
  Yet, drawn by love, I flutter to your side,  
  And in your blaze, my fears and hopes collide.

919

My heart, a lantern starved for kindled light,  
Still aches to blaze within your shadowed gaze,  
It flickers hope through corridors of night,  
And yearns to set the coldest dark ablaze.  
Your silence drapes the moon upon the floor,  
A silver shroud that chills my longing hands;  
Yet in that hush, I seek your warmth the more,  
A prisoner bound by love’s unyielding bands.  
Each breath I draw is ink upon the page  
Of dreams composed where once our laughter played;  
Yet time, that thief, has set us on a stage  
Where memory’s bright lanterns slowly fade.  
  Yet should you turn and offer light anew,  
  My heart would blaze a thousand suns for you.

920

Within my chest, a wildfire aches for you,  
Desire’s tide pulls reason far from shore;  
The moonlight bathes my longing heart anew,  
And shadows whisper secrets I implore.  
Your voice, a gentle breeze in summer’s heat,  
Enchants my soul, and draws the night’s embrace,  
While every glance makes trembling pulses beat,  
And blushes bloom like roses on my face.  
Yet distance holds a barricade of fears,  
A silent wall that I would brave and break,  
For hope persists through all its doubts and tears,  
And dreams of love refuse my heart to slake.  
  So let this wildfire burn and never die,  
  Its flame a beacon ‘neath the endless sky.

921

If I am but a moth to your bright flame,  
Then let me burn, though you not know my name.  
Within your light, my shadows lose their claim,  
Desire consumes the wings from whence I came.  
Your radiance turns the midnight into gold,  
Each fleeting glance a sun I dare not face.  
Though by your warmth my fragile heart is told  
To spin and spiral closer to your grace.  
I know the cost of longing is my fall,  
Yet sweet the risk to drift within your gaze.  
Oblivion, if you should heed my call,  
Is wrapped in velvet fire and gentle haze.  
  Better to perish drawn to what you are  
  Than live untouched and never reach your star.

922

Like thirsty roots that ache for summer rain,  
My soul bends low and pleads to touch your light,  
Each day bereft grows heavy with its pain,  
Yet hope still stirs the hollow of my night.  
Your laughter weaves through shadows like the breeze,  
That stirs the leaves and coaxes buds to bloom;  
In absence, longing deepens by degrees,  
And paints your memory on my heart’s dim room.  
What alchemy resides within your gaze,  
That turns my leaden hours into gold?  
I count the moments, lost within your maze,  
A prisoner of desire, yet bold.  
  Return, and let your presence end this drought—  
  For you alone my spirit sings devout.

923

As thirsty roots crave rain that will not fall,  
So do I yearn for you, yet thirst in vain.  
The silent dusk upon my heart does call,  
And shadows bloom in absence, fed by pain.  
Each dawn’s pale fire reveals your shape in dreams,  
A ghostly trace that morning steals away;  
Desire, a river lost in stony streams,  
Finds not your touch to guide its winding way.  
Yet hope, persistent as the budding rose,  
Defies the drought and lifts its head anew.  
Though barren winds in empty fields impose,  
I tend my longing, watered still by you.  
  Should fortune’s storm at last release the skies,  
  May love descend, and roots in joy arise.

924

My soul, a lantern grasped in trembling hands,  
Burns through the fog of want it can't withstand.  
Each step uncertain, shadows press and swell,  
While distant hopes like silent beacons dwell.  
The night enfolds my heart in sable thread,  
Yet still I wander, longing for the dawn,  
And chase the fleeting light that lies ahead,  
Though every promise seems already gone.  
Yet soft! The gloom is pierced by subtle gleam,  
A memory of warmth from days before;  
It stirs a tender, half-forgotten dream  
And bids me trust the morning evermore.  
  So though the dark may threaten and confound,  
  Within my fragile light, my strength is found.

925

My soul, a moth that flutters near your flame,  
Caught in the heat of longing, pleads your name.  
Each trembling wing, a hope I cannot tame,  
Drawn ever closer, heedless of the blame.  
The night enfolds me, velvet, deep, and wide,  
Yet in your light my restless spirit yearns;  
No shadow’s comfort bids my heart to hide,  
While every thought toward your fire returns.  
I burn with dreams that flicker in your gaze,  
A silent ache that dances on the air;  
Desire consumes the hours and the days,  
A gentle peril woven through despair.  
  If love be ruin, let me thus be lost—  
  For in your flame, my soul would pay the cost.

926

My soul’s a lantern burning for your light,  
Yet shadows beg me turn from what I crave;  
I wander, lost between the day and night,  
A captive heart, too timid to be brave.  
Your voice, a distant chime upon the breeze,  
Awakes the dawn within my sleeping chest,  
But doubts arise like ever-arching trees,  
And cloud the hope I long to manifest.  
What fate decrees, I know not nor protest,  
For love’s a tide that pulls me unaware;  
My thoughts are ships that cannot find their rest,  
Adrift upon the storm your eyes declare.  
  If you but turn and let your own heart see,  
  This lantern’s flame shall burn eternally.

927

My soul, a thirsty field beneath your rain,  
Still aches for drops that never touch the ground.  
The clouds of longing gather, swell with pain,  
Yet in their brooding silence, I am drowned.  
Your distant thunder stirs the sleeping seed  
And tempts the hope of green where dust has lain;  
But parched, I wait—no roots nor shoots succeed,  
Just shadows shifting o’er a barren plain.  
If dawn would break with gentle, falling dew,  
And mercy soften all this hardened earth,  
Then life might rise in colors bold and true,  
Restored by love, remade in blooming worth.  
  Yet here I lie and yearn beneath your sky,  
  Unwatered still, and wondering why.

928

My soul, a lantern hung outside your gate,  
Burns bright with longing none but you ignite.  
Each evening’s hush, I linger, patient, wait,  
And hope your shadow graces me with light.  
The moon, a silent witness in the sky,  
Records my vigil with her silver pen.  
She weaves your name through every whispered sigh  
That slips between the world of now and then.  
If dawn should find me weary, still I keep  
The flame alive, though winds of doubt prevail.  
No storm nor midnight chill can make it sleep,  
For in your presence, all my fears grow pale.  
  O, let this humble lantern guide your way,  
  And lead you home to love’s enduring day.

929

My soul, a moth, is drawn to your bright flame,  
Yet burns in longing, never called by name.  
Thy radiance paints the night in golden hue,  
And bids my heart awake from shadow’s claim.  
Each hour apart is sorrow born anew,  
While hope and fear in silent warfare vie.  
Thy laughter stirs the dawn, the morning dew  
Reflects thy smile, and teaches stars to die.  
What curse is this, to flutter close, yet veiled,  
To circle round thy warmth and never land?  
By unseen walls my trembling wings are jailed,  
Yet still I reach, though reason countermands.  
  O grant but one sweet glance to ease my plight,  
  And I shall brave the dark for one more night.

930

Desire’s fire consumes my fragile will,  
A storm within, I beg for peace in vain,  
Each whispered wish the night air seeks to fill,  
Yet leaves me bound in longing’s golden chain.  
The moon observes my restless, sleepless plight,  
Her silver gaze reveals what I conceal—  
A heart aflame and trembling in the night,  
Afraid to hope, yet aching still to feel.  
Temptation’s voice is honey on the breeze,  
It lures me deeper in the tangled maze,  
Where reason falters, brought down to its knees,  
Confounded by the heat of passion’s blaze.  
  Yet in this fire my spirit learns to rise,  
  Transformed by longing, tempered by its cries.

931

My heart, a lantern starved of its own flame,  
Begs night to feed what daylight cannot name.  
In silent rooms where hope and shadow meet,  
I pace the hours on faltering, restless feet.  
The moon spills silver secrets on my skin,  
Each beam a promise whispered from afar;  
Yet longing grows while dreams are growing thin,  
And wounds awake beneath a vanished star.  
O gentle dark, enfold my weary soul,  
And mend the cracks that sorrow leaves behind.  
Let memory drift where kinder waters roll,  
And hush the storm that reason cannot bind.  
  Still, dawn may find this lantern softly lit,  
  For hearts endure when night would smother it.

932

Desire burns within—a wildfire caged,  
Its embers plead for rain to cool their flame,  
Yet passion’s prison cannot be assuaged,  
For longing ever sets the heart to shame.  
In silent nights, the restless spirits yearn,  
A hush of hope upon the air is cast;  
Each breath inflames the wish for its return,  
The future haunted by a fervent past.  
Oft have I tried to douse the hungry spark,  
To banish dreams that flicker in my mind,  
But shadows dance where reason leaves it dark,  
And chains of will grow loose as they unwind.  
  So let desire blaze bright, though it betray—  
  For love’s fierce fire outshines the break of day.

933

My heart’s a lantern starved for want of flame,  
It flickers, pleading, hungry for your spark.  
Within its hollow glass, it murmurs blame  
Against the chilling silence and the dark.  
Each breath you take, a breeze my fire would crave,  
Yet distance drapes its shadow o’er my light.  
I dream you near—a beacon I would save—  
To banish all the phantoms of the night.  
If but your gaze would kindle embered hope,  
This trembling vessel might ignite anew,  
For with your touch, my soul could learn to cope  
And paint the midnight sky with golden hue.  
  O, grant my lantern warmth, and let it burn,  
  For only in your glow may I return.

934

Desire burns within, a silent flame,  
Yet leaves me cold beneath your distant gaze.  
My thoughts are shackled, captive to your name,  
And wander hopeless, lost within your maze.  
The moon ascends and paints your face in light,  
A silver ghost that haunts my waking dreams.  
Each hour undone by longing’s gentle blight,  
My heart—adrift—unravels at the seams.  
If hope could span the gulf that grows each day,  
Or words could breach the fortress of your mind,  
Then love, unmasked, might find its rightful way  
And in your arms its sanctuary find.  
  Yet silence reigns, and hope must learn to wait,  
  For love’s true hour is patient with its fate.

935

My heart’s a lantern, begging for your flame,  
It flickers out when shadows whisper no.  
Yet in the dark, it softly calls your name,  
A beacon where the silent longings go.  
The night winds tease my hope with gentle chill,  
While candles dream of dawn within the gloom.  
Each memory, a ghost, that lingers still,  
Pale petals scattered in an empty room.  
Oh, grant me warmth that never will expire,  
Let kindled light abide when dusk is near.  
For I am but a vessel of desire,  
Who trembles at the chance you might appear.  
  So set alight my soul, and let it burn,  
  That in your glow, my wandering heart return.

936

O Desire, do not haunt my waking hours so,  
For every thought of you becomes my plea;  
You steal my peace and set my heart aglow,  
Yet bind me fast in longing’s tyranny.  
Your whispered promise dances on the air,  
A phantom touch that lingers on my skin;  
I chase your shade through dreams none else may share,  
And taste the sweet defeat of what’s within.  
Though reason bids me cast your spell aside,  
And cautions me with wisdom’s measured tone,  
Still, in your fire, my trembling hopes reside—  
A captive soul, unwilling and alone.  
  Yet if thy spark such torment must bestow,  
  Let me be burned, and ever crave thee so.

937

Desire, relentless, knocks upon my chest,  
And begs my reason yield to longing’s reign;  
It weaves a fever, robbing me of rest,  
And plants wild roses where my thoughts remain.  
My waking hours are haunted by its call,  
A siren’s whisper threading through my mind;  
No steel resolve can build so firm a wall  
That yearning’s tide will not erode in time.  
I pace the night’s confessional embrace,  
And bargain with the stars for sweet release;  
Yet every shadow wears your vanished face,  
And silence speaks your name without surcease.  
  O, may desire in gentle embers lie,  
  Or grant me wings to soar, or else to die.

938

Desire, relentless guest, invades my chest,  
And begs your mercy, trembling at your door,  
It will not sleep, nor grant my spirit rest,  
But stalks my waking thoughts forevermore.  
It weaves your name in every whispered breeze,  
And writes your likeness in the molten sky,  
It haunts the hush of nights with aching pleas,  
And sets my blood ablaze when you are nigh.  
Yet hope, though fragile, lifts a fervent prayer,  
That you might look with kindness on my pain;  
For in your eyes, I find both balm and snare—  
A gentle touch that soothes, a sweet refrain.  
  So let me dwell where longing finds release,  
  Within your arms, my heart at last in peace.

939

O gentle heart, why turn thy gaze away,  
When longing’s hands reach out for thee each night?  
The moon in silver silence bids thee stay,  
Yet shadows steal thy memory from sight.  
Upon the breeze thy whispered vows depart,  
Their echoes fading where the willows bend.  
I clutch the fragments of a hopeful heart,  
Uncertain if thy coldness shall amend.  
The stars in council weep for love’s decline,  
While dawn in sorrow paints the distant sky.  
Still do I yearn to call thy spirit mine,  
Though hope and fear in equal measure vie.  
  Yet if thou wouldst return, my soul would sing:  
  From winter’s thaw comes bright and boundless spring.

940

O Desire, knock not so upon my chest,  
For she, my sun, denies thy urgent plea,  
Her gaze, a dawn that leaves my soul unblest,  
Yet binds my thoughts in golden slavery.  
The night, in hush, conspires with my unrest,  
To paint her shadow deep inside of me.  
Though stars may urge my longing to protest,  
Her silence seals my lips with chastity.  
What hope have I, when fortune turns away,  
And leaves me stranded in the briny deep?  
I count the hours, each a lengthened day,  
While memory and longing nightly creep.  
  Yet if she smiled, the world would brighter seem,  
  And I would wake within a lover's dream.

941

Desire knocks nightly at my guarded heart,  
And pleads with Reason, begging to remain;  
She paints with longing’s brush a secret art,  
And kindles embers Reason would restrain.  
A thousand whispered dreams invade my sleep,  
And tempt my will with honeyed, haunted song;  
Yet Reason stands as sentinel to keep  
The tide of yearning from where it belongs.  
But oh, how sweet the pull of passion’s fire,  
That stirs beneath the silence of the mind!  
Each thought betrays a glimpse of true desire,  
And chains of logic gently come untwined.  
  Though Reason argues, love will have its day—  
  The heart, once swayed, will seldom turn away.

942

O restless soul, why do you beg the night  
To grant the fire your waking hopes ignite,  
When dawn’s soft promise lingers out of sight,  
And dreams like distant ships slip into light?  
The moon’s pale counsel cannot soothe your ache,  
Nor hush the fever burning in your breast;  
The stars confess the wishes you forsake,  
Yet leave your heart unanchored and unblessed.  
You chase the shadows dancing on the wall,  
Mistaking fleeting shapes for destiny,  
While silent hours heed your silent call  
And whisper truths you scarce allow to be.  
  Yet trust, sweet soul, the coming of the day:  
  New fire is born when night has passed away.

943

O restless soul, why do you pull me near,  
And whisper hungry wishes in my ear?  
Thy shadowed longing lingers in my chest,  
A silent tide that grants my heart no rest.  
Thou haunt’st the midnight hush with secret fears,  
And paint’st with trembling hand the world unclear.  
Thy murmurs wake the dreams I thought had died,  
And stir the embers I had cast aside.  
Why must thy voice, like wind through autumn leaves,  
Summon my hopes to dance, though reason grieves?  
Yet in thy ache a bitter sweetness grows,  
A yearning thorn entangled with the rose.  
  O spirit wild, thy hunger is my own—  
  Together lost, together still unknown.

944

My soul, a restless tide, cries out for more,  
It pleads with want, the hunger in its core.  
Upon the moonlit waves of longing's sea,  
I drift through shadows that refuse to flee.  
Each night, desire’s lantern softly gleams,  
Illuminating paths I’ve yet to tread,  
While hope, a distant star, ignites my dreams,  
And stirs the silent doubts within my head.  
The wind of fate, both gentle and unkind,  
Blows ever toward horizons yet unknown,  
Unraveling the knots that bind my mind,  
And leaving me to wander, still alone.  
  Yet in this ceaseless search, my heart may find  
  The peace that waits beyond the restless tide.

945

My soul, a beggar knocking at your gate,  
Implores the night to whisper you my name,  
While hope and doubt in silent counsel wait,  
And every star burns witness to my shame.  
The moon, pale herald, weeps upon my plea,  
Her silver tears dissolve in trembling air;  
Yet still I stand, a shadow by your tree,  
Uncertain if you know or ever care.  
Your window glows with secrets I adore,  
Each lighted pane a promise or a lie;  
My heart, a vagrant, wanders at your door,  
Longing for home, yet fearing your goodbye.  
  Have mercy then, and let your kindness fall—  
  For love is richest when it begs at all.

946

O Soul, thou knock’st on doors that will not yield,  
Desire’s wild envoy lost upon her field,  
Each hope, a seed unburied in the shield  
Of stubborn earth, by weight of silence sealed.  
The moon attends thy footsteps in the night,  
Yet casts no silver path to guide thee home;  
In longing’s fog, the stars withhold their light,  
And wandering thoughts in shadowed corners roam.  
How oft hast thou implored the fates to see  
The secret gate swung open at thy cry?  
Yet patient stone resists thy urgent plea,  
And echo’s laughter answers every sigh.  
  Still knock, brave soul, though doors remain untried—  
  For faithful hearts find passage where they bide.

947

My soul, a beggar knocking at your gate,  
Implores your heart to see its silent ache;  
Each hope a coin I cast to court my fate,  
Yet find your gaze a sea I dare not wake.  
The night unfolds in whispers of your name,  
A tapestry of longing, veiled in doubt;  
Desire’s flicker, smothered by its shame,  
Still braves the dark to seek your spirit out.  
In dreams, I wander halls your laughter fills,  
A shadow trailing where your footsteps go;  
The dawn arrives, but longing lingers still,  
Its petals bruised by every no I know.  
  Yet if you grant my soul one tender sign,  
  This beggar’s heart, at last, would call you mine.

948

Desire, you haunt me like a restless ghost,  
You whisper hunger where I beg for peace,  
Within my chest, your shadow lingers most,  
And bids the gentle voice of calm to cease.  
You stir the embers I believed were dead,  
And conjure dreams that tremble in the night,  
You paint forbidden visions in my head,  
And turn the dawn to aching from delight.  
Why must you draw me where no rest is found,  
And surge within my veins as heated fire?  
I chase your phantom footsteps, spellbound,  
Yet every step enkindles more desire.  
  Oh, leave me not, tormentor nor my friend—  
  For in your grasp, I live and meet my end.

949

O Desire, thou tyrant king within my chest,  
Why do you haunt my thoughts and steal my rest?  
With fevered whispers, night and day possessed,  
You stir the calm within my soul’s behest.  
Unbidden flames ignite beneath my skin,  
A longing born of shadows and of light;  
I chase thy phantom form through realms within,  
Yet find thee always just beyond my sight.  
Thy honeyed promises, so sweet and dire,  
Entice my reason to forsake its throne;  
I burn in silence, prisoner of thy fire,  
And dream of joys that may not be my own.  
  Yet, cruel Desire, I love thy ceaseless pain—  
  For through thy chains, I know I live again.

950

Desire, thou tyrant, whisper in my ear,  
And pull my heart with hands I cannot see,  
Thou draw’st me close to shadows I should fear,  
Yet promise light where darkness ought to be.  
Thy honeyed breath enfolds my waking thought,  
And paints illusions bright upon my mind,  
With every longing sigh thy net is wrought,  
And Reason’s chains thy cunning fingers bind.  
I chase thee blindly down uncertain ways,  
My footsteps echo soft on empty ground,  
In every dream thy golden visage stays,  
Yet in the dawn, no trace of thee is found.  
  Still, in thy thrall, I barter peace for pain,  
  And pray thy voice will call me once again.

951

O Desire, whisper mercy to my soul,  
Why tempt my heart with dreams you won't fulfill?  
Thy honeyed voice does beckon with control,  
Yet leaves me hollow, haunted by thy will.  
A phantom flame that dances in the night,  
You paint illusions on my sleepless mind,  
And chase away the gentle touch of light,  
With shadows of the bliss I cannot find.  
Thou art the thief that steals my reason's crown,  
And sets my thoughts on fire with longing's spark;  
For every hope thou buildest, thou dost drown  
Within the deepest corners of the dark.  
  Yet though my spirit breaks beneath thy art,  
  Still, sweet Desire, thou dost possess my heart.

952

Desire knocks nightly at my trembling door,  
Her whispered pleas I dare not yet ignore,  
She weaves her longing through the silent floor,  
And paints her wishes on my chamber’s core.  
The candle’s flicker shadows on the wall,  
As secret hopes take shape within the gloom,  
Each heartbeat answers to her urgent call,  
A budding rose that aches in silent bloom.  
Yet reason’s voice attempts to bind my will,  
To cast her siren beckonings aside;  
But passion’s tide flows stronger, stronger still,  
And reason’s chains in heated waves subside.  
  O, let me yield where midnight dreams conspire,  
  And in her arms be kindled by desire.

953

Desire, my captor, whispers in my ear,  
And begs my heart to hope against your will.  
It paints your absence sharper, crystal clear,  
And makes the silent night grow colder still.  
Your shadow haunts the corners of my mind,  
A phantom touch that lingers on my skin;  
I chase the traces you have left behind,  
Yet find no solace, only ache within.  
Each longing sigh becomes a secret prayer,  
Each dream of you, a bittersweet delight.  
I dare to wish that you, too, feel this snare,  
And wrestle with such hunger in the night.  
  Yet if my yearning brings me naught but pain,  
  Desire, relentless, binds me with its chain.

954

Desire’s ember burns against my will,  
Yet reason’s voice commands my heart be still.  
A tempest rages where my hopes distill,  
Yet cautious thought would bind my veins and chill.  
Sweet longing’s fire consumes the careful night,  
While wisdom’s shadow cools the fevered brow.  
In secret silence, passion courts its light,  
Yet sober doubts would chain what dreams allow.  
What war within, when longing vies with sense,  
And every heartbeat answers reason’s scorn!  
I chase the flame, then build a stronger fence,  
Divided thus, in pleasure’s cause forlorn.  
  Yet in this strife, my soul is forged anew,  
  For love is wise, but longing still is true.

955

O let me crave the flame that scorches deep,  
Yet beg release from longing’s cruel embrace,  
For love’s bright fire denies the balm of sleep  
And paints false hope upon my wearied face.  
A torch I bear, though shadows cloud my way,  
Its fevered light both guide and curse to me;  
Desire’s tempest tosses night and day,  
While reason’s voice is drowned within the sea.  
What folly, then, to chase a fleeting gleam,  
When every step but leads to further pain!  
Still, in my breast, the embers brightly teem,  
Defying wisdom’s plea to break the chain.  
  Yet if this fire be all that makes me live,  
  Let flames consume—my soul to longing give.

956

Desire pulls me near while reason holds me back,  
Your eyes ignite a fire my vows attack;  
A tempest stirs within my trembling breast,  
Yet prudence bids my reckless heart to rest.  
The gentle night reveals what day would hide,  
In whispered dreams, forbidden truths arise;  
My longing strains against the chains inside,  
Yet wisdom dims the hunger in my eyes.  
What cruel delight, to stand upon this brink,  
Where passion’s tide and caution’s current meet;  
If I but taste the nectar that I think,  
Would ruin trail behind its honeyed sweet?  
  Yet still, between my heart and mind I stray,  
  Torn by the night, restored by break of day.

957

I beg thee, turn thy gaze from distant stars,  
Yet crave the warmth denied by what thou art;  
The heavens glow, indifferent to our scars,  
While chilly silence overtakes the heart.  
What profit lies in seeking realms above,  
If earthly arms lie empty at the close?  
The night conceals what daylight cannot prove,  
And longing lingers where affection goes.  
Yet in thy eyes, a universe is born,  
Which humbles every planet in its grace;  
The galaxies may glitter and adorn,  
But none can match the light upon thy face.  
  So let the stars be mute, their brilliance pale—  
  Thy nearness fills the void they cannot scale.

958

When night reveals the hunger of my heart,  
Your silence grows where all my wishes start.  
The moon in silver arcs across my pain,  
Its borrowed light recalling what I miss.  
Within the hush, my dreams wax and depart,  
Each shadowed hope dissolving in your bliss.  
The air is thick with words we never said,  
Unspoken vows that haunt the restless dark.  
Desire stirs in chambers cold and dead,  
While longing burns and leaves a crimson mark.  
I count the hours that tremble till the dawn,  
Imagining your hand within my own.  
  Yet still, the empty quiet lingers on,  
  And I am left to dream of you alone.

959

If cold denial stands where longing burns,  
Let mercy rise where cruel silence turns.  
When hope is caged behind a guarded wall,  
And trembling hearts must mask what they discern,  
Let gentle words like summer rain befall  
The barren soul where winter’s lessons churn.  
If shadows steal the promise from the day,  
And trust lies buried deep beneath the frost,  
Let light of kindness guide the weary way  
And teach the wounded spirit what was lost.  
For silence wields a sharper blade than hate,  
Yet mercy’s hand can heal the deepest scar.  
Where hearts endure the icy weight of fate,  
Let love ascend, more steadfast than a star.  
Thus may denial’s chill at last be gone,  
And hope restored when mercy’s dawn is drawn.

960

My soul, divided, yearns yet fears to crave,  
It pleads for fire but trembles in the cold,  
A secret prisoner, yet not a slave,  
Desires burn bright where silent doubts take hold.  
The night’s soft whisper bids my courage rise,  
Yet shadowed hopes in wary corners hide,  
A thousand dreams concealed behind my eyes,  
Conflicting tides that battle deep inside.  
What bitter sweetness stirs within my chest,  
Where longing’s ache and caution’s chill entwine?  
In seeking solace, I am dispossessed,  
A wanderer on borders undefined.  
  Yet dawn may find me whole despite the fray—  
  For hearts, though torn, are stronger by the day.

961

Within my soul, desire wars with doubt,  
A silent plea that will not be confessed,  
Though hope, like dawn, would drive the shadows out,  
My reason tempts my longing heart to rest.  
Yet every star that pricks the velvet sky  
Reminds me of the dreams I dare not name,  
And every whispered breeze that passes by  
Ignites anew the old, forbidden flame.  
Oft have I sought to smother what I feel,  
To bury want beneath a mask of pride,  
But passion, chained, will struggle to reveal  
The truth no trembling lips have yet supplied.  
  So let this inner tempest rage and burn,  
  For from its fire, perhaps my soul shall learn.

962

Within my soul, two hungers wage their war,  
One reaching out, one pulling back in fear,  
Desire’s flame that aches to wander far,  
Yet caution’s whisper holds my spirit near.  
A golden hope that beckons me to strive,  
While shadows threaten all my dreams to blight,  
Ambition’s wings that teach the heart to dive,  
Restraint’s cold hand enfolds me in the night.  
Oft have I stood between the dusk and dawn,  
Uncertain which bright path the fates would bless,  
Each longing pulse a world to build upon,  
Each trembling doubt a plea to wish for less.  
  Yet through this storm, my soul must find its way,  
  And grow by night as truly as by day.

963

My soul, a lantern lit with longing's fire,  
Yet shivers cold beneath your distant gaze,  
It dances, moth-like, near its own desire,  
Confined in shadows cast by love's delays.  
The night expands with each unspoken word,  
A tapestry of silence, taut and wide,  
My heart, a trembling song, remains unheard,  
Its melody imprisoned deep inside.  
Upon the verge of hope's elusive light,  
I wander fields where memory is sown,  
And dream you near, though out of mortal sight,  
The seeds of sweet reunion yet unknown.  
  Yet in this ache, my spirit finds its gleam,  
  For love, though distant, kindles every dream.

964

My soul, in longing, pleads for what you shun,  
Desire burns bright where cold indifference lies.  
A shadowed hope persists when day is done,  
Yet in your gaze, the gentle promise dies.  
Each whispered dream is silenced by your will,  
And every glance, a tempest in disguise.  
My heart, though wounded, aches for you still,  
Entwined in wishes reason oft denies.  
Should fate be kind, and melt your frozen heart,  
Perhaps the dawn would banish all this night;  
But till that moment, longing is my art,  
And hope, though frail, endures the bitter blight.  
  For you, I pen these verses, line by line—  
  A testament to love that won't resign.

965

My soul is torn by longing’s silent cry,  
Yet finds no echo in your distant gaze,  
The moon ascends, a lantern in the sky,  
Its silver light awash in misty haze.  
I pace the night, a ghost in lovers’ lore,  
Each footstep falls upon remembered ground,  
Where laughter bloomed, but blooms for me no more,  
And hope’s bright petals wither without sound.  
Yet still I dream, though dreams are frail and thin,  
That time may turn and fortune’s wheel may spin,  
Restoring warmth that once did dwell within,  
And melting ice that chills my soul and skin.  
  O, grant me mercy, love, and let me see  
  The dawn that brings your heart again to me.

966

Within my chest, two voices wage their war,  
One begs for more, the other fears to need.  
Desire’s whisper tempts me to the door  
Where caution’s shadow plants a prudent seed.  
My longing yearns to chase the distant gleam,  
Yet reason tugs me gently from the brink;  
Ambition’s fire kindles every dream,  
While doubt compels me ever back to think.  
Between their cries, my restless heart is torn,  
A ship that’s battered by opposing tides.  
The bright horizon calls with hope new-born,  
But stormy silence in the deep abides.  
  Yet through this strife, I learn my soul’s true art—  
  To hold both fear and yearning in one heart.

967

My heart ignites with longing’s bitter fire,  
Yet reason bids me quench this wild desire.  
Between these warring flames my thoughts conspire,  
And hope is tangled in a thorny briar.  
The night reveals thy visage in the dark,  
A gentle ghost that will not let me be;  
Thy voice, remembered, leaves a glowing mark  
Upon my soul, a silent melody.  
Oft have I sworn I would forget thy name,  
And still it lingers, sweet upon my tongue.  
No wisdom learned can ever dull the flame  
That in the chambers of my breast hath sprung.  
  Thus, captive to a love I must conceal,  
  I bear the wound that time can never heal.

968

My heart desires that which my mind forbids,  
A flame within, yet reason bids it die,  
In shadowed corners passion gently skids,  
While logic's hand would teach me to deny.  
Between the warring camps my soul is torn,  
Each argument a thunder in my breast;  
By daylight's rule, such longing is forsworn,  
Yet moonlit hours will grant my heart no rest.  
The silent war of yearning and restraint  
Paints furrows deep upon my troubled brow,  
For visions sweet my waking thoughts acquaint,  
Though wisdom counsels not to disavow.  
  Yet love, unbridled, will not be contained—  
  A sovereign force, by neither thought enchained.

969

I beg for warmth, yet shiver in your shade,  
Desire burns on though hope begins to freeze,  
Your distant eyes, with gentle light, invade  
The silent winter rustling in the trees.  
I reach for comfort veiled in frosted mist,  
A trembling hand that finds no tender hold,  
Yet still my longing lips by frost are kissed,  
And dreams of summer’s touch grow ever cold.  
You stand aloof, a star beyond my grasp,  
Whose brilliance mocks the darkness at my side;  
My heart, ensnared in memory’s icy clasp,  
Would melt if but your gaze it once supplied.  
  Yet in this chill, my love will not abate—  
  It blossoms, evergreen, despite its fate.

970

Within my chest, a fire that will not fade,  
Yet colder grows your heart with every breath;  
Once laughter danced where now the shadows wade,  
And hope retreats before impending death.  
Your eyes, once suns, now bear the frost of night,  
Their warmth withdrawn as winter claims the day;  
I reach for you, but silence veils my plight,  
Unanswered words like birds that lose their way.  
O memory, tormentor of the soul,  
You conjure smiles from seasons buried deep;  
Yet time, relentless, will not make me whole,  
Nor grant reprieve from promises I keep.  
  Though love may dwindle, passion yet remains—  
  A stubborn ember burning through my veins.

971

Within my chest, a fire aches to grow,  
Yet cold indifference chills my every plea.  
Desires flicker where passions used to flow,  
And hope lies shrouded, lost in apathy.  
A silent war is waged beneath my skin,  
Where longing’s embers struggle against frost,  
The warmth of dreams once bright now growing thin,  
Each whispered wish in icy silence lost.  
Yet still, some stubborn spark resists the night,  
Refusing to let numbness take the throne,  
It gathers strength from memories of light,  
And dares to claim a destiny its own.  
  Though winter’s hand may try to quell the flame,  
  Within my chest, desire shall stake its claim.

972

Upon thy visage wends a woven shade,  
A phantom mask my longing cannot pierce;  
The candle’s gold upon thy brow doth fade,  
Its trembling light surrendered to the terse.  
Thy laughter, muffled, dances through the gloom,  
A distant bell whose chime I scarce recall;  
Yet hope, like ivy, climbs the silent room,  
And clings to whispers that behind thee fall.  
Oft have I dreamt thy gaze might turn to mine,  
And in that meeting, shadows would depart,  
Unveiling petals, tender and divine,  
To loose the secret chambers of my heart.  
  Yet till that dawn, in yearning I remain,  
  Adoring thee beyond this mask’s domain.

973

Upon thy visage, shadows softly play,  
Like veils of mist that steal the truth away.  
A gentle twilight dances o’er thine eyes,  
Concealing hopes that in thy bosom rise.  
Thy silent gaze, a garden wreathed in gloom,  
Where roses blush and yet refuse to bloom.  
The hush of midnight gathers in thy hair,  
And weaves a tapestry of dark despair.  
Yet in the hush, a whispering delight  
Shall pierce the doom and summon forth the light.  
For though the world may shroud thy soul in night,  
The dawn’s embrace shall set thy heart aright.  
  So let the shadows linger where they may—  
  The sun awaits to gild thy hidden day.

974

Upon the glass of dreams thine image lies,  
A phantom wreathed in sorrow’s trembling shade,  
With silent lips that utter voiceless sighs,  
In moonlit tapestries of hope unmade.  
The night doth press its secrets to thy brow,  
While memory’s fingers trace thy vanished light;  
Yet time, relentless, will not teach me how  
To turn from longing’s ever-yearning blight.  
Thine eyes, twin lanterns in a tempest tossed,  
Do haunt my path with glimmers faint and far,  
Recalling all the laughter I have lost  
And binding wounds that never truly scar.  
  Yet in this mirrored dusk, thy soul remains—  
  A ghost of love, enduring through my pains.

975

Upon the stage where shadows paint thy face,  
I chase the moon, yet grasp but empty air,  
Thy laughter lingers, haunting every space,  
A ghostly echo draped in midnight’s care.  
The velvet curtain falls on dreams unspoke,  
While candlelight betrays my secret fears;  
Each whispered line by trembling voices broke,  
Reminds my heart of love that disappears.  
Yet in this play, we’re bound by fate’s decree,  
Our roles inscribed beneath the watchful stars;  
What mask I wear, what soul thou show’st to me,  
Are truths concealed by time’s relentless bars.  
  But when the final bow must claim its due,  
  Remember love performed was ever true.

976

Thou art a phantom dancing in mine eyes,  
A moonlit shade that slips through waking dreams,  
Thy whisper lingers where the silence lies,  
And weaves its longing through the silver beams.  
Thy presence flickers on the midnight wall,  
A shadow cast by hopes I dare not claim,  
Yet in my heart thy name I softly call,  
And kindle once again forbidden flame.  
The world grows pale beneath thy haunting gaze,  
And time slows down to watch thee drift afar;  
I chase thee, lost within a velvet haze,  
Led ever onward by thy distant star.  
  Though dawn shall banish phantoms from the sky,  
  Still in my soul thy memory shall lie.

977

Upon thy visage, misted veils descend,  
And shadow’d dreams thy distant heart defend,  
The night’s cool whisper lingers on thy brow,  
While silent hopes in hidden corners vow.  
Thy eyes, twin lanterns lost in endless mist,  
Conceal the longing lips have never kissed;  
A trembling hush upon thy gentle cheek  
Speaks truths that neither tongue nor tear may speak.  
The stars confess what thou wouldst leave unknown,  
And trace thy secret sorrows all their own.  
Yet in soft silence, love’s sweet embers gleam,  
Reflected faint upon the glass of dream.  
  Oh, would that I could pierce this shrouded art,  
  And draw thee nearer to my waiting heart.

978

Within the glass of dreams my soul is caught,  
A phantom’s hand doth shape its veiled unrest,  
Dark shadows flicker where my fears are wrought,  
And longing stirs the silence in my breast.  
The moon’s pale whispers slip through curtained night,  
And chase the gentle truth I cannot hold;  
Each hope, half-formed, retreats beyond my sight  
As dawn draws forth new sorrow from the old.  
Yet still I wander through this haunted sphere,  
Where memory and mist entwine as one,  
Seeking the echo of a voice once near,  
And mourning for the days that now are gone.  
  Yet in this glass, though phantoms round me twine,  
  A spark endures—this restless heart is mine.

979

Mine inward eye doth conjure spectral shade,  
A glass wherein my soul doth scarce appear,  
As twilight’s hush upon the meadows laid  
Enfolds the heart with distant hope and fear.  
The silent hours, dressed in silver gloom,  
Steal whispers from the corners of my mind;  
Each shadowed memory within the room  
Doth leave a trembling trace of self behind.  
Yet from the mist, a gentler vision grows—  
A beacon kindled by remembered light—  
And in its warmth, my troubled spirit knows  
A solace found within the arms of night.  
  Thus, though the glass reflecteth shade and woe,  
  In dreams, a fairer self begins to glow.

980

Within the glass where fleeting visions dwell,  
Mine inward eye doth chase a shadowed gleam,  
Reflected hopes that whisper tales to tell,  
Yet fade as morning breaks the midnight dream.  
A thousand faces shimmer, lost in mist,  
Their silent stories woven through the years;  
Each memory by time and longing kissed,  
Each laughter echoing beneath my tears.  
The silvered pane recalls what once was bright,  
Whilst present longing dances with regret;  
In mirrored depths I search for vanished light,  
And mourn the suns my soul cannot forget.  
  Yet still I gaze, and in that searching see  
  The ghost of future joys awaiting me.

981

Upon the glass of dreams mine image lies,  
A phantom soul that yearns yet never wakes,  
In silvered depths where silent longing flies,  
And every hope its trembling shadow makes.  
The night enfolds my thought in velvet gloom,  
While distant stars their whispered counsel send;  
Each flickering light a promise or a tomb,  
A path uncertain, winding without end.  
Yet in this hush, my heart would dare implore  
The fragile truth that shimmers out of sight,  
And trace with trembling hand the misty shore  
Where dawn and darkness mingle into light.  
  So let my image, longing to be whole,  
  Find in the dreaming glass its waking soul.

982

Mine eye perceives thee as a phantom shade,  
A dream that slips the grasp of midnight’s hand,  
Thy visage, in the silvered gloom, doth fade,  
Like footsteps trailing soft upon the sand.  
The hush of distant hopes within me stirs,  
When memory’s lamp illumes thy fleeting trace;  
The silent wind recalls what once was ours,  
Yet leaves no sign upon this shadowed place.  
Thy laughter, like a bell, rings in my mind,  
Resonant in the hollows of the years;  
No mortal chain thy spirit’s form can bind,  
Nor draw thee forth from out the mist of fears.  
  Yet though thou dwell’st beyond my longing’s art,  
  The ghost of thee still lingers in my heart.

983

Upon the stage where phantoms drape the mind,  
My soul, a lantern, seeks what it may find.  
Each shadowed thought in silent rows aligned  
Unveils a truth that reason leaves behind.  
The velvet curtains whisper tales of woe,  
While footlights cast my longing into gold.  
I pace the boards where dreams and doubts may grow,  
And chase the echoes I can barely hold.  
The audience of memories attend,  
Their eyes agleam with hope or haunted dread.  
A soliloquy, begun, may never end,  
Its words a winding path that must be tread.  
  Yet as the final act draws close at last,  
  I bow to futures woven from my past.

984

Upon the glass of self I gaze, beguiled,  
Where shadows dance and truth is exiled,  
Reflections shift with every fleeting thought,  
In silver depths, my likeness comes to naught.  
A thousand masks parade behind my eyes,  
Each fashioned by the world’s unspoken fears;  
The honest visage hidden in disguise,  
Distorted by the press of passing years.  
Yet in that shimmer, hope will softly gleam,  
A glimpse of self unburdened by pretense.  
It flickers faint, as if within a dream,  
Unraveling the tangled threads of sense.  
  So may I learn, amid these shifting shades,  
  To find the soul that neither warps nor fades.

985

Upon the glass of self mine eyes do gaze,  
Yet find but phantoms dancing in the mist,  
Each shifting shape in dim, elusive maze,  
A visage lost, forgotten or dismissed.  
What art thou, soul, that flickers in the light,  
A candle’s flame that trembles at the air?  
Dost thou not flee when shadows conquer sight,  
Or linger long, imprisoned by despair?  
I chase thee through the corridors of thought,  
Yet, swift as wind, thou slipp’st beyond my hand.  
In vain I seek the substance I have sought,  
And trace thine echo on the barren sand.  
  Yet in this search, my heart is gently taught:  
  To know thyself, let mystery expand.

986

Within this glass of dreams mine image lies,  
A phantom wreathed in veils of shifting shade,  
The silent silver deep reflects my eyes,  
Yet shows a soul in borrowed light arrayed.  
Each glance reveals a self I scarce may know,  
A flicker caught between the dusk and dawn,  
Where secret hopes like whispered breezes blow  
And all my certainties are overthrown.  
O fragile vision, trembling on the brink,  
You shimmer with the promise of release—  
But if I reach, you vanish as I blink,  
And leave an ache that time may never cease.  
  Yet in this glass, my longing finds its form,  
  A fleeting truth within illusion warm.

987

Upon the glass of dreams mine image lies,  
A phantom love that mocks my waking eyes.  
Within the silvered mist, thy visage gleams,  
Yet slips away amidst the dawning skies.  
Each breath of hope dissolves as morning beams  
Unravel veils where silent longing dies.  
The heart, a captive bird, in shadow schemes,  
Unfurls its wings yet never truly flies.  

If fate be kind and grant a whispered chance  
To bridge the gulf that keeps our spirits twain,  
Would trembling hands extend in gentle dance,  
Or would thy ghost dissolve and leave but pain?  
Still, nightly on this glass my tears remain—  
A testament to love’s enduring stain.

988

Upon the glass of dreams mine image lies,  
A phantom wreathed in hope that ever dies.  
Each breath of dawn upon the surface plays  
And stirs the vision with its shifting light;  
The future, veiled in softly woven haze,  
Retreats, elusive, from my earnest sight.  
My whispered wishes, feathered as the breeze,  
Dissolve and scatter with the morning dew,  
Yet still I gaze, entranced by memories,  
Entrapped by longing for a world untrue.  
The silver pane reflects what might have been,  
A thousand selves that flutter, fade, and blend,  
  Yet in that fragile glass my soul is seen—  
  A dreamer’s heart that hopes and breaks again.

989

Upon the glass of dreams mine eyes do gaze,  
Where shadows wear thy form in fleeting light,  
A phantom waltz amid the silver haze,  
That shuns the grasp of waking from the night.  
Thy whisper rides upon the midnight air,  
A petal lost upon the restless sea,  
Yet in my heart, thy presence lingers fair,  
A silent song that softly calls to me.  
The hours depart as dew upon the grass,  
And morning breaks to wash thy face away,  
Yet in the mirror’s depth, thy visions pass—  
A golden ghost the sun cannot allay.  
  So let me dwell where dreams and longing blend,  
  For in that glass, thy love shall never end.

990

Fair Illusion weaves her silver threads about  
My heart, that aches beneath her gentle doubt;  
She paints the dawn with promises untrue,  
Yet bids my soul to trust the morning’s hue.  
Her laughter rings like bells in distant halls,  
A phantom echo drifting on the breeze;  
Her touch is soft as rain when twilight falls,  
Bestowing fleeting hope with artful ease.  
She dances just beyond my outstretched hand,  
A vision swathed in veils of shifting light;  
Each step she takes, she blurs the solid land  
And leads me deeper into restless night.  
  Yet though I know her splendor cannot stay,  
  I chase her shadow till the break of day.

991

Fair Illusion weaves her silken veil anew,  
Betwixt mine eye and thee, my heart’s undo.  
She paints with shadowed light a visage true,  
Yet leads my longing soul where dreams pursue.  
Her whispers gild the dusk with honeyed lies,  
And every promise hides a gentle snare;  
Within her gaze, the steadfast reason dies,  
While hope and folly twine a fragile pair.  
I reach with trembling hand to pierce the mist,  
But fingers grasp at phantoms in the air;  
What once was clear by morning rays is kissed,  
Now slips away, elusive, debonair.  
  Yet though she tricks my sight with cunning art,  
  Fair Illusion ever reigns within my heart.

992

Sweet Illusion weaves her gauzy shroud anew,  
Upon my heart where dreams and doubts ensue,  
Her silken fingers trace the morning dew,  
And paint the shadowed hopes in trembling hue.  
She dances lightly on the edge of thought,  
A whisper clad in iridescent light,  
Unraveling the truths that time has wrought,  
Yet cloaking longing in the guise of night.  
Her perfumed breath ignites both fear and fire,  
A phantom promise trembling on my tongue,  
She teases fate with every frail desire,  
And sings of youth forever fair and young.  
  Yet when I reach to hold her in my hand,  
  She slips away, like hourglass’s sand.

993

Illusion weaves her silken threads around  
Thy shadowed heart, where silent hopes are bound;  
She dances softly where old dreams are found,  
And scatters longing petals on the ground.  
Within thine eyes a phantom light does gleam,  
A lantern flickering through mists of doubt;  
Thy mind entangled in a waking dream,  
Where whispered truths like restless moths flit out.  
What is the shape of love within thy breast,  
When every promise wears a mask of gold?  
Can yearning find a haven, ever blest,  
Or must it in illusion’s arms grow cold?  
  Yet still, amid the shadows, hope doth rise—  
  A fragile star within uncertain skies.

994

Illusion weaves her veil about thy face,  
Her spectral hand denies my soul its due;  
A phantom beauty haunts this haunted place,  
Mistaking false for fair and false for true.  
Thy laughter rings as chimes through shadowed air,  
Yet echoes fade before I grasp their sound.  
I chase thy shade, but find but empty stare,  
My hopes like petals scattered on the ground.  
Sweet trickster, why dost thou delight to play  
With longing hearts that hunger for thine eyes?  
Each promise made dissolves in morning gray,  
Each whispered vow a fleeting, feathered lie.  
  Yet though I know thy truth is but disguise,  
  Still in thy dream I live, and there I rise.

995

Illusion weaves her veils about thy face,  
And hides thy truth in shadows of despair,  
Her subtle fingers mark thee with their trace,  
And leave thee lost amid the midnight air.  
A silver shroud doth cloud thy honest eyes,  
Where longing seeks the sun but finds the mist;  
Thy laughter rings with half-remembered lies,  
Thy promises in shifting dreams persist.  
Yet in the mirror's depths, thy soul doth yearn  
To cast aside the mask that night hath sewn,  
And feel the golden dawn at last return  
To claim a heart that once was all thine own.  
  O fragile hope, thy gentle light restore,  
  Let truth be thine, and shadows fall no more.

996

Illusion weaves her veils about my mind,  
And binds my soul in shadows none may see,  
She paints with subtle strokes, my sight confined,  
A world of dreams that dances mockingly.  
Her whispers drift as mist on midnight air,  
Distorting truth with every soft caress,  
She clothes the barren earth in beauty rare,  
And leads my heart through mazes of excess.  
I chase her phantoms down the winding lane,  
Mistaking hope for forms that fade at dawn,  
Yet find within sweet longing’s gentle pain  
A comfort in the visions nearly gone.  
  Though daylight breaks her spell, I would not part  
  From all the lovely falsehoods of my heart.

997

Illusion weaves her veils about my soul,  
And bids my reason bow to shadows’ reign;  
She dances lightly where the dark tides roll,  
And paints fair sunlight on the face of pain.  
Her whispered promises like honey fall,  
Sweet lies that glimmer just beyond my sight,  
She leads me deeper through her tangled hall  
Where day is twilight and the truth is night.  
Yet still I chase the shimmer of her dress,  
Though every step dissolves the ground I tread,  
And in her arms I taste both curse and bless—  
A dream’s soft cradle and a waking dread.  
  But when her mist at last is pulled away,  
  What’s left: the dawn, or night that will not stay?

998

Illusion weaves her veils about my soul,  
A phantom queen who bids my truth depart,  
She paints my hopes upon a shifting scroll,  
And cloaks with mist the chambers of my heart.  
Her whispers turn the daylight into dream,
