Do charm the ear but cannot move the heart;  
For every vow in golden light arrayed  
Is shadowed by deceit’s more subtle art.  
The stars may shine upon thy silken hair,  
Yet darkness lingers deep within thy gaze;  
No summer’s breeze can banish cold despair  
That dwells beneath the warmth thy smile displays.  
  Yet still I stand, ensnared by beauty’s snare,  
  And love the mask, though truth be thin as air.

1122

These shadows ply their trade upon my soul,  
And light’s sweet promise feeds a darker pain;  
The dawn, once gentle, now exacts its toll  
By casting hope where only doubts remain.  
The silent hours linger, thick as mist,  
And every thought becomes a wayward guest;  
Desires, like specters, gather to insist  
That peace is but a myth, denied my rest.  
Yet through the gloom, a single star defies  
The tyranny of night’s unyielding reign;  
Its flicker bids my weary heart arise  
And dream of morning’s freedom once again.  
  Though shadows press and weary hearts endure,  
  The light within grows steadfast, silent, sure.

1123

Oft hath my soul with shadows been beguiled,  
Yet wisdom’s torch reveals the fool within;  
I wander through the night, a wayward child,  
With dreams as fragile as a serpent’s skin.  
The moon doth paint her silver on my brow,  
While silent doubts like ivy round me cling;  
I question what is lost and what is now,  
And mark the passing hours on sorrow’s wing.  
How vain the search for peace in fleeting light,  
When tempest winds within the spirit rage!  
Yet hope, a gentle star, endures the night,  
Unfolding promise on a tattered page.  
  So let me learn from shadows, dark but true,  
  And rise each dawn with soul restored anew.

1124

Within the glass of self, mine image lies,  
Yet truth and shadow wrestle for mine eyes.  
A silent war betwixt what’s seen and known,  
Reflected doubts that chill me to the bone.  
The visage shifts with every passing thought,  
As fleeting moods the mirror’s tale recast;  
What once was certainty is dearly bought  
When confidence dissolves into the past.  
Yet hope’s faint candle flickers in the gloom,  
Illuminating corners dark and deep,  
And bids the seeds of self-regard to bloom  
Where tangled roots of old misgivings sleep.  
  O, may I learn to greet my gaze anew,  
  And in that glass, find both the false and true.

1125

Thou art the shadow cast on longing’s grief,  
A painted solace veiling truth beneath,  
Thy gentle words, a balm of sweet belief,  
Yet mask the ache that lingers in my teeth.  
When moonlight spills its silver on the floor,  
And silent wishes flutter in the night,  
I seek thee as the tide pursues the shore,  
Drawn helpless by thy ever-distant light.  
Though hope may stitch my wounds with golden thread,  
The seams unravel at thy ghostly touch;  
For dreams are spectres haunting empty bed,  
And love, a flame that flickers out as such.  
  Yet in thy shadow, bittersweet and true,  
  My yearning heart endures, and beats for you.

1126

False hopes, like phantoms, haunt my weary soul,  
Whilst truth in shadows mocks what love hath stole.  
The moon’s cold eye reveals what dreams conceal,  
And every whispered promise takes its toll.  
Upon the silent shore where wishes reel,  
I cast my longing to the restless deep,  
Yet tides return what time would fain conceal,  
And bid the heart remember how to weep.  
Beneath the mask of night, illusions grow,  
Their silver threads entangle thought and mind;  
Yet dawn’s faint blush dispels the ghostly show,  
And leaves the tattered remnants hope designed.  
  Still, in the fragile light, I dare to see  
  The truth that lies beyond what hope can be.

1127

When shadows court the longing of my soul,  
Fair truth is masked by love that turns to spite,  
The silent hours beneath the moon’s control  
Do veil my hopes in curtains void of light.  
Yet in this gloom, a gentle whisper calls,  
Reminding me of seasons lost to pain,  
As midnight’s hush upon my spirit falls,  
And dreams, like petals, scatter in the rain.  
O fickle fate! Why tempt the heart to yearn  
For sweeter days that shimmer out of reach?  
Each lesson borne, in memory must burn,  
A tender wound that time, alone, can teach.  
  Yet through the dusk, my steadfast soul shall rise,  
  To seek the dawn reflected in thine eyes.

1128

How oft mine eyes do hunger for the truth,  
Yet feast on shadows, bitter-sweet uncouth,  
The gilded mask that time bestows on youth  
Conceals the ancient ache of loss, forsooth.  
In silent halls where whispered secrets dwell,  
I chase the fleeting light of honest day,  
Yet tangled lies, like ivy, weave their spell  
And lead my trusting heart so far astray.  
Oh, that my gaze could pierce the veiled disguise  
And sift the silver from the dusk of doubt,  
To see the world unclouded by such guise,  
And bid the falsehoods lingering fade out!  
  Still, hope endures that, through the dark and blind,  
  The truth, at last, my seeking soul shall find.

1129

Mine eyes, deceiv’d, do hunger for the shade,  
While inward truth in bitter daylight burns,  
Each gilded hope in borrowed light arrayed,  
Yet from such gleam my weary spirit turns.  
The gentle moon, in silver silence dressed,  
Doth comfort give where honest shadows lie,  
And in the hush, my troubled heart finds rest,  
Far from the glare that scorns a lover’s sigh.  
O false illumination, bright but cold,  
Thy promises dissolve with morning’s breath,  
Yet in the dusk, forgotten dreams take hold,  
And whisper softly of defiant death.  
  So let me dwell where twilight’s truths abide—  
  For there, my soul and solace coincide.

1130

Fair glass, thou liar, bent on cloudy schemes,  
Thou mask’st my substance with thy painted dreams,  
Reflecting not the soul that softly gleams  
Within, but only what the surface deems.  
Thou flatterer, with silvered tongue and face,  
Art quick to show the lines that time doth write,  
And yet conceal’st the virtues I embrace,  
Preferring fleeting beauty to the right.  
When morning’s light upon thy pane is cast,  
Thou dost betray my secrets to mine eyes,  
Revealing moments vanished in the past,  
And weaving truths with carefully spun lies.  
  Yet still I seek thee, mirror, day by day,  
  To find myself, or let myself decay.

1131

Upon the glass of dreams my hope was thrown,  
Yet truth, unkind, reflects my love alone.  
The silent night with shadowed secrets rife  
Distills the ache that haunts my waking days.  
A whispered wish, too fragile to be known,  
Finds no safe harbor in the world’s wide ways.  
The moon observes, unmoved by mortal strife,  
While stars in distant fellowship remain.  
The hour grows late, yet longing does not wane,  
Its gentle fire consumes my heart anew.  
Though reason pleads for calm, I love in vain,  
And seek the light where once our promise grew.  
  Yet in this glass, my hope shall ever gleam—  
  A silvered shard within a vanished dream.

1132

Though dreams do dress my heart in silken lies,  
Cold truth, unyielding, strips me to my bone;  
The moonlight weaves deceit in midnight skies,  
Yet dawn reveals I wanderwards alone.  
What gentle hope the shadowed night bestows  
Dissolves beneath the glare of honest day.  
A garden sprung from longing never grows,  
Its roots consumed by fear and slow decay.  
Yet still I chase the phantoms of the night,  
Entranced by visions bright but never true,  
And bargain sleep for one more fleeting sight  
Of worlds imagined, painted in their hue.  
  Thus torn between the dream and daylight’s throne,  
  I find my heart is neither’s, but my own.

1133

Upon the stage of dreams my heart doth bleed,  
Yet truth, unmasked, denies my soul its part;  
The velvet night conceals what daylight freed,  
And shadows cloak the chambers of my heart.  
Ambition’s whisper tempts me to the fray,  
While hope’s frail lantern flickers in the mist;  
Each step I take, the dawn seems swept away,  
My longing lips by silent sorrow kissed.  
Yet in this gloom, a stubborn light prevails,  
A spark that memory kindles in the dark;  
Though battered sails are torn by bitter gales,  
The vessel seeks the shore, a fading mark.  
  For though the stage is set by fate’s decree,  
  My soul persists, and dares the world to see.

1134

You spun your love like smoke behind a glass,  
A phantom touch that vanished with the dawn,  
Its memory a shadow I let pass  
Across my heart, though all its warmth is gone.  
The whispered vows dissolved in morning’s light,  
As dew retreats before the sun’s advance,  
I search the silent corners of the night  
For echoes of a long-forgotten dance.  
Yet hope, that cunning thief, returns anew,  
Painting your absence with a golden hue;  
Desire, like restless tides, must ebb and flow,  
Though reason bids my longing not to grow.  
  Still, love’s illusion lingers in my mind,  
  A ghostly trace no waking hours can find.

1135

You wove your love from threads of painted smoke,  
A phantom’s kiss that chills me when I wake.  
Thy whispered vows, like dew upon an oak,  
Dissolve in morning’s light, and softly break.  
The echo of thy laughter haunts the air,  
A silver shadow trailing where I tread;  
Thy absence weaves a sorrow past compare,  
A silent song of longing in my head.  
Yet still I reach for phantoms in the gloom,  
And grasp at dreams that vanish with the dawn.  
My heart remains a captive to thy bloom,  
Enthralled by petals scattered, lost, and gone.  
  Though love was but a mist that could not stay,  
  Its memory endures beyond the day.

1136

You wear a mask of light that veils your truth,  
Yet shadows bleed beneath your painted face;  
Each smile you wear recalls a vanished youth  
That time and sorrow ever seek to chase.  
Within your eyes, a storm begins to swell,  
A tempest held by lashes long and dark;  
No silvered laughter hides the tale you tell,  
Nor borrowed brightness dims the hidden mark.  
The world applauds the brilliance you display,  
And yet, behind the curtain, silence grows—  
A fragile hope that longs to find its way  
Through tangled doubts that no one truly knows.  
  Yet in your gaze, the dawn breaks soft and clear:  
  A soul unmasked, unburdened, drawing near.

1137

Your love’s a mirror veiled in silver lies,  
Reflecting hope that never meets my hand.  
Within its depths, a thousand silent cries  
Are scattered like the footprints on the sand.  
You cast your gaze—a tide that waxes cold—  
And draw the moonlight from my shadowed soul.  
In whispered winds, your promises grow old,  
Yet still, upon your absence, I am whole.  
The dawn betrays the secrets of the night,  
Dispersing dreams as petals in the breeze.  
I chase your phantom glimmer into light,  
But longing’s echo brings me to my knees.  
  Though truth may shatter what the heart denies,  
  The glass remembers every lover’s sighs.

1138

Your eyes, a glass that bends the shape of truth,  
Reflect a sun that never warms my skin,  
They hold the golden innocence of youth,  
Yet mask the storm of secrets held within.  
A mirror’s depth where hidden tempests grow,  
Soft trembling light that dances on your cheek,  
In every glance, a thousand shadows show,  
And words unspoken ache but dare not speak.  
The world is bent around your silent gaze,  
Refracted hopes dispersed in silent hue,  
A prism’s art that paints the night with blaze,  
Yet leaves my longing heart alone and blue.  
  So let me drown within that twisted glass,  
  Where truths dissolve, and dreams may come to pass.

1139

You wear your love as actors don their masks,  
A hollow show that leaves my heart undone,  
Each whispered vow dissolves in brittle tasks,  
And all our mornings pale before the sun.  
Your eyes, once lanterns bright, now flicker low,  
Their warmth concealed by curtains drawn with care;  
A stage of shadows cast where feelings go,  
And silence lingers heavy in the air.  
Yet still I linger, bound by fragile thread,  
Entranced by dreams that memory invokes,  
Believing truth lies somewhere in words said,  
Though every promise breaks as morning smokes.  
  Unmask thy heart, let honest passions start—  
  For only then may love restore my heart.

1140

My soul, a stage where painted shadows play,  
Performs in masks that never melt away,  
The footlights flicker with deceptive gleam,  
Illuminating truths in borrowed theme.  
Each act rehearsed beneath a borrowed name,  
Emotes a joy or sorrow, none the same,  
Yet deep behind the velvet, silent veil,  
An audience of memories prevails.  
The scripts are penned by longing and regret,  
The laughter haunts where old illusions met,  
Applause rings hollow in the empty air,  
While yearning lingers, heavy with despair.  
  Still hope awaits behind the curtain’s line—  
  That I might step unmasked, and call it mine.

1141

My soul, a shattered mirror warped by lies,  
Reflects a world that mocks what’s true within,  
Where every hopeful spark in shadow dies,  
And virtue’s voice is drowned by cunning din.  
The light that once adorned my tranquil mind  
Is fractured now by doubts’ relentless reign;  
Each shard reveals a truth I cannot find,  
A silent echo haunted by my pain.  
Yet through the cracks, a glimmer dares to gleam,  
A promise hid beneath the broken glass,  
Reminding me that darkness cannot deem  
The end of hope, nor let all beauty pass.  
  If I but gather fragments torn apart,  
  Perhaps I’ll mend the mirror of my heart.

1142

My soul, a glass that warps the world to lies,  
Reflects a mask where truth behind it dies.  
The visage worn, a shifting, spectral guise,  
Conceals the ache that silent hope belies.  
Within this chambered heart, the shadows play,  
Distorting every joy that once was bright,  
Turning to dusk the promise of the day,  
And cloaking dawn in veils of endless night.  
Yet through the trembling haze, a whisper gleams,  
A fragile thread of honesty remains,  
It weaves between the fracture of my dreams,  
And binds the scattered pieces through the pains.  
  Though masks may fall and all illusions part,  
  The truest glass is found within the heart.

1143

My soul, a mirror cracked by your disdain,  
Reflects a love that flickers, false and thin,  
Each shard recalls the glimmer through the pain,  
A ghost of warmth that once had burned within.  
Your careless words, like pebbles in a stream,  
Disturb the depths where silent longings lie;  
My hopes dissolve, as waking from a dream  
To find the dawn withheld from morning’s sky.  
Yet in the ruin, beauty dares to bloom,  
A fragile rose that roots in broken ground,  
For though your absence fills my nights with gloom,  
A quiet strength in sorrow’s shade is found.  
  The heart, though marred, endures when love is gone,  
  And learns to mend its cracks and still go on.

1144

My soul, a mirror cracked by longing’s hand,  
Reflects your ghost in silvered, bitter lies,  
A thousand shards upon the shadowed sand  
Refracting hope that nightly, softly dies.  
Each whispered ache, like wind upon the pane,  
Disturbs the dust of memories long past;  
Desire grows wild, untempered and insane,  
Yet every hour apart seems ever vast.  
I trace your name in frost upon the glass,  
The letters melting with the rising dawn;  
Though time decrees that all such ache shall pass,  
Your absence lingers, haunting and withdrawn.  
  Yet in this fractured glass your face I see—  
  Longing’s cruel gift: you dwell yet still in me.

1145

My soul, a mirror fogged with phantom grace,  
Reflects your shadow, never shows your face.  
In midnight’s hush, your memory takes its place  
And haunts the corners time cannot erase.  
A distant star, you flicker through my mind,  
Yet leave no warmth, no solace for my ache.  
I search for truths your silence keeps confined,  
But find illusion with each step I take.  
Your laughter drifts like perfume on the air,  
A fleeting scent that stirs my heart anew.  
I reach for you, but grasp at empty care,  
Your presence gone before I ever knew.  
  Yet in this haze, a hope begins to shine:  
  That love remembered is its own design.

1146

I wake to mirrors made of painted lies,  
Each morning draped in veils my soul despise,  
The sun ascends with judgment in its eyes,  
And shadows mark the truth I can’t revise.  
A mask of smiles I don, though hope is thin,  
While silent doubts attend my every breath,  
The world demands a face I wear within,  
Yet costs me dreams and renders joy to death.  
Yet in the hush before the day unfolds,  
A whisper stirs beneath my hollow guise,  
A spark of self the masquerade withholds,  
That waits for dusk to draw aside disguise.  
  Though false reflections haunt the waking day,  
  The night restores the self I cast away.

1147

Within the mirror’s glass I chase a ghost,  
A phantom self that mocks me as I strive,  
Whose silent laughter lingers in the host  
Of memories that flicker, half-alive.  
His eyes reflect the dreams I once did wear,  
Now faded tapestries of hope and doubt;  
He dances just beyond my reach, aware  
Of every longing I am drawn about.  
With every dawn I beg his form to stay,  
To merge the present with what might have been,  
Yet shadows lengthen as I turn away—  
He slips through fingers, lost in time unseen.  
  Still, in the glass, our gaze entwines once more,  
  A silent truce between the then and yore.

1148

Within the glass I see a borrowed face,  
A mask of dreams that time will not defend.  
Reflections dancing, shifting into place,  
Yet in their depths, what truths do they portend?  
A whisper stirs behind the painted eyes,  
A longing veiled within a silver sheen;  
The world observes, but never truly spies  
The shifting soul that hides itself unseen.  
Each line and shadow tells a tale anew,  
Of fleeting hopes and memories estranged;  
The visage borrowed, yet the heart is true,  
Unchanged by all the years that leave it changed.  
  So let the mask and mirror both remain,  
  For through the glass, I find myself again.

1149

I wear the mask of hope, a painted lie,  
While bitter truths parade behind my eyes,  
My laughter rings, though inwardly I sigh,  
And sunlight falters where my spirit lies.  
Yet bravely do I don this bright disguise,  
For sorrow finds no welcome in the light;  
The world adores the face that never cries,  
And shuns the shadowed soul that flees the night.  
Within this masque, my silent sorrows dwell,  
A secret waltz of heartache, veiled and deep;  
No outward glance betrays the tales I tell,  
Nor knows the restless ghosts that haunt my sleep.  
  But hope, though false, sustains me through the storm—  
  A mask that warms until I can transform.

1150

I built my love on glass, convinced it stone,  
Yet every touch revealed the hollow lie,  
The gleam that veiled the cracks was not my own,  
But hope’s illusion shining in my eye.  
I traced a future on its brittle face,  
Unknowing how the pressure made it thin,  
The gentle warmth mistaken for embrace  
Could never mend the fragile heart within.  
Each whispered vow a tremor in the pane,  
A promise pressed too hard, a careless word,  
And through the fissures blossomed silent pain  
Until the shatter’s echo could be heard.  
  Yet from the shards, I gather wiser art:  
  Love must be built on truth, not wishful heart.

1151

I dressed my soul in glass to catch your gaze,  
Yet found reflection’s lie in every pane.  
The light that paints me bright is just a haze,  
A shivering mask that cannot halt the rain.  
Your eyes, like lanterns, search for what is true,  
But in my shine, your vision cannot rest.  
Each facet turns and hides my heart from view,  
A prison gleaming, beautifully dressed.  
I long to shed this brittle, shining shield,  
And meet your gaze uncolored by the glass.  
Yet fear persuades my truth to stay concealed,  
Lest, once revealed, your warmth should swiftly pass.  
  O break these panes, and let no falsehood stay—  
  See me, though flawed, and love me anyway.

1152

Illusion drapes her smile in silver lies,  
While bitter truth stands trembling at the gate,  
Her laughter veils the darkness in disguise,  
And paints a dawn upon the hands of fate.  
She weaves a gentle mist across the eyes,  
Beneath whose fog the honest heart may break,  
Yet hope, beguiled, in dreaming silence flies,  
And clings to shades that memory will make.  
The mirror cracks, revealing what is real—  
A whisper frail as wind among the trees;  
Yet still we chase the phantoms that we feel,  
Forsaking peace for fleeting fantasies.  
  Though lies may glimmer, fragile as a breath,  
  The truth endures, untouched by time or death.

1153

Illusion comes, my love, in borrowed skin,  
She whispers lies that ache beneath my truth;  
Her velvet shadow creeping soft within,  
Confounding wisdom learnt in tender youth.  
Her painted smile reflects in every glass,  
Yet cannot warm the chamber of my heart.  
She waltzes deftly as the hours pass,  
Unraveling the seams where hope may start.  
I reach for you beyond her haunted gaze,  
To brush aside the mask of fleeting dreams;  
In honest light, your gentle spirit stays,  
Unmoved by all her ever-shifting schemes.  
  Though phantoms dance and falsehoods may entwine,  
  My soul remembers, love, your touch is mine.

1154

Illusion drapes thee in her painted veil,  
While truth, a silent judge, stands pale behind.  
She weaves her golden words that softly sail  
Upon the winds, to dazzle trusting mind.  
Her laughter rings where honest voices fade,  
And shadows flicker where her footfalls go.  
She crafts a world from dreams and gentle shade,  
Yet leaves the heart adrift in ebb and flow.  
But truth, though silent, waits with steady gaze,  
Her torch unquenched by storms of fleeting lies.  
She bides her hour beneath illusion’s haze,  
And in her light, the painted vision dies.  
  O, let me seek the judge, though cold and bare,  
  For truth, once found, is worth the world’s despair.

1155

Illusion sits beside thee, sweet and sly,  
She weaves her silver nets around thy heart,  
Her whispers paint a fairer, softer sky,  
And teach thine eager hopes a gentler art.  
She veils the thorn with petals newly spun,  
And bids thee taste the honey of her dream,  
Yet, ere the golden pageant is begun,  
She slips away, more fleeting than a beam.  
Thou reachest out to grasp her shining hand,  
But find'st it mist that slips through trembling air;  
Her promises are castles built on sand,  
Her laughter, echo lost in empty glare.  
  Yet, though her spell be false and swiftly flown,  
  Still lov’st thou lies more dearly than thine own.

1156

Illusion drapes her face in silken lies,  
And scorns my heart with laughter cold and bright;  
She weaves her spell with ever-shifting eyes,  
And turns the day’s pure gold to shadowed night.  
Her whispers flutter soft as autumn leaves,  
Yet wound me deeper than the thorn’s sharp sting;  
A fleeting hope, in which my spirit grieves,  
Is scattered swiftly by her phantom wing.  
With honeyed words she lulls my soul to sleep,  
Then steals away the comfort of my dreams;  
Her promises are shallow, frail, and deep  
As moonlit rivers running dark with schemes.  
  Yet still I chase her shade, though reason flies,  
  Enthralled by beauty born of perfect lies.

1157

Illusion grins and whispers in her ear,  
She paints my hope, then lets it disappear.  
A silver fog that dances on the dawn,  
She wraps my reason tight and draws it wan.  
Her laughter weaves a maze about my sight,  
And dreams, like fleeting phantoms, lose their way.  
She turns the sun to shadow, day to night,  
And bids my trembling heart in darkness stay.  
Yet still I chase the shimmer of her hand,  
Believing truth resides within her eyes.  
She leads me barefoot o’er enchanted sand,  
And spins her stories sweet as honeyed lies.  
  Though reason warns, I follow without fear—  
  For even loss is dear when she is near.

1158

My soul, a jester cloaked in borrowed skin,  
Performs for truths it never lets begin.  
Upon the stage of doubt I pirouette,  
Each laugh a mask, each sigh a whispered sin.  
The audience applauds what I forget,  
While shadows lengthen underneath my grin.  
A fragile crown of folly on my brow  
Commands my tongue to jest when heart would break.  
In revel’s garb I dance, though none knows how  
The silent ache beneath the mirth’s opaque.  
Yet in the hush between the lines I play,  
A flicker stirs—my self, not cast away.  
  Oh, may the fool one honest note impart:  
  A jesting soul still bears a beating heart.

1159

Illusion wears the mask upon my soul,  
She whispers truths that rot beneath the skin,  
Her painted laughter swallows reason whole,  
And shadows blossom quietly within.  
She weaves her silks around my waking thought,  
Distorting daybreak’s honest, golden light;  
With every gentle promise I am caught,  
Entranced by phantoms conjured out of night.  
Her silver tongue recites a lover’s part,  
Yet in her gaze cold emptiness is found;  
She plunders hope and bargains with my heart,  
Her footsteps falling soft on hollow ground.  
  Yet still I chase her down the endless lane,  
  Content to court the comfort of her chain.

1160

Illusion stalks my soul with sharpened claws,  
It whispers lies that curdle every thought,  
A shadow weaving doubt with silent jaws,  
Unraveling the truths that I have sought.  
Its silken words, a web about my mind,  
Ensnare my dreams and paint them pale with fear;  
What once was certain, now I cannot find,  
For trusting eyes grow blind when phantoms near.  
Yet in this fog, a candle’s shimmer gleams,  
A stubborn hope that flickers in the night;  
It battles specters born of fractured dreams,  
And strains to guide me toward a truer light.  
  Though illusion’s grip may ever seek control,  
  I’ll chase the dawn and thus reclaim my soul.

1161

My soul, a jester draped in bitter jest,  
Performs for love, though love denies her stage.  
Her motley cloak, with longing, brightly dressed,  
Conceals a heart that trembles in its cage.  
She tumbles, laughs, and juggles dreams in air,  
Yet courts an audience of empty eyes,  
Her cap and bells a mask for bleak despair,  
While hope, unbidden, ever silent lies.  
Applause is naught but echoes in the gloom,  
A theater cold, where shadows mock and jeer,  
Yet still she treads, though banished from love’s room,  
Each step a wish, each smile marred by a tear.  
  For though denied, she dances, undeterred—  
  A fool for love, by sorrow’s hand interred.

1162

My soul, a jester draped in painted lies,  
Performs for love, unseen by blinded eyes.  
Upon the stage of hope my laughter springs,  
A mask that hides the ache of silent sighs.  
The court applauds the folly that I bring,  
Yet none discern the truth my heart denies.  
Each jest, a plea that longing’s arrow flings,  
A dance concealed beneath the world’s disguise.  
By candle’s end, my colors start to fade,  
The merry role dissolves in midnight’s gloom.  
No golden praise can lift the debt I’ve paid,  
No echo breaks the stillness of my room.  
  Yet in the hush, I dream of love’s embrace,  
  And hope one gaze might see beyond my face.

1163

Illusion dressed my soul in borrowed light,  
Yet left me starved beneath your blinded gaze,  
Each hope adorned in garb of silvered night,  
Yet found no dawn to set the heart ablaze.  
Fair phantoms danced upon the trembling wall,  
Their whispers weaving dreams from empty air,  
I reached for truth, but grasped at shadows small,  
And met the hollow echo of despair.  
Your words, a gentle mask for silence grown,  
Did promise warmth, yet lent a colder chill;  
I wandered realms where only doubt is known,  
A pilgrim lost upon a moonless hill.  
  Yet from this darkness, wisdom’s seed is sown:  
  A soul reborn is worth the pain we feel.

1164

Illusion pours her wine into my cup,  
Each sip she offers, bitter as regret,  
Her crystal laughter fills the vessel up,  
Yet leaves my longing spirit in her debt.  
She drapes her silks across the waking mind,  
And paints with borrowed light the shadow’s grace,  
She whispers hope, and bids the heart be blind  
To truths that flicker just beyond her face.  
I chase her promise down the winding lane,  
The further on I run, the less I find,  
Her petals scatter softly in the rain,  
A tapestry unraveled by the wind.  
  Yet still, I drink, though wisdom bids me flee—  
  For in her dream, I find what life could be.

1165

Illusion grins and drapes my mind in lies,  
She paints my truths with venom on her tongue;  
Her shadowed fingers fog the clearest skies,  
And twist the songs of hope that once were sung.  
She weaves a silken net of gilded dreams,  
Each thread a whisper spun from wishful thought,  
Replacing daylight’s gold with silver beams,  
While reason’s voice is tangled, caught, and fraught.  
I chase reflections fleeting on the glass,  
Believing substance in her phantom dance;  
Yet every promise, as the moments pass,  
Dissolves to mist beneath her fickle glance.  
  Still, though I know her art is but disguise,  
  I long to dwell beneath her painted skies.

1166

Illusion grins and paints my thoughts with lies,  
She dances in my mind with spiteful grace,  
Her silken veils obscure my searching eyes,  
And twist the honest sunlight on my face.  
She whispers sweet distortions in my ear,  
Transforming hopes to shadows on the wall;  
Her laughter fills my waking heart with fear,  
Yet in her arms, I rise and ever fall.  
She weaves her spells upon the fragile night,  
And drapes my reason in her shifting hue,  
Her phantom touch turns certainty to blight,  
The real dissolves where she would pass it through.  
  Yet as I struggle, tangled in her art,  
  I long to see the world as from the heart.

1167

Illusion sits beside me on my bed,  
She whispers hope, yet leaves my longing fed.  
Her silken voice entwines about my mind,  
Unraveling the truths I thought to find.  
She paints with moonlit hands a dream so bright,  
While shadows flicker just beyond my sight.  
Her laughter dances softly through the gloom,  
A fleeting fragrance haunting in my room.  
She weaves tomorrow’s promise with today,  
And wraps me gently in her gentle sway.  
Yet when I reach to hold her, she is air,  
A phantom sigh dissolving in despair.  
  Still, in her arms, my restless heart is drawn—  
  A lover’s hope that lingers when she’s gone.

1168

Illusion courts me with her painted face,  
She whispers lies that taste of honeyed spite,  
Her velvet glove conceals a hollow grace,  
A phantom dancing just beyond my sight.  
She weaves a tapestry of golden thread  
And beckons me to wander through her maze,  
Her laughter echoes softly in my head,  
A siren song that lures me through the haze.  
Her promises dissolve like summer rain,  
Yet still I reach for shadows on the wall;  
I chase her footsteps, though my hope is vain,  
Entranced by dreams that ever rise and fall.  
  Yet in her arms, sweet longing finds its art—  
  For truth is born where fantasy departs.

1169

Illusion courts my heart with painted lies,  
She dons your face, yet laughs at my despair.  
Her whispered promises in midnight skies  
Unravel hope, then vanish in the air.  
A spectral hand that strokes my fevered cheek,  
Yet leaves no warmth, no trace of memory’s fire,  
She weaves your voice in songs I dare not seek,  
And kindles longing, frail as funeral pyre.  
Oft have I chased her shadows down the lane,  
Mistaking phantoms for thy gentle tread;  
Each time I grasp, she slips away again,  
A fading echo of the words we said.  
  Yet though I know her artful game untrue,  
  Still, through her mask, my heart remembers you.

1170

You wear a mask more cunning than your face,  
While honesty dissolves in sweet disguise,  
A fleeting smile conceals what thoughts embrace,  
And truth lies hidden deep behind your eyes.  
The words you speak are veiled in silken art,  
Each gesture measured, crafted to deceive;  
Yet silent longing dwells within your heart,  
A secret hope your lips will ne’er reprieve.  
Yet, know the mask, though fair, will one day fade,  
And bare the countenance that dwells beneath,  
Revealing wounds that careful lies have made,  
Unmasking sorrow’s shadow and its teeth.  
  So cast aside the mask and let truth show—  
  For love, unmasked, is all we long to know.

1171

I see your smile, a mask that shadows truth,  
Yet ache to trust the lies your eyes convey,  
A fleeting grace that wears the guise of youth,  
While silent storms within your spirit play.  
Your laughter rings, a bell in distant halls,  
Yet echoes hollow through the gilded night,  
As shadows gather when sincerity falls,  
And candor hides itself away from sight.  
What secrets gather where your glances land,  
What memories flicker in your guarded gaze?  
I reach, but falter, longing for your hand  
To guide me through the labyrinthine maze.  
  Yet hope persists behind this fragile art—  
  That one true smile will open up your heart.

1172

Your dazzling smile, a mask that veils the truth,  
I worship shadows cast by your deceit,  
A fragile faith that blossoms in my youth,  
Yet wilts beneath the warmth of your conceit.  
Each silver word you speak, a gilded lie,  
Entwines my heart in webs I cannot see,  
A fleeting hope that dares not question why  
The sun that shines on you burns cold on me.  
Your laughter, like a bell in empty halls,  
Echoes with secrets hidden from my sight,  
And though my spirit answers when it calls,  
It stumbles blindly through the endless night.  
  Still, in your eyes, a softer sorrow gleams—  
  I find my truth within your haunted dreams.

1173

Thy smile, a mirror’s lie, deceives the night,  
A beacon false that leads my hope astray,  
Upon thy lips, the dawn feigns borrowed light,  
Yet shadows linger long beyond the day.  
Thy laughter, silvered bells within the gloom,  
Conceals the silent ache thy heart would hide;  
In masquerade, thou wearest false costume,  
While truth, like distant thunder, waits outside.  
I chase the promise painted in thine eyes,  
And stumble o’er illusions deftly spun;  
Sweet words, like perfumed air, the soul belies,  
Yet still I yearn for warmth that never won.  
  If thou wouldst drop thy mask, let candor start,  
  Two honest souls might heal a fractured heart.

1174

Thy painted smiles, a mask that love contrives,  
Yet in thy gaze, my hope is made to die,  
Fair words thou speak’st, but none that truth derives,  
A sweetness veiled in shadows of a lie.  
The moon doth pale before thy cunning art,  
Yet none may see the tempest in thy breast;  
A silent war concealed within thy heart,  
Where sorrow finds no peace, nor soul its rest.  
Oft have I watched the dawn upon thy cheek,  
And traced the trembling whispers of thy sigh,  
Yet all the joy I seek is cold and meek,  
For love’s bright flame within thine eyes runs dry.  
  Yet still, I linger, prisoner of thy guile,  
  Enthralled by hope behind thy painted smile.

1175

Your smile, a painted mask that fools the night,  
Yet shadows flicker where the truth should gleam,  
A tapestry of laughter, woven tight,  
Concealing every thread of broken dream.  
The moonlight dances softly on your face,  
But cannot pierce the veil your eyes have drawn;  
A silent sorrow lingers in its place,  
Awaiting gentle dawn to find it gone.  
If I could lift the burden from your brow,  
And trace the path where silent teardrops fall,  
I’d whisper hope and teach your heart to bow  
To joy’s return and love’s unbidden call.  
  For every mask must falter, fade, and part,  
  And leave the honest daylight of the heart.

1176

The soul, it feasts on dreams that taste of lies,  
Yet starves for truth beneath illusion’s veil,  
A banquet set with hollow lullabies,  
Where hope is thin and certainties grow pale.  
It wanders nightward fields of shadowed thought,  
Pursuing phantoms clothed in borrowed light,  
And finds but fragments of the peace it sought,  
While dawn remains forever out of sight.  
Desire and doubt within its chambers war,  
A silent tempest tearing at the core,  
Each longing more elusive than before,  
Each answer fading as it seeks for more.  
  Yet wisdom blooms when all pretenses fall:  
  The soul is truest when it owns its thrall.

1177

My soul, a mirror cracked by dreams untrue,  
Reflects the hope it knows it cannot keep,  
Each shard a wish the midnight never knew,  
A silent vigil held when all should sleep.  
In shadows cast by longing’s gentle hand,  
I gather fragments gleaming in the dust,  
And trace the seams with lines I understand,  
Yet find no balm to mend this quiet trust.  
The dawn arrives with promises so frail,  
A golden hush that trembles on the glass,  
While memories, like ghosts, behind me trail,  
Unraveling the future from the past.  
  Yet in the splinters, light begins to gleam—  
  A fractured soul reborn within its dream.

1178

My soul wears masks of hope, yet bleeds beneath,  
False mirth entwined with wounds it cannot sheath.  
A painted smile upon a weary face  
Conceals the storm that time will not bequeath.  
Night’s silent veil doth shroud my secret place,  
Where silent cries find neither voice nor ear,  
And longing’s ache doth slow my hurried pace,  
While shadows feed on all I hold most dear.  
Yet in the dawn, I search for gentle light,  
Each ray a promise whispered soft and low,  
To heal the scars that haunt my heart each night  
And teach my wary soul again to grow.  
  Though sorrow lingers, hope endures the test—  
  In fragile dreams, my spirit finds its rest.

1179

My soul wears masks to hide its hollow ache,  
Yet every smile betrays the love I feign.  
Beneath the laughter, bitter sorrows wake,  
And silent cries like ghosts within remain.  
A painted mirth adorns my weary face,  
While shadows haunt the corners of my gaze.  
Each jest and jesture veils my empty place,  
A play rehearsed through countless weary days.  
Yet hope, a trembling candle, flickers low,  
Its fragile flame defiant in the night.  
Within this ruse, a seed begins to grow—  
A quiet wish for dawn’s unclouded light.  
  If ever truth should part these veiling lies,  
  Perhaps my soul’s own sun might someday rise.

1180

My soul wears masks for love it cannot claim,  
Yet bares its truth beneath the painted lie;  
A silent player in a fleeting game,  
Whose whispered hopes the stars alone descry.  
The night reveals the longing I conceal,  
As shadows stretch their fingers through my mind,  
And dreams, like gentle thieves, begin to steal  
The sorrowed joys I’m doomed to never find.  
Within the crowded halls of masquerade,  
My heart beats secrets none may ever see;  
Behind each jest, a tender vow is made  
To love unseen, and nurse its agony.  
  Yet in this dark, my spirit learns to cope—  
  For masked or bare, the soul persists in hope.

1181

My soul, deceived by phantoms dressed as truth,  
Still aches for love that scorns its honest plea,  
As winter winds that rob the rose of youth,  
So cold denials steal the spring from me.  
In shadows deep, where whispered hopes reside,  
I seek the dawn that patience might restore,  
Yet trembling fears within my breast abide,  
And haunt the silent chambers evermore.  
Oft have I wandered through the maze of doubt,  
Enticed by dreams that shimmer, then depart,  
While reason's torch, in flickering, fades out,  
And leaves the gloom to fester in my heart.  
  Yet from this night, if truth and love remain,  
  Let morning’s promise heal my soul’s old pain.

1182

I wear a mask that fools my very soul,  
Yet see its cracks reflected in my eyes,  
A fragile guise I struggle to control,  
Its painted smile betraying whispered lies.  
The world applauds the part that I portray,  
Unknowing of the tempest held within;  
Each laugh rehearsed, each word a shadowed play,  
Disguising sorrow veiled behind my grin.  
At dusk, alone, the mask begins to slip,  
Revealing fears I dare not name aloud;  
A silent ache that presses on my lip,  
While hope retreats beneath a heavy shroud.  
  Yet dawn may bring a courage newly spun—  
  To face the world unmasked, and be as one.

1183

I dress my truths in garments spun from lies,  
Yet curse the mirrors showing what I feign.  
Each whispered vow betrays with silent sighs,  
A masquerade concealed beneath my pain.  
The silvered glass recalls my secret art,  
Unraveling the stories I have sown,  
And in its depths I see my fractured heart,  
A tapestry of threads I call my own.  
Yet dawn reveals the ruse that I maintain,  
As golden light strips shadows from my face.  
I long to shed the mask, release the strain,  
And step unburdened into truth’s embrace.  
  For though deception shields me from the night,  
  I yearn to stand unveiled in honest light.

1184

I chase the shadowed truth within my mind,  
While lies parade as friends I cannot name;  
Each whispered doubt, with silken thread, entwined  
Around my hope, and softly dims its flame.  
The mirror shows a face I scarce recall,  
A mask that shifts beneath the weight of thought;  
Yet still I seek the meaning of it all,  
Unraveling the knots confusion wrought.  
The night, a confidant with secrets deep,  
Invites my restless heart to wander free;  
I barter peace for mysteries I keep,  
And dream of what the waking eye might see.  
  Yet though the path is tangled, dark, untrue,  
  I walk in search of light—and find it too.

1185

I chase the ghost your shadow leaves behind,  
Yet grasp at air where once your warmth had been,  
The empty halls of memory unwind,  
And haunt the silent places I have seen.  
Each echo lingers, whispering your name,  
While distant laughter flickers in the dark.  
The stars grow dim, their silver not the same,  
Since you departed, leaving not a mark.  
I wander nights that never seem to end,  
Entrapped within the maze of yesteryear,  
And search for solace time cannot amend—  
A fleeting hope that you might reappear.  
  Yet morning breaks, and shadows fade away,  
  While I remain, still longing for the day.

1186

I dress my wounds in veils of borrowed light,  
Yet ache beneath the mask I wear for you.  
Each smile I shape, a shield against the night,  
A fragile ruse that hides what I go through.  
The silver threads of hope are stretched so thin,  
Entwined around the sorrows I conceal;  
Within this silent war I wage within,  
No gentle hand arrives to help me heal.  
You see the painted face, the careful poise,  
Unknowing of the tempest in my chest;  
The laughter, but a soft and hollow noise,  
A trembling song that never finds its rest.  
  And yet, for love, I don this bright disguise,  
  Longing for solace burning in your eyes.

1187

I wear a mask of hope to hide my ache,  
Yet every smile betrays the love I lack.  
The dawn arrives, yet shadows will not break,  
Old longing clings and pulls my spirit back.  
My laughter, thin, is woven from deceit,  
A fragile thread that barely holds me whole;  
My heart beats on, reluctant in defeat,  
Desiring warmth to soothe my weary soul.  
I dance among the crowds, alone, unseen,  
My secret sorrow tucked behind my eyes.  
The world believes my joy, bright and serene,  
But inward storms betray my calm disguise.  
  Yet hope, though masked, still softly calls to me—  
  Perhaps one day my aching heart breaks free.

1188

O sweet illusion, veil that clouds mine eyes,  
Thou dost transform my truth to painted lies,  
With silken threads thou dost my mind entwine,  
And lead my wandering thoughts where shadows rise.  
Thy gentle hand doth every hope refine,  
Yet oft betrays what honest heart implies;  
Thou mak’st the common fair, the humble fine,  
And turn’st the plainest noon to gilded skies.  
Beneath thy spell, the world is soft and bright,  
Each sorrow masked in beauty’s fleeting gleam;  
Yet when thou fad’st before the coming light,  
How harsh appears the day, how false the dream!  
  O precious shade, though thou art false and fleet,  
  Without thee, truth itself were incomplete.

1189

O gentle shade, thou phantom of my mind,  
Thy visage weaves false gold my heart would bind.  
A whisper'd breath that haunts the silent eve,  
Within thy shadow's grasp I dare believe.  
Each midnight hour thy memory appears,  
A fleeting ghost who dances in my dreams,  
And stains the pillow with regretful tears,  
Soft as the moonlight on the restless streams.  
Yet still I chase thy ever-fading light,  
Entwined in longing's delicate embrace,  
For hope endures the sorrows of the night,  
And paints with yearning every empty space.  
  Though thou art but a specter in my soul,  
  Thy absence carves a presence, deep and whole.

1190

Sweet shadow, thou whose visage mocks the light,  
Let not thy silken veil my truth betray,  
For in thy dusk the world is tipped to night,  
And honest words in trembling silence stay.  
Thou glidest soft, a whisper on the breeze,  
Unseen, yet ever present in my wake;  
Thy touch confounds my heart with gentle ease,  
And bids my reason falter and forsake.  
Yet in thy gloom, a subtler hue I find,  
A beauty born of secrets left unspoken;  
Thou art the keeper of my restless mind,  
The lock and key of vows I leave unbroken.  
  So, linger near and let thy darkness roam—  
  For in thy shade, I find my truest home.

1191

O gentle shade that haunts my waking sight,  
Thou art the glass wherein my hopes do lie,  
A ghostly form that shimmers in the light,  
And in thy silence, soft and low, I sigh.  
Thy memory dances on the edge of day,  
A whisper carried on the morning breeze;  
With every dawn, thou seem’st to fade away,  
Yet leave’st thy touch in trembling, yearning pleas.  
Upon the silent hours my thoughts are hung,  
Each one a thread that binds me to thy name,  
The sweetest songs remain forever sung,  
Though time would seek to turn thy gold to flame.  
  Still, in my heart thou art a gentle tune,  
  A shadowed dream beneath a watching moon.

1192

O sweetest shade, thou art my waking dream,  
A phantom fair that mocks mine earnest sight;  
Thy whispered sighs within the moonlight gleam,  
And turn the shadowed hours to silver bright.  
Thou dost evade the grasp of morning’s hand,  
Yet lingerest still in corners of my mind;  
Like fleeting mist that drifts upon the land,  
Or music lost when gentle winds unwind.  
No summer rose could rival thy soft grace,  
Nor autumn’s gold thy subtle, secret hue;  
Within the hush I seek thy vanished face,  
But find instead the memory of you.  
  O gentle shade, remain and do not part,  
  For thou alone dost haunt and hold my heart.

1193

Sweet shade, thou art the glass that mocks mine eyes,  
A phantom's veil betwixt my heart and prize.  
Thy gentle fingers draw the light away,  
And in thy cool embrace, my sorrows rise.  
Where sun once danced upon the fields of day,  
Now shadows stretch and mingle with my sighs.  
Yet in thy dusk, a subtler charm holds sway,  
A tranquil hush where all confusion lies.  

Thou art the balm that soothes the burning noon,  
The silent keeper of the weary soul,  
A velvet curtain drawn on dreams too soon,  
Inviting nighttime’s peace to make me whole.  
Though darkness falls and golden visions fade,  
In thee I find a grace that will not jade.

1194

O soul, thou art a glass of shifting hue,  
Entreat thee, show me truth through mists untrue.  
Within thy depths the moon and sunlight vie,  
Reflections dance, yet shadows oft ensue.  
I peer within and fragments I espy,  
Each ripple shaping forms I scarce construe;  
What lies beneath thy surface, clear or sly,  
Is veiled in dreams that reason can't subdue.  
Oft have I sought thy essence, pure and bright,  
Yet found but echoes, whispers on the air.  
Thy colors change with every fall of light,  
A shifting prism, lovely and unfair.  
  Yet still I gaze, and with a trembling heart,  
  Pray truth may in thy mystery impart.

1195

O gentle shade that veils my weary soul,  
Forsake me not within thy silken lies,  
Thy whisper'd hush does make my spirit whole,  
And guards my heart from sorrow’s prying eyes.  
When daylight wanes and heavy shadows creep,  
Thou art the balm to soothe my troubled mind;  
Within thy arms, the world dissolves to sleep,  
And peace, once lost, in midnight’s hush I find.  
Yet fear I still the breaking of thy spell,  
When dawn’s bright lance shall pierce thy tender gloom,  
And leave me stranded in the waking hell,  
Far from the comfort of thy dreaming room.  
  O shade, remain till hope and night are one,  
  And keep me safe until the rising sun.

1196

O soul, thou art a glass of shifting hue,  
Where shadows dance and feign the light as true,  
A restless tide that glimmers into view,  
Then melts to mist before the morning dew.  
Thy secrets, veiled in folds of silken thought,  
Are woven dark with threads of hope and fear;  
Each longing spark with silent meaning fraught,  
Each joy, a star that trembles but draws near.  
How oft thou wander’st through uncertain skies,  
In search of suns that never fixedly shine,  
Yet in thy depths a thousand dawnings rise,  
Reflected in the mirror of the mind.  
  Though fleeting be thy form and faint thy gleam,  
  Within thy glass abides the deathless dream.

1197

O soul, thou art a phantom in my sight,  
Thy shadow weaves a longing through my mind,  
A silent whisper trembling in the night,  
That seeks the peace my waking hours can’t find.  
Thou drift’st between the candle and the gloom,  
An echo left by dreams that fade at dawn,  
A fragrance lingering within my room  
When all the world and memory are gone.  
Thou art the mist that veils the far-off hill,  
A song half-heard, a vision half-espied,  
A fleeting warmth that lingers, gentle still,  
And stirs the restless heart I hold inside.  
  Though changeling, ghost, and shadow thou may be,  
  In thee I find the soul’s true company.

1198

O gentle shade that veils my longing soul,  
Why dost thou weave thy silks about my heart?  
Thy dusky fingers take a silent toll,  
Entwining hope and gloom in equal part.  
Beneath thy curtain, dreams are softly spun,  
Their silver threads enmeshed with midnight’s hue;  
The world’s bright clamor fades, and I am one  
With hush of eve and every shadowed view.  
Yet in thy cool embrace I seek release,  
A lantern’s promise trembling in the dusk,  
For yearning cannot slumber nor find peace  
Where phantoms linger, clad in sorrow’s musk.  
  But if thy shade be love’s uncertain art,  
  Let me be bound, and never to depart.

1199

O gentle shade, thou veilst my yearning sight,  
My soul doth chase the phantom of thy grace,  
Thou art the hush that softens edge of night,  
A whisper’d balm upon my weary face.  
In silver’d gloom thy silent fingers draw  
A veil of dreams across the fretful mind,  
And in thy arms, the heart forgets its flaw,  
Forgets the wounds that waking hours do find.  
Yet fickle shade, thou wilt not tarry long,  
Thy comfort fades when dawn's first blush appears,  
The lark ascends with bright, unyielding song,  
And scatters shadows with the weight of years.  
  Still, when the day’s harsh glare begins to fall,  
  I seek thee, shade, and to thy peace I call.

1200

O gentle glass, reflect not lies to me,  
For shadows dance where truth should steadfast be.  
Thy silvered face doth twist what eyes would see,  
And mocks the soul with painted mimicry.  
Within thy depths, a world both bright and wan,  
Where smiles may wilt and frowns may blossom fair,  
Each fleeting glance, a ghost of self now gone,  
Each whispered hope dissolved in empty air.  
Yet still I come, compelled by longing’s art,  
To seek myself in thy beguiling frame;  
Thou hold’st a cipher, locked within my heart,  
A riddle wrought in light, in shade, in shame.  
  O glass, be kind, and grant me this reprieve—  
  Show not the mask, but that which I believe.

1201

O gentle shade, thou art my veiled disguise,  
That warrest with the truth within mine eyes,  
A silent witness to my secret pain,  
Enrobing hope in melancholy guise.  
Beneath thy hush, my restless thoughts remain,  
Like petals closed against the morning’s sun,  
Yet in thy comfort, none can me arraign,  
For in thy arms, my masquerade’s begun.  
Thou art the balm for wounds I dare not show,  
The cloak that hides the trembling of my heart;  
Within thy dusk, my weary spirits grow,  
Apart from daylight’s bright and piercing dart.  
  Yet shade, thou know’st the soul thou dost defend:  
  In darkness true, the truer self may mend.

1202

O gentle shade that veils my waking eyes,  
Why dost thou paint my soul in borrowed light?  
Dost thou conceal the truth with sweet disguise,  
Or shield my heart from sorrow’s fateful blight?  
Upon the silent dawn thy fingers play,  
In silver streams that ripple o’er my mind,  
Thou steal’st the golden promise of the day,  
And leave’st the hope of dreams yet undefined.  
Yet in thy hush, my thoughts find gentle ease,  
And every care dissolves beneath thy wing.  
Thy silent song persuades the winds to please,  
And in thy dusk, the weary sparrows sing.  
  O shade, remain, till sun and shadow blend—  
  My quiet comfort, and my secret friend.

1203

O, bid thy phantom love release my sight,  
For shadows dance where truth should be revealed,  
Thy visage veiled in ever-changing light,  
A promise sworn, yet evermore concealed.  
Thine eyes, like distant stars, do mock the night,  
With glints of hope by cloudy sorrow steeled;  
Each whispered vow dissolves in morning’s bright,  
And leaves my heart, betrayed, yet unannealed.  
I chase the echo footfalls of thy grace,  
Yet grasp but air where once thy warmth had been;  
The memory of laughter haunts this place,  
And paints thy absence deeper on my skin.  
  Release me, love, from dreams that cannot be—  
  So truth, not phantoms, might abide with me.

1204

O gentle shade that veils my longing sight,  
Why dost thou dance beyond my grasping hand?  
Thy whispers melt the granite edge of night,  
And strew with silver dreams the barren land.  
I chase thy form through thickets dark and deep,  
Where hope and memory entwine their song,  
Yet ever dost thou distant vigil keep,  
And draw thy misty boundaries along.  
Thou art the hush that follows tempests’ wail,  
The secret sigh that trembles on the breeze,  
A silent promise folded in a veil,  
That bids my weary heart its torment ease.  
  Still, evermore, thy vision lights my way,  
  A star unseen that turns my dusk to day.

1205

O gentle shade that haunts my waking eyes,  
Why dost thou mask thy heart in painted grace?  
Thy visage fair conceals thy silent sighs,  
And laughter hides the shadows on thy face.  
With every dawn, thy presence softly grows,  
Yet slips away when sunlight warms the land;  
A phantom waltz upon the breeze that blows,  
A fleeting touch that slips from out my hand.  
I beg thee, shade, unveil thy secret soul,  
Let honest light illuminate thy form;  
From false attire, let true affection roll,  
And in thy gaze, let faithful fire be warm.  
  If thou wouldst stay, let no disguise remain—  
  For love unveiled shall evermore sustain.

1206

O gentle Illusion, veil mine eyes anew,  
Let not thy shadowed dance my heart undo,  
For in thy silken web the world seems true,  
And falsehood wears the blush of morning dew.  
Thou conjurest dreams where hope and sorrow meet,  
And paint’st the barren field with blossoms fair,  
Whispering solace soft, both bittersweet,  
That lifts the soul and lulls away despair.  
Yet every golden vision melts at dawn,  
And leaves the yearning spirit lost and bare,  
Still reaching for the light now fled and gone,  
Embracing empty air with desperate care.  
  O gentle Illusion, linger yet with me,  
  For truth is oft less kind than fantasy.

1207

O gentle Illusion, veil my heart’s despair,  
Thou weav’st thy silken threads around mine eyes,  
A subtle mist that sweetly dims the glare  
Of truth’s harsh beams, and softens sorrow’s cries.  
In dreams thou paint’st a world with gilded hue,  
Where hope’s frail blossom never droops nor dies,  
And every shadowed thought is born anew  
Beneath the gentle arch of gentle skies.  
Yet oft thy tender hand betrays my soul,  
For all thy pretty phantoms melt at dawn,  
And I, bereft of comfort, pay the toll  
Of waking to a world where light is drawn  
  With lines so sharp, illusions fade and part—  
  Yet still, I beg thee, shroud my aching heart.

1208

O gentle shade, thou weav’st thy silken snare,  
Deceitful Love, dost mask thy face so fair.  
Thy whispered vows, like petals on the air,  
Dissolve to mist and leave my fond heart bare.  
Thy eyes, twin lanterns burning soft and low,  
Do lead me forth through labyrinths of night;  
Yet every path where thy sweet breezes blow  
Conceals a thorn beneath the blossom’s light.  
Enchanted by thy ever-shifting guise,  
I chase the phantom hope thou dost beget;  
For every truth thy honeyed tongue belies,  
I pay with tears that memory can’t forget.  
  Yet though I bleed, I seek thy gaze anew,  
  For none can wound me deeper, Love, than you.

1209

O gentle shade, why dost thou flee mine eyes,  
When Hope herself doth wear thy fair disguise?  
Thy silent step doth cross the silver dew,  
And paints pale dreams upon the waking skies.  
Thou art the hush that follows midnight’s hue,  
The trembling veil where whispered wishes rise;  
Yet swift thy form dissolves at morning’s view,  
And leaves me yearning for thy soft replies.  
Within thy arms, the weary soul would rest,  
And let the world’s harsh clamor fade away.  
Yet absence deepens longing in my breast,  
As dawn’s bright fingers steal thy peace from day.  
  Return, sweet shade, and in thy shadow keep  
  The watch that guards my heart until I sleep.

1210

O gentle Muse, do weave thy silken lies,  
That mask my pain beneath thy shadowed art,  
Let honeyed words descend from distant skies  
And bind with golden thread my broken heart.  
Upon the stage of night, thy whispers play,  
In veiled disguise they soothe my aching soul;  
With every line, thou steal’st my grief away,  
And in their place, thy dulcet pleasures roll.  
Yet oft thy comfort proves a fleeting shade—  
A candle’s glow beset by jealous gloom;  
For when the dawn dispels the dreams we've made,  
My sorrow lingers on within my room.  
  Still, Muse, return and let thy verses flow;  
  For in thy song, my weary hopes may grow.

1211

O gentle shade, thou weav’st thy silken guise,  
Deceitful mist that dims my longing eyes,  
Thy tender cloak in twilight’s hush doth rise,  
And veils the truth with softly whispered lies.  
Beneath thy folds the world is half-concealed,  
The honest light transformed by shadow’s hand;  
What once was certain now is left unsealed,  
As dreams and doubts together softly stand.  
Thy fleeting touch enchants the weary mind,  
Inviting hearts to wander and to stray;  
In shadowed corners, hidden hopes we find,  
And memories in silence fade away.  
  Yet when thy shroud at last is torn apart,  
  The dawn restores the vision of my heart.

1212

O gentle Soul, why dost thou don thy mask,  
And hide thy visage in deceitful hue?  
What secret pain compels thee, may I ask,  
To veil thy light from eyes both false and true?  
Dost thou, in shadows, hope thy wounds to heal,  
Or fear the world’s unkind, unpitying gaze?  
The heart, though armored, cannot help but feel  
The press of longing through the thickest haze.  
Yet every dawn uncovers what we feign,  
And gentle truth will pierce the stoutest guise;  
No artifice can long our grief contain,  
Nor silence wholly sorrow’s plaintive cries.  
  Unmask, sweet Soul, and let thy beauty rise—  
  For honest light outshines the boldest lies.

1213

O soul, thou phantom draped in silken guise,  
Why dost thou weave false dawns before mine eyes?  
Like mist upon the moors thy promise lies,  
Yet melts to naught beneath the noonday skies.  
Thou art a wily painter of the air,  
Who draws bright hopes upon a shadowed wall,  
And whispers dreams with ever-gentle care,  
Then lets them, like the autumn leaf, to fall.  
Thy secrets flutter silent in the dark,  
As moonlight dances on a midnight stream.  
In every sigh is hid a vanished spark,  
And in thy laughter, echoes of a dream.  
  Yet still I chase thee through the waking hours,  
  For thou dost turn my thorns to phantom flowers.

1214

O gentle Soul, why dost thou don false guise,  
And court Illusion’s hand with longing eyes?  
What shadows whisper sweetly in thine ear,  
That bid thee shun the day and hold the night so dear?  
Why mask thy truth with veils of painted art,  
And hide thy golden core in webs of doubt?  
Each borrowed mask, though fair, illumes the heart  
With flickering flames that soon are guttered out.  
Yet still thou roam’st through dreams on airy feet,  
Pursuing phantoms spun of fading light,  
Forgetting substance, yielding to deceit,  
Neglecting fortunes born of honest sight.  
  Take off thy mask, let daylight make thee whole;  
  No false attire adorns a noble soul.

1215

O gentle Soul, why dost thou chase the shade,  
That Illusion weaves with hands of painted light?  
Upon the dawn, thy hopes are softly laid,  
Yet vanish swift as dreams that flee the night.  
The golden lure that dances on the wind  
Entices thee to realms that are not real;  
In labyrinths of longing thou art pinned,  
Mistaking phantom touch for what is steel.  
Still onward press thy feet on shifting ground,  
While true contentment waits, unseen, apart.  
The sweetest music oft is never found  
When ears attend the siren of the heart.  
  Awake, dear soul, and let the shadows fly—  
  All truth abides beneath the honest sky.

1216

Sweet Illusion, bend thy will to me,  
And veil my soul with dreams thou dost devise,  
Unfurl thy silken mists o’er memory,  
And paint with gold the truth that underlies.  
Let gentle lies like rosebuds softly bloom,  
Enshroud my heart in fragrance faintly sweet,  
And shield mine eyes from sorrow’s certain doom  
With visions wrought where hope and longing meet.  
In twilight’s hush, thy phantom fingers trace  
A world transformed by wishes dared and spun;  
I lose myself within thy fond embrace,  
Enthralled by shadows dancing from the sun.  
  Yet when the dawn dispels thy scented art,  
  It leaves a sweeter ache within my heart.

1217

O gentle Illusion, veil mine aching soul,  
Why dost thou mock me with thy fleeting light?  
Thy silken whispers vanish as they roll,  
And leave me stranded in the weary night.  
Thou paint’st false hopes upon my yearning eyes,  
Like morning dew that fades with no embrace,  
And though I chase thee ‘neath the watchful skies,  
Thou dost retreat and leave an empty trace.  
Yet, in thy glow, sweet comfort I have found,  
A tender balm that soothes my hidden pain;  
For though thy promises may not resound,  
Thy presence lingers, soft as summer rain.  
  O stay, Illusion, be my gentle friend—  
  And let thy gentle dreams until the end.

1218

O gentle Illusion, veil my waking sight,  
And lead my soul through shadows of my mind,  
Enfold me soft in dreams that mimic light,  
Where hope’s bright visage is by doubt confined.  
Within thy arms, reality grows thin,  
And sorrow’s edges smooth as whispered air;  
Let phantoms sweet my restless thoughts begin,  
And turn my grief to visions faint and fair.  
If daylight’s truth must pierce my fragile rest,  
Let not its blade be cruel nor too soon;  
Grant me the peace by waking hours suppressed,  
A hush as tender as a silver moon.  
  For in thy shade, my weary heart is free—  
  O gentle Illusion, stay near to me.

1219

O gentle Shade, why dost thou mask my sight,  
And steep my soul in dreams that feign as true?  
Thou lead’st my thoughts astray from golden light,  
And paint’st the world in shifting, sombre hue.  
Thy silken fingers trace upon my mind  
Sweet images that flicker and depart,  
While truth behind thy curtain stays confined,  
And longing worms its way into my heart.  
Yet in thy hush, a solace can be found,  
When weary day retreats behind the hill;  
Thy shadowed arms in silent peace abound,  
And aching hopes grow mercifully still.  
  O Shade, though dark, thy gentle wiles I bless,  
  For in thy gloom, I find my soul’s caress.

1220

O Illusion, thou dost haunt my waking mind,  
With silken words thou bind’st my soul in shade,  
Thy painted visions leave the truth behind,  
And lure my reason where thy webs are laid.  
Thou whisper’st hope when hope is but a dream,  
And cloak’st the hollow with a golden guise;  
Thy shifting light makes all but falsehood seem,  
Yet dims the brightness of the clearest skies.  
I chase thy phantoms down the winding lane,  
And grasp at shadows fearless of the gloom;  
Each promise bears the sweetness of thy bane,  
Each moment blossoms only to consume.  
  Yet though I know thy touch is but deceiving,  
  My heart, beguiled, persists in still believing.

1221

O gentle Illusion, veil mine aching eyes,  
With silken lies thy silver tongue hath spun,  
Thou paint’st the dawn in ever-brighter dyes,  
And promise’st peace before the day is done.  
Thy fleeting balm dost soothe the weary mind,  
A honeyed mist upon the furrowed brow,  
Yet truth, unmasked, in shadows waits behind,  
To claim the heart from thy beguiling vow.  
Sweet phantasm, lead me not through thorny dreams,  
Nor whisper hope where only sorrow grows,  
For though thy beauty softer than it seems,  
It withers swift as moonlight on the rose.  
  Still, let me dwell within thy gentle art,  
  If but to shield for one brief hour my heart.

1222

O gentle Illusion, veil mine eyes anew,  
And cloak my heart in dreams thou dost imbue,  
Thy silver mist conceals the bitter hue  
Of waking hours that memory doth rue.  
Thou art the painter of a softer scene,  
Where sorrow’s shadow cannot quite intrude,  
And hope’s faint lantern glimmers in between  
The silent doubts by waking thought pursued.  
Yet fragile is thy gossamer embrace,  
A breath, a sigh, and thou art fled from me;  
Reality returns with solemn face,  
And strips away thy sweet opacity.  
  Still, let me linger where thy phantoms lie,  
  For in thy arms, the truth itself is shy.

1223

O gentle Illusion, veil my weary sight,  
And paint thy hope upon my longing heart,  
With silvered dreams that blossom in the night,  
And bid the ache of waking fears depart.  
Thy whispers glide where silent sorrows dwell,  
Enrobing shadows in a golden hue;  
Thy tender hand doth every doubt dispel,  
And lifts my gaze to skies of brighter blue.  
Yet fragile is the comfort thou dost lend,  
A gossamer that fades with morning’s breath,  
For when the dawn and sober truths descend,  
Thy phantoms vanish, leaving only death.  
  Still let me bask, though fleeting be thy art,  
  In visions wrought to heal a breaking heart.

1224

Sweet shade, thou art the phantom of my sight,  
Yet realer far than all the waking day,  
Thy gentle veil transforms the noon to night,  
And bids my wandering thoughts in silence stray.  
Within thy hush, the golden hours grow fleet,  
And cares dissolve beneath thy dark embrace,  
The weary heart in cool contentment beats,  
Forgetful of the world’s relentless pace.  
Oft have I sought thy refuge in despair,  
When noisy tumult filled the sultry air,  
And found within thy breast a solace rare,  
A tranquil balm no mortal pains impair.  
  Stay near, sweet shade, until the dawn is nigh,  
  And let thy comfort with my dreams ally.

1225

O fairest one, thou art both false and true,  
Thy seeming light doth cast my heart in shade,  
With eyes that glisten bright as morning dew,  
Yet words thou speak’st like autumn leaves do fade.  
Thy laughter rings as if the lark were near,  
Yet oft it hides the whisper of a sigh.  
Thy gentle touch can banish every fear,  
Yet in its warmth, cold secrets still do lie.  
Thou art the sun, whose beams both burn and heal,  
A fickle breeze that dances on the moor,  
Thy promises like shifting shadows steal,  
And leave me longing, lost upon the shore.  
  Still, in this maze of doubt and sweet delight,  
  I chase thy love, through darkness into light.

1226

Fair shadowed form, thou dost my senses blind,  
Yet substance flees whene’er I seek thee true,  
Thy whispers echo softly in my mind,  
As morning mists dissolve in daylight’s hue.  
I chase thy presence through the silent air,  
A phantom hope that dances out of sight,  
With every breath, I taste a sweet despair  
That fades like stars before the birth of light.  
Thy visage haunts the corners of my sleep,  
A ghostly grace that will not let me rest,  
Yet in my waking hours, thy secrets keep  
My yearning heart both troubled and impressed.  
  Though fleeting as the dusk’s retreating gleam,  
  I love thee still, sweet shadow of my dream.

1227

Sweet shadow, dost thou mock my waking eye,  
Or art thou truth in falsehood’s fair disguise?  
Thy silent form in silvered corners lie,  
A ghostly twin that daylight oft belies.  
Thou cling’st to walls, and glide’st along the ground,  
A fleeting echo of my mortal frame,  
Yet when I turn, thou follow’st swift and sound,  
Unquestioned witness, neither wild nor tame.  
If thou be trick of light, then light deceives,  
Yet if thy substance holds a deeper lore,  
What secrets linger in the dusk’s reprieves,  
What veiled confessions darkness keeps in store?  
  Still, shadow sweet, thou art my constant friend—  
  I find in thee beginnings and the end.

1228

O sweetest shade that dances in mine eye,  
Thou art the truth that wears a mask of lies,  
A fleeting wisp that passes softly by,  
Yet lingers long in memory’s disguise.  
Thy silver laughter stirs the silent air,  
And weaves a spell upon the waking mind;  
A shadowed hope, a dream beyond compare,  
Whose gentle light no mortal heart can bind.  
Within thy depths, I see both night and dawn,  
The promise hid in sorrow’s veiled embrace.  
Thy phantom touch remains when thou art gone,  
A ghostly warmth that time cannot erase.  
  Though thou dost vanish with the break of day,  
  In longing thoughts, thy shade shall ever stay.

1229

O gentle shade that dances on my sight,  
Why dost thou flee whene’er I call thee near?  
Thy presence weaves the gold of morning light,  
Yet fades as dusk’s cool silence draws thee clear.  
Thy subtle grace, like whispers on the breeze,  
Doth trace its fingers lightly through my mind,  
And though I seek thee, beg thee not to tease,  
Thou slipp’st between the hours I cannot bind.  
A fleeting ghost upon my lonely wall,  
Thou paint’st with silver hues my shadowed room;  
I reach, but shadows answer to my call,  
And leave me longing in the gentle gloom.  
  Yet still, sweet shade, I wait thy soft return,  
  For in thy dance, my soul hath much to learn.

1230

O soul, that doth in shadows seek thy light,  
Yet shunn’st the truth that burns with honest flame,  
Within thy breast the day is turned to night,  
And self-deceit doth mask thy secret shame.  
The mirror cracks beneath thy searching gaze,  
For what it shows, thy heart would fain deny;  
Thy hopes, like mist, dissolve in morning’s rays,  
And leave thee naked to the open sky.  
Yet in the silence, courage softly grows,  
Each trembling step a whisper of the dawn;  
The rose that through the stony pavement shows  
Proclaims that strength from sorrow may be drawn.  
  Embrace the light, though shadows haunt thee still—  
  For truth alone thy emptiness can fill.

1231

O gentle soul, why mask thy truth with shade,  
When light and shadow in thy breast contend?  
What fearful tempest in thy heart is made  
That bids thy voice in silent doubt suspend?  
The dawn itself doth not its blushes hide,  
But greets the world with candor in its hue;  
Yet thou dost let thy deepest self abide  
Within a veil, withholding what is true.  
If courage were the sun upon thy brow,  
Its beams would scatter all thy shadows’ guise;  
Yet still thou lingerest in silence now,  
Afraid to meet the morning in thine eyes.  
  Unmask thyself, let gentle truth take wing,  
  For only then can heart and spirit sing.

1232

O soul, why dost thou chase the fleeting shade,  
When truth and falsehood war within thy breast?  
In silent halls where whispered doubts invade,  
Thou seek’st in dreams a peace that grants no rest.  
The golden dawn doth promise light anew,  
Yet shadows trail behind thy every stride.  
Each hope that blossoms, pale in morning dew,  
By evening’s hand is withered, cast aside.  
What tempest stirs within thy secret core,  
That turns thy purpose ‘gainst its destined star?  
Wilt thou still wander, lost upon this shore,  
Or claim the helm and sail to lands afar?  
  Let wisdom’s lamp dispel the night’s control:  
  Embrace the truth, and find thy journey whole.

1233

O soul, thou art a mirror cracked by dreams,  
Yet long’st thou still for truth that never seems.  
In fractured glass, thy hopes and sorrows blend,  
Reflected light that time cannot amend.  
Each shard recalls a wish once held so dear,  
Now scattered fragments, sharp with doubt and fear.  
Thou wander’st halls where shadowed visions gleam,  
A restless pilgrim in a waking dream.  
Yet in thy fissures, gentle dawns arise,  
And hope slips softly through thy aching guise.  
If fortune frowns, or tempests rend the night,  
Still, from thy wounds, emerges trembling light.  
  Thus, though thy glass be marred by fate’s decree,  
  In brokenness, thy soul is made more free.

1234

O gentle soul, ensnared by veiled delight,  
Why dost thou chase the shade, yet shun the day?  
The silver moon bewitches dreams at night,  
Yet truth’s bright sun would show a safer way.  
Thy footsteps wander where the shadows blend,  
Forsaking warmth for phantoms in the gloom;  
Thou seek’st the comfort darkness doth pretend,  
And plant'st within thy breast a secret tomb.  
Awake, sweet heart, and lift thy longing eyes,  
Let not fair hope be strangled by thy fear.  
The morning’s promise in the east doth rise,  
And scatters every sorrow lingering near.  
  For beauty dwells not only in the mist,  
  But in the dawn, by golden sunlight kissed.

1235

O gentle soul, dost thou not see my pain,  
Thy shadowed love doth mock my truth so bright,  
Within thy eyes a storm I can’t restrain,  
Yet hope still clings to every fall of night.  
Thou art the moon, and I the tide’s embrace,  
Drawn helpless by thy silent, distant call.  
A fleeting glance, a blush upon thy face,  
Doth raise me up, then cast me in my fall.  
What cruel fate hath tangled thus our thread,  
To bind my heart where cold indifference lies?  
Each word unspoken, every tear unshed,  
Builds unseen walls that neither can despise.  
  Yet if thy soul would turn and pity mine,  
  A sun would rise where now the stars decline.

1236

O grant me leave to pierce this seeming veil,  
For truth and falsehood war within my breast;  
The moon’s pale gaze grows watchful, wan, and frail,  
While doubts like shadows rob my mind of rest.  
I seek the dawn where honest light prevails,  
Yet night’s conceit would hold my hope suppressed;  
With every sigh, my spirit’s strength assails,  
And burdened thoughts refuse to be confessed.  
If courage, like the lark, would upward soar,  
And pierce the mists that shroud my weary sight,  
Then faith might lead me to that golden shore  
Where certainty dispels the shade of night.  
  Let wisdom’s hand unveil the world anew,  
  That I may know the false and hold the true.

1237

O gentle shade, thou dost my truth betray,  
For what I seem and am stand worlds apart;  
Thy twilight veils the honest light of day,  
And cloaks in mist the secrets of my heart.  
When silver moon upon the meadow lies,  
It paints my visage fairer than my soul,  
Yet in thine arms, my silent spirit sighs—  
A prisoner masked, not truly whole.  
Thou whisper’st comfort, soft as summer air,  
Yet in thy quiet promise, shadows grow,  
And hope, like dew, dissolves in dark despair,  
For I am lost in forms I do not know.  
  Yet let me linger, wrapped in thy embrace,  
  If only thus my faults I may efface.

1238

O gentle shade that veils my waking sight,  
Release thy hold and grant my soul the day,  
For in thy grasp I wander far from light,  
And night’s soft chains would lead my heart astray.  
The morning calls with fingers tipped in gold,  
Yet still I linger where thy silence reigns;  
In dreams, thy whispered secrets I behold,  
And feel the sweetness mingled with the pains.  
O shadows deep that cradle hope and fear,  
Let not thy fingers clutch my fragile mind;  
The dawn breaks forth, and clearer skies appear,  
Its gentle winds would leave thy sleep behind.  
  Yet, grateful am I for thy dark embrace,  
  For through the night, I find the sun’s true face.

1239

O gentle shade, thou dost my heart beguile,  
Yet truth, unmasked, doth turn thy grace to woe;  
Thy whispered comforts, sweet as summer’s smile,  
Are phantoms that the dawning rays forego.  
I walked with thee beneath the silvered trees,  
Entranced by dreams thy quiet hands did spin,  
Yet shadows linger in the trembling breeze,  
And daylight shows the hollowness within.  
Would I could rest forever in thy arms,  
Escaping reason’s sharp and bitter light,  
But honest morning strips away thy charms  
And leaves me cold, abandoned by the night.  
  Yet still, O shade, I seek thy soft embrace,  
  Preferring dreams to truth’s unyielding face.

1240

O gentle shade that dances on mine eyes,  
Why dost thou flee whene’er I seek thee near?  
Thy silken veil doth mask the golden skies,  
Yet slips away when I would hold thee here.  
Thou art the whisper’d breath of summer’s eve,  
A fleeting ghost upon the grassy lea,  
Thy soft caress the day is loath to leave,  
A lover’s touch that will not stay with me.  
When sunlight wanes and shadows grow anew,  
Thy presence deepens in the silent glade;  
Yet even then, thou art too swift to woo,  
And leave’st my longing heart in twilight’s shade.  
  O shade, remain and grant my soul thy peace,  
  Let not thy gentle beauty ever cease.

1241

O grant me leave to see with eyes untrue,  
For truth doth pierce where fantasy would woo.  
Let gentle mist enshroud the morning’s hue,  
And shade the world in softer, fairer view.  
If reason’s blade should cleave my visions bright,  
Let folly’s hand restore forgotten gleams.  
For wisdom oft doth turn my day to night,  
While dreams revive the color of my dreams.  
O let me wander fields where hope prevails,  
And taste the air that memory distills;  
Where every shadow weaves enchanted tales,  
And longing’s echo through the silence thrills.  
  Yet if delusion’s veil must swiftly fall,  
  Grant one more glimpse—then let me know it all.

1242

O let me dwell behind thy haunted glass,  
Where shadows mimic truths I scarce believe,  
Each fleeting vision, pale as moments pass,  
Entices hope, yet bids my heart to grieve.  
Thy crystal pane reflects what once was mine,  
A whispered laugh, a glance I cannot hold,  
Yet time, relentless, blurs each sacred sign,  
And chills my longing with its silent cold.  
Yet still I linger, searching through the mist,  
For echoes of the warmth thy gaze bestowed,  
For phantoms of a love I once had kissed,  
Though now I wander memory’s winding road.  
  So let me dwell where dreams and sorrows blend,  
  And haunt thy glass until all longing end.

1243

O let me dwell within thy painted light,  
Where shadows whisper truths I dare not see,  
And dawn’s pale fingers wrestle with the night,  
Unveiling hopes that softly beckon me.  
Upon thy canvas, dreams in colors bloom,  
The hush of longing stirs the blushing air;  
Each brushstroke banishes the quiet gloom,  
And weaves a gentle promise through despair.  
If I could linger where thy hues reside,
