And taste the golden hush of fleeting hours,  
Then sorrow’s voice would slowly turn aside,  
Replaced by joy that in thy vision flowers.  
  So grant me leave to wander in thy gaze,  
  And find my soul restored within thy blaze.

1244

Beloved, let thy visage not deceive,  
For shadows mask the truth within thine eyes;  
A gentle smile thy guarded lips conceive,  
Yet storms do roil beneath thy sweet disguise.  
The silver moon doth pale beside thy light,  
Yet secrets linger softly in thy breath;  
Each whispered word, both delicate and slight,  
Unfolds a tale of longing and of death.  
Thy laughter rings as bells in vernal air,  
But echoes sorrow veiled in summer's hue;  
Thy touch, though warm, betrays a wistful care,  
As if thy heart recalls what once it knew.  
  Yet, though the night may cloak thy soul in fear,  
  My love shall wait until thy truth appear.

1245

O, let me wear thy shadow as my skin,  
And chase the mirage where thy heart has been,  
As thirsty roots that yearn for rain begin  
To dream of streams that never once were seen.  
Thy absence paints the day with silent hue,  
A ghostly veil upon the waking light;  
Yet in my breast thy memory feels anew,  
A lantern gleaming softly through the night.  
I wander realms where summer’s promise lies,  
Yet every path returns me to thy name;  
The stars conspire to mirror longing’s eyes,  
And whisper hope where once there burned a flame.  
  If I may dwell within thy shade, so near,  
  Then even shadows lose their cause for fear.

1246

O let me dwell within your painted dream,  
Though shadows blur the edges of your heart,  
I’ll walk enchanted by the moon’s pale beam,  
And gather hope where silent sorrows start.  
Your laughter rings like bells in distant halls,  
Yet sometimes sorrow tints your gentle gaze;  
I watch the golden light that softly falls  
And trace your memory through twilight’s haze.  
If night should close and leave me lost and blind,  
Still would I seek you in the mists of sleep,  
For in your world my truest self I find,  
And in your arms my soul its vigil keeps.  
  So grant me leave to linger by your side,  
  A faithful ghost where waking dreams abide.

1247

If love be smoke, then let me breathe the haze,  
Your distant light refracts my hopeful gaze.  
The stars conspire to mask your gentle face,  
Yet in their midst, your beauty finds its place.  
My longing heart, a moth to flame it flies,  
Despite the night that pulls you far from me.  
In every dusk, I search the clouded skies  
For echoes of your warmth my soul might see.  
The hours stretch thin as moonbeams on the dew,  
While shadows whisper secrets of your name.  
Desire’s ember burns my thoughts anew,  
And hope, though frail, endures without a claim.  
  So let this smoke enfold me in its fold—  
  For dreams of you are all I wish to hold.

1248

My soul’s a stage where shadows play their part,  
Illusions whisper truths I scarce can see,  
Dim actors strut with masks upon my heart,  
Enacting scenes that might or might not be.  
A flicker’s hope attends each shifting face,  
While memory’s lantern casts uncertain light;  
Desires parade in melancholy grace,  
And reason’s voice grows fainter with the night.  
Yet in this theatre of restless thought,  
Where phantoms dance and silent secrets dwell,  
Some honest glimmer, through the gloom, is caught,  
A spark of self no shadow dares dispel.  
  Though veiled in dreams, I seek the rising sun,  
  To find the truth when all the play is done.

1249

O soul, why chase the mirage in the mist,  
When truth within your shadowed halls persists?  
Dost thou neglect the hearth where wisdom kissed  
Thy brow, and trade thy peace for fleeting lists?  
What phantom promise lures thee through the night,  
To wander wastelands veiled in silver hue?  
The lantern of thy heart emits its light,  
Yet blinds thee, seeking what thou always knew.  
Awake from dreams that scatter in the dawn—  
The luster of the world is but a mask.  
Within thy breast, the truest gold is drawn,  
If thou but dare to set thyself the task.  
  Turn inward, soul, and let illusions fade;  
  There, find the Eden thou thyself hast made.

1250

O fragile veil that cloaks my searching soul,  
Release thy hold and let me see the light,  
Thy shadows weave a prison from the whole,  
And hem my dreams within the bounds of night.  
I yearn to pierce the mist with hopeful gaze,  
To find the dawn beyond this weary gloom,  
For every star that falters or betrays  
Yet kindles fire defiant of my doom.  
What silent fears do haunt the midnight air,  
What gentle truths lie hidden from my view?  
The heart persists though burdened with despair—  
It beats, it breaks, yet ever seeks the true.  
  Unmask, O veil, thy secrets unto me,  
  That in thy lifting, I at last am free.

1251

O soul, thou wear’st a mask of borrowed light,  
Yet ache for eyes that never seek thy face;  
Within the hush of ever-watchful night  
Thou roam’st unseen, estranged from time and place.  
Thy laughter echoes low in empty halls,  
A melody the daylight dares not claim;  
Soft shadows cradle thee as silence falls,  
And hopes unspoken flicker in thy name.  
Yet in thy chest a secret longing stirs,  
A wish to shed the veiling of the skies;  
For truth, though trembling, evermore prefers  
The honest gaze that strips away disguise.  
  O soul, unveil thyself and taste the sun,  
  For only bare and bright are hearts begun.

1252

O soul, entangled in your phantom light,  
Why chase the shimmered hope that flees the day?  
The shadows dance and cloak the paths of night,  
Yet dawn awaits to guide you on your way.  
You haunt the halls of memory and dream,  
In search of truths that slip from out your grasp,  
Each promise falters like a distant gleam,  
Too fragile for your longing hands to clasp.  
Yet in the silent hush where doubts reside,  
A gentle voice within you softly calls:  
Release the chains that in your breast abide,  
Let go the veil that on your vision falls.  
  For when you turn from specters of despair,  
  The morning’s golden grace dissolves the air.

1253

O gentle soul, why chase the painted mist,  
That flatters eyes but leaves the spirit cold?  
The fleeting hues by fickle breezes kissed  
Dissolve to naught, their promises grown old.  
Thy heart, entangled in illusion’s thread,  
Pursues a phantom dancing just ahead,  
Yet finds but emptiness where dreams have led,  
And mourns the time and hope that thus have fled.  
Oh, turn thee from the shadow’s sweet deceit,  
And seek the quiet light that does not fade;  
Within thy breast, let true contentment meet  
The honest love that never is betrayed.  
  For painted mists shall ever drift away,  
  But steadfast hearts endure beyond the day.

1254

O tangled glass, reflect a truer face,  
Undo the veils that shift upon my mind,  
Within thy depths, uncertain truths I chase,  
Yet find but shadows, fleeting and unkind.  
Thy silvered surface shimmers with deceit,  
Confounding sight with visions unaligned;  
What lies beneath, my gaze cannot entreat,  
For falsehoods in thy gleam are intertwined.  
Oh, would that thou couldst show my soul unmasked,  
And strip illusion from my searching eyes,  
Reveal the heart for which I long have asked,  
Not fractured dreams that in thy mirror rise.  
  Let honest light within thy frame be cast,  
  So I may know myself in truth at last.

1255

O mirror false, why do you bend the light,  
And cast my longing shape in borrowed hue?  
You trick the day and counterfeit the night,  
And paint a face my soul scarce ever knew.  
Your silver tongue invents a tale untrue  
Of beauty smudged or youth already fled,  
While hopes reflected fade and dreams accrue  
In whispered glass where silent doubts are bred.  
Yet still I come, compelled to seek my fate  
Within your depths, though reason bids me flee,  
Entranced by visions that you fabricate,  
Entrapped by phantoms only I can see.  
  O faithless glass, my heart you undermine—  
  Yet still I search for truth within your shine.

1256

O fragile glass that veils my aching mind,  
Why must I chase the shadows I’ve designed?  
Reflections dance upon the pane, unkind,  
And haunt the silent corners left behind.  
A whispered doubt upon the midnight air  
Distorts the truth with every trembling sigh,  
Yet hope, a trembling spark, still lingers there,  
Beneath the mask of fears I can’t deny.  
The morning weaves its gold through fractured light,  
Inviting dreams to blossom from the pain;  
My soul, though wounded, rises from the night  
To seek the sun beyond the windowpane.  
  Though shadows threaten what I long to see,  
  The glass reveals the strength inside of me.

1257

O let me dwell behind this painted glass,  
Where hope refracts the truth I cannot bear,  
Each colored shard denies what came to pass,  
Transforming shadows into softened air.  
The world beyond is harsh and overbright,  
Its voices echo with a biting cold,  
Yet here, the hues transmute the searing light  
To gentle warmth, and sorrow’s edge grows old.  
Let others name my haven coward’s flight,  
Or chide me for the veils that I compose;  
For solace blooms where colors blend with night,  
And peace is found in self-forgiving throes.  
  So leave me wrapped in stained and dappled hue:  
  The world is harsh, but here, my heart is true.

1258

Let not your eyes be prisms for my heart,  
For what they see is not the soul I bear.  
The outward form and inward self depart,  
A painted mask conceals what’s truly there.  
Bright glances skim the surface of my mind,  
Yet miss the depth where silent wishes grow.  
You judge by light, but love by truth is blind,  
And finds its purpose where the shadows flow.  
If only vision pierced beyond the skin,  
And sought the gentle pulse that hides within,  
Then would you know the storms that rage and spin,  
And all the secret worlds beneath my grin.  
  So look not with your eyes, but with your soul—  
  There, unobserved, my truest self is whole.

1259

O let me dwell within your painted light,  
Though shadows prove my heart a phantom’s plea,  
Upon the stage where day dissolves to night,  
Desire’s flame burns bright, yet flickers free.  
Your laughter weaves a tapestry of gold,  
While silence reigns, yet speaks in tender tune;  
In dreams, your gentle visage I behold,  
A rose that blossoms neath the watchful moon.  
Each whispered hope upon the restless air  
Takes wing and soars beyond the reach of time,  
Entreating fate to render fortune fair,  
To join two souls in harmony, sublime.  
  Yet if denied your grace, let longing be  
  The star that guides my wandering heart to thee.

1260

Illusion, gentle thief, undo thy hold,  
Release my love from veils that cloud her face,  
Thy silver webs around her beauty rolled  
Do dim the light that once adorned her grace.  
What shadows linger in thy subtle art,  
That bid my reason falter, sense to stray?  
A painted dream invades my waking heart  
And turns the honest noon to misty gray.  
Yet in her eyes, a truer vision lies,  
Beyond thy cunning glass, thy borrowed gleam;  
For though thou dress her in a fair disguise,  
My soul recalls the truth beneath the dream.  
  Unbind thy charm, let daylight make me wise—  
  Let love stand clear, as morning breaks the seam.

1261

Illusion, do not steal my love from me,  
Nor paint her voice with echoes of your shade;  
Let truth’s clear glass protect what eyes should see,  
And keep sweet memory from being frayed.  
Her laughter rings more honest than the dawn,  
Unwarped by fogs your cunning hands devise;  
Resist the urge to make her image drawn  
Upon the shifting canvas of your lies.  
For though the world may shimmer and deceive,  
And phantoms tempt the heart with fleeting art,  
My trust in her no treachery shall cleave,  
Nor false reflection sever us apart.  
  So, begone, shadow, tremble at the light—  
  My love endures, untouched by phantom night.

1262

O Illusion, veil my sight from bitter truth,  
And paint my love in colors bright and fair,  
Let dreams restore the innocence of youth,  
And mask the wounds that waking hours bare.  
Upon thy stage, let shadows softly play  
To shield my heart from sorrow’s cruel sting;  
In gentle mists, let fading hopes delay  
The dawn that harsh realities may bring.  
Enshroud the flaws that time would soon reveal,  
And grant me solace in thy sweet disguise;  
With whispered lies, my aching soul you heal,  
And in thy arms, the broken spirit flies.  
  Yet when your silken curtain drifts away,  
  I beg you, gentle phantom, let me stay.

1263

O gentle Illusion, veil her heart from me,  
For shadows court her smile where I would be.  
The silver dawn that paints her sleeping face  
Is borrowed light, not meant for my embrace.  
Her laughter rings like bells on distant air,  
Yet ever falls beyond my outstretched hand.  
I walk where silent hopes and sorrows pair,  
A suitor banished from her promised land.  
Her eyes, twin stars, invite my soul to stray,  
Yet vanish quick as dreams at break of day.  
I chase her shade through forests old and deep,  
Where memory and longing softly creep.  
  O kindly mist, protect her from my view,  
  Lest I pursue what I must not pursue.

1264

Illusion, gentle thief, possess her eyes,  
And paint me love where only friendship lies.  
Let shadowed longing in her glances bloom,  
And turn my silent hope to fragrant prize.  
The whispered truth that dwells within the room  
Doth flutter nervously behind disguise,  
For every smile she gives is sweet perfume  
That stirs my heart’s rebellion as it tries.  
Yet reason counsels patience, bids me wait,  
While passion’s fever seeks a swift release;  
I linger, captive to a fickle fate,  
And barter restless dreams for borrowed peace.  
  So let thy magic, wily and unwise,  
  Entwine our souls in artful, tender guise.

1265

Illusion, drape her eyes in gentle mist,  
And whisper truths that longing hearts resist,  
Let shadows dance upon her trembling sight,  
And paint the dawn with colors none exist.  
In dreams she treads where silent wishes twist,  
Her hopes adrift between the wrong and right,  
The silver veil of reverie persists,  
Concealing daybreak from the velvet night.  
Her laughter rings with echoes faint and far,  
A fragile tune that memory pursues,  
Yet every glance reveals another scar  
Where fantasy and waking life confuse.  
  Still, let her wander worlds that can't be true—  
  For sometimes lies are kinder than the view.

1266

O Soul, why don thy mask of painted lies,  
And barter truth for shadows in disguise?  
What gain hast thou in veiling honest light,  
To trade the day for comfort of the night?  
Within thy breast, a tempest softly stirs,  
A silent war that no deceiver blurs;  
Each borrowed shade, though fair, doth slowly fade,  
While conscience frets beneath the web that's laid.  
How sweet the air when burdens are confessed,  
How pure the heart when guile is dispossessed!  
Yet still thou weav’st thy veil with trembling hand,  
And fear exposure none may understand.  
  O soul, cast off the mask and let love see—  
  For truth alone shall set thy spirit free.

1267

Illusion, why dost thou possess my soul,  
And lead my truth through shadows, not my goal?  
Thou paint’st sweet gardens where but deserts lie,  
And gilds the night with counterfeit of sky.  
Thy siren whispers mask the path ahead,  
Enticing hope where reason would have fled,  
So dreams parade as honest memory,  
And longing’s echo veils reality.  
Yet in thy thrall, my heart would fain remain,  
For bitter truth oft brings a keener pain;  
I chase thy visions though they fade with light,  
Preferring golden haze to honest sight.  
  Yet must I break thy spell, or be thy thrall—  
  To walk in truth, or in thy shadow fall.

1268

O Soul, thou trickster clothed in shrouds of mist,  
Release thy hold on dreams that twist my mind;  
Thy whispered bargains, oft too sly to list,  
Entangle hope and leave all peace behind.  
Thou art a shadow dancing on the wall,  
A silver tongue that speaks of fate’s delight,  
Yet leads me blind through corridors that call  
With phantom promises that shun the light.  
I chase thy riddles down the winding lane,  
Each answer lost within thy shifting guise;  
Desire’s echo, pleasure’s fleeting bane,  
A jest concealed behind thy clever lies.  
  Yet still I seek thee, though my trust be torn—  
  For from thy tricks, new wisdom is reborn.

1269

O gentle Soul, why chase the painted mist,  
When Love, unmasked, denies thee even this?  
The silver moon by nightly shadows kissed  
Reflects thy longing in its trembling bliss.  
Thine eyes pursue the dawn’s elusive hue,  
Yet morning breaks upon a barren field;  
The promises that once seemed fair and true  
Lie hidden now, by time’s cold hand concealed.  
Thou wander’st still, a pilgrim through the haze,  
While hope, a flickering lamp, begins to fade.  
What dreams may come to those who count the days  
And mark the hours by every vow betrayed?  
  Yet gentle Soul, take comfort in thy pain—  
  For even loss may teach the heart to reign.

1270

O gentle Soul, why chase the phantom’s gleam,  
Entwined in love’s illusion, aching true,  
Thy heart adrift within a waking dream,  
Yet blinded by the hope that guides thee through.  
The moonlight weaves its silver in thy hair,  
While shadows dance upon thy wistful face.  
Desires like restless tides, beyond compare,  
Return thee ever to that haunted place.  
Yet pause, sweet Soul, and let the silence speak—  
For oft in quiet dawns the truth is found.  
The trembling stars attend the words you seek  
And sow their wisdom gently on the ground.  
  In time, the dream shall fade as morning’s dew,  
  Revealing love’s bright world, serene and true.

1271

Illusion, be not cruel with my soul,  
Release thy silken grip, I beg thee, go—  
Thy painted visions slip beyond control,  
And leave me lost within thy subtle flow.  
Thou weav’st thy webs in moonlight’s gentle shade,  
Where truth and fancy dance in pale embrace,  
My reason, by thy haunting hand, betrayed,  
Seeks daylight’s mercy in thy phantom chase.  
Each fleeting hope a shadow in thy dream,  
Each whispered promise melts like morning dew;  
Yet still I wander, captive to thy scheme,  
And crave the world thy trembling veils construe.  
  But gentle specter, loosen now thy art,  
  That I may find the daylight in my heart.

1272

Illusion, spare me from thy silver tongue,  
For every dream by thee is deftly spun.  
Thy whispered promises, so sweetly sung,  
Dissolve like morning dew before the sun.  
Thou paint’st with colors born of cloud and mist  
A world more fair than ever truth could yield;  
Yet in thy arms, true joy is oft dismissed,  
And hope, unrooted, withers in the field.  
Thy gentle veil doth lead the heart astray,  
Ensnared by visions fashioned from the air;  
Reality, unkind, will have its say,  
And strip the soul of garlands false and fair.  
  Yet still I’m drawn where phantoms softly dwell—  
  For lies, in beauty wrapped, are sweet as well.

1273

Illusion, spare me from thy painted lies,  
Release my mind from webs that you devise.  
Thy filmy veils enchant the waking eyes,  
Yet underneath, the truth in shadow lies.  
Thou art a master, weaving dreams so fair,  
That hearts are fooled and reason led astray;  
A phantom’s touch, a whisper in the air,  
That turns the brightest noon to misty gray.  
What comfort lies in visions finely drawn,  
If substance flees before the morning sun?  
Let not my hopes be built on breath and fawn,  
Nor let my soul by empty promise run.  
  Let honest light dispel thy gilded art,  
  And grant me peace within an anchored heart.

1274

Illusion, gentle thief, release thy hold,  
Unmask thy silken lies entwined in me,  
Thy whispers paint the common dusk with gold,  
Yet leave my soul in want of clarity.  
Thou draw’st a veil o’er reason’s keenest eye,  
And bidst me wander halls of phantom light,  
Where dreams, like drifting mist, uncertain lie,  
Conceal the bounds of day and hush of night.  
How sweet thy comfort, though it breeds deceit,  
A honeyed balm that soothes the weary mind,  
Yet bitter in the waking’s cold retreat,  
When truth, uncloaked, proves gentler and more kind.  
  Release me now—let honest dawn unfold,  
  For in true light, my heart shall not be sold.

1275

Illusion, spare me from your painted lies,  
Why must you dress my longing in disguise?  
You shimmer, shifting gold before my eyes,  
A phantom promise draped in sweet surmise.  
Your gentle whispers haunt the silent night,  
They braid my reason with a silken thread,  
And draw my gaze away from honest light,  
To chase the shadows where my hopes are led.  
Yet, every vision born of you will fade,  
As morning’s truth dissolves the fragile dream,  
I wake, bewildered by the part you played,  
Unraveling the heart’s intricate seam.  
  Still, though I beg for clarity anew,  
  I find my soul enchanted still by you.

1276

O Illusion, gentle thief that haunts my night,  
Why dress my longing heart in borrowed grace?  
Thou draw’st my weary soul beyond my sight  
And paint’st a fairer world upon my face.  
With silver tongue thou whisper’st hope anew,  
Yet leave’st me grasping shadows at the dawn;  
What dreams hast thou entangled and untrue,  
That with the morning’s breath are swiftly gone?  
Yet in thy arms, sweet comfort do I find,  
A balm to wounds reality hath made.  
Though reason chides and would unmask my mind,  
Still gladly in thy woven spell I wade.  
  For life without thy tender, veiling art  
  Were stark indeed—a burden to the heart.

1277

Illusion, do not weave thy silk around  
My longing heart, nor mask what can’t be found.  
Let not thy golden mists my wits confound,  
Nor leave me lost where empty hopes abound.  
Thou art a gentle thief, with whispers sweet,  
Enticing dreams to blossom, then decay;  
Thy phantom promise draws me to defeat,  
While honest day would drive thy shades away.  
Yet still I feel thy touch upon my mind,  
A subtle warmth that chills when light is near.  
Why must I seek what truth forbids to find,  
And chase a vision fading year by year?  
  O, grant me strength thy snares at last to see,  
  And dwell in what is, not what cannot be.

1278

O let me see thee clear, though veiled in lies,  
For truth and falsehood wear thy borrowed face,  
The shifting shadows dance within thine eyes,  
And every glance conceals another grace.  
The heart, perplexed, doth falter at the gate  
Where hope and fear in silent battle meet,  
Yet still thy voice, though tremulous of late,  
Can draw my soul to kneel before thy feet.  
If trust be blind, then let me don its shade,  
And venture forth unguarded through the night,  
To seek the dawn where all deceits must fade,  
And morning sun reveals thee in its light.  
  For whether masked or bare, thy form I’ll see:  
  The truth of love resides in mystery.

1279

O let me pierce the veil that clouds your face,  
For truth and dreams within your eyes contend,  
A shifting shadow time cannot erase,  
Nor fickle winds of memory amend.  
Thy silence weaves a tapestry of doubt,  
Yet hope, a stubborn flower, lifts its head;  
I seek the words thy trembling lips leave out,  
And gather meaning from the tears unshed.  
The moonlight bathes thy brow in silver beams,  
Revealing secrets hidden in thy gaze;  
Thou art the keeper of a thousand dreams,  
A riddle wrapped in ever-changing haze.  
  O grant me passage to thy secret heart,  
  And let not fear our kindred souls depart.

1280

If all you are is shadow to my sight,  
Then let me dwell in dreams, not waking’s pain,  
For phantoms hold me closer in the night  
Than day’s cold truth that draws my hope in vain.  
Your voice, a whisper threading through my sleep,  
Entwines with longing’s echo in my breast;  
Its silver promise lulls my heart to weep  
And bids the restless soul in me find rest.  
Though dawn may chase your image from my eyes,  
Yet memory, like moonlight, clings to me,  
Preserving gentle ghosts that daylight flies  
And painting love on air I cannot see.  
  So let me linger where your shadows fall—  
  For dreams, not daylight, grant me you at all.

1281

If shadows are your truth, then let me yearn,  
For substance in the dream I cannot hold,  
While candle hopes within my spirit burn,  
Yet flicker in the draught of stories told.  
The moonlight paints illusions on the wall,  
Its silver brush distracts my waking mind,  
But when I reach, my grasp reveals the pall  
Of emptiness that longing leaves behind.  
Shall I pursue the echo of your sigh,  
Or anchor fast to earth, forsake the mist?  
Your phantom presence haunts my inward eye,  
A memory that lips have never kissed.  
  Still, through the shade, my heart’s desire is cast—  
  To love the ghost, until the night is past.

1282

Let not thy gaze betray what heart conceals,  
For truth and dream in thee are woven tight,  
A silent war that neither speaks nor heals,  
Yet kindles hope anew with every night.  
Within thine eyes, a tempest softly sways,  
Where longing treads the line 'twixt fear and fire,  
And shadows pirouette in gentle haze,  
Unmasking silent wounds and shy desire.  
The moon attends thy secrets with her light,  
And stars bear witness to thy veiled unrest;  
Yet dawn, in gold, dispels the lingering night,  
And bids thy guarded soul to be confessed.  
  So trust this heart, whose patience cannot feign,  
  For love unveiled brings balm to every pain.

1283

O let thy eyes not conjure dreams untrue,  
But pierce the veil that keeps my hope from thee;  
For in their depths, a world is born anew,  
A realm where love and longing wander free.  
The night bestows its secrets to thy gaze,  
And shadows flee before thy gentle light;  
Within thy glance, my soul would ever laze,  
And find its day amid the heart’s delight.  
Yet distance weaves a tapestry of doubt,  
A web that binds the heart in silent pain;  
But still I trust, though storms may rage without,  
Thy steadfast eyes will guide me home again.  
  Thus let me live beneath thy constant view,  
  And dream no dream unless I dream of you.

1284

O soul, why chase the shadows in the glass,  
When truth and falsehood struggle in your eyes?  
Each fleeting vision, ghosted as they pass,  
Distorts the heart with ever-shifting lies.  
The dawn dissolves illusions of the night,  
Yet stubborn dreams within thy core remain;  
They linger softly, trembling at the light,  
And murmur sweetly, promising no gain.  
What profit lies in haunting phantoms’ trace,  
When honest day awaits to warm thy face?  
Let broken mirrors fade without a trace,  
And bid the hollow specters yield their place.  
  Awake, O soul, from labyrinths of shade,  
  And seek the sun—be not by shadows swayed.

1285

O soul, why chase the shimmer of the mist,  
When solid ground beneath thy feet is missed?  
Dost thou pursue what never can exist,  
And find thy fortune in the shadows kissed?  
The morning dew dissolves upon the lawn,  
Yet longing leads thee where the vapors stray;  
The hope of dawn is oftentimes withdrawn  
When dreams like fleeting phantoms slip away.  
Yet, in thy quest for truth beyond the veil,  
Thou learn’st the weight and measure of desire.  
For though the starry visions sometimes fail,  
Their distant glow can set thy heart afire.  
  Embrace the earth and sky in equal part:  
  For both the mist and stone compose the heart.

1286

O soul, why chase the shadow, not the sun,  
When truth and falsehood wrestle in thy chest?  
What profit lies in dreams when day is done,  
Or restless longing granting little rest?  
The golden beams await behind the cloud,  
Yet fixèd eyes see only fleeting shade;  
Thy inner war grows silent, never loud,  
As fears like evening mist begin to fade.  
Seek not the hollow praise of fickle days,  
Nor let the phantom hopes thy thoughts enthrall;  
Within thy heart, let steadfast reason blaze,  
And banish night’s illusions, one and all.  
  Turn to the dawn, where honesty is spun—  
  O soul, embrace the light, and be as one.

1287

O gentle soul, why chase the phantom gleam,  
When truth and longing war within thy chest?  
Dost thou pursue a shadow in thy dream,  
Or seek the light that grants the spirit rest?  
The silver moon doth whisper from the skies  
Of hopes that flicker, fragile in the night;  
Yet dawn reveals how many a sweet disguise  
Can turn to ash beneath the morning’s light.  
What solace lies in chasing fickle shade,  
When every hour slips silent through thy hand?  
Let not thy heart by empty promise swayed,  
But anchor deep where steadfast truths shall stand.  
  For dreams may fade as morning songs depart,  
  Yet truth’s bright flame shall kindle in thy heart.

1288

O let me dwell where shadows mask the truth,  
And beg your heart to see my soul anew,  
For in the silent chambers of my youth  
Did hope take root, and sorrow gently grew.  
The moonlight weaves its silver on my face,  
While memories, like ghosts, around me play;  
I chase the echoes time cannot erase,  
Yet dawn will drag the darkest dreams away.  
If you but grant one glance with softened eyes,  
And lift the veil that hides your tender mind,  
My spirit, long imprisoned by disguise,  
Might soar again, unburdened and unlined.  
  So let affection’s gentle hand impart  
  The light that breaks the shadow from my heart.

1289

O soul, why chase the shadows love has thrown,  
When truth stands shivering outside your door?  
The moon laments in silence, all alone,  
While hope lies weeping, trembling on the floor.  
What spectral promises entice thy heart,  
That in the dusk it wanders, tempest-tossed?  
Each whispered vow, a ghost that will depart,  
Each tender glance, a dream already lost.  
Yet dawn awaits beyond the midnight’s reign,  
Her golden fingers warming frostbitten fears.  
Forsake illusions spun of longing’s pain,  
And let the gentle truth dissolve thy tears.  
  Embrace the morning’s honest, shining ray:  
  Where shadows flee, let love and reason stay.

1290

Grant me the grace to know what’s false from true,  
For shadows veil the light I seek within,  
The lies and truths entwined in tangled hue,  
A silent war of virtue and of sin.  
My heart, a compass turning in the mist,  
Longs for the dawn to pierce this clouded night,  
Yet doubts, like creeping ivy, still persist,  
And choke the roots of reason from the light.  
O Muse, bestow the wisdom of the sage,  
To read the signs that honest souls impart,  
Unmask deception’s ever-shifting stage,  
And plant the seeds of honor in my heart.  
  So may I walk with clarity anew,  
  And hold the torch of truth in all I do.

1291

O let me break the mirror of my mind,  
For shadows shape the truth I seek to find.  
Within the glass, my hopes and doubts entwined,  
A thousand selves in flickering light confined.  
Each phantom thought doth cloud the inward eye,  
Reflections deep as night obscure the day;  
What once was certain now doth twist and lie,  
Reality and dream dissolve to gray.  
Yet if I shatter all these fragile panes,  
And sweep away the shards of false disguise,  
Perchance the soul unmasked at last remains,  
And in the open air, my spirit flies.  
  So let me dare the break, though sharp it be—  
  To find, at last, the truest form of me.

1292

O let me not be captive to my dream,  
While truth’s cold fingers tear my visions down,  
Yet hope, like moonlight, casts a silver beam  
That softly weaves a halo for my crown.  
Within the shadowed halls where wishes stray,  
I pace the borders twixt delight and dread,  
And watch the dawn extinguish night to day,  
While silent ghosts of longing haunt my bed.  
Though reason bids me lay illusions low,  
And cautions me to trust the waking light,  
Still, in my heart, forbidden rivers flow  
And carry fragments of my soul’s delight.  
  Let not the world deny what dreams impart:  
  For in their fire, I find my truer heart.

1293

O, let me break the glass of your disguise,  
For shadows claim the truth my heart denies.  
Thy laughter veils the sorrow in thine eyes,  
A storm concealed beneath the calmest skies.  
What secrets linger on thy guarded tongue,  
What tales are caged behind thy silent art?  
If every whispered hope had once been sung,  
Would I have found the threshold of thy heart?  
Yet still I beg the dawn to show me clear  
The lineaments that night would keep unknown.  
For in thy presence, doubt and faith appear  
Entwined like ivy climbing coldest stone.  
  So drop thy mask, let honest daylight see—  
  And let thy soul stand unadorned to me.

1294

O gentle eyes, deceive my heart no more,  
For truth and fantasy in me contend,  
Thy glances tempt me to a distant shore  
Where dreams and waking reason never blend.  
I wander lost between thy gaze and sense,  
A captive to the promise in thy light,  
And seek in hope a love without pretense,  
Yet shadows veil thy soul from mortal sight.  
If looks be lies, then let me ne’er be wise,  
But dwell in sweet delusion’s fair embrace;  
For in thy gaze, a thousand heavens rise  
And grant my spirit wings in fleeting grace.  
  Yet still I beg—let honesty impart,  
  A gentle peace to my divided heart.

1295

Oh, let me dwell within your mirrored gaze,  
Where truth and falsehood twist in shadowed light,  
A labyrinth of dreams in silver haze,  
Reflections dancing softly out of sight.  
Each glance reveals a world both old and new,  
A thousand selves that flicker in the glass,  
The selves I lost, the selves I never knew,  
Each moment shifting as the ages pass.  
Your eyes, a portal deep as hidden seas,  
Invite my soul to wander and inquire,  
To chase the phantoms carried by the breeze,  
And kindle hope from embers of desire.  
  Yet in your gaze, I find both night and sun—  
  The mirrored truth that we are both as one.
